T12.67 / T12.68 done (cards added to M12 on 2026-10-08 at the maintainer's request) — `archetypes/arpg/projectile.gd` (`RpgProjectile`: `create(shooter, from, direction, team, speed, range, style)`, layers of its team's hitbox plus world 1, `advance`, `hit` (spent on the first hurtbox of the other team, even an invulnerable one), `build_info` (the melee formula with the attack captured at firing, `heavy`), `finish`; drawn in `_draw`; group `rpg_projectiles`; held while a sequence runs), `components/enemy_shoot.gd` and `components/enemy_charge.gd` (windup with a telegraph tint; the shot flies parallel to the feet-to-feet line from the body centre, because aiming from the body at the player's feet passed under the hurtbox — found by the smoke step; the charge dashes with `movement.speed` raised and the hitbox live, ends after `dash_s` or when blocked three frames, recovers, and a cancel restores speed and hitbox). `RpgEnemyAi.kind` from `behavior.kind` (unknown kinds fight as melee); the components are made in `_ready` when an older scene lacks them; `_update_chase_ranged` (shooters back off inside `keep_away_px`, close in beyond 0.9 x the range, else stand; turrets never move and never wander). `RpgItemDefinition.ranged`; `RpgPlayerCombat.weapon_is_ranged()` / `fire_arrow()` (14 tiles/s, 12 tiles, at the moment the swing would go live; no swing effect). `RpgUtil.map_tile_px` replaces three copies of the tile-size lookup. Recipes: `arpg/enemy_recipes.gd` (`RECIPE_VERSION` 2: the new behavior keys, `kind_for(slug)` from KIND_WORDS, `<starter>_charger.tres` / `_shooter.tres` when the demo is on); `second_map_recipe` gives the action-RPG field's two enemies those definitions; `data_recipe` (`RECIPE_VERSION` 6) marks the bow ranged. Found by `test_rebuilding_leaves_the_arpg_files_untouched`: the first version picked the starter slug through `starter_scene_at`, which only counts scenes that exist, so the input hash moved after the first build and a no-op rebuild rewrote the enemy scene (Godot's random node ids); the slug now comes from the catalog. Tests: `test_ranged_combat.gd` (8); `godot-test` 407. Smoke steps `ranged_enemy_shoots`, `charger_charges` and `player_bow_shoots` (after `dodge`, in `M9_STEPS`); `test_m9_sample.py` passes (10). Not verified: how the drawn shots look in a window, and a turret in a real game (its chase branch is shared with the shooter's).

T12.69 done (card added 2026-10-08) — `world/followers.gd` (`RpgFollowers`: boot creates it under SceneRouter as `Followers`, `enabled` from the new boot export `followers`; `update()` each physics frame (PAUSABLE) rebuilds the follower nodes when the map, the party or a freed node says so, records the player's position into a trail (newest first, a point per pixel moved, `trail_points_needed` long) and places member i at `(i + 1)` tiles back along it (`point_along` follows the corners), facing the way the path goes; the walk animation plays while the player moved in the last `WALK_HOLD_FRAMES` (6) frames, because a slow walk does not move a whole pixel every frame). A follower is a Node2D with an AnimatedSprite2D: the member's `sprite_frames`, else the player's (then also the player's offset); a member's own frames stand bottom-centre on the feet (a new SpriteFrames has an empty `default` animation, so the size comes from the first animation with a frame). Followers are added beside the player (its Y-sorted parent), have no body and are not saved. Tests: `test_followers.gd` (4); `godot-test` 411. Smoke step `followers_trail` (after `party_members`, in both M6 lists, basic16 slice `[:24]`): a member joins, its follower stands beside the player, 80 px walked to the right leave it one tile behind and walking, it stands when the player stands, and leaving the party removes it. The step switches the player's collision mask off while it walks them: the first run walked into a wall, physics pushed the teleported player back and the follower rightly stood still. `test_m6_sample.py` and `test_generate_basic16.py` pass (12). Not verified: how followers look with a real party in a window, and around doors (they start on the player after every map change).

T12.70 done (card added 2026-10-08) — `world/gate.gd` (`RpgGate`, StaticBody2D on world 1, and interactables 5 when it takes a key; `should_open()` = the flag `<entity_id>.unlocked` or the condition `open_when`; `refresh()` on every `entity_state_changed` and `variable_changed`, so it also closes again; `set_open` disables the collision shapes deferred and hides it; `try_unlock` uses the key up unless `consume_key` is off; `item_name` shared with the door; a default 1-tile shape standing on the origin and drawn bars unless there is a "Visual" child), `world/switch.gd` (`RpgSwitch`: PLATE watches the player body (mask 2), latches unless `hold`; LEVER on layer 5, flips on interact, `once`; flag `<entity_id>.on`; `toggled` and `Events.switch_toggled`; drawn plate or lever), `RpgDoor.key_item` / `consume_key` / `entity_id` with `is_locked` and `try_unlock` (a walk-in door that stays locked shows the message on every entry). Doors now have an `entity_id` property, so `EntityIds.entity_nodes` lists them; that changes nothing for the id tools because doors are not persistent and empty ids are skipped (the existing tests are unchanged). Strings `UI_LOCKED`, `UI_LOCKED_NEED`, `UI_UNLOCKED`; events `switch_toggled`, `gate_changed`; an opening gate emits `door_used` for the door sound. Dock: Add gate / Add floor switch / Add lever (`placement.gd` `plan_lock` / `build_lock` / `add_lock` / `switch_condition`: under YSorted/Entities, snapped, ids `<map>_gate_<n>` and `<map>_switch_<n>`, a switch planned with one gate selected sets that gate's `open_when` in the same undo action). VAL-025 (`_check_locks`). The generated README gains section 15. Tests: `test_locks.gd` (6); `godot-test` 417. Smoke step `keys_and_switches` (after `sequence_plays`, both M6 lists, basic16 slice `[:25]`): three gates and two switches made around the player; a physics point query shows the closed gate blocks; pressing interact without the key leaves it shut, with the key opens it (key used, flag set, no longer blocking); the lever opens and closes its gate; stepping on the plate opens the third; all removed afterwards. Passed on the first run on the sample and basic16. Not verified: the dock buttons in the GUI editor (the plans are unit-tested), drawn gates and switches in a window, and a locked walk-in door in a real game (unit-tested).

T12.71 done (card added 2026-10-08) — battle depth for the turn-based archetype. `RpgStats.max_mp` / `mp` (in `STAT_NAMES`, `to_dict` / `from_dict`; a save without them reads 0; `add_experience` adds `MP_PER_LEVEL` 2 only to someone with MP and refills it); `RpgUtil.max_mp`; refills in `new_game`, `join_party`, `heal_party_full`, `give_experience_to`; `_set_worn` clamps mp like hp; the "mp" item effect in `RpgInventory.use`; the status menu and the member picker show MP when there is some. `archetypes/turn_based/skill_definition.gd` (`RpgSkillDefinition`: kind, targets, mp_cost, power, element, status + chance, cures; `label()` humanizes an untranslated SKILL_ key; `problems()`; `load_dir(dir)`). It lives in the turn-based folder, not `data/`, because an action-RPG project has no turn-based folder and the class needs `RpgBattleStatus`; for the same reason the skills path is `RpgBattleController.SKILLS_DIR` (the M6 content-path rule allows that file) and VAL-026 in `content_checks.gd` loads the turn-based scripts by path and calls their static functions through the Script, skipping the check when they are absent (the first M9 run failed with "RpgBattleStatus not declared" in the content checks). `battle_status.gd` (`RpgBattleStatus.TABLE`: poison, regen, sleep, silence, blind, haste, slow with rounds / tick / skip / wake / mute / hit / speed / bad / on). `RpgBattleActor`: `statuses`, `chosen_skill`, `used_rules`, `stat()` applies the speed factor (so turn order and escape see haste and slow), `stat_f`, `max_mp`, `take_damage` / `restore_hp` / `restore_mp`, `pay_mp` / `can_pay` / `free_casting` (an enemy with max_mp 0), `worn()`, `element_rate` (the lowest rate the definition and the worn items list, 1.0 when none does), `is_immune`, `attack_element`, `attack_status`, `add_status` (haste and slow cancel), `can_act`, `can_cast`, `hit_factor`, `wake`. `RpgBattleMath.hit_chance` (0.95 + speed difference x 0.02 - evade, clamped 0.3..1, x blind), `roll_hit` and `roll_chance` (no draw at 1 or 0), `roll_magic`, `roll_heal`, `apply_rate`. `battle_effects.gd` (`RpgBattleEffects`: `attack`, `strike` (a sleeping target is always hit), `damage` (immune / absorb / weak / resisted lines, waking, falling), `heal`, `inflict`, `cure`, `use_skill`, `end_of_round`). `battle_ai.gd` (`RpgBattleAi`: `from_rules`, `plan` (a command or a skill id; a status skill aims at someone without the status), `pick` (random / weakest / strongest / self), `holds` and its atoms, `problems_of`). Controller: `skills`, `skill()`, `skills_of()`, `targets_of()`, `candidates(actor, command, skill_id)`, `aims_at_all`, `skill_id` in choices, an idle choice for whoever cannot act and an "is asleep" line for whoever fell asleep before its turn, `_end_round` after the actions (only while undecided), `_log`; enemy choice = rules, then the heal item, then the weights through `RpgBattleAi.plan`, so the old draw order (one weighted roll, one pick) is unchanged. `TARGET_ALL_ALLIES`; the attack command goes through `RpgBattleEffects.attack`; the skill command is real (label `BATTLE_SKILL`; `BATTLE_SKILLS_NONE` kept as "Skill" for older seeded .tres files that store that label); the item command handles "cure" (kept when nothing is cured) and "mp". UI: the skill list reuses the item submenu, MP in the numbers, statuses after the name, `_active` for the turn marker. Scene: "Miss" and the status name float over the target. Strings: 35 battle keys plus SKILL_* (the battle key test now allows the SKILL_ prefix), `UI_MP`. Recipes: `battle_commands_recipe` v2 seeds `game/data/skills/{fire,cure,sleep,venom}.tres`; `data_recipe` v7 gives the turn-based hero magic 1, 6 MP and fire, the companion 12 MP and cure / sleep (fire at 3), and seeds `antidote` and `ether` (in the shop); turn-based `enemy_recipes` v2 cycles three trait sets (weak to fire; resists fire + a 40 % venom rule when a foe is not poisoned; 6 MP, fire every third round on the weakest, sleep-immune). VAL-026. Tests: `test_battle_depth.gd` (20, one of them drives the battle UI outside the tree: the skill list, picking an all-enemies skill, MP numbers, status tags, silence); two battle-controller tests changed: the failed-escape test's fixed roll of 0.99 also made every attack miss (hit chance 0.99), so it goes back to 0.5 after the escape round, and the placeholder-skill test now checks `BATTLE_SKILL`; `godot-test` 437. Smoke step `skills_and_status` (M7 list, after `random_encounter`): a copy of the starter enemy that is weak to fire, moves first, never misses, has evade -5 (never dodges) and poisons with every hit; round 1 the hero casts the seeded fire (members defend), the log of round 1 must show the spell, the weak spot, the poison and its tick, and the hero must have paid 3 MP; round 2 wins. The M7 data test checks the seeded skills and items; the M9 test checks an action RPG gets neither. Passed with Godot 4.7.2 (M6, basic16, M7, M9). Not verified: how the skill list, the MP numbers, the status tags and the floating Miss look in a window, choosing a one-target skill with the arrow (it shares `_begin_target` with items), and translations of the new keys beyond English.

Smoke fixes found by the synthetic-pack suite (`test_all_synthetic_packs`, which the T12.53 / T12.69 work had not been run against). `followers_trail` failed on the `strips` pack: it has no idle animation, so a stopped follower correctly shows the walk paused on frame 0 (`RpgAnimationDriver.play`'s idle fallback), and the step only looked at the animation name; it now also asks `is_playing()`. `random_encounter` failed once on `rpgmaker_like` ("walking in an every-step zone did not start a battle", the step took 578 ms) while the machine was busy with other Godot runs, and passed on every rerun, so the cause is not confirmed. The likely one is timing: steps are not counted while the router is busy (the fade after the previous step's map changes), so the battle could start on the fourth and last tile, and the step looked for the battle scene two frames later, before the fade had swapped it in. The step now waits for the router to be idle, stops walking as soon as `combat.is_in_combat()`, and walks up to eight tiles. Both packs passed twice in a row under the same load afterwards.

T12.25 done — view-models for the Project and Options pages. `rpggen/gui/models/` imports nothing from PySide6 (a unit test starts a fresh interpreter, imports both models and checks `sys.modules`). `forms.py`: `form_error` / `form_missing` / `form_ok` and the codes moved here from `pages/base.py` (which re-exports them), plus `FieldStatus`, `hint` and `status_of` (the worst issue, else the ok text) that replace the page's `set_hint` / `set_status`. `project_model.py`: `InputCheck` and `check_inputs`, `prefill_name`, `format_size`, `sample_target`, `update_summary`, `Inputs` (the four fields; `key()`), `ProjectModel` (the touched flags, the suggested name and folder, the check generation, pack updates; the four field lines, the pack cards and row texts, `validate`, `offers_download`, `new_sources`, `suggestions`, `diff_update`, `record_update`, `load` / `store`), `godot_choices`, and the config steps `analysed_hash`, `config_with_packs`, `default_config`, `load_previous`, `needs_analysis`, `refresh_config`. `options_model.py`: the page's constants and `OptionsModel` (the draft and its copy on entry, `apply_to`, the git box, the demo wish and `dependent_features` -> `FeatureState`, the screen hint, ground choices, `starter_state` -> `StarterState`, `store_starter`, `preview_options`, imported maps, the input table (`input_rows` / `read_input`), `collect(OptionValues)`, `validate`, the summary and the cached pack size, presets). The models take a `tr` callable; the pages pass their own `self.tr`, so nothing changes for T12.56 (no translator is installed yet). A model has no widgets, so the page splits needed widget modules too, or neither page gets under 500 lines (most of the Options page's rules were already in `core/options.py`): `widgets/pack_list.py` (`PackCardDelegate`, `PackList`, `CARD_ROLE`), `widgets/project_form.py` (the Project page's fields), `widgets/godot_download.py` (the confirmation and the download), `widgets/size_editor.py`, `widgets/options_sections.py` (`PresetBar`, `FeatureGroups`, `StarterMapBox`, `ImportedMapsBox`, `ScreenBox`, `LayersBox`, `VersionControlBox`, `AdvancedOptions`) and `dialogs/options_dialogs.py`. The pages keep every attribute and override hook the tests and the window use (`page.pack_list`, `page.feature_boxes`, `page.confirm_godot_download`, `page.run_dialog`, ...) as names for the section widgets, and still export `CARD_ROLE`, `format_size`, `prefill_name`, `DEFAULTS_ITEM` and `ManagePresetsDialog`; `OptionsPage.state` and `draft` are now read-only properties of the model. Two section attributes were renamed because they hid `QWidget` methods (`size` -> `size_editor`, `move` -> `move_row`; the page's `screen` section is `screen_box`). No GUI test changed. Pages: `project_page.py` 1144 -> 473 lines, `options_page.py` 1301 -> 488. Tests: `test_project_model.py` (15), `test_options_model.py` (13). Not verified: the pages by eye in a window (the layout is the same widgets in the same order; the squeezed-layout test passes).

T12.51 done — credits from the pack licences. `rpggen/core/licenses.py`: `read_text` (text suffixes only, the first 64 KiB, UTF-8 then cp1252, HTML and RTF markup removed), `detect` over (name, text) pairs with licence files first, then credits / attribution, then readmes (the first licence found wins, author and URL fill in from any file): OGA-BY, the Creative Commons BY family from a licence URL, the spelled-out name or the short form (`CC-BY-NC-SA-3.0`; no version reads 4.0), CC0, MIT, RPG Maker terms ("only ... RPG Maker"), then "commercial use" wording; author lines (`Author:`, `Art by:`, `Created by X`, `Copyright (c) 2022 X`, "please credit X"), URL lines (`Website:`, `Homepage:`, ...; else the first URL that is not a licence host). `attribution_required` comes from the licence (CC BY, OGA-BY, MIT yes; CC0 and RPG Maker no) and, for a vendor's or an unknown licence, from the wording ("credit is required", "please credit"; "no credit required" wins). `label`, `license_url`, `effective` (a licence chosen on the Audit page without "credit required" brings its own rule). `CatalogPack.license` (always written) and `license_overrides` (written when not empty; carried to a new analysis by pack id in `_carry_previous`); both optional in the schema, so older catalogs load (an unknown licence); `CATALOG_VERSION` stays 2. ASSET-028 is raised by `requirements.check` (generation time, from the effective licence), not during the analysis, so an author entered on the Audit page clears it. `GENERATION_REPORT.md` shows the licence, author and the credit rule under each pack; ASSET-028 is in its Licenses group. Audit page: a *Packs* tab (`widgets/pack_info.py`, `PackInfoPanel` / `PackLicenseBox`: licence combo, author, URL, "credit the pack", the licence files read, a warning while credit is required and no author is known); edits go through `edits.set_pack_license` (one undo step; a value equal to the one without the correction removes it). Builder: `credits_recipe.gd` (after `data_recipe`) writes `generated/data/credits.tres` on every build and seeds `game/data/credits_extra.tsv` once (header and notes only, so nothing shows until the user adds a line); it mirrors `licenses.effective` / `label` / `license_url` in GDScript (tested on both sides). Credits data is a `.tres` (`RpgCredits`), not JSON, because the export preset has no include filter, so a .json or .tsv would not be in an exported game; the extra lines are baked in, and `RpgCredits.extra_lines()` reads the .tsv when it exists so a run from the editor shows an edit without a rebuild. Runtime: `RpgCreditsMenu` (own lines by section, "Art and sound" with "Name by Author", the licence with its link and the pack URL, then the engine's MIT notice; up / down scroll in `_input`, Back or cancel closes), the *Credits* button on the title screen and in the pause menu (looked up with `get_node_or_null`), `ui_recipes` v9, the focus walk covers the new menu (VAL-011 list). `credits.gd` joins the files the M6 content-path rule allows (it names `game/data/credits_extra.tsv`). Pack names are the humanized pack id. Tests: `test_licenses.py` (22, fixture texts in `tests/unit/license_texts/`), `test_pack_info.py` (3), `test_credits.gd` (6); `godot-test` 443. Smoke step `credits_screen` (M6 list after `keys_and_switches`, basic16 slice `[:26]`): both menus have the button, the resource and the seed exist, every pack of the build is named with a licence line, the engine is listed. Goldens: every pack gains `license` (CC0 for 15 synthetic packs, unknown for one); the sample pack is unchanged. Not verified: the credits screen and the Packs tab by eye in a window, and real vendor licence files beyond the fixtures.

T12.50 done — frame sequences and more actions. `adapters/sequences.py` (`SequencesAdapter`, priority 55, between `lpc` and `strips`): the still images of a folder that share a stem without its trailing number are a sequence (`split_number`: the number follows `_`, `-` or a space, or stands in brackets; a number glued to the name, as in `tileset2`, is part of the name). The character and the animation come from the stem and then from the folders above it (`hero/walk/0001.png`, `hero/walk/down/0001.png`, `hero/walk/down_01.png`). A sequence needs two frames of one size; frames sort by number, then path; a missing number gives ADAPT-004 with the missing numbers (five at most) and the frames that exist are used. Each animation is composited into `generated/frame_strips/<sha>.png`, where the sha is over the frame hashes and the column count, so the name does not depend on the PNG encoder; a strip wider than 4096 px wraps into rows (`frames` are `[column, row]`). The sequences of one character (folder, name) with one frame size are one sprite asset whose path is the first frame of the first sequence in (folder, stem) order; the other frames are `consumed`. Frames of another size become a second asset. `side` gets a mirrored left with ANIM-002 as in `strips`; `directions` is 8 when a diagonal exists. `naming.py` now owns the action and direction words (`ACTIONS`, `ACTION_PATTERN`, `DIRECTION_PATTERN`, `CANONICAL_ACTIONS`, `DEFAULT_FPS`, `fps`), and `STRIP_NAME` is built from them, so whole strips know the new actions and directions too. `normalize_name` joins a vertical and a horizontal word in either order into a diagonal and maps sw / se / nw / ne (also southwest, downleft, ...). Of the new actions fall, block, climb and swim loop; the others play once. Runtime: `RpgAnimationDriver.play(base, facing, dir)` and `dir_to_facing8`, see the runtime changelog; `godot-test` 449. Synthetic pack `sequences` (46 images: unpadded numbers up to 10, eight directions, `-` and `(n)` separators, a folder per animation, a gap, three numbered gems that must stay items) and its golden; no other golden moved and the corpus stays at 1.000 on all four measures. Decisions and deviations: (1) a sequence needs an **action word**, which the card does not say: without it the twelve `items/item_NN.png` of the `mixed` pack and every numbered variant set would be merged into one animation and disappear from the audit; an animation without an action word (`slime_01.png`) therefore stays separate images, to be joined by hand. (2) Frames of different sizes are not composited (trimmed exports have no common origin to align on); the group is left to the other adapters. (3) `sprite_frames_recipe.gd` is in the card's file list but did not change: it already reads a per-animation `source` and `[column, row]` frames, so `RECIPE_VERSION` stays 2. (4) Files outside the list: `actors/actor.gd` and `world/followers.gd` pass the direction of travel to the driver (the driver alone only knows the four-way facing); `cache_policy.py` and `storage_dialog.py` list the new `frame_strips` cache folder (used when `assemble` is called without a work folder, as for GIF strips). (5) `RpgActor.facing` stays four-way: hitboxes, interaction, saves and sequences read it, so the diagonals are a matter of which picture is shown. Enemies get them too, because the actor reads its velocity. (6) "Nearest direction" is limited to 45 degrees before the old `down` fallback, because the existing test `test_missing_up_falls_back_to_down` fixes that order. Not verified: an eight-direction character by eye in a running game, and real packs with numbered frames beyond the synthetic one.

T12.52 done — Web, Linux and macOS export presets. `export_presets.template.cfg` now holds one section per target after a `; target: <key>` line (`[preset.{index}]`); `export_check.TARGETS` gives each target its preset name, export path, release template file and test file, in the order of `config.EXPORT_TARGETS` (windows, web, linux, macos). The default render (Windows only) is byte-identical to the old one, so no golden moved. `export_targets` (optional, omitted when `["windows"]`) chooses the presets; `selected_export_targets()` orders them and falls back to Windows. `write_presets` on an existing file keeps every line and appends a preset for each selected target whose name is missing (numbered after the highest `[preset.N]`): the config decides which presets exist, the user's edits to them win. The check exports once per selected target with installed templates (`web_nothreads_release.zip`, `linux_release.x86_64`, `macos.zip`, `windows_release_x86_64*`), logging to `godot_export_<target>.log` (`godot_export.log` for Windows, as before); the phase is reported as skipped (ENV-010) only when no target ran. ENV-010 now names the target (`{target}`), and the report's *Checks not run* uses the run's issue texts. Probe and keys: `godot_api_verified.md`. Deviation: the spec says macOS *unsigned*; the preset uses Godot's built-in ad-hoc signature (no identity, no notarization) because a binary with no signature at all does not start on Apple Silicon. Only Windows templates for 4.7.1 are installed here, so the per-platform integration test skips locally.

T12.63 done — Linux and macOS app packages. `tools/package.py` builds the packages of the platform it runs on: Windows unchanged (portable zip, installer); Linux `rpggen-<v>-linux-x86_64.tar.gz` and `rpggen-<v>-x86_64.AppImage` (AppDir from `packaging/linux/`: `AppRun` with a `cli` sub-command, `rpggen.desktop`, a 256 px `rpggen.png` made from `rpggen.ico`; appimagetool 1.9.1 and type2 runtime 20251108 pinned by the SHA-256 GitHub lists for the assets, run with `APPIMAGE_EXTRACT_AND_RUN=1` and `--runtime-file` so nothing else is fetched); macOS `rpggen-<v>-macos-<arch>.zip` of `dist/rpggen.app` made with `ditto` (keeps links and modes). The spec only sets the Windows version resource on Windows and adds `BUNDLE` on macOS (`io.github.docdamage.rpggen`, `LSMinimumSystemVersion` 13.0 from the PySide6 universal2 wheel; the GUI is `CFBundleExecutable`, `rpggen-cli` sits beside it). Nothing is copied into the app after the build, because PyInstaller's ad-hoc signature would no longer match. `release.yml` has `windows`, `linux` (ubuntu-22.04), `macos` (macos-14, arm64) and `publish` jobs; publish writes one `SHA256SUMS.txt` and fails when a platform's file is missing. Locks: `requirements/release-linux.txt` and `release-macos.txt` are compiled with uv with `release.txt` as a constraint (same versions; a test checks it); macOS adds `macholib`, both drop pefile, pywin32-ctypes and colorama. PySide6 6.11.2 has Linux wheels for glibc 2.34+ only, so the Linux floor is set by Qt (Ubuntu 22.04 is 2.35). `godot_fetch`: `current_platform()`, the macOS zip (`Godot_v<v>-stable_macos.universal.zip`, pinned SHA-512 checked against the release list on 2026-10-08) is extracted as a tree; every member must lie under `Godot.app/`, links are refused, the executable bit becomes 0755; the console is `Godot.app/Contents/MacOS/Godot` (layout read from the zip's central directory with a range request). `managed_executable()` now defaults to this computer's platform: before, Linux looked for the Windows file name. `DOWNLOAD_MB` per platform (85/80/170) replaces the "about 60 MB" text. `auto_find_godot(platform)`: PATH (`Godot*.exe` on Windows, any executable `godot*` elsewhere), the managed copy first, then `%LOCALAPPDATA%/Programs/Godot*`, `/Applications/Godot*.app` and `~/Applications` (`godot.MAC_APP_DIRS`), or `~/.local/bin/godot*`.

Found while testing the Linux build in WSL (Ubuntu 24.04, the Linux Godot 4.7.2): the frozen CLI's first smoke run failed with Godot import exit -6, and a stand-in Godot showed that the bootloader passes `LD_LIBRARY_PATH=<bundle>/_internal`, `QT_PLUGIN_PATH` and `_PYI_*` to every child, so Godot could load the bundle's copies of system libraries. `rpggen/procenv.py`: the entry points call `restore_library_path()` (the user's `LD_LIBRARY_PATH_ORIG`, or none; the loader read the variable at process start, so the app is unaffected), and Godot, git, 7-Zip and the detached editor get `child_env()`, which also drops the Qt plugin paths and `_PYI_*`. After the fix the stand-in sees none of them; the smoke test via `package.py`, the AppImage's `cli generate`, the tar.gz and `sha256sum -c` all pass in WSL, and the frozen GUI starts offscreen. The failure did not come back in eight runs before the fix either, so it was intermittent; the leak was real either way. macOS was not built here (no Mac): its job, the `BUNDLE` and the `ditto` zip are checked only by CI.

T12.58 done — conflict resolver. `rpggen/core/conflicts.py`: `list_conflicts` (every `*.rpggen-new` outside `.rpggen_*`, `.git`, `.godot`), `find_base`, `versions`, `merge3`/`merge2`/`merge_text`, `resolve(target, rel, keep_mine|take_new|merge)`. Ledger entries get the optional `declined_sha256` (schema and 02_SCHEMAS); `promote.plan` skips a MODIFIED file whose new hash equals it, and `final_ledger` already kept the old entry of a modified file it did not write, so the field survives. `ledger.hash_bytes(data, rel)` hashes a `.rpggen-new` copy as the file it stands for (CRLF normalised for text) — hashing the copy by its own name would never match. Actions: each backs up the file, its `.rpggen-new` and the ledger into a new `.rpggen_backups` folder first (no rotation then; the next regeneration rotates), so *Restore previous* undoes them; a failure writes the backed-up files back (and removes a file Take new created) and raises REGEN-004. Keep mine sets `declined_sha256` = hash(new). Take new writes new and sets `sha256` = hash(new), without a declined hash: the file is then UNTOUCHED and later runs overwrite it. Merge sets both `sha256` and `declined_sha256` to hash(new): the same generated content is not offered again, and the next different one conflicts against the right base. Deviation/addition: the spec takes the base only from backups, but on a *first* conflict no backup has it (promotion backs up only what it overwrites, and the user's modified file is not overwritten). `find_base` therefore also searches git (`vcs.committed_versions`: the last 20 commits that changed the file, `git show <id>:<rel>` as bytes), since rpggen commits each generation by default (T12.12). Merge: diff3 over `difflib.SequenceMatcher(autojunk=False)` matches; a region only one side changed takes that side, an identical change is taken once, anything else (including changes on neighbouring lines, as in GNU diff3 and git) gets `<<<<<<< yours` / `||||||| last generated` / `=======` / `>>>>>>> rpggen`; without a base each difference is a region. The result keeps the user's line endings; markers always start a line. Binary files cannot be merged (REGEN-010); a file without `.rpggen-new` is REGEN-009 — two codes beyond the milestone's reserved list, added to 03_ERROR_CODES first. GUI: `dialogs/conflict_dialog.py` (file list, Last generated / Yours / New panes with changed lines highlighted via the new `diff_changed` theme token, the base pane hidden without a base, Merge disabled for binaries, a note for `.tscn`/`.tres`); the Results page shows **Resolve conflicts (N)…** while any are left, the REGEN-001 fix opens the dialog at the issue's file (`Issue.file`; the placeholder opened the folder), and *File > Resolve conflicts…* works on the open project. CLI `rpggen conflicts --target X [--keep-mine|--take-new|--merge] [file]`, `--json`.

T12.55 done — input glyphs. `addons/rpg_runtime/ui/input_glyphs.gd` (`RpgInputGlyphs`, static): `device_of(event)` (pressed key or mouse button -> keyboard; pressed pad button or an axis past 0.5 -> the pad's layout; releases, drift and mouse motion -> ""), `layout_for_name` (PlayStation / Nintendo / Xbox by name fragments, else `pad`), `glyph(action, device="")`, `binding_text`, `event_text`, `button_name` (SDL positions: index 0 is A / Cross / B), `axis_name`, `hint(pairs)`. Decision: the device is tracked by the existing `RpgSettings` autoload (`input_device`, `input_device_changed`, `note_input` from `_input`) instead of a new autoload, so `project.godot` and the project-settings recipe do not change; the settings node already owns key remapping. `_input` of an autoload runs after the scene's own `_input` handlers, so an event a scene marks handled there does not switch the device; the game's handlers use `_unhandled_input`. Text glyphs only: no art is bundled, so `LICENSE` and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` are unchanged; `glyph()` returns text, never a texture. Users: the interaction prompt (`[E]`; `key_text` keeps its bare-text contract for older callers), a hint line in the turn-based battle interface that follows its step through a `_mode` setter (commands, items, target, log), a hint on the title screen (`ui_accept`), and the settings remap list (pad buttons by the pad's names). New string keys `UI_HINT_SELECT/CONFIRM/CHOOSE/NEXT` (older projects get the English text from `RpgStrings._add_missing_defaults`; the translations recipe hashes the key table, so no version bump). Signals are connected to bound methods so that a freed screen is disconnected. Tests: `tests/unit/test_input_glyphs.gd` (switching devices and the signal, a remapped key, an unknown pad, layouts by name, fallbacks, the prompt and the battle hint).

T12.56 done — languages. Game: `RpgSettings.language` ("" = the system's; saved under `[text] language`), `set_language`, `effective_locale`, applied by `apply()` with `TranslationServer.set_locale`; `RpgStrings.locales(folder)` lists the locales of `generated/i18n` (the columns of `game/i18n/strings.tsv`); the settings scene gets a `Language` option row (ui_recipes `RECIPE_VERSION` 10; the script tolerates an older scene without it). Deviation: the spec says menus rebuild on `changed`; the settings menu does that (synchronously, when the locale really changed), and the title screen and battle hints use `NOTIFICATION_TRANSLATION_CHANGED`, which Godot delivers a frame later — labels whose text is a key need neither. New keys `UI_LANGUAGE`, `UI_LANGUAGE_SYSTEM`. Wizard: `rpggen/gui/translations.py` (`available`, `choose` — the setting, else the first of `QLocale.system().uiLanguages()` with a translation, "pt-BR" -> "pt_BR" -> "pt"; `install` also loads Qt's `qtbase_<lang>` for the standard dialogs), called by `create_app` before any widget exists; `AppSettings.language` (validated as a short letters-and-underscore code); *View > Language* (System, English, each compiled language) saves the choice and says it applies at the next start — the wizard sets its texts once, at construction, so a live switch would need a retranslate pass over every page. `tools/update_translations.py extract [--lang]` runs `pyside6-lupdate -no-obsolete -locations none` over `rpggen/gui/**/*.py`; `release` runs `pyside6-lrelease`; `tools/package.py` builds the `.qm` before PyInstaller and the spec bundles them; `.qm` files are git-ignored. `PAGE_NAMES` are marked with `QT_TRANSLATE_NOOP` (a `tr(variable)` is invisible to lupdate), and `show_conflicts` no longer passes a conditional to `tr()`. Pilot: French, 83 of 578 texts (step names, navigation, menus, welcome view), written for this card and flagged in CONTRIBUTING for a native speaker's review; the rest shows in English. The GUI test session installs English after `create_app`, so a developer's own language setting or system cannot change test texts. Error messages stay English. Found on the way (not fixed here, a task was offered): the GDScript runner reports a test as passed when a script error aborts it, and prints Godot's errors only when a test fails; several tests in `test_party.gd` stop early that way.

T12.57 done — contact sheet export. `rpggen/core/contact_sheet.py`: `SheetEntry` (plain data built on the UI thread from the catalog's effective facets and the audit's path resolver), `entries_for` (the grid's visible ids in their order, or every asset by path), `render_pages` (4x5 cells per page at 150 dpi; the whole file or its region, pixel art scaled by whole steps, on a checkerboard; optional tile or frame grid; name, role · status, confidence, tile or frame size; audio and font assets and missing files get a labelled box), `save` (one PDF with every page, or one PNG, or `name-01.png`...), `export` (nothing is written when cancelled). Deviation: the spec says PDF through `QPdfWriter`; the pages are drawn and the PDF written with Pillow (already a dependency) so that core stays free of Qt and the PNG is the same pixels on every platform. Pillow's built-in font has no dash or ellipsis glyphs, so the texts use "·" and "...". GUI: `dialogs/contact_sheet_dialog.py` (format, paper, shown or all, grid overlay; it starts on the filtered set only when that is smaller than the whole), *Export contact sheet…* in the Audit page's Profile menu (no new toolbar width) and in the File menu; the drawing runs in a worker through `run_task` with a determinate progress and Cancel.

T12.59 done — learn from my corrections. `rpggen/core/learned_rules.py`: a rule is `{kind: folder|name, token, facet: role|usage|collision, value, hits, misses, enabled}`; `learn(book, edits, weight)` adds a hit for every token pattern of an edited file (tokens from `classifier.tokens`, letters only, 3+ characters, without stop words such as "assets" or "png") and a miss for other values of the same pattern and facet; `weight=-1` takes it back (undo) and a rule left without evidence is dropped; at most 500 rules. A rule is used with 2+ hits and a hit rate of 60%+; its confidence is the hit rate capped at 0.85. Stored in `data_dir()/learned_rules.json`. `adapters/learned.py` (priority 5, after the profile adapter) proposes each file's best matching rules as a partial fragment with the new `AssetFragment.preferred`: `_merge_fragments` orders full claims, then preferred, then by confidence, and caps the asset's confidence at the learned fragment's when one of its values was taken; `detected_by` becomes "learned" when it decided the role or usage, and `explain.why` then starts with "learned from your earlier fixes (n times)" (n from `learned_rules.cached()`, read again only when the file changes). Decision: the rules reach `catalog.assemble(learned=...)` explicitly, and only the wizard's analysis passes them, so the CLI, goldens, the accuracy corpus and the tests never depend on a user's file; with no rules nothing changes (no golden moved; the learned adapter is not in `adapters_matched` unless it produced something). Learning hooks into the undo stack: `EditSession.learner` (installed by the Audit page) gets the role, usage and collision overrides a step set (`learnable_edits`) on redo, and the same with -1 on undo. Excluding an asset sets role NONE, which is learned like any role. Edit → Learned rules… (`dialogs/learned_rules_dialog.py`; the wizard has no Settings menu, and Edit already holds Storage…) lists, turns off, deletes, and adds selected rules to the open project as folder rules in one undo step (`AuditPage.add_folder_rules`). A corrupt rules file is ignored with a warning (`jsonio.read` raises `RpgGenError`, which `load` catches).

T12.60 done — custom recipe hook. `build.gd`, after the standard recipes: `_seed_example_recipe` writes `game/builder_recipes/example_recipe.gd` when the folder does not exist (user_seed; the ledger keeps a deleted one from coming back), then `_run_custom_recipes` runs `custom_recipe_paths()` (the folder's `*.gd`, sorted by name, not recursive) through `run_custom_recipe`. A recipe gets `lib/recipe_context.gd` (`RECIPE_API_VERSION` 1, documented as stable for 1.x in `docs/recipes.md`), a facade over the internal `BuildContext`: config copy, `rng_seed`, catalog reads with effective facets, `save_scene` / `save_resource` / `write_text` that refuse paths outside `res://game/` and `res://generated/custom/`, `say`, `fail`. Names avoid GDScript's globals (`rng_seed`, `say`) because strict mode reports a shadowed global. Failure detection: a runtime error only ends `build()`, which then returns null like a successful void function, so `build.gd` installs a `Logger` (`ScriptErrors`) for the custom phase and compares the script-error count before and after each call; that, `ctx.fail()`, a script that does not load, or one without `build` gives BUILD-002 with the recipe's project path as `{recipe}` ("Recipe game/builder_recipes/x.gd failed: ...") and fails the build; the files it wrote are listed as failed. Custom recipes never skip (no input hash): they run on every build. `validate.gd` needs no change: it already loads every script and resource under `res://game` and `res://generated`. Tests: `tests/unit/test_custom_recipes.gd` (a scene is made, failures named, name order, the path guard); the script-error path is not unit-tested because the GDScript runner now fails any test that logs a script error.

T12.61 done — dialogue authoring dock. `addons/rpg_project_tools/dock/dialogue_tab.gd` (the asset dock's third tab): the `.tres` files of `res://game/data/dialogue`, a line list (a ⚠ in front of a line with a problem), a form per line (id, speaker, portrait from the catalog's PORTRAIT images, text with a RichTextLabel BBCode preview, condition with a preview against the test state, events one per line, choices as `text | next | condition` per line, jump), each change through `EditorUndoRedoManager` (`create_action` with the line as context, do/undo property, then a refresh) or directly when there is no editor (tests). Inline checks come from new pure functions in the runtime: `RpgCondition.problems` / `atom_problem` (syntax and atom shapes, dangling operators, mixed and/or) and `RpgDialogueRunner.event_problem` (`EVENTS` lists the names), plus jump targets that no line has. The test player is an `RpgDialogueRunner` with `condition_check` and `event_runner` set to `RpgCondition.evaluate_on` / `execute_event_on` on a scratch `RpgGameState` (never added to a tree); the toggles are the `flag:` and `item:` atoms the dialogue's conditions mention. `RpgDialogueJson.to_json` / `to_dict` export what `from_json` reads (a portrait as its resource path, which the import now loads); export writes `<name>.json` next to the `.tres`, import makes `<name>.tres` in the dialogue folder. Saving is explicit (Save), as for any resource edited in the editor. Tests in `test_tools_dock.gd`: the edit round trip, the condition preview and inline problems, JSON round trip, the test player.

T12.62 done — lighting and day/night. Ambient: `RpgMapRoot._ready` adds (or reuses) a `CanvasModulate` named `Ambient`, so maps already in a project's `game/` folder get it without being rebuilt; interiors are `is_interior` (set by `interior_recipe`, version 2) or a map id starting with "interior"; `ambient_override` takes a colour of your own. Only an outdoor map with the cycle on processes (every 0.5 s). `RpgDayNight` is pure: `hour_at(playtime_s)` (24 minutes a day from 8 o'clock) and `ambient_at(hour)` (blended keys: night, dawn, day, dusk), so the colour is a function of the saved play time. The cycle is `RpgSettings.day_night` (saved under `[game]`), with a *Day and night* toggle in the settings scene (ui_recipes version 11; the script tolerates an older scene). Lights: `classifier.suggested_tags` gives "light" for torch, lamp, lantern, candle, campfire (and plurals) in a file name or the nearest folder, added to an image asset's detected tags and to sheet objects' tags (nothing in the synthetic or sample packs matches, so no golden moved); `lighting_recipe` (before `object_scenes_recipe`) saves a radial `GradientTexture2D` (128 px) to `generated/lighting/light_radial.tres`; `object_scenes_recipe` (version 4) adds an `RpgLightSource` to objects tagged "light": four tiles wide, warm colour, at the upper third of the picture, flickering unless the name says lamp or lantern; without the generated texture (tests) it embeds the same gradient. `RpgLightSource` culls itself with a `VisibleOnScreenNotifier2D` the size of its reach: off and not processing outside the camera. Compatibility renderer: verified with a windowed probe (godot_api_verified.md).

T12.64 done — minimap and world map. `minimap_recipe` (after `imported_maps_recipe`, so imported maps are there; outputs one `generated/minimaps/<key>.tres` per `.tscn` under `game/maps`, recursively; input hash = recipe version + md5 of every map and every `generated/tilesets/*.tres`, so painting a map or a tile-set change rebuilds them, which is also what "Regenerate affected resources" runs). `render_layers` takes the union of the TileMapLayers' used rects, one pixel per cell, later layers on top, a cell's colour the alpha-weighted average of its tile's atlas region (every 4th pixel, cached per tile set, source and atlas coords; a fully clear tile is skipped); `origin_cell` and `tile_size` are stored as resource metadata, which `ResourceSaver` keeps in the `.tres`, so the runtime maps positions to pixels without the scene. A map with no painted cells has no minimap (and the HUD hides). Runtime: `RpgMinimap` is built in code (no scene), so Boot adds it to every project on a CanvasLayer (9) without a UI rebuild; it refreshes every 0.2 s: player cell, `fog_mark` radius 5, markers. Fog: per map key `{w, h, bits}` with a `PackedByteArray` bitset (base64 in the save, under the optional `map_fog` key, omitted when empty; a malformed entry or one whose size does not match is dropped; a map whose size changed starts over). Quest markers use a new optional stage key `target` (an entity id), set on the demo quest's stages (data_recipe version 8). The world map is a UI scene (`ui_recipes` version 12: `world_map_menu`, a *Map* button in the pause menu, a *Minimap* toggle in settings; the scripts tolerate older scenes); `RpgWorldMapMenu.fogged` paints unseen cells and scales ×4 with nearest filtering. `dump_map_view.gd` was not needed. Tests: `tests/unit/test_minimap.gd` (picture size = used rect, layer order and clear pixels, a saved map scene, markers, the fog and its save, quest targets, the world map picture and menu, the setting).

Batch fixes after T12.57–T12.64 (found by the integration suite). The dialogue data scripts (`RpgDialogueLine`, `RpgDialogueChoice`, `RpgDialogueResource`) are `@tool`: in the editor a non-tool script resource is a placeholder instance, and the dialogue tab's typed iteration over `line.events` read garbage and crashed the editor of any project with dialogue (only the editor path, so the script-mode tests missed it); the tab frees its scratch `RpgGameState` on predelete. `RpgStrings.format(key, arg)` falls back to the English text when no translation has the key, so the toasts no longer log a formatting error in the editor or in tests. The minimap hides while the router's current map is out of the tree (between scenes); its recipe hashes maps and tile sets with `content_hash` (Godot's per-save `unique_id` and file uid removed), so builds are deterministic and a no-op regeneration skips it; the minimap key is the project path (`game__maps__starter_map`) so the runtime holds no `res://game/` path. `build.gd` seeds `example_recipe.gd.uid` with a fixed uid (written after the import, the script would get a random one at the next import), and numbers the custom-recipe steps after the standard ones (one total).

Status 2026-10-08 (`84f239b`, pushed; CI not checked) — 69 of the 71 M12 cards done; T12.65 has its text done (user guide: version control, skipped checks, `--tile-size`, `conflicts`, a map of the game features; generated README sections 18 to 21 for equipment and shops, quests, light and the minimap, the dialogue tab, the party, and the archetype part renumbered 22 after the sections it followed; the runtime README's equipment, shops, quests, gates, light and minimap; both changelogs; `00_START_HERE.md` lists M11 and M12; a status column in the M12 index; the new `docs/status.md`) but not its screenshots; T12.66 is open. `docs/status.md` lists what is left, by who can do it. Also fixed: a form-feed character in the corpus command of docs/coverage.md, where the backslash and f of the forest path had become one control character.

T12.65 done — screenshots. `tools/make_doc_screenshots.py` renders 16 pictures; new: `pack_cards` (the Project page's pack list with the sample pack), `regeneration_preview` (`PromotionDialog` over a made-up `PromotionSummary`), `conflict_resolver` (`ConflictDialog` over a small project the tool builds: `world/sign.gd` as generated in a backup with its ledger hash, an edited copy and a `.rpggen-new`) and `storage_dialog`; the user guide shows each (a paragraph on pack cards in section 3, the resolver under the Results page, the change list in section 5, the Storage dialog in section 9). Three things made the old pictures wrong and are fixed in the tool: `RPGGEN_CACHE_DIR` was set after `create_app`, so the pictures used this machine's settings (a saved audit splitter position cut the detail panel off); no event loop runs in the tool, so a row the Mapping page had replaced with `deleteLater` stayed painted over the new rows (`_pump` now sends the posted `DeferredDelete` events) — the app itself was never affected; the audit picture selected `town_theme.wav` instead of `town.png`. Deviation (a file outside the card's list): `rpggen/gui/pages/audit_page.py` gets `_fit_side()`. At 1100 px the splitter's 3:2 start gave the side panel 303 px while the detail form needs 452, so it scrolled sideways and cut its buttons off (the T12.32 scroll area hid this from T12.16). Until the user places the splitter, selecting an asset widens the side panel to what the form needs, at most 55% of the page and never below the grid's minimum; a saved position is kept. Test in `tests/gui/test_audit_queue.py`. Not run after this card: the full suites (the audit, layout and release tests and ruff pass).

T12.66, CI on `main` (2026-10-09) — the runs of `84f239b` and `99c486a` failed in two places. Ubuntu, GDScript: `test_the_language_is_saved_and_system_means_the_os_locale` compared `TranslationServer.get_locale()` with `OS.get_locale()`, but the server standardizes a locale and a runner's `C` becomes `en`; the test now compares with what the server makes of the system locale (the assertion on `effective_locale()` is unchanged). Windows, integration: smoke step `exit_building` failed once in `test_2_user_paint_and_files_survive` ("player is 17.3 px from the exit spawn", tolerance 16) after passing in the run before, with no code change between them: the step held `move_down` until the router was idle, so the player walked on while the starter map faded in, further on a slow machine. It now lets go when the scene changes or the router gets busy, as `_walk_through` already did; the tolerance is unchanged.

M13 started 2026-10-09 on the branch `m13`, before the 1.0 tag (the user asked to keep coding while the gate's human work and CI wait); `main` stays the 1.0 candidate. The version stays 1.0.0 until T13.22.

T13.14 done — Zstandard for Tiled. `tiled._decode` handles `compression="zstd"` through `_unzstd`: `_zstd_module()` returns the first importable of `zstandard` (the new optional extra `maps`, in all three lock files and a hidden import of the spec), `compression.zstd` (Python 3.14) and `backports.zstd`; output is capped at `MAX_TILE_BYTES` like zlib. Decision: the spec names only `zstandard`, but py7zr already installs `backports.zstd` everywhere, so a checkout without the extra reads zstd layers too; the "install the zstandard package" reason appears only with no reader at all (tested by monkeypatching `_zstd_module`). Any other compression gives the reason "it uses an unsupported compression (<name>)"; TILE-009's hint names Zstandard. LDtk has no compressed layers, so `ldtk.py` is unchanged. The `tiled_map` pack's `zstd_layer.tmx` now has a real zstd layer (a hand-written frame of one raw block, so the pack needs no library and stays byte-identical) and a third layer with `lz4` that is skipped; golden updated. The lock commands in `ci.yml` and `docs/release.md` gain `--extra maps`. Not checked: the frozen builds carrying the module (T13.23).

T13.21 done — lock-and-key planner. `rpggen/core/lockkey.py`: `plan(graph, keys, start, goal, rng)` returns `Plan(placements, path, needed)`, `check(graph, placements, start, goal)` returns `Check(ok, unreachable, goal_reachable, order)`, `reachable` and `nodes_of` are public helpers. Decisions: edges go one way as written (a two-way door is listed at both ends), so one-way drops work for M14; a key never lies in the start or the goal and two keys never share a node; a key prefers the nodes the key before it opened (that is what chains the locks), chosen by `rng.randrange` over the candidates sorted by `str`, so the mapping's order does not change the result. "The goal needs them all" is a property of the graph as much as of the placement, so a key the goal does not need is not an error: `Plan.needed` lists the keys it does need and the caller decides. `LockKeyError(reason, needed, available, key)`: `reason` is `nodes` (the key that found no free node, `needed` counts it and the keys after it) or `goal`.

T13.13 done — validator ids. `RpgDialogueRunner.event_refs(event)` gives `[[kind, id]]` (shop for `open_shop`, quest for `quest_start` / `quest_stage` / `quest_complete`, actor for `join_party`); `event_problem(event, ids = {})` reports `unknown <kind> '<id>'` when `ids` has a list for the kind (the old body is `_syntax_problem`; a malformed event keeps its own message). `ContentChecks.known_ids(dirs)`: a shop and an actor by file name (how the game finds them), a quest by its `id`; `_check_dialogue` reports each unknown id once per file as VAL-019, whose text is now "Dialogue {file} line {n} references unknown {kind} {item}" (the detail gains `kind` and `n`, for items too). The dialogue tab reads the ids on every rescan (`data_ids`; `data_dirs` for tests) and passes them to `line_problems` and the event notes. Deviations: the spec's `quest_advance:` does not exist, the event is `quest_stage:`; no new code was reserved for the demo-content failure, so `validate.gd` adds BUILD-002 (recipe `data_recipe`) beside the VAL-019 warning when the file is the seeded `npc_greeting.tres` and the kind is not an item — by path, so a user's typo in that one file also stops the run (the text says so); that branch has no unit test, the integration suite covers the clean case. `leave_party:` is not checked (leaving with an unknown id does nothing).

T13.11 done — shop quantities and sell all. `RpgShopMenu`: a row's quantity is changed with left / right on the row (its `gui_input`, accepted so the focus stays; past either end it wraps between 1 and the most), `max_quantity` is the bag's room and what the gold buys (Buy) or what you hold (Sell), never below 1 so a row you cannot afford still answers with the refusal; the row text names a quantity above one and the total (`Potion (3) — 30 G`, `Potion x4 (3) — 15 G`), the description gets a hint line when more than one is possible; a trade resets the row to 1. Sell all: the confirmation is the button itself (first press: "Sell everything for N G? Press again", second press sells, leaving the button takes it back) — no dialog, so the gamepad ring stays one column. `RpgShopDefinition.buys` (category names, case-insensitive, empty = all; KEY and QUEST never), and `sell_all_items` / `sell_all_value` / `sell_all`; equipped gear is not in the bag (T12.45), so it is skipped by construction (tested). `ui_recipes` version 13 adds the hidden `SellAll` button; the script uses `get_node_or_null`, so a 1.0 scene works without it (tested by removing the node). Smoke step `shop_buy_sell` buys and sells two. The READMEs are T13.22.

T13.01 done — audit review mode. `rpggen/gui/widgets/review_mode.py` (`ReviewMode`): a large `ImageView`, a note for assets without a picture, the counter, the key line, and a `side` layout; it emits `quickKey` (the grid's keys) and `leaveRequested` (Esc) and edits nothing. `AuditPage`: the splitter and the review widget sit in a `QStackedWidget` (`body`); `set_review_mode` / `toggle_review_mode` / `in_review_mode`; entering selects the queue's first asset unless one of the queue is already selected, moves the page's own `detail_scroll` into the review widget (at least the width its form needs, at most half the page) and turns the panel's small picture off (`AssetDetail.show_preview`); leaving moves it back. The grid's selection stays the asset shown, so `C`, `X`, `1`-`9`, undo and the advance-after-confirm run through the page's existing handlers unchanged. `N` / `Shift+N` go through `_step`, which walks the filter (wrapping) once nothing needs attention, in review mode only. The counter reads "12 of 37 need attention" or "Nothing needs attention · 3 of 27 shown". Leaving the page leaves the mode. View > Review mode (`F7`) acts only on the Audit page; both shortcut lists name F7. The overlay over the preview is the asset's grid for tiles and sprites (as the detail panel draws it); T13.03 adds the rest.

T13.02 done — audit grid grouped by folder. `catalog.folder_of(catalog, asset)` (pack-relative folder, as the rule dialog's glob). `AssetListModel.grouped`: the header rows follow the asset rows (an asset keeps its row, so `asset_at` and `row_of` are unchanged); `HeaderRole` holds a header's folder key (`pack_id
folder`), `FolderRole` every row's; a header is enabled but not selectable. `AssetFilterProxy`: a header passes while one of its folder's assets passes; `collapsed` folds a folder (its header stays, with the counts of the whole folder); grouped sorting is folder, header first, then the usual order; `folder_counts` (cached until the filter or the data changes) gives need attention / confirmed / shown. A status change emits `dataChanged` for the folder's header too, so the proxy re-decides whether to show it. View: while grouped the fixed grid and uniform sizes are off and the delegate's size hint makes a header a little narrower than the viewport, which gives it a line of its own (checked by eye and by the size test; the flat grid is exactly as before). The three buttons are painted by the delegate and hit-tested in `editorEvent` (`headerAction`); a click elsewhere on the header folds. Decision: the buttons are mouse-only; with the keyboard the same is done by selecting and `C`, or the context menu's rule. `AuditPage.confirm_folder` (the folder's assets that pass the filter and are not confirmed, one undo step) and `make_folder_rule` (the existing `FolderRuleDialog` with `<folder>/**`). Setting `group_by_folder` (optional, default off). `visible_ids()` skips headers, so the queue, `N` and review mode do. `bulk_panel.py` needed no change.

T13.03 done — live preview. `anim_preview.AnimPlayer(source)` (a QObject: `index`, `playing`, `speed_percent`, `interval_ms`, `set_playing`, `restart`, `step`, `tick`, signals `frameChanged` and `finished`) is the dialog's timer logic moved out; `AnimEditorDialog` uses it (`dialog.index`, `.playing`, `.timer` and `interval_ms()` still work, the dialog's tests are unchanged). `ImageView.set_pixmap` and `draw_marks` (`footprint`, `origin`, `active`, `current`); `asset_detail.overlay_marks(eff, size, anim, frame)` is pure. `AssetDetail`: a transport (`anim_combo` in the dialog's grouped order, `anim_play_button`, `anim_frame_label`) for a sprite with animations; `render_picture()` draws the sheet, its grid and the marks into `views()` (the panel's own view, and `extra_view`, which review mode sets to its large view); while playing the views show the frame alone (cut from the animation's own sheet, mirrored for `flip_h`), paused they show the sheet with the frame boxed. There is one panel and one player, so it never plays in two places; it stops when the asset changes, the panel is hidden (`hideEvent`), the page is left and when review mode is entered or left. `ReviewMode.show_picture` became `show_note` (the panel draws the picture). Decisions: an object without a `footprint` facet shows the default the schema describes (the bottom 30%); the `current` box is not drawn for an animation whose frames come from another file (`source`). Tests drive the player with `tick()` as their clock.

T13.04 done — select similar. `image_facts.dhash(a)` (grey with transparent pixels as black, reduced to 9 x 8 with Pillow's BOX filter, 64 bits as 16 hex digits) and `image_facts.hashes(a, regions)` (`dhash` and `region_dhashes`, one per region of a sheet) are part of `analyze_array`'s result; `ANALYZER_VERSION` stays 4, and `analyze_file` adds the two keys to a cache entry that lacks them and writes it back (tested with `analyze_array` forbidden). `rpggen/core/analyzer/similarity.py`: `distance`, `informative`, `hash_of(asset)` (read from the analysis cache by the file hash, so the catalog file and every golden without a repeated picture are unchanged), `hashes_of`, `near(catalog, id, max_distance=10)`, `mark_duplicates`, `duplicate_of`. `catalog.assemble` collects each image's hash from the analysis the adapters already used and calls `mark_duplicates` before the terrain step. Decisions (the card's spec says "different packs" and its test asks for the tag inside `mixed`): across packs the rule is the spec's (same size, at most 2 bits, and a hash that says something: 6 to 58 set bits, because every flat picture hashes to zero); inside one pack only a byte-identical file is tagged, since a pack's own tiles are near each other on purpose. A copy is not itself a reference for later files. `explain.why` adds "Looks like forest/tree from pack_a". `mixed` gains `items/spare/item_01.png` (identical to `items/item_01.png`); the goldens of `mixed` and `weird_names` (which already had a repeated file) gained the tag, no other golden moved. Audit page: `select_similar()` (context menu and `Ctrl+Shift+S`) keeps the selected asset, adds the near ones (switching to All when the filter hides some) and writes "N similar assets selected" into a new note beside the progress line; off for sounds, fonts and multi-selections. Not run: the private corpus (T12.26) is not on this machine; detection code did not change, only the tag.

T13.05 done — mapping gallery and jump to the audit. `AssetPickerDialog` moved to `rpggen/gui/dialogs/asset_picker.py`. It is still the audit's `AssetGrid` (so: the same thumbnails at the saved size, loaded in the background, the filter bar and its search), now grouped by pack and in `mapping.candidates` order inside a pack: `AssetListModel.set_grouped(on, by_pack)`, `AssetFilterProxy.rank` (`AssetGrid.set_rank`) and `AssetCardDelegate.header_actions = ()` for headers without buttons; `groups()` reports what is shown. Decision: the "confidence badge" is the card's confidence bar (as in the audit) plus the percentage in the card's tooltip, not a new drawn badge. Mapping rows have `audit_button` (a tool button with a standard icon, tooltip and accessible name; hidden on a row without an asset); `audit_asset(role)` is the chosen asset or, for an object cut from a sheet, its sheet; the page emits `showInAudit(asset id, role)`. `MainWindow.show_asset_in_audit` leaves the page, shows the Audit page and calls its `show_asset`; `_return_to` remembers the page and role, and `go_back` from the Audit page returns there and calls `focus_row(role)` (the rows are rebuilt on entry, so the new row's combo gets the focus). Any other page change forgets the side trip.

T13.06 done — factory presets and option dependencies. `options.DEPENDENCIES` (`demo`: tilesets, dialogue, combat, save; `navigation`: combat; `FEATURE_DEPS` is the same object). Encounter zones, party and shops are not options, so they have no entry. `options.BUILTIN_PRESETS` and `builtin_preset(key, archetype)`: the defaults with only the preset's features on; the two small ones also carry `export_targets: [windows]`, which `apply_preset` now takes (a user preset never has the key, so the user's targets are untouched by their own presets and by Everything). Decision: Minimal has no terrain (the card lists what is on; Just the tilesets names terrain explicitly). Model: `BUILTIN_PREFIX` + key is a built-in's combo data and `DEFAULTS_ITEM` is Everything (the item "Defaults" is gone from the combo); `builtin_items()`, `user_items()` (a user preset whose name is a built-in's label shows as "<name> (yours)" and is applied by its name), `save_preset` refuses a built-in's label (the page then says to choose another name), `edit_presets` never writes a built-in's data. Manage… lists only the user's presets. The model's `demo_wish` became `wishes` for every dependent feature, so navigation also returns to what the user wanted when Combat comes back.

T13.07 done — remaining time. `AppSettings.phase_history` (phase -> the last ten `[assets, seconds]`; optional, a 1.0 file loads, a damaged list is read pair by pair), `add_phase_times`, `estimate(history, phase, assets)` (median seconds per asset times the count; the median seconds for `preflight`, `promote`, `report`, for a history without counted runs and when the count is unknown) and `estimate_rest`. The orchestrator's `_remember_times` runs after every run, also a failed or cancelled one, with the phases in `phases_done` and the catalog's asset count (0 before the catalog phase). Generate page: `estimates_for(phase)` is read when a phase starts; the running row says "about 40 s left" and the top line the rest of the run, both fixed for the phase (the clock does not count them down), both replaced by "taking longer than usual" once the phase has used its estimate; nothing is shown without history. Numbers are rounded to five seconds. `estimate_left` is gone (its test now checks `estimates_for`); `phase_timings` and `timing_assets` stay for the Options summary. CLI: `phase_estimate_line` prints "usually about N s (about M s left)" on stderr after a phase's log line; the asset count is not known there, so the times are not scaled. Added to `tests/conftest.py`: without `RPGGEN_CACHE_DIR` the whole test session uses a temporary folder, because a generation now writes the settings and a test run must not write this computer's (a file outside the card's list).

T13.08 done — project dashboard. `pack_sniff.stamp(path)` (an archive's size and mtime; a folder's file count, total size and newest mtime, by `os.scandir`, nothing is read) and `changed_since(path, recorded)` (the time of the change, or None when the pack is as recorded, when nothing was recorded, and when the pack is away). `RecentProject.packs` (`[{path, stamp}]`, optional; a 1.0 entry has none) is written by the Generate page after a successful run; `add_recent` keeps an entry's packs when it is called without them. Welcome page: each recent project is a `ProjectCard` set as the item's widget (the list, its selection, Enter, Locate… and Remove work as before; the item's data is still the folder). The availability worker now also returns the changed packs (`pack_changes`), so no folder is touched on the UI thread; a card's picture is read only after its folder answered. `MainWindow.regenerate_project` opens the project and sets `_walk_to_generate`; `continue_walk` (called from `refresh`) presses Next up to Generate as soon as the pages allow it and gives up when a page stops the walk or the user presses Back. The run then shows the regeneration preview as any regeneration does. Deviation: no `rpggen/preview.png` is written. The picture the Results page shows already lies in every project as `rpggen/results/starter_map.png` (outside the ledger by design, T11.27); the card reads that, so a project made by 1.0 shows its map too, and the orchestrator, the ledger and the file lists the integration tests assert are unchanged. A project without that file shows the drawing of its kind of game (`MockScreen`).

T13.09 done — first-run tour. `rpggen/gui/widgets/tour.py`: `TourStep(widget, title, text)`, `place(anchor, size, host)` (below the widget, else above, else beside, always clamped into the host) and `Tour(host, steps)`, a small panel drawn over the host with Skip / Back / Next (Done on the last step), a counter, an accessible name per step and Esc; it dims and blocks nothing, follows the host's resizes, and emits `finished(skipped)`. `AppSettings.tours_seen`. `AuditPage`: `tour_steps()` (the grid, the progress line, the side panel, the Shortcuts button, the Profile button), `maybe_start_tour()`, `start_tour()`, `end_tour()`. The start is a 400 ms single shot from `enter` that only starts the tour when the page is still shown, which is what keeps "Try the sample" (it walks through the page) from starting it; no flag for the sample was needed. Finishing and skipping both mark it seen; leaving the page (also by any path that only hides it) takes the callout away without marking it, so it comes back. Help > Show the tour again forgets the mark and starts the tour at once on the Audit page, else says it starts at the next visit. `tools/make_doc_screenshots.py` marks the tour seen, or it would be in the audit pictures. The tour has no slide animation (T13.10's Reduce motion has nothing to turn off there).

T13.10 done — accessibility and display scaling. Scaling: `thumbnails.request_for(..., ratio)` renders for `size * ratio` device pixels and puts the ratio into the cache key (keys at ratio 1 are unchanged); `pixmap_from_bytes(data, ratio)` tags the pixmap. `AssetListModel.set_device_ratio` (set from the view's `showEvent`) asks for the pictures again; the card delegate fits a picture by its `deviceIndependentSize`. `theme.crisp_scale(scale, ratio)` gives the largest scale at which an image pixel is a whole number of device pixels; `ImageView.fit` uses it (the detail panel, review mode, the dialogs). The map preview scales to the label's device pixels and tags the result; `archetype_page.scaled_to_height(pixmap, height, ratio)` does the same for the drawing's art. The overlay pens were already cosmetic (one device pixel at any zoom). Accessibility: the grid's model answers `AccessibleTextRole` ("hero.png, Sprite or character, Player, needs review"; a folder header says its folder) and `AccessibleDescriptionRole` (the path); the status glyph's meaning is the last word of that name; the icon-only buttons added in M13 have accessible names. A test walks the window's focus chain on all seven pages and the welcome view, for both archetypes, and finds every button, combo, field, spin box and list in it. Reduce motion: setting `reduce_motion` (`system` / `on` / `off`), `theme.system_reduces_motion()` (Windows: SPI_GETCLIENTAREAANIMATION; false elsewhere, Qt 6 has no hint for it), `theme.apply_motion()` switches Qt's animated UI effects (menus, combos, tooltips, tool boxes). Decision: the card names a thumbnail animation, a progress pulse and the tour's slide; none of the three exists (thumbnails appear at once, the progress bar is determinate, the tour does not slide), so the switch covers what does move. Taskbar: `rpggen/gui/taskbar.py` calls `ITaskbarList3` (`SetProgressState`, `SetProgressValue`) by vtable through ctypes, and `QGuiApplication.setBadgeNumber` on macOS; any failure turns it off for the run and returns False. Checked on this machine (Windows 11) with a real window: every call returned S_OK; what the button looks like was not looked at. The Generate page sets it at each phase and clears it at the end (red after a failure). Both limits are gone from `docs/status.md`.

T13.12 done — one interior per house. Both planners return `interiors` (`[{id: interior_<n>, size: [max(10, house width), 8], door: the house's door column clamped off the corners}]`; `map_preview.interiors_for` and `MapBuild.interiors_for`, kept equal by the regenerated `map_plan_cases.json`). `interior_recipe` version 3 builds `interior_<n>.tscn` (user_seed, never rewritten) for each house: in a new project the houses are the plan's (the starter map is built later in the same run from the same plan); when a starter map exists they are the instances of the building scene in that map, and an interior's exit goes to the `ExitSpawn` id that instance really has. That is what makes a 1.0 project safe: its houses share one spawn id, the added `interior_2..n` return to it, no door points at a spawn that is not there, and the map is untouched (integration test with a map rewritten to what 1.0 made). `starter_map_recipe` points house n's `Entrance` at `interior_<n>` and gives houses after the first the spawn id `<stem>_exit_<n>` (the first keeps `<stem>_exit`, so `interior_1` and the smoke steps are as before). Decisions: the interior of a new default project is now 10 x 8 with its exit under the house's door (it was 12 x 9, exit in the middle); a bed and a table are placed only when the packs have an object named or tagged so (the sample pack has none, so its interiors are empty rooms); no second chest. The map preview's legend says "N interiors"; the Options summary already did. The generated README explains pointing a 1.0 house at its interior. Found by the new integration test and fixed (it was there before): smoke step `sequence_plays` put its test trigger 64 px above the player; on a planned (non-default) map the start is three tiles below the first house's door, so the player was sent into the house instead and the step failed with "ran its sequence 0 times" for every map with non-default layout options and a house. `_free_spot` now picks a place with no body and no area near the player, and the player returns to where it stood. Also seen: a parse error in a GDScript test file hangs `godot-test` instead of failing it.

T13.15 done — followers in the action RPG. `archetypes/arpg/components/assist.gd` (`RpgAssist`): `attach(follower, stats, tile)` adds Stats, Health, Hurtbox (layer 9, team player), AttackHitbox (layer 7, mask 10) and the component to a follower node at run time; `step(delta, player_at, enemies)` is the whole behaviour (tested outside the tree): nearest living `RpgEnemyAi` among the follower's siblings within 3 tiles of the player, walk to one tile beside it (leashed to 6 tiles of the player), `swing` every 1.2 s for 0.15 s with `hitbox.damage` = the member's effective attack, `go_down` for 5 s at 0 HP (also when a save has the member at 0 HP), then `Health.revive`. The shared `RpgFollowers` knows nothing of the archetype: it calls the combat mode's `customize_follower(node, member, index)` when it has one (`RpgArpgMode` attaches the assist to the first two) and leaves a follower whose meta `held` is true alone, writing its trail point to meta `home`; the assist walks back there before it lets go (`place_all`). HUD: `RpgArpgMode.party_health_changed`; `hud.gd` makes a `Party` column of `Member_<id>` bars (smaller copies of the HP bar) at run time. **Deviations:** the parts are added at run time, so `arpg_build.gd` did not change and no recipe version moved (a 1.0 project gets the feature by regenerating, its scenes untouched); `archetypes/arpg/ui/hud.gd` is not in the card's list but the HUD bars live there; `core/game_state.gd` only had its comment corrected. `data_recipe._has_party_demo` no longer asks for the archetype, so an ARPG demo seeds `companion.tres` and the villager's offer (an existing ARPG project gets the companion file, its greeting stays as it is). Smoke: `follower_assists` (before `enemy_dies`; the enemy is lent 500 HP so the follower cannot kill it, and the assist is switched off after the first hit); `followers_trail` switches the assist off, as that step is about following.

T13.16 done — custom sound slots. `sound_slots`: `SLOT_PATTERN`, `SURFACES`, `is_valid_name`, `invalid_names`, `is_custom`, `kind_of`, `custom_slots`, `surface_of`; `slot(key)` makes a Slot for a custom key; `default_sounds` fills `sfx_step_<surface>` from sfx whose name has a step word and the surface; `resolve_sounds` keeps a custom slot as long as its name is valid (without a sound it is CLEARED, so an added row stays). `classifier.suggested_surface(path)` (file words before folder words, nearest folder first) and `catalog._suggest_tags` adds `surface:<name>` to GROUND assets only. Schema: `sounds.patternProperties`; `config.check_sound_names` raises `CAT-006` in `load` and `save` before the schema check (new code, in `03_ERROR_CODES.md`, tables regenerated). GUI: `SoundSection` builds its rows from `slot_keys()` (fixed, then custom by name), `add_slot`, `remove_slot`, `ask_new_slot`, `slot_problem`, `restore`, and `aboutToChange`, which `MappingPage` connects to `_push_history`; the history tuple carries the sounds, so every change in the Sound group (choose and clear included) is one Undo step. Godot: `audio_bus_recipe` v3 also writes `rpggen/surfaces.json` (asset id -> surface from the tags; generated-owned); `RpgSoundBank.surface_of`, `has_step_surfaces`, `step_slot`, `set_surfaces`; `RpgMovement.surface_at(layers, at, bank)` (topmost tile with a surface: its `footstep` custom data, else its asset's surface through the `asset_id` custom data); dialogue event `play_sound:<slot>`. README: `{custom_sound_slots}`. **Deviations:** the footsteps are in `actors/components/movement.gd` (where 1.0 has them), not `actor.gd`; the surface reaches the game through `surfaces.json` and the tiles' existing `asset_id` custom data rather than by writing `footstep` into the TileSet, so no TileSet of a 1.0 project is rebuilt for it; the dialog test is in `tests/gui/test_mapping_page.py` (the Sound group is on the Mapping page), not `test_options_m11.py`; `tests/integration/test_sound_slots.py` is new (the recipe had no test that reads the bank); `tests/unit/test_runtime_version.py` learned the new README placeholder.

T13.17 done — audio waveforms and loop points. `audio_info.soundfile_module()` (None when soundfile or libsndfile cannot load), `peaks(path, columns=96)` ((low, high) per column over the decoded samples, channels averaged, read in blocks of `PEAK_BLOCK_FRAMES`; None for a file that cannot be decoded, never raises) and `guess_loop(path)` (RMS of 50 ms windows; for every lag of at least 2 s the best start after the first second by the mean difference of the 0.4 s after both windows, or before them when the track ends there; of the lags within 2 % of the loudest window of the best one, the longest; numpy, about 5 ms for a 10 s track; windows double above 6000 of them). Catalog schema: `audio.loop_start_ms` / `loop_end_ms` (detected facets: integers; overrides: integer or null, and an override no longer needs `type`, since overrides merge). GUI: `audio_preview.WaveformView` (peaks, loop region, markers dragged with the mouse, play head; `loopChanged` on release = one undo step) and `position_ms()`; `AssetDetail` gets the view for every audio asset and, for music, `loopBox` (`loopStart`, `loopEnd`, `loopGuess`, `loopClear`), `set_loop`, `clear_loop`, `guess_loop`, `loop_points`; loop points are stored with `session.set_overrides({'audio': {...}})`. Cards: `render_audio_card` draws from `peaks` (`CARD_VERSION` 2, so cached cards are drawn again). Godot: `audio_bus_recipe` v4 writes `rpggen/audio_loops.json` (file -> {start_ms, end_ms} for assets whose `audio.loop` is true); `RpgAudio.apply_loop(stream, info)`, `loop_of`, `set_loops`; `play_music` applies it. **VERIFY done:** `AudioStreamWAV.loop_mode` / `loop_begin` / `loop_end` (sample frames) and `AudioStreamOggVorbis` / `AudioStreamMP3` `loop` + `loop_offset` (seconds) exist in 4.7.2 (the GDScript tests set and read them); the import options of a real 4.7.2 `.import` file are `edit/loop_mode`, `edit/loop_begin`, `edit/loop_end` (WAV) and `loop`, `loop_offset` (OGG). **Deviations:** (1) the loop is set on the stream when the music starts, from `audio_loops.json`, instead of being written into the `.import` options: the `.import` files are written by Godot's import step, which runs before the recipes, and they carry the `uid://` rpggen never writes; changing a loop point therefore needs no re-import, and no sidecar of a 1.0 project is touched. Godot's OGG and MP3 streams have no loop end, so only WAV uses `loop_end_ms` (the Loop row's tooltip says so). (2) In 1.0 no music looped at all (`edit/loop_mode=0`, `loop=false` and nothing set at run time); every track whose `audio.loop` is true (all music) now loops, also in a regenerated 1.0 project.
