(3) Peaks are not computed in the analysis worker: the card's are drawn by the thumbnail worker (cached with the thumbnail by file hash) and the detail panel decodes files up to 4 MB directly and larger ones on a worker thread, which is the lazy path the card allows; the analysis ceilings of T12.27 are untouched because analysis decodes nothing. (4) `waveform()` (the WAV-only reader of 1.0) stays for its test. Packaging: `soundfile>=0.12` in `pyproject.toml`, the three lock files regenerated with the documented commands (new pins: soundfile 0.14.0, cffi 2.1.1, pycparser 3.1), `rpggen.spec` collects `_soundfile_data` (data and dynamic libraries) and names `soundfile`, `_soundfile_data`, `_cffi_backend`; `rpggen-cli audio-check [file]` (without a file it writes half a second of a tone as Ogg Vorbis and decodes it), which `tools/package.py --smoke` runs in the frozen build; `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` lists python-soundfile and libsndfile (LGPL-2.1-or-later, a separate shared library). Not run here: the frozen build on the three systems (the gate, T13.23).

T13.18 done — recolour variants. `rpggen/core/variants.py`: `render(image, op, params)` (numpy int64 only: an integer HSV with 1530 hue steps, so 120° is exactly red → green; `palette` replaces exact colours; alpha and the colours of fully transparent pixels are kept), `render_bytes`, `palette_of`, `normalized(definition)` (canonical form or `VariantError`), `new_id` (`<pack>.variants.<slug>`), `path_of` (`generated/variants/<id with _>.png`), `build_asset`, `apply(catalog, definitions, pack_roots, issues, previous)` and `cached_file(sha256)` (rendered PNGs live in `<cache>/variants/<sha256 of the PNG>.png`, which is also what the wizard's resolver shows). `Catalog.variants` (optional in the JSON, like `maps`); `assemble` applies the previous catalog's variants after the folder rules (so a variant has its source's effective facets, overrides included) and hands the rendered files to the installer through the `generated` map that GIF strips and terrain atlases already use, so `staging.py` needed no change; `_carry_previous` no longer turns a previous variant asset into a MISSING one. The effective asset: the source's pack id, status CONFIRMED, `detected_by: variant`, the source's effective facets plus `derived_from`; overrides and UNUSED set on the variant itself survive. A source that is gone or a definition that cannot be rendered: `ASSET-029` (warning, new), no asset, the definition stays. Schema: `variants` / `$defs.Variant` (with the optional `stats` multipliers, which the card describes but its schema line does not list) and the facet `derived_from`. `mapping.candidates` and `best_for` rank a variant behind the pack's own assets, so a variant never becomes the starter enemy by itself (found by the integration test). GUI: `edits.add_variant` / `remove_variant` and the session wrappers (one undo step each); `dialogs/variant_dialog.py` (`VariantDialog`: `PictureLabel` source and result, `QDial` hue wheel, saturation and brightness sliders, the palette table with `eyedrop` on the source and `pick_colour`, name, stats spins for ENEMY/BOSS sources, `definition()`); Audit context menu **Make variant…** / **Remove variant** (`can_make_variant`, `make_variant`, `remove_variant`); grid `VariantRole`, the ⧉ glyph in the picture's corner, the variant's name as the card's label. Godot: `catalog_reader.recolour(asset_id)`; both `enemy_recipes.gd` list variants after the packs' own enemies (those keep index and slug, so no existing definition or scene changes and no recipe version moved), give a variant its source's index, `recoloured_stats` (multipliers, at least 1) and its name; the ARPG's `starter_scene_at` skips variants unless one is the chosen starter enemy. **Deviations:** the enemy definitions are made by `recipes/turn_based/enemy_recipes.gd` and `recipes/arpg/enemy_recipes.gd` (where 1.0 makes them), not `data_recipe.gd`; `rpggen/core/mapping.py`, `rpggen/gui/edits.py`, `rpggen/gui/widgets/asset_grid.py` and `builder/lib/catalog_reader.gd` are touched beyond the card's list for the reasons above; `tests/integration/test_variants.py` is new (both archetypes: the PNG, the ledger owner, the definition's name and numbers, nothing placed, a second generation changes nothing).

T13.19 done — quest and cutscene tabs. Runtime: `RpgQuestDefinition` gains `DONE`, `next_of` / `next_in` (a stage's optional `next`: a stage id or `done`; else the stage below), `edges`, `problems` (`problems_of`, `reward_problems`), `reached(state)` (the editor's test; changes nothing) and `to_dict` / `from_dict`; `GameState.refresh_quests` follows `next_of` and gives a stage's own `rewards` when its condition finishes it (a `next` that names no stage completes the quest rather than leaving it stuck). Both are optional keys of the stage dictionary, so a 1.0 quest behaves as before. `RpgSequence.STEP_KINDS` (ten entries: the card's nine kinds, with fade as `fade out` and `fade in`; each `{kind, cmd, fields: [[argument, type]]}`), `step_kind`, `new_step`, `entity_problems(commands, entities)` (`entity` and `toward`; `player` is always known and `set_flag`'s `entity` is not an actor). `RpgSequenceRunner` registers the debugger capture `rpg_sequence` while the engine's debugger is active and runs `sequence_from(commands, skippable)` on the message `run`. Dock: `dock/data_tab.gd` (the shared base, not in the card's list: file row, status, `record(action, method, new, old)` for one undo step through the editor's `EditorUndoRedoManager`, a plain `UndoRedo` in tests, or directly; `map_entities()` from the edited scene or `map_root`), `dock/quest_tab.gd` (stage list, form, `GraphEdit` with one `GraphNode` per stage plus `done` and one connection per stage, `graph_nodes` / `graph_edges`, the pretend state with toggles and gold, `run_test`, rewards as text lines, JSON) and `dock/sequence_tab.gd` (step list, a form built from `STEP_KINDS`, every command also editable as JSON, `problems` = `ContentChecks.sequence_problems` + the open map's entities, `run_test` -> `played` / `checked` / `problems`). `plugin.gd` adds `DebugLink` (an `EditorDebuggerPlugin` whose `send` goes to every active session); `asset_dock.gd` adds both tabs. **VERIFY done:** `GraphEdit.add_child(GraphNode)`, `GraphNode.set_slot(index, left, type, color, right, type, color)`, `position_offset`, `draggable`, `GraphEdit.connect_node`, `clear_connections` and `get_connection_list` (dictionaries with `from_node` / `to_node`) work headless in 4.7.2 (the graph tests read the connections back); `EngineDebugger.register_message_capture` / `has_capture` / `unregister_message_capture` and `EditorDebuggerPlugin.get_sessions` / `EditorDebuggerSession.send_message` compile under the strict settings. Not exercised here: the message crossing from a real editor to a real running game (a manual row of the gate). Tests: 17 new in `test_tools_dock.gd` and `test_quests.gd` (517 in all).

T13.20 done — a world of connected maps. `rpggen/core/world_plan.py`: `normalized(areas)` (the table's canonical form or `WorldPlanError`), `plan(areas, seed, catalog, starter_size)` (the village at grid (0, 0), the first area at (1, 0) where the starter map's road leads, the others on free cells beside the world; every new area gets a door pair with one neighbour and with each other neighbour at 35 %; `lay_out` reserves an L-shaped way from every door's floor cell to the middle and places chests, enemies (at least 6 cells from every arrival), decorations and a shore's water band; `_lock_the_cave` tags every door into the first cave with `cave_key`, asks `lockkey.plan` for the area that holds the key and adds a gate per door; a cave next to the village only is not locked, with a note), `extend(stored, areas, seed)` (rows the plan lacks, joined to `join` or the nearest area with room), `reuse` / `for_project` (a fresh plan until an area map other than the starter map exists), `check` (unreachable / unpaired, both empty for every plan made: 1,200 plans over 300 seeds), `resolve_biome` (forest needs tree decorations and prefers grass ground, cave needs wall or cliff tiles and prefers stone, shore needs water; a missing one becomes the biome before it, with a note), `record_hashes`, `load` / `save` / `exists`, `summary`. Schema `world_plan.schema.json`; `starter_map.areas` in the config schema (rows may carry `join` and `reroll`). Orchestrator: `_plan_world` after the catalog (the plan goes into the staging copy), `_record_world_hashes` after the build (`map_hash` for the maps this run built: not for a map that was in the project before); report section *World*. Godot: `builder/lib/world_plan.gd` (read, `free_edge_cell`, `add_door`, `add_gate`); `starter_map_recipe` gives the village its doors (the east one is the planner's own exit and road; the others are moved along the wall to floor that the planner left free) and skips the demo's east exit to `field_1`; `second_map_recipe` v3 is the area recipe when a plan exists (one `game/maps/<id>.tscn` per area, written once, plus `game/data/items/cave_key.tres`), and the field of 1.0 otherwise; `tools/map_graph.gd` (doors, spawns, `reachable`, `unreachable`, `dangling`); `content_checks` reports VAL-027 and VAL-028 (both new, warnings); `world_map_menu` shows *World* first (`overview`: a box per area on the plan's grid, lit when visited, a mark per door pair) and names areas by the plan; smoke step `world_doors` (through the village's first door and back). GUI: `widgets/areas_table.py` (`AreasTable` with its own undo stack and Ctrl+Z, `WorldPreview` with one tile per area, the chosen area drawn cell by cell), wired into `StarterMapBox` (`show_world`, `reroll_chosen_area`), `OptionsModel.world_names` / `world_preview`, and the starter map's lock no longer drops the areas table. **Deviations:** (1) VAL-016 and VAL-017 stay errors for a door outside the world; a door whose own map or target is an area of the stored plan gets the warning VAL-028 instead, because rpggen can mend those (the card wants a broken door of the world to warn and the build to succeed). VAL-027 leaves out `game/maps/imported/` (the library of imported maps is not placed in the world by design). (2) `map_graph.gd` lives in `addons/rpg_project_tools/tools/`, not `builder/lib/` (T13.24 names it there): the dock and the headless builder both load it, and the addon's path is the same in the template and in a generated project. The checks are in `content_checks.gd`, which `validate.gd` already runs, so `validate.gd` itself did not change. (3) The village's size, enemies and chests are the Starter map box's own options (its row shows them disabled). (4) A row added to a world that exists gives a one-way door until the target map gets its side (VAL-028; T13.24's repair adds it), because an existing map is never rewritten here. (5) Per-area *Rearrange this area* is the optional row key `reroll`. (6) The world map's seeding is the *World* overview entry rather than fog data. (7) `tests/integration/test_regeneration.py` is not touched: the stored-plan regeneration is in the new `tests/integration/test_world.py` (three areas generate, validate and play; a regeneration with another seed and a fourth row builds only the new map, the others byte-identical; a hand-broken door warns), with an action-RPG world with a locked cave beside it. `data_recipe.gd`, `preflight.py` and `map_preview.py` needed no change.

T13.20 follow-up — the smoke steps. `world_doors` is appended only when `rpggen/world_plan.json` exists (the sample tests forbid a logged "step passes"), and returns the player to where the step started. Found by the action-RPG world test with a 48 x 32 starter map, whose start point is two cells above the bottom wall: `keys_and_switches` put its plate into the wall (now `_is_clear` picks a clear spot from a short list) and `dodge` dodged down into it (now `_roomy_facing` rays 60 px in each direction and faces the first free one, down first as before). Both were 1.0 behaviour for any starter map whose start is near a wall, not something the world introduced.

T13.24 done — world repair. GDScript: `addons/rpg_project_tools/tools/world_repair.gd` (the library): `survey(root)` (tile size, the rectangle of painted cells, solid cells = tiles with a collision polygon), `walkable`, `nearest_walkable` (rings within 3 tiles), `edge_of`, `place_on_edge` (the door on the first floor cell in from the wall and the spawn one cell further in, scanned from a wanted fraction of the edge outwards), `plan_repairs(maps, plan, instance_of, start)` -> actions `{kind: retarget | spawn | door | unfixable, map, node, what, cause_map, cause_node, ...}` in the card's order: (1) a door to a missing map goes to the map the stored plan paired it with, else to the nearest area map on the plan's grid, else unfixable; (2) a door to a missing spawn point gets the spawn point on the target (the plan's cell when the pair is planned, else the opposite edge at the same offset) and, when the target has no door to the source, a door back (and a spawn point on the source when none is near its door); (3) an unreachable map gets a door pair with a reachable neighbour on the plan's grid (the start map when the map is not in the plan), unfixable when no neighbour or no free floor (the message names the edges tried). Planning updates its own graph, so a second run finds nothing; a spawn point or door a map already has is not added twice. `apply(action, root)`, `build(action)`, `actions_for(actions, file, node)`, `line(action)`. `builder/tools/world_repair.gd` is the headless runner (`-- [--dry-run] [--only=<maps>]`): it prints `[rpggen] WORLD_REPAIR {json}` and writes `rpggen/world_repair.json` (`repairs`, `skipped`, `unfixable`); maps are instantiated with `GEN_EDIT_STATE_MAIN` and packed again, so instanced children stay instances. Dock: `validator_panel.gd` gets **Fix** and **Fix all** (`plan_world_fixes`, `do_fix(action, root, undo_redo)` through `EditorUndoRedoManager`, a plain `UndoRedo` in tests, or directly; `apply_fixes` opens each map and leaves it modified). Python: `rpggen/core/world_repair.py`: `unedited_maps(target)` (file hash == the plan's `map_hash`), `run(target, godot, only=None, dry_run=False)` (a dry run first; then a backup of the maps to be changed, the plan, the result file and the ledger into `.rpggen_backups`; then the real run; a mended map that was rpggen's gets its new hash in the plan and the ledger, so it is still rpggen's), `auto` (only for a project with a stored plan, only the unedited maps), `report_lines`, `summary`, REGEN-005 while the editor has the project open. Orchestrator: `_repair_world` at the start of the report phase, only when the validator reported VAL-027 or VAL-028 (no extra Godot start otherwise); when everything was mended those warnings are dropped from the result; `RunResult.world_repair`; `run(..., repair_world=)`. Config `repair_world` (default true, stored only when false). CLI `repair-world --target X [--dry-run] [--json]` (a `repair_world` document in `cli.schema.json`) and `generate --no-repair-world`. GUI: `fixes` registers **Repair the world…** for VAL-027 and VAL-028; `ResultsPage.repair_world` (dry run -> the list -> yes -> run); *Advanced → Repair generated maps after a build*. `docs/regeneration.md` has the section *The world of areas and its repair*. **Deviations:** (1) the library is in the addon's `tools/` folder with `map_graph.gd` (T13.20's note), and the builder's `tools/world_repair.gd` is only the runner. (2) A repaired door stands on the floor beside the wall, not in the wall: the wall tile is the map owner's and a repair only adds. (3) The card's integration sentence (delete a generated spawn point from an area's map, regenerate, the spawn is back) contradicts its own hash rule: deleting the spawn point changes the map's hash, so the map is the user's and is only offered the repair. The hash rule won. `tests/integration/test_world.py` proves the automatic path with the case that really happens (a new area joined to an untouched map, which then gets its spawn point and door back; switched off, nothing is mended; Restore previous undoes it) and the asked path with an edited map (offered in the report, untouched; `world_repair.run` mends it; it stays the user's). (4) `content_checks.gd` did not change in this card (it reads the graph since T13.20), and `test_regeneration.py` is not touched. Tests: `test_world_repair.gd` (11), `tests/unit/test_world_repair.py` (14, with a stand-in for Godot), 4 GUI tests, the integration test above.

T13.19 and T13.24 follow-up — found while making the documentation pictures. The Quests tab's `GraphEdit` kept the scroll position of its first layout, which left every stage node just below the visible part: `_frame_graph` now sets `scroll_offset` two frames after a rebuild and on resize; the graph's own toolbar is hidden (`show_menu = false`, nothing in a read-only picture to zoom or snap) and a node is at least 120 px wide. `ValidatorPanel.do_fix` clears the owner of a node it takes out on undo, so a redo does not warn about an inconsistent owner.

T13.22 done — documentation, screenshots and changelogs; the version is 1.1.0. `docs/user_guide.md`: the welcome cards, the View menu and display scaling, the audit (folder groups, review mode, the tour, the animated preview, sounds with waveform and loop row, select similar, variants), Mapping (the gallery behind More…, the jump to the audit, custom and surface sound slots), Options (the built-in presets, options that need each other, an interior per house, the world of areas, Zstandard), Generate and Results (the estimate, the taskbar progress, Repair the world…), In Godot (the Quests and Cutscenes tabs, Fix and Fix all, shop quantities, the World entry of the map); the action RPG's followers on the Archetype page. Every name in it was checked against the program (the first draft had three preset names, two button labels and one dependency wrong). Pictures: `tools/make_doc_screenshots.py` adds `review_mode`, `areas_table` (the table is switched off again, so the generated project is the usual one), `variant_dialog` and `quests_tab`; the last one is drawn by Godot itself (`tools/doc_shots/quests_tab.gd`, run with a window, not headless), so `test_every_page_screenshot_is_rendered` accepts its absence on a machine without a display. All 20 pictures in `docs/images` were rendered again. `docs/status.md`: M13's state, the empty list of known limits, and *What is left before 1.1.0* (the open gate with its manual rows). `docs/architecture.md` §48 (and its copy `docs/spec/ARCHITECTURE.md`, which a test keeps equal): the deferred list without what M13 did. `CHANGELOG.md` `## 1.1.0` with the compatibility paragraph; the runtime's `CHANGELOG.md` (the compatibility notes, and every API of M13) and `README.md` (party members in both archetypes, `play_sound`, quest `next` / `rewards`, custom and surface slots, loop points); `README.template.md` (the maps of a world, doors that lead nowhere, shop quantities, the Quests and Cutscenes tabs). Version 1.1.0 in `rpggen/__init__.py`, `pyproject.toml`, the runtime's `VERSION`, the tools' `plugin.cfg`, `packaging/version_info.txt` and `installer.iss`. Two integration test files were renamed (`test_sound_bank.py`, `test_variant_enemy.py`): they had the base names of unit test files, which pytest cannot collect in one run.

T13.19 follow-up — found by the full integration suite (`test_editor_plugin_loads_in_the_editor`): a quest or sequence loaded from a file in the editor is a placeholder instance unless its script is a tool script, so the Quests tab's calls (`stage`, `problems`, ...) failed there. `quest_definition.gd` and `sequence.gd` are now `@tool`, like the dialogue resources. The headless GDScript tests could not see this: they do not run in the editor.

T13.23 (the 1.1.0 gate) — open; what one Windows machine could check on 2026-10-09 is done. **Passed here:** ruff and mypy; the unit, GUI and perf suites (1,590 tests with the new layout cases); the GDScript suite (536); the integration suite (93 run: 91 passed on the first run, and the two that failed pass after their fixes, an editor bug of the Quests tab and a test of T13.20 that T13.24 made out of date); T12.16 at the minimum size in both themes for both archetypes with review mode and folder groups on (`test_the_audit_page_fits_in_review_mode_with_folder_groups`) and the options page with a world of areas; the frozen smoke test on Windows (`tools/package.py --smoke`: the sample generates from `rpggen-cli.exe` and `audio-check` decodes Ogg Vorbis with the packaged libsndfile 1.2.2); the upgrade check with a project the code of `main` (1.0.0) generated, regenerated by 1.1.0 from its own config, for both archetypes: no prompt, exit code 0, no issue above a note, no `.rpggen-new`, every file under `game/` byte-identical (the action RPG gains `game/data/actors/companion.tres`, the party demo of T13.15), runtime 1.0.0 -> 1.1.0. **Not done, and not something this machine can do:** CI on `m13` (Ubuntu, the second PySide6 leg); the frozen smoke on Linux and macOS; loading a save written by the 1.0 build in the regenerated game (no 1.0 build exists to write one; the save version is unchanged and the GDScript save tests pass); the private corpus of T12.26 (not on this machine); the manual rows listed in `docs/status.md`; the tag `v1.1.0`, which also waits for `v1.0.0`.

## M14 (release 1.2.0)

M14 was started on 2026-10-09 on the branch `m14` (from `m13`) at the user's request, before the `v1.1.0` tag exists: the 1.1 gate (T13.23) is still open for CI on other systems and the manual rows. The milestone file says it starts after the tag; nothing in M14 depends on the tag itself, and the upgrade tests use the `m13` branch (or the tag once it exists) as "the 1.1 build".

T14.01 done — shared real-time combat primitives. `damage_info.gd`, `hitbox.gd`, `hurtbox.gd`, `health.gd`, `projectile.gd`, `state_machine.gd`, `pickup.gd` and `screen_feedback.gd` moved (`git mv`) from `archetypes/arpg/` to `combat/realtime/`; class names and behaviour are unchanged. At each old path a script without a `class_name` extends the moved one, with a comment naming the new path and the 2.0 removal. Deviations, all because the shared folder must load in a project that has no `archetypes/arpg/`: (1) the moved scripts used `RpgArpgMath` and `RpgArpgText`, so the formulas moved too, to `combat/realtime/realtime_math.gd` (`RpgRealtimeMath`), and `arpg_math.gd` is now `class_name RpgArpgMath extends RpgRealtimeMath` with no body, so every `RpgArpgMath.<name>` still resolves; `combat/realtime/realtime_text.gd` (`RpgRealtimeText.t`, `item_name`) holds the one sentence the pickup shows, under its 1.0 key `ARPG_ITEM_GAINED` so a project's translations stay valid, and `RpgArpgText` forwards to it. (2) `RpgHitbox.owner_actor()` returns `Node2D` (was `RpgActor`): the nearest ancestor that has `component(name)`, which an `RpgActor` is and the side-view actor of T14.03 will be; `RpgRealtimeMath.actor_of`, `stats_of`, `facing_of` (a facing name or a side-view facing number). (3) No `.uid` files moved or were added: the template project has none for any script under `archetypes/arpg/` (they were never opened in the editor after M9), so scenes name these scripts by path, and the shims keep those paths. `arpg_build.gd` builds new scenes with the new paths. Tests: `test_arpg_runtime.gd` (the old paths load, extend the moved scripts and give the same classes; a hitbox owner by components), `test_template_project.py` (every class name is defined once, the eight classes live in `combat/realtime/`, each shim is one `extends` line, the shared folder names no archetype class) and `test_regeneration.py::test_an_action_rpg_made_by_1_1_regenerates_and_old_combat_paths_still_load` (an action RPG generated with the template project of `v1.1.0`, or of `m13` while the tag does not exist, regenerates without a REGEN code, `game/` byte-identical, and a user script that preloads three old paths runs and loads every enemy scene). The generated README's upgrade note is written with the other README changes in T14.10.

T14.02 done — side-view detection. Collision: `CollisionSpec.profile` gains `ONE_WAY` and `LADDER` (the spec writes `one_way` and `ladder`; the profile names are upper case like the nine existing ones), appended after `CUSTOM` in the catalog schema and both copies of the profile schema; the audit's Collision list and the bulk panel (they share `COLLISION_PROFILES`) and the dock's inspector offer them; `geometry.gd` gives neither a shape, so a top-down TileSet is the same with either (the metroidvania's layers come with T14.03). Tags: `classifier.side_view_tags(name)` from the file's words and its nearest folder's: `platform`, `one_way` (also the pairs "one way" and "thin platform"; a one-way platform is not also tagged `platform`), `ladder`, `hazard`, `save_point`, `ability` (also "power up") and `parallax:<n>` (`classifier.parallax_depth`: far/mid/near are 1/2/3, or the number after bg, background, layer or parallax, 1 to 9; only for a name that says it is a background). `catalog._suggest_tags` adds them to an asset's detected tags and `_add_objects` to a sheet object's. `classifier.pack_view(inventory, sheets=())` -> `side` or `top`: one point per distinct folder or file name with a side-view word (platformer, sidescroller, metroidvania, jump, "side scroller", "side view") or a top-down word (rpg, jrpg, topdown, overworld, "top down"), two per character sheet (a jump or a fall and no `walk_up` / `idle_up` is side view; a walk up the screen is top-down); a tie or no evidence is `top`. A side-view pack gets the pack warning `view:side` (`catalog.VIEW_SIDE`, `catalog.pack_view(pack)`); a top-down pack gets no line, so no golden moves. Decision (detection had to learn two things for the platformer pack to be usable at all): `catalog._side_view_usage` runs per pack on pictures the generic rules took for a single sprite: a `parallax:<n>` picture with the role BACKGROUND becomes usage `background` (parallax layers are smaller than 256 px or have holes), and a picture tagged platform / one_way / ladder / hazard under a tiles folder whose size is a whole number of the pack's commonest tile cell becomes a `tiles` sheet on that grid with the role DECORATION (so `mapping.best_for("GROUND")` never picks a ladder) and the collision `ONE_WAY` or `LADDER` by its tag. Only assets with those tags are touched; `test_no_top_down_golden_has_a_side_view_tag_or_view` proves no existing golden has one. Roles: `requirements.REQUIRED["metroidvania"]`, `OPTIONAL` (the top-down archetypes share `RECOMMENDED`), `recommended(archetype)`, `REQUIRED_BY_ARCHETYPE` and `required_by(role, archetype)`; `mapping.rows` and `used_for(role, archetype)` use them; `ROLE_KEYS["BOSS"] = "boss"` (a new optional key of `config.starter`), BACKGROUND and DECORATION need no key. `naming.py` is listed by the card but did not need a change: `jump`, `fall`, `climb` and `dash` were actions already; a strip without a direction is still `<action>_down` (T14.03's `play_side` reads that). Synthetic pack `platformer` (22 images): a ground sheet, one-way planks, a ladder, spikes, a hero of seven strips (24 x 24 frames: the strips adapter only finds square frames, a limit that is not new), a crawler, a bat, a guardian of three strips, a villager, a save bench, a sign, two orbs and three backgrounds; golden added, no other golden moved. The private corpus (T12.26) is not on this machine and must be checked at the gate. Tests: `tests/unit/test_side_view_detection.py` (51).

T14.09 done — side-view sample pack and *Try the sample*. `tools/build_sample_pack.py --side` draws `sample_pack_side/` (28 images, 14 sounds, 1.9 MB like the first pack): `tiles/cave.png` (8 x 2 tiles of 16 px: mossy ground and rock, a three-cell plank, a two-cell ladder, spikes, a cracked block), the hero as eight strips of 24 x 24 frames facing right (idle, run, jump, fall, climb, attack, dash, hurt), a crawler, a bat, the guardian (four strips of 32 x 32), a hermit with a portrait, a save bench, a sign, two ability orbs (`orb_double_jump`, `orb_dash`), a potion, a coin, a chest, three parallax layers of 320 x 180 and the UI panel; `tools/sample_audio.py` gains `jump`, `land`, `dash`, `powerup` (`SIDE_FILES` = the first pack's sounds and these four). `LICENSE.txt` (CC0) and `SOURCE.txt`. `sample_side.rpgprofile.json` is the audit made by the normal pipeline with everything confirmed; its `tile_overrides` of the cave sheet say which cells are `ONE_WAY`, `LADDER`, a hazard (`custom.hazard`) and breakable (`custom.breakable`). `paths.sample_pack_side_root()`, the PyInstaller spec, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`. Welcome view: with `features.METROIDVANIA_AVAILABLE` (new, False until T14.13) *Try the sample* is a split button: a click is the top-down village as before, the arrow's menu has *Top-down village* and *Side-view cave* (`trySideSampleRequested` -> `MainWindow.try_side_sample`: `state.archetype = metroidvania`, then `ProjectPage.try_sample(side=True)` with the pack, the profile and the name *Sample Cave*). With the flag off the card is the plain button of 1.1 (no menu, the same tool tip). Decisions: the pack's licence and source files are `LICENSE.txt` and `SOURCE.txt` (the card says `LICENSE` with `SOURCE.txt`; the licence detection and the first pack use `.txt`). `rpggen.dev update-goldens` also writes `tests/golden/sample_pack_side.catalog.json`. Two detection fixes the pack needed: an opaque parallax layer whose texture looked like a tile grid is a background too (`_side_view_usage`), and the strips adapter leaves a character alone when an earlier adapter (the pack's profile) has claimed one of its strips: before, the profile kept the hero under its first strip and the strips adapter made a second hero from the remaining strips (no synthetic golden moved; the first sample pack has no character of several strips, so it never showed). The Archetype page does not know the third archetype until T14.10, so the test of the side entry checks the pack, the name, the target and the confirmed audit; the archetype it ends on is tested there. Tests: `tests/gui/test_sample_pack.py` (+9).

T14.03 done — platform actor. Runtime (`archetypes/metroidvania/`): `platform_math.gd` (`RpgPlatformMath`: `gravity` 2h/t², `jump_speed` 2h/t, `apex`, `fall_step`, `simulate_jump`, `cell_of` and the constants of the drop, the ladder and the hazard); `platform_actor.gd` (`RpgPlatformActor extends CharacterBody2D` with the exports of the card and `tile_size`; it is driven by intentions: `move_x`, `move_y`, `jump_held`, `press_jump()`, `release_jump()`, `press_dash()`; `step(delta)` is the whole physics step: coyote time, the jump buffer, a jump cut to half its speed when released, down + jump through a one-way platform (the mask bit of layer 11 is off for 0.2 s; on solid ground down + jump is a jump), ladders (70 px/s, no gravity; jump or leaving the tile ends it), wall slide and wall jump, dash and double jump and high jump behind `has_ability(id)`, `landed` / `jumped` / `dashed` / `facing_changed`; half of a step's gravity is added before `move_and_slide` and half after, so a jump reaches its height within 2 % at 30, 60 and 120 steps a second); `platform_player.gd` (`RpgPlatformPlayer`: the abilities of `GameState.abilities` when the saved game has them, the attack with the shared `RpgHitbox` on the facing side on the animation's active frames (a generic 0.06 to 0.2 s window without an attack animation), hurt by the shared `RpgHurtbox` with a knockback along x, hazards (1 HP, 0.6 s invulnerable, back to the last safe ground after 0.3 s with a fade of the router), safe ground (a cell stood on for 0.25 s with no hazard beside the feet), `break_blocks()`, `find_target` / `try_interact` for whatever has `interact` within a tile on either side, a key hint above it, and at 0 HP the combat mode's `apply_defeat` after 1 s); `platform_input.gd` (`RpgPlatformInput.poll(actor)`, called by the actor at the start of its own step so a press acts in the same frame; `enabled = false` for a test); `side_tiles.gd` (`RpgSideTiles`: what a room's tiles say at a point); `metroidvania_mode.gd` (`RpgMetroidvaniaMode`: no encounters, the shared defeat policy); `metroidvania_strings.gd`. `RpgAnimationDriver.resolve_side` / `has_side` / `play_side(base, facing, restart)`: `<base>_left` for the left when the sprite has it, else `<base>_right`, `<base>_side`, `<base>` or `<base>_down` mirrored. Decisions: `<base>_down` counts as "no direction" because the adapters store a strip without a direction under that name, and `run` also finds `walk` (the adapters' synonym). `RpgStatsComponent` gains `holder` (the top-down actor, or any ancestor with `component(name)`), so the side-view player shares `GameState.player_stats` too; `actor` is unchanged. Builder: `builder/lib/side_tileset.gd` (in `lib/`, not in `recipes/metroidvania/`, because the shared TileSet recipe preloads it and a top-down project has no metroidvania folder): for `config.archetype == metroidvania` only, the TileSet gets a second physics layer on collision layer 11 with a one-way polygon (the top 5 px of a 16 px tile) for `ONE_WAY` tiles and the bool custom data `ladder`, `hazard`, `breakable` from the tile's profile, the `custom` of its tile override and the sheet's tags; a GROUND, WALL or CLIFF sheet that names no collision is solid. `tileset_recipe.gd` calls it behind `_side`, and adds `side` to its input hash only for that archetype, so a top-down project's hash and bytes are those of 1.1. `recipes/metroidvania/metroidvania_build.gd` (body, health, hurtbox, hitbox builders), `player_recipe.gd` (a helper, not a recipe of its own: `actor_recipes.gd` asks it for the player's node tree through `ACTOR_BUILDERS`, so `generated/scenes/actors/player.tscn` keeps its path; the player has no camera and does not collide with enemies), `mode_recipe.gd`; `build.gd` runs it; the translations recipe knows the archetype's strings; the project settings name layer 11 `one_way` for this archetype only. The four input actions are not added by the project settings recipe as the card says but, like the action RPG's, by `config.default_input("metroidvania")` (`jump` Space / Z / pad A, `dash` Shift / C / right shoulder, `attack` J / X / mouse / pad X, `down` S / Down / pad down; `interact` moves to E / Up / pad Y because Space jumps). `ASSET-030` (informational) is raised by `requirements.check` for a metroidvania whose player has no jump or fall animation (`requirements.missing_air_animations`). Probe `tests/probes/probe_m14_api.gd`; six rows in `docs/godot_api_verified.md`. Tests: `test_platform_actor.gd` (15, in a room made in code, awaiting real physics frames), `test_tileset_merge.gd` (+3: a top-down TileSet has the same bytes with `NONE`, `ONE_WAY` or `LADDER` for both top-down archetypes and without a config; the side-view layers; a sheet's tags), `test_side_view_detection.py` (+2). The GDScript suite has 555 tests.

T14.04 done — side-view NPCs, objects and the camera. `archetypes/metroidvania/side_npc.gd` (`RpgSideNpc extends RpgPlatformActor`: `entity_id`, `dialogue`, `start_facing`; it stands on the floor by the platform actor's gravity, and `interact(actor)` turns it to the actor and opens the dialogue box), `side_interactable.gd` (`RpgSideInteractable`: an Area2D with `entity_id`, `can_interact(actor)` and `interact` -> `used` and the virtual `_use`), `side_camera.gd` (`RpgSideCamera`: `set_limits(rect)`, `limits()`, `find_target()` (the player inside its room), `wanted()` (1.5 tiles ahead of the facing and above the feet), `snap_to_target()`, `slide_from(point, 0.25)`, and a follow in physics time). `player_recipe.build_npc` makes NPC scenes with it (`actor_recipes.gd` asks the archetype's builder for the `npc` kind too; the `base` scene stays the top-down one). `recipes/metroidvania/objects_recipe.gd` builds `generated/scenes/metroidvania/side_camera.tscn` (the later cards add the save station and the pickup to it). Shared scripts, each change inert unless `side_view` is set: `RpgChest` and `RpgSign` gain `side_view`, `tile_size` and `can_interact(actor)` (the side-view player asks it: within a tile up or down, so not from a platform above; a top-down chest refuses nobody and the top-down interaction never asks); `RpgDoor.side_view` makes it a door entered with up or interact; `RpgSpawnPoint.side_view` and `side()`; `RpgUtil.beside(thing, actor, tile)` (in `core/util.gd`, which the card does not list: chest and sign are shared scripts and cannot name a class of the metroidvania folder). `RpgSceneRouter._set_facing(player, facing)` calls a side-view actor's `apply_facing` (a spawn's "left" / "right", a saved "-1" / "1") and sets a top-down actor's facing as before. `RpgAnimationDriver.play` ends with the side-view lookup when nothing else was found, so a side-view sheet on a top-down actor is shown (mirrored for the left) instead of not at all. `object_scenes_recipe.gd`, for the metroidvania only (its hash has the key `side_view` only then): a chest or a sign is taken off the world layer and gets `side_view`, and an obstacle is built as a prop (scenery of a room is in nobody's way). The dock: `placement.is_side_view(root)` (the root has `side_view`) and `floor_below(root, point)` (the top of the nearest tile with a collision shape, one-way platforms included, at or below the point); `plan` uses it in a side-view room. Tests: `test_side_objects.gd` (7); the GDScript suite has 562 tests.

T14.05 done — abilities and gates. `GameState.abilities` (`Array[String]`) and `broken_cells` (`{room_id: [[x, y], ...]}`) with `has_ability`, `grant(id)` (once; emits the new `Events.ability_granted`), `remember_broken_cell`, `broken_cells_of`; both are optional save keys written only when not empty (`_side_view_keys`), so a top-down save has exactly the keys of 1.1, a save without them loads empty and a damaged value is read as far as it makes sense; `new_game` clears both. `archetypes/metroidvania/abilities.gd` (`RpgAbilities`: the five ids, `ORDER`, `TABLE` (the action a hint names and the colour of the generated icon), `name_key` / `description_key` / `hint_key` (`MV_ABILITY_<ID>_NAME` ...), `display_name`, `description`, `hint` (with the glyph of the device in use: "Press [Space] twice"), `toast`, `glyph_texture`, `apply_broken_cells(root, room_id)`); `ability_pickup.gd` (`RpgAbilityPickup`, an `RpgSideInteractable`: touching or interacting grants, plays `sfx_ability`, shows the toast, sets `<entity_id>.collected` and removes itself; a pickup whose ability the player already has removes itself when it enters the room, so it is gone after a reload and also when the guardian granted the ability first); `ability_gate.gd` (`RpgAbilityGate`, a marker with `ability` and `kind`; it does nothing at run time). The player reads the saved game on every step (`RpgPlatformPlayer.has_ability`), so an ability granted in the air works at once. `RpgGate.required_ability`: open once the player has it (it listens to `ability_granted`), on the interactables layer so the player can try it, with the new text `UI_NEEDS_ABILITY`. `RpgCondition`: the atom `ability:<id>` (in `ATOMS`, so the dialogue tab's checker and completion know it). The archetype's strings gain the names, descriptions and hints. Found while testing: strict GDScript refuses `(cell as Array)` on a Variant even after `cell is Array`; a scratch script now parse-checks every changed template script with `--check-only` before the suite is run. Tests: `test_abilities.gd` (10: the round trip, a save without the keys, the table, the pickup, the double jump granted in the air, the dash's distance, cooldown and once per airtime, the wall slide and wall jump, a broken block after a reload, the condition, the gate); the GDScript suite has 572 tests.

T14.06 done — rooms and the world graph. **Python.** `rpggen/core/room_plan.py`: `defaults()` and `normalized(options)` (rooms 5 to 12, `room_size` [32..80, 23..45], abilities from the table in the table's order, enemies 0 to 4 per room, pickups 0 to 12, `added_rooms`; `RoomPlanError` otherwise, and `rooms >= len(abilities) + 3` through `min_rooms`; the config schema says the same with three `if/then` clauses), `vertical_reach` / `horizontal_reach(abilities)` (from the platform actor's numbers) and `gate_size(ability, before)`, `plan(options, seed)`, `extend`, `graph_of`, `placements`, `check` (unreachable and unpaired, both empty for every plan it makes), `summary` / `describe` ("6 rooms, 2 abilities, a guardian"), `world(rooms, seed)` (the world plan file of a metroidvania: no areas, a `rooms` section), `existing_rooms`, `exists`, `for_project`. The world is a chain of rooms from the start room to the end room on a grid of screens: east, except that one plain room may be a shaft whose way on is a ladder through its ceiling into the room above. `abilities` are the keys of `lockkey.plan` over that chain: an edge per ability is gated (in order), and each ability lies in a room the ones before it reach; the first is the guardian's, the others are pickups. A gate is geometry on the way out of a room: a ledge for the double jump (5 tiles) and the high jump (4), a pit of spikes for the dash, a wall of two breakable blocks, a three-wide chimney for the wall jump. **Decisions.** (1) A room is stored as `tiles`, one string per row (`#` ground, `.` air, `=` one-way, `H` ladder, `T` ladder with a platform on top, `^` spikes, `B` breakable), and everything that stands in it by its feet cell: the plan decides the geometry once, and the recipe, the preview and the tests read it, so there is no second planner to keep equal (the card's "`make_map_plan_cases.py` gets side-view cases" is a fixture of three planned worlds, `room_plan_cases.json`, which the GDScript tests build rooms from). (2) Gates are sized from what the player reaches with the abilities held before them, so the card's "6-tile gap needs dash" holds when dash is the only jump-changing ability before it, and the gap is 9 tiles after the double jump (which alone would cross 8). (3) A ledge's top reaches two tiles out over its face, because the wall jump would otherwise climb any ledge; a room with a gate has no platform to stand on but the floor, because a platform before a ledge or a gap is a way past it. (4) Every side exit is at the base floor, and a ledge steps down again in 3-tile steps, so the way back is always a plain jump. (5) The guardian grants the first ability, so the world has one pickup room per ability after the first (the card lists a pickup room per ability and also has the guardian grant the first). (6) An added room hangs on a free side of the room its `join` names (else of the nearest room that has one); it is built with its own exit, and its neighbour's side is made by the world repair, since a room that exists is never rebuilt. `orchestrator._plan_rooms` stores the plan with `world_plan.save`; `world_plan.record_hashes` and `world_repair.unedited_maps` count rooms as maps; the world plan schema gets the optional `rooms` section and no longer needs an area. `rpggen/core/side_kit.py`: a generated sheet of four tiles (plank, ladder, spikes, cracked block) is added to the catalog of a metroidvania whose packs lack one of those kinds (`kinds_in`, `missing_kinds`, `make_image`, `add`), so a top-down pack makes a playable metroidvania too; the generation log says which kinds are stand-ins. `map_preview.render_world(rooms, scale, selected)` (a cell per room where the plan put it, drawn tile by tile in flat side-view colours, a notch per exit, dots for the start, the save station, the pickups, the guardian, the enemies and the hermit, the letter of each gate), `world_rects`, `room_at`, `render_room`, `world_markers`. `config.starter_map_defaults("metroidvania")` is `room_plan.defaults()`; the config schema knows the archetype and the new `starter_map` keys; `staging.ARCHETYPES` has the third archetype (so its folders stay out of the other two archetypes' projects). **Runtime.** `room.gd` (`RpgRoom extends RpgMapRoot`: `side_view`, `room_size`, `tile_size`, `grid_cell`, `kind`; it sets the camera's limits, takes the broken blocks out again, `exits()`, `exit_named`, `player_placed` (the router's hook: the camera goes to the player at once) and `arrive(exit_id, carry)`), `room_exit.gd` (`RpgRoomExit`: an area just outside the edge with `exit_id`, `edge`, `target_room`, `target_exit`; `carry_of(player)`; `use`), `RpgSceneRouter.go_to_room(map, exit, carry)` (no fade), `RpgSideCamera.slide_in(from, 0.25)` (the view starts six tiles toward the side the player came from and may show past that edge while it glides), `item_pickup.gd`. Found by the first generated project: `RpgPlatformInput` named `RpgPlatformPlayer` and `RpgRoomExit` named `RpgRoom` while those named them back, and with exactly those two pairs Godot reported 39 resources still in use when the validation run quit (VAL-013); both now find the other by what it can do. **Builder.** `recipes/metroidvania/rooms_recipe.gd` (a room per planned room whose scene is missing, `game/maps/rooms/<id>.tscn`, user_seed: layers Ground, Platforms, Hazards, Decoration, Foreground, the exits with a spawn point named like each, the player, the camera, the gate markers, the sign, the hermit, the pickups, a chest, and two seeded dialogues), `side_kit.gd` (which tile of the TileSet is which kind: read from the TileSet's own one-way polygons and custom data), `parallax_recipe.gd` (a `Parallax2D` per `parallax:<n>` background at 0.15 / 0.4 / 0.7 of the camera's speed, a whole-number zoom at least as tall as the view), `objects_recipe.gd` (the camera, a pickup scene per ability with the pack's art by name or a generated glyph, two item pickups). `build.gd` leaves out the interior, starter map and second map recipes for this archetype and runs the rooms recipe in their place; `data_recipe.gd` starts a new game in the start room and seeds none of the village's data; `validate.gd` round-trips the start room and does not ask a side-view room for Y-sort parents. **Graph and repair.** `map_graph.gd` reads a room exit as a door (`start_for(plan)` is the start room); `content_checks.gd` reports an exit without a partner as VAL-028 and an unreachable room as VAL-027; `world_repair.gd` mends rooms the way it mends doors, digging nothing away: the new exit (`kind: exit`) stands on the floor beside the wall of the matching edge and its players arrive two and a half tiles further in (`room_exit_place`, `_add_room_arrival`, `_join_rooms`). Tests: `test_room_plan.py` (32, with 200 seeds without a finding), `test_side_world.py` (9), `test_rooms.gd` (11). The scenarios of the stored plan that need a generated project are in the integration tests of T14.11.

T14.07 done — side-view enemies and the guardian. `enemies/enemy_base.gd` (`RpgSideEnemy extends RpgPlatformActor`: the shared health and hurtbox, a hitbox around the body that hurts on contact (armed again every 0.5 s), a knockback along x and 0.2 s of stun when hit, `find_player`, `tiles_to`, `think(delta)` and `move(delta)` for the kinds, and on death the flag `<entity_id>.defeated`, the definition's experience and gold, its loot as `RpgPickup`s, `Events.entity_defeated`), `patrol.gd` (`RpgPatrolEnemy`: turns at a wall and where a ray ahead of its feet finds no floor), `flyer.gd` (`RpgFlyerEnemy`: floating, bobs at its anchor, comes for a player within 5 tiles, goes home), `shooter.gd` (`RpgShooterEnemy`: an `RpgProjectile` every 1.5 s at a player within 8 tiles with no wall between), `guardian.gd` (`RpgGuardian`: `phase_for(hp, max_hp)` at two thirds and one third; it walks, stops for a 0.4 s tinted telegraph, then lunges at 2.5 times its speed; from the second phase every other attack is a jump whose landing shakes the camera and drops a rock from the ceiling over the player; the third phase runs 1.5 times as fast; its defeat grants `grants`). `RpgPlatformActor.speed_scale`. `recipes/metroidvania/enemy_recipes.gd`: a definition (once) and a scene per ENEMY asset, the kind from the asset's tags and the words of its name (the definition's `behavior.kind` wins, so it can be changed), and `guardian.tscn` from the BOSS asset, else from the last enemy drawn one and a half times as large; without any enemy art the player's sprite stands in (ASSET-025). Decision: the definitions are made by the archetype's enemy recipe, as the other two archetypes do, not by `data_recipe.gd` as the card lists. The rooms recipe places the enemies (the kinds take turns, a flyer three tiles up), and in the guardian's room the guardian and an `RpgGate` in each side exit with `open_when = flag:<room>_guardian.defeated`; a project without enemy art gets the guardian's ability as a pickup there instead, so no world is left without its first key. Respawning at a save station is T14.08's. Tests: `test_side_enemies.gd` (11); the GDScript suite has 593 tests.

T14.08 done — HUD, room map and save stations. **HUD.** `archetypes/metroidvania/ui/hud.gd` (`RpgMetroidvaniaHud`, a CanvasLayer on layer 20): health as pips (`pip_count`, `pip_fill`: one per 4 HP, the last one part full; the pack's `pip_texture` or a drawn square), the abilities of the world as icons that are dim until they are granted (`ability_states`), the weapon worn (`weapon_text`) and the gold, refreshed by Events (the new `ability_granted` among them). `recipes/metroidvania/hud_recipe.gd` builds `generated/scenes/ui/hud_metroidvania.tscn` with the abilities of the stored plan and the art of their pickups; `boot_recipe.gd` uses the first archetype HUD that exists (`hud_arpg`, `hud_metroidvania`), else `hud_basic`. **Room map.** `GameState.room_fog` (`{room_id: true}`, `visit_room`, `room_visited`): a third optional save key, written only when not empty; `map_fog` keeps its meaning. `RpgMinimapData.room_list`, `room_boxes`, `room_map(plan, visited, current, held)`: the rooms the player has been in as boxes on the world's grid, the current one outlined in white, a notch per exit, a green mark for the save station and a yellow one for an ability that still lies there (rooms not visited are not drawn). `RpgMinimap` shows it for a side-view room, centred on the current room (`_refresh_rooms`, made again only when the room, the rooms visited or the abilities changed); `RpgWorldMapMenu` has the room map as its one entry in a world of rooms (`room_overview`). The mode marks a room visited when it is entered. **Save stations.** `save_station.gd` (`RpgSaveStation`, an `RpgSideInteractable`): `rest()` restores the party's health, clears the `defeated` flag of every ordinary enemy (ids with `_enemy_`; the guardian's stays), stores the place to return to in the saved game's flags (`respawn.map / x / y`, `respawn_point(state)`) and puts the current room's enemies back (`RpgRoom.respawn_enemies`); interacting rests and opens the save menu (`RpgUiController.open_menu`, new: a menu of its own that resumes the game when it closes); `save_to(slot)` for a caller without a menu. `objects_recipe.gd` builds `save_station.tscn` from the sprite tagged `save_point` (a drawn bench without art) and the rooms recipe places it. **The archetype's rules** (`RpgMetroidvaniaMode`): `save_at_stations` (the pause menu's Save reads "Save at a station" and is disabled; `pause_menu.gd` is not in the card's list, the change is five lines), `autosave_on_every_map` (the save manager then saves on every room change without the 10 s wait and without a toast), `respawn_after_defeat()` (full health, at the last station used, or at the start of the game before any, with everything the player had), and the hermit's `emit:credits` opens the credits when the dialogue closes. `RpgDefeatPolicy.can_continue()` (a save exists, or the mode respawns) is what the game-over screen and the automatic choice ask; Continue calls the mode's `respawn_after_defeat` when it has one. For the other archetypes each of these reads a property their modes do not have, so nothing changes. The room map's colours are whole 8-bit values so that a pixel read back from the picture is the colour that was drawn. Tests: `test_metroidvania_ui.gd` (6); the GDScript suite has 599 tests.

T14.11 done — Metroidvania smoke steps and integration test (done before T14.10: the smoke run is the first time the whole game is played, and the wizard only needs it to work). `builder/smoke/smoke_steps_metroidvania.gd`: sixteen steps read from the stored world plan (`rpggen/world_plan.json`), so they follow whatever world the seed made: `title_new_game`, `sign_dialogue`, `inventory_item`, `jump_clears_gap`, `one_way_platform`, `hazard_hurts_and_returns`, `gate_blocks`, `room_transition`, `npc_talks`, `guardian_dies`, `double_jump_reaches_ledge`, `pickup_dash`, `dash_crosses_gap`, `save_station_persist`, `defeat_returns_to_station`, `end_room`. The player moves by the real input actions where a step is about moving and is put in place where it is about something else; a step about a thing the world does not have passes with an INFO line (`step passes`), which the sample's test refuses. **Deviation:** the card appends the steps to the M6 sequence; that sequence walks the top-down village (a villager below the player, a house, a field) and cannot run in a room, so the archetype has a whole sequence of its own (`orchestrator.OWN_SMOKE_STEPS`, `smoke_steps_for("metroidvania")` is that one file) whose first three steps are the M6 ones that make sense here (title and new game, a dialogue, an item from the bag); save and load is step 14, through the station, the title and Continue. `defeat_returns_to_station` is an extra step (T14.08's rule had no end-to-end check). **Found by the run:** `RpgSceneRouter.go_to_room` changed the scene inside the exit's `body_entered` (a physics callback: "Removing a CollisionObject node during a physics callback"); it now waits for the next process frame when called in a physics frame. `world_repair._after_writing` recorded the new hash of a mended map only for areas, so a mended room looked edited afterwards; rooms are included. **Tests.** `tests/integration/test_m14_side_sample.py` (10): the side-view sample passes `M14_STEPS` with no vacuous step; only the metroidvania's folders are installed; six rooms and no starter map; scenes, data, text and input actions; a rebuild changes no file; the synthetic `platformer` pack and the top-down `basic16` (ASSET-030, the side kit, no jump animation) pass the sequence (the only step allowed to be vacuous there is the sign, which those packs have no art for); and the stored plan of T14.06 through five runs of one project: another seed builds nothing and changes no room, an added room is built and the room beside it gets its exit through the world repair, an exit removed from an edited room is VAL-028 as a warning and is offered, the same on a room whose hash is the recorded one is mended and reported. `test_determinism.py` has the archetype (the `platformer` pack, twice). It found two things that are Godot's and not the build's, now normalized like the node ids: the world plan stores the hash of each map's raw bytes (random `unique_id`s), and **a Translation resource is written in the order of its StringNames in memory**, which differs between runs now and then (the same lines in another order; seen once in three runs, on `strings_en.tres`; top-down projects have the same file and the same risk). `tests/perf/test_perf.py`: the side-view sample as a metroidvania, cold cache, smoke included, under 180 s (31 s here). **CI:** pytest markers `arpg` and `metroidvania`; the two main jobs run `integration and not arpg and not metroidvania`, and a new matrix job `archetype-integration` runs each archetype's suite on Windows and Linux.

T14.10 done — Wizard: the Metroidvania card, roles, options and preview. **Archetype page.** A third card (`metroidvania_card`, `cards`, `selected()` by key): Run → Jump → Find an ability → Open the way → Explore, drawn by `MockScreen._paint_side` (a floor with a gap, a plank with an orb, the player in the air, an enemy, health pips; with the pack's art the floor is its ground tile and the hero is its `jump` or `run` frame: `SceneArt.side_player`, `thumbnails.first_frame_image(prefer=, only=)`, which now also reads the frame from the action's own strip when the character is one file per action). The card is disabled with "Coming in a later version" while `features.METROIDVANIA_AVAILABLE` is false. `view_hint` reads `pack_view_of(catalog)`: "This pack looks side-view: a metroidvania fits it." for a side-view pack (always shown), "Your characters have walk-down animations; a metroidvania uses side-view sprites." for a top-down one (shown once the card is chosen; the card stays enabled). `air_warning` is ASSET-030 in the page's words, shown like the action RPG's swing warning. Three cards in the minimum window: a card's minimum width is 250 px (was 360), the flow diagram wraps into rows (`FlowDiagram.rows_for`, a step is never narrower than its text), the list has a small indent, and the controls line is hidden below 280 px of card width (the README has the controls). **Mapping.** The rows are the archetype's roles (`labels.MAPPING_GROUPS` puts Background and Decoration under World and Boss under Characters), "Used for …" and the missing-role message name what the archetype uses the role for. **Sounds.** `sound_slots.ARCHETYPE_SLOTS`: `sfx_jump`, `sfx_land`, `sfx_dash`, `sfx_ability` are fixed slots of the metroidvania only (`slots_for`, `slot_keys`, and an `archetype` argument on `is_custom`, `custom_slots`, `default_sounds`, `resolve_sounds`; the section's `set_data` takes it), filled by name (jump, land, dash, powerup). **Deviation:** the card lists them as plain fixed slots; as fixed slots of every archetype a pack with a `jump.wav` would have changed the sound table of top-down projects, so for the others such a name stays a custom slot. **Options.** `StarterWorldBox` replaces the Starter map box: rooms, room width and height, the abilities as check boxes in the plan's order, enemies, pickups, the bound (`rooms >= abilities + 3`: the spin box's minimum follows the abilities, so the value rises with it), the side-view preview (`map_preview.render_world` of the plan the options give now, with `room_plan.summary` under it) and the table of added rooms (name, beside which room). Once a room of the stored plan exists (`room_plan.exists`) the options are disabled with a note and only added rooms still count (`OptionsModel.kept_key`, `built_added_rooms`: the rows of rooms that were built cannot be edited). Imported maps and the Navigation feature are hidden (`FeatureGroups.set_hidden`), the layer list shows the six layers of a room and is fixed (`LayersBox.set_fixed`; `map_layers` of the config is left alone), the bindings table has the archetype's actions (it lists `config.input`). The summary card says "World: 6 rooms, 2 abilities, a guardian" (`options.Summary.world`); a preset applies with `navigation` off and without its layers. **Elsewhere.** `dry_run` plans the world (`summary.map.world`, `rooms`, and the counts of what stands in the rooms); the report's World section names the rooms, the guardian's room and where each ability lies (it said "0 areas"); the CLI takes `--archetype metroidvania`; `cli.schema.json` knows the archetype, the world keys and the repair kind `exit` (T14.06's, which a JSON result of a mended world would have failed on); the Results page opens the first room, lists the rooms folder and shows the world's side-view picture, which `orchestrator._world_picture` writes under the starter map picture's name (so the welcome view's project card shows it too); `README.metroidvania.md` (controls, rooms, adding a room in the wizard or by hand, the abilities table, gates, enemies by tag, the guardian, save stations and the map, animations); `README.arpg.md` got T14.01's upgrade note (old and new paths of the combat scripts, Reload Current Project); `dev.LAYOUT_ARCHETYPES` has the archetype, laid out with the side-view sample. Tests: `tests/gui/test_metroidvania_wizard.py` (16), and cases in `test_results_m11.py`, `test_sound_slots.py`, `test_cli_scripting.py`, `test_report.py`, `test_staging_regen.py`; T12.16 passes for the three archetypes in both themes at both sizes.

T14.12 done — Documentation, screenshots and changelogs. `docs/user_guide.md` has section **3a. Metroidvania** (trying the side-view sample, choosing the card and what the page says about the pack, the roles and what a pack may lack, the Starter world options with the bound and the letters of the preview, the controls, abilities, rooms, save stations and the map, editing rooms in Godot, adding a room), the Archetype paragraph names three kinds, and the first run mentions the second sample. `README.md` names the three kinds of game. `docs/regeneration.md`: *Rooms and the stored plan*. `docs/spec/02_SCHEMAS.md`: the archetype's use of the config (`starter_map` as the starter world with its keys and bound, `starter.boss`, the four sound slots, the input actions), the three optional save keys and the `respawn` flags, the table of added fields, the repair kind `exit`, and §12.1, the `rooms` section of the world plan (Room, Exit, Gate, the tile letters). `ARCHITECTURE.md` §48 (and its copy `docs/architecture.md`): *v1.2, more archetypes*, the list of what a new archetype brings and where it lives; "both archetypes" became "every archetype" in three places, and in `docs/coverage.md`. `CHANGELOG.md` 1.2.0; the runtime's `CHANGELOG.md` 1.2.0 (compatibility, the moved scripts and their shims until 2.0, the archetype's classes, the shared additions, the text keys, the builder) and `README.md` (real-time combat, metroidvania). The generated README's first paragraph says for a metroidvania that its world is the rooms and that section 22 is the place to start (`staging.KIND_NOTES`, `{kind_note}`; nothing is added for the other archetypes, so their README is what it was). **Version 1.2.0**: `rpggen/__init__.py`, `pyproject.toml`, the runtime's `VERSION`, the dock's `plugin.cfg`, `packaging/`, and the goldens (their `generator_version` line only). **Screenshots:** `tools/make_doc_screenshots.py` makes `welcome_samples.png` (the sample card with its menu of two samples, drawn into one picture: a menu is a window of its own) and `world_preview.png` (the Starter world box); `wizard_archetype.png` shows three cards, and `about_dialog`, `welcome`, `wizard_generate`, `wizard_results` and `conflict_resolver` were rendered again (version and times). The other pictures of the top-down sample came out byte for byte the same and were left. The screenshot test knows the two new files (the sample menu only exists with the flag on). M14's index marks T14.01 to T14.12 done. `docs/errors.md` is unchanged (ASSET-030 was added in T14.02). **Not changed:** the Archetype page's line "You can generate the other kind later…" (three kinds now): it is the source of a French translation, and changing it would drop that line from the pilot translation.

T14.13 (the automated part) — Gate (1.2.0). `features.METROIDVANIA_AVAILABLE = True`. `tests/gui/test_wizard_flow.py::test_the_metroidvania_path_generates_a_project_that_passes_its_smoke_test`: the welcome view's *Side-view cave* leads to the Options page with the archetype chosen, the card is left and chosen again on the Archetype page, the project is generated and its sixteen smoke steps pass, and the Results page opens the first room. **Upgrade test** (`test_regeneration.py`, both top-down archetypes): a project whose runtime came from the 1.1 templates (the `m13` branch stands in for the tag, which does not exist) regenerates without a `REGEN-` issue, and `game/`, `generated/tilesets/` and `generated/sprite_frames/` are byte for byte what they were. **Every synthetic pack as a metroidvania** (`test_all_synthetic_packs.py`, 17 packs, at the pack's own tile size, smoke test on): this found that a pack without ground tiles could not build a room at all, because the stand-in for the GROUND role is made for a top-down map and has no collision; in a side-view TileSet a stand-in of a solid role is now solid (`side_tileset.sheet_collision`). All 17 pass, among them packs of 32 and 48 px tiles. **Suites, locally on Windows:** ruff and mypy clean; `docs/errors.md` up to date; GDScript 599; unit and GUI 1,713 (3 skipped) and the 5 slow GUI tests; integration 89 shared (4 skipped), 11 action RPG, 28 metroidvania; perf 5. T12.16 passes for the three archetypes in both themes at both sizes with the flag on. The goldens of the top-down sample are unchanged but for the version line, and its screenshots other than the Archetype page, the welcome view and the pictures with a version or a time in them came out byte for byte the same. **Not done, and not doable here:** CI (nothing is pushed), Ubuntu and the second PySide6 leg, the private corpus (T12.26), the upgrade test against the real `v1.1.0` build, and the manual rows; `docs/status.md` lists them under *What is left before 1.2.0*. No tag was made. M14 was started before `v1.1.0` was tagged, at the user's request; the branch `m14` is on top of `m13`.

Smoke fix before 1.0 (2026-10-09, found while testing T13.12 on the `m13` branch) — step `sequence_plays` put its test trigger 64 px above the player. On a planned map (any non-default layout option) the start is three tiles below the first house's door, so the player was sent into the house and the step failed with "ran its sequence 0 times": a generation with a house and a changed map size or counts failed its smoke test. `_free_spot` now picks a place near the player with no body and no area, and the player returns to where it stood. Checked with the sample pack at 64 x 40 with three houses (it failed before, passes now).

## M15 (release 1.3.0)

M15 was started on the branch `m15` from `work` without a `v1.2.0` tag (the repository has no tags; M14 is merged into `main`). The spec asks for one pull request per card; the cards are one commit each on `m15` instead, as M14 was.

T15.16 done — Generate log: no colour-escape codes. `godot_log.strip_ansi` (CSI sequences and the two-character escapes); `godot._run` strips every line before it is kept, written to the log file and given to `on_line`, so the three agree; `run_godot` sets `NO_COLOR=1`; `LogView.append_line` strips too. The probe on 4.7.2 could not show that `NO_COLOR` changes Godot's piped output (it printed no codes with or without it for a failed `--script`), so the variable is only a courtesy and the strip is the guarantee (`docs/godot_api_verified.md`). Found on the way: `godot.py` used `godot_log` in a drain thread without importing it and the thread's `contextlib.suppress` did not hide the `NameError` but the test did show empty output: the unit test runs a real child through `_run`.

T15.18 done — Window title. `WindowMenus.title_text`: `<percent> % · <name> [regenerating] · <n>. <page> — rpggen`; the name is the project name, else the output folder's name; `rpggen` alone on the welcome view. `GeneratePage.run_percent` (None while nothing runs) and `_retitle` after each phase. The old form `rpggen — My Game` is gone and its test follows.

T15.01–T15.15, T15.17, T15.19, T15.21 (the page's half) and T15.22 done — the design system, the shell, every page and the dialogs. They were built together in one working tree (each page needed the tokens, the cards and the header at once) and are one commit; the notes below are per card. Correction to the T15.16 note above: `godot.py` used `godot_log` in a reader thread without importing it; the thread died with a `NameError` and the output came back empty, which is what the new unit test (a real child process through `_run`) showed.

**T15.01 Tokens.** `theme.TOKENS` (one dict per theme, colours as `#rrggbb` or `rgba(r, g, b, a)`), `theme.SIZES`, `theme.token`/`color`/`hex_of`/`px`. `style.qss.in` is the one template; `theme.render` replaces `@token` and `@icon(name, token)` after removing comments, and a placeholder that is not a token raises `KeyError`. `style.qss` and `style_light.qss` are gone. The palette reads the same tokens. The six status colours stay in `asset_grid.STATUS_COLORS` and reach the template as `@status_<name>`. **Values that differ from the spec's table** (the contrast tests decided, as the spec says they may): dark `border_strong` `#6b7486` and light `#868e9b` (3:1 against `bg` and `surface`); dark `accent` `#5c9bff`, light `#2b67c8`; dark `on_accent` is `#0b1220`, not white (white has under 3:1 on the dark theme's blue); dark `danger` `#f2706d`; the tokens `text_disabled` and `on_status` were added. `radius_chip` is 10, not 999: Qt draws no rounding at all for a radius larger than half the height.

**T15.02 Stock widgets.** Rules for buttons, inputs, combos, spin boxes, check boxes and radios (indicator images from the icon set, written once per process to a temporary folder in the token's colour, because a stylesheet cannot colour an SVG), tabs (underline), scroll bars, progress bars, sliders, group boxes (cards with the title inside), item views, headers, menus, tool tips, splitters. **Deviations.** (1) There is no `QProxyStyle`: Qt's stylesheet style draws every styled control itself and never asks a proxy for the focus rect. The keyboard-only focus ring is `theme.FocusRing`, an application event filter that sets the property `kbfocus="true"` on a widget focused by Tab, Backtab or a shortcut, and the template draws the 2 px ring from it inside the widget's own rectangle (padding shrinks by a pixel, so nothing moves). (2) Menus, tool tips and combo popups have square corners: a rounded top-level popup needs a translucent window, which costs the shadow on Windows. (3) The macOS indicator fallback was not written (not testable here).

**T15.03 Button kinds.** `widgets/buttons.py`: `set_kind` (repolishes; a primary says "main action" to a screen reader), `icon_only`, `style_box`. The bar: Quit quiet, Back secondary, Next primary, 96 px. Found on the way: `setText` takes a button's shortcut away, so Alt+N was lost in 1.2 as soon as the label became *Generate*; the window sets it again. The Generate page's Cancel is the page's own button (`bar_button`), shown in the window's bar in Next's place while the page runs.

**T15.04 Icons.** 47 outline icons in `rpggen/gui/icons` plus `app.svg`. **They are not Lucide**: fetching third-party files needed a download I had no go-ahead for, so they were drawn for rpggen in the same manner (24 px view box, 2 px stroke, `currentColor`) by `tools/make_ui_icons.py`, which is their source (`--check` in a test). No third-party notice is needed; a Lucide file with the same name drops in. `icons.icon`/`pixmap`/`set_icon`/`ThemedIcon`; the cache is cleared on a theme change.

**T15.05 Inline styles.** The eight inline styles became properties and rules; the two that carry a data colour stay (status badge, legend dots) and a test names them.

**T15.06 Header.** `PageHeader` (title, the page's own controls, help, and the purpose under them over the whole width). `WizardPage.page_layout()` gives every page the same margins (24, 20, 24, 16) and the header; `page_header` is the header whatever a page calls `header`. The window's body has no outer margin any more: the sidebar and the pages reach its edges, which is also what makes the page 930 px wide in the default window. The problem line is a danger `Banner`.

**T15.07 Stepper.** `widgets/stepper.py` `StepDelegate` paints the sidebar rows (and, compact, the Generate page's phases); the list items keep their texts, tooltips and accessible names.

**T15.08, T15.10 Cards and the welcome view.** `widgets/card.py`: `Card`, `Banner`, `CardButton`. The welcome view: three card buttons of one height, *Choose a sample* as a quiet button on the sample card, the recent projects in two columns from 900 px or the *How it works* strip, the Godot status as a banner with *Download* (primary) and *Choose…*. The banner's text stays *Godot 4.7 not found* (tests and the French file know it), not the spec's *No Godot 4.7.2 found*.

**T15.09 Motion.** `wizard.fade_in` (120 ms, removed when it ends; nothing with reduce motion). Buttons have no `:focus` rule; inputs keep an accent border while focused.

**T15.11 Project.** Two cards. The empty pack list is a 96 px drop zone that holds the Add buttons and the sample link; with packs the list is as high as its cards up to three. A hint is the field's placeholder and *… is fine* is no longer said in words (the page fits 880 × 540 that way); errors and warnings stay under the field; the Godot version is a chip under the label.

**T15.12 Audit.** The review line, its bar and *Review mode* are in the header; one wrapping filter row (chips with status dots, the two combos, search with an icon, the folder toggle as an icon button, *Profile* at the end); a footer with the queue line, the keys and *Shortcuts*; the detail panel is a card with four sections; cells have rounded corners. **Not reached: the filter row on one line at 1100 px.** The row needs about 1000 px and the grid's pane has about 520 beside the detail panel, so it is two lines there (it was three, with a clipped *Profile*). Putting it over the whole page would mean moving the splitter into the Assets tab, which a dozen tests and the saved splitter sizes depend on; left for a decision. The bulk panel was not turned into a banner-shaped bar.

**T15.13 Mapping.** The summary and *Undo* are in the header, each group is a card, a row is the picture (32 px), the name with its hint, the status word, the combo and three quiet icon buttons. A missing role has a warning edge; the placeholder choice is still the radio button of 1.2, not a new button.

**T15.14 Options.** Presets and *Reset to defaults* in the header, the feature groups in two columns from 920 px of page width, every other section full width, *Advanced* a card with a chevron.

**T15.15 Generate.** Time in the header, the percentage beside the bar, the phases as a checklist with a turning ring (still with reduce motion), the log in a card.

**T15.17, T15.21 Results.** A banner (success, danger, info for a cancelled run) with the run id; after a failure the action row under it, the cause under the issue's message (*Godot said: …*), the fix and its buttons, and the last 30 lines of the failed phase's log with *Show more*; after a success the picture in a card, the main action, and the counts as tiles. The body scrolls. `godot_log.first_error`/`tail`; `orchestrator._attach_cause` puts `cause`, `log` and `file` into the detail of the failed phase's errors; `fixes.fixes_for_issue` adds *Open the log*, *Show this asset in the audit* and *Open failed folder* from the detail. **Deviations.** The fix buttons are under the list for the selected row (the first is selected at once), not inside each row: the tests of 1.2 drive them through the selection. There is no ledger table from generated files to assets; `asset_of_file` matches the pack's own path, an object's name, or a unique file stem.

**T15.19 Dialogs.** `FocusRing` also styles every `QDialogButtonBox` when it is shown (accepting button primary; Close when there is none), so message boxes follow too. Storage's *Clear* of your data is danger; the resolver has pane headers with a count chip and 300 px panes; the regeneration preview has state icons and an info banner; About shows the icon.

**T15.22 Sample.** The welcome card and both entries of *Choose a sample* walk to the Generate page and start; the page shows an info banner and Back stays open until `promote` (`stop_for_back` cancels the run). The Project page's own button stops at Options as before.

**Tests.** `tests/gui/conftest.py` now deletes the widgets pytest-qt closed after every test. Without it every window of the session stayed alive and each theme change restyled them all: the layout-fit file took 6 minutes before M15 and would have taken far longer with the full stylesheet; it takes 45 seconds now, the whole GUI suite under 3 minutes. `theme.apply_theme` no longer sets a stylesheet that is already set.

T15.20 done — Regeneration that skips what did not change. `orchestrator.inputs_fingerprint` (the catalog, the config once it has its defaults, the placeholders, a hash of the template project, the generator's version) is taken in the catalog phase and stored in the ledger with the Godot version (`inputs_sha256`, `godot_version`, both optional, `ledger_version` stays 1). Before the import, `repeat_reason` decides: a regeneration with the same fingerprint and Godot, after a run that succeeded, over a project whose generated files nobody changed or deleted (`ledger.edited_generated_files`; `game/`, `project.godot`, the config and the catalog do not count) skips import, build, validate, smoke and export through `on_skip`, and `promote(repeat=True)` removes the work folder without touching the project, not even its ledger. Measured on the sample pack: the project is byte-identical, reports and logs included. **Deviations.** (1) The note code is `REGEN-011`: `REGEN-010` was taken (a file that cannot be merged). (2) The report is not rewritten by a repeat, so its *Checks* section does not say that the checks were skipped: the spec asks for that and for a byte-identical project, and the two exclude each other; the skip is said on the Generate page, in the log, in `--json` and by `--dry-run`. (3) *The last run failed or was cancelled* is kept in rpggen's data folder (`core/run_memory.py`), because such a run must not write into the project. (4) The checkbox *Run every check again on every regeneration* is in the regeneration preview as the spec says, but that dialog only opens when a run has changes, so it sets the project's standing choice (`run_every_check` in the ledger) rather than this run's; `--full` is the one-run switch and the wizard has none. (5) The perf rows and the new ceiling `REGENERATE_UNCHANGED_S` are not written yet. Found on the way: a first generation fills `sounds` into the config during the catalog phase, so the fingerprint is taken after that default, or the first regeneration would never be a repeat. Godot crashed once on exit from `--import` (0xC0000005) in the very first test run and not in the six runs after it; noted because an Ubuntu CI job once ended with exit 139 at the same place.

Tests added for the pages (`tests/gui/test_m15_pages.py`): the drop zone and the two-pack fit at 880 × 540 (pack cards are two lines now, 50 px, with the chips on the name's line), the audit's filter row and the *Profile* button inside the page at both sizes, the mapping rows, the options grid, the phase states, the results banner, cause, log tail and fix buttons.

T15.20 finished — the perf rows, and a regeneration that never was a repeat. The two rows are in `tests/perf/test_perf.py`: `regeneration of 1000 images, nothing changed` under `REGENERATE_UNCHANGED_S` and `regeneration of 1000 images, full` (`full_checks=True`) under the old ceiling. **Writing the row found a bug**: the perf project fills the player with a placeholder, and such a project was never a repeat. A regeneration reads the project's catalog, which holds the placeholder of the last run; its file is in no pack, so `catalog.assemble` carried it over as MISSING with a CAT-004 warning, `add_placeholder_assets` then added a second asset with the same id, and the catalog grew by one asset per run (three runs, three `debug_placeholders.player`). The side-view kit of a metroidvania had the other half of it: `side_kit.add` found its asset and returned, so the kit stayed MISSING after the first regeneration.
