Both were there before M15 (the warning and the doubled asset since M5, the kit since M14); T15.20 made them matter, because the fingerprint was taken from a catalog that was never the same twice. Fixed: `catalog.RUN_MADE_PACKS` names the two packs a run makes itself and `_carry_previous` does not report their assets as vanished (they are still carried, so a placeholder ground keeps its tile-set source id); `add_placeholder_assets` and `side_kit.add` replace the asset of the last run instead of adding to it or leaving it; `orchestrator.add_run_made_assets` does both, and **the fingerprint is taken after it**, from the finished catalog, in the run and in the dry run (which makes them in a copy, their files in a folder that goes away). Measured on this machine (slower than the one of the M15 review: a full generation of the pack takes 78 s here against 58 s there): nothing changed 15.3 s, full 89.5 s. **Deviation:** the ceiling is `10 * TOLERANCE` (20 s), the allowance every other ceiling of the file has for CI hardware, not a bare 10 s; what is left of the 15 s is the copy of 1,000 pack files into the work folder and the hashing of them, which a repeat still does to know that nothing changed. A project generated by an earlier build of this branch regenerates in full once (its fingerprint was taken at the other point).

T15.20, a correction — the user's own files count. The whole integration suite (which the T15.20 commit had not run) failed three tests of M11 and M13 on the branch as it was: a map whose door was removed by hand was regenerated without the validator's VAL-028; a project made to look like 1.0's (edited starter map, interiors missing) did not get its interiors; a run with the world repair on, after one with it off, mended nothing. All three for one reason: *files under `game/` do not count* made such a run a repeat, and a repeat does not validate. The spec only names the generated files, and the first version followed it; the older tests say what a regeneration is for after a map was edited, and they are right. Now: `run_memory` notes, with the outcome of a successful run, one hash over everything under `game/` and `project.godot` (in rpggen's data folder, as the outcome is: a repeat leaves the project byte for byte, and the note is taken after the world repair, which writes maps); `repeat_reason` says *a file of your own changed since the last run* when it differs or nothing is known (another computer: one full run); `inputs_fingerprint` takes the world repair's switch (the run's, else the config's). The ledger could not answer this: it keeps what a seed was when it was made, for the world repair. The sentence of `docs/regeneration.md` that sent the user to `--full` after editing a scene is gone. No test was changed to pass; the unit tests of the rule got the new cases.

T15.23 done — Visual regression goldens. `dev.ui_golden_pictures` renders the welcome view and the seven pages in both themes and both window sizes (32 pictures, `tests/gui/goldens`, 2.4 MB); `dev.compare_pictures` gives the mean difference, the largest difference of a 32 x 32 block and a picture of the difference; `tests/gui/test_visual_goldens.py` (the page test is `slow`) fails with the page, theme and size and leaves both pictures in `goldens/_failed` (ignored by git, uploaded by the Windows CI job); `python -m rpggen.dev update-ui-goldens` rewrites them. What keeps a picture the same on every machine: reduce motion is on (a page grabbed during its fade is half transparent, which the old `layout-shots` pictures were), the welcome view is a first start with no Godot found, the Project page has no folder and no Godot yet (their paths and the two error lines that wrap by the length of a path would be this machine's), and the recent projects are cleared before each welcome view (the Generate page records one). Two renders on this machine are identical pixel for pixel; a `margin-top: 6px` on the button rule failed 28 of the 32 pictures. **Deviations.** (1) There is no bundled test font: `ensure_real_font` takes Segoe UI on Windows and DejaVu on Linux, and adding a font file needed a download nobody asked for. The goldens are rendered with Segoe UI (`made_with.json`) and the test **skips** where `ensure_real_font` gives another family, which is the Ubuntu legs; the spec allows the skip for a platform without the font. A bundled font would make the Ubuntu legs compare too (both systems render through FreeType when Qt runs offscreen). (2) The renderer is `dev.py`, on the state `layout_state` already made for the layout test, not `make_doc_screenshots.render`: that one analyses a pack in a worker and runs Godot. `render` got its `size=` and `font=` parameters (and `--size`, `--font`). (3) The goldens were made on a machine other than CI's: if Segoe UI of the CI image draws differently enough, the first run there says so with pictures, and the goldens are then made again from that run's `_failed` folder.

T15.24 done — Documentation, screenshots and changelog. The user guide follows the moved controls (the two-click first run, the stepper, the title, the Project page's hints, the audit's one row and footer, the Mapping page's three icon buttons, *Reset to defaults*, the Results page's tiles and what it shows after a failure); `CHANGELOG.md` has 1.3.0; `CONTRIBUTING.md` has *Look and feel* (tokens, no inline styles, button kinds, icons, the golden workflow) and how to make the screenshots on a scaled display; the README says the first screen makes a project in two clicks. All 23 pictures of `docs/images` are regenerated at 1100 x 720, among them the new `results_failed.png` (T15.21's *Done when*: a cause and a button), which `make_doc_screenshots.failed_result` makes without a failing Godot. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` is unchanged: the icons are rpggen's own. Found while making the pictures: with a picture of the starter map the Results page cut the count tiles in half at the bottom of its scroll area in the default window; the picture is at most 300 px high now (it was 340) and the tiles show. The version is still 1.2.0 in `pyproject.toml` and `rpggen/__init__.py`: the release procedure bumps it (docs/release.md).

T15.25 (the gate) — the part a program can do on this machine, 2026-10-10: see docs/status.md. Godot crashed once more on exit from `--import` (exit 3221225477, 0xC0000005, `BUILD-001`) in `test_an_action_rpg_world_with_a_locked_cave`, in one of three runs of that test; the same test passed alone right after. That is the crash the T15.20 note saw: it is not tied to a test, and a CI job that ends this way needs a second run, not a fix here. And `tests/unit/test_godot_cancel.py::test_on_line_receives_lines_before_the_process_exits` failed in one of three runs on this machine: it expects a line of the child's stderr between two of its stdout, and the two are read by two threads. Left as it is (a test is not weakened to pass); it is from before M15. Not done here: CI (nothing of `m15` after `e1e94cd` has run there), the frozen builds, the upgrade test with real 1.0, 1.1 and 1.2 builds, the manual rows, the tag.

T15.23, the test font — bundled on the owner's word (2026-10-10), which takes back deviation (1) above. `tests/gui/fonts` has DejaVu 2.37 from the project's GitHub release (`dejavu-fonts-ttf-2.37.tar.bz2`, SHA-256 `fa9ca4d1…e17d7`): the regular face, the bold one and the mono one, with the licence. Three files and not one, because Ubuntu has the bold and the mono face installed and would draw them where Windows, with the regular face alone, would make a bold of its own. `dev.golden_font()` loads them for the length of a block, sets DejaVu Sans 9 pt without hinting (the hinter is where two FreeType builds differ most) and afterwards removes them from the font database, so the layout test, which measures with the machine's font, is not changed by the order of the tests. The goldens are rendered again with it, the page test no longer skips for a font, and `ensure_real_font` is as it was. **Not seen yet:** the Ubuntu legs. Both systems draw through FreeType when Qt runs offscreen, but the system's build and fontconfig's settings are Ubuntu's own; and a text that asks for a monospace family gets DejaVu Sans Mono from fontconfig there, here whatever Qt picks from the three files.

M16 was started on the branch `m16` from `m15` on 2026-10-10, on the owner's word, before `v1.3.0` exists: the gate of M15 waits for CI and the manual rows. `v1.0.0`, `v1.1.0` and `v1.2.0` are tags of the development checkout only.

T16.21 started — the first three of its points; the card stays open. **Files that are not assets.** `inventory.not_an_asset`: a `._*` resource fork, a `.html`/`.htm`/`.url`/`.webloc` link, an empty file, and a file named *In development* (also *coming soon*, *placeholder*) are skipped with the reason `not_an_asset` (`__MACOSX`, `.DS_Store` and `Thumbs.db` already were, as `os_junk`). A licence-like name is never skipped, an empty `LICENSE` included; a `readme` stays what it was, a licence text, although the card lists it with the files to skip: the licence detection reads it. **Previews and guides** are *unused* with the reason on the card, not left out: `classifier.not_game_art` (a `preview`, `previews`, `screenshot(s)` folder; a picture at the pack's root with *preview, test, grid, guide, slots, template, mockup* or *showcase* in its name; a picture anywhere whose name is nothing but such words, which is what *Wheels in Pixels* needs: its `Grid Slots.png` lies in `Enviroment/`, not at the root). The folder an archive wraps everything in does not count as a folder. The asset gets the role NONE and the tag `unused_because:<reason>`, and `explain.why` turns that into the line. **Deviation:** the card has the inventory skip a preview folder; here its pictures are catalogued and unused, like the root pictures. A project of 1.0 to 1.3 may have such a picture in its catalog, and a file that leaves the inventory comes back as *missing* with a warning (CAT-004), which the compatibility contract forbids. **Names as artists write them.** The tokeniser already split on every character that is not a letter or a digit and dropped numbers, so `10. Doors, Windows & Architectural Pieces`, `BACKGROUND (1) .png` and an emoji needed nothing but tests. New is `classifier.fold_typo`: a word of six letters or more that is one letter away from exactly one word of the word lists is read as that word (`BACKGROUD`, `Enviroment`), and the *Why* line says `backgroud read as background`. Two limits the card does not have, both found by the tests: a plural is not folded to its singular (grammar, and it would move the corpus), and the first and the last letter must be the same, because without that `village` was read as `villager` and `status` as `statue`. `environment` is in the vocabulary for the fold although no role asks for it. **Sizes in a name.** `naming.name_facts` takes `_<W>x<H>`, `_<N>frames`, `_<N>split` and `_<N>dir(ections)` off the end of a stem; the strips adapter matches the action on what is left and cuts a strip by the name when the picture has that size (one row of N cells), by the picture's gaps when the name does not fit. A strip with frames and no action word (`corn_16x32_8frames`) is one looping animation, with the tag `growth` when the name says so. `sleep`, `fly` and `dead` are action words (`move` already was `walk`). **Not done of these three:** `_<N>split` and `_<N>dir` are parsed and not used yet (nine-slice pieces and direction counts go to the detection with the engine layouts). **Open in the card:** numbered frames without an action word and the blank-frame split (ADAPT-005), the binary `.aseprite` reader (ADAPT-006), engine layouts of the same art, the bundle sniff, sheets named by a number, RPG Maker names inside other packs, the cutter's manifest (ADAPT-007), `.jpg` and copies, colour variants, the three synthetic packs. Seen on the real racing pack with this much: 226 files, `Grid Slots.png` unused with its reason, the `Enviroment` folder read; its vehicles are still 185 *decorations*, which is T16.02's rotation sheets.

T16.21, second part — numbered frames without an action word. `sequences.find_frame_exports`: a folder whose pictures are all `<STEM><n>` with one stem, one size and no other picture (the number glued, after a space or in brackets: `CHARACTER1`, `PORTAL 10`, `BACKGROUND (1) `); a bare number is no stem, so the numbered sheets of a bundle are never frames. `split_export` cuts it at the fully transparent frames, which belong to no animation (several in a row are one cut), else at the gaps of the numbering. The adapter makes one sprite with `anim_1` … `anim_n`, confidence 0.5 (*needs review*) and the note **ADAPT-005**. **Narrower than the card for now:** only an export with a blank frame is taken. The card takes every such folder, but `icons/icon_01` … `icon_64` is one too, and nothing in the names tells an animation from a set of variants; the other evidence the card names, the `.aseprite` source beside the frames, counts once its frames and tags are read (the next part: the reader). `FrameExport.source` already finds it. The adapter's `match` asks the pictures as well, so a pack with a folder of numbered icons does not list the adapter. **Not done:** typing a name for an animation in the audit (there is no animations dialog to extend; the names are edited where the animations of an asset are). On the real village pack: `CHARACTER1` … `168` is one sprite with 8 animations of 8, 16, 17, 7, 41, 18, 24 and 8 frames (139 frames and 29 blank ones); its boxes, flames, portal and post have no blank frame and are still 80 pictures.

T16.21, third part — the binary `.aseprite` file. `adapters/aseprite_file.py` (a module of its own, not in the JSON adapter's file: it has no adapter in it) reads the header, each frame's duration and the tags chunk `0x2018` (name, first and last frame, direction, repeat count), steps over every other chunk, and raises `AsepriteError` for a file that is cut short, is no Aseprite file, or has a tag over frames it does not have. `write` makes a file of frames and tags with no layer and no cel, for the tests and the synthetic packs. **What the real pack taught** (all twelve sources of *Dark Cursed Village* are read): the tags of its character are all called `Tag`, Aseprite's name for a new tag, so the source gives **ranges, not names**. The card expected names. The ranges are still the better cut: nine animations, where the blank frames give eight, because two of them have no blank frame between them. So in `SequencesAdapter._export`: a source beside the frames that has as many frames as there are pictures (numbered without a gap) is the file they were exported from; its tags are the animations, named by the tag when its name has an action word (`Run right` → `walk_right`, with the speed from the frames' durations and the loop from the tag's repeat count), `anim_n` and ADAPT-005 otherwise; a source without tags makes frames of a folder no blank frame separates (the pack's box, flame and post, 16 frames each); a single animation without a name is `idle_down` and needs no review, since it is what a prop does all the time. A source of another shape says nothing (the one-frame `BACKGROUND.aseprite` beside four background layers: four pictures). A source that cannot be read is **ADAPT-006**, a note, and the pictures are cut at their blank frames as without it. **Deviations.** (1) No pixel is decoded, so a `.aseprite` with no export beside it is not rendered to a strip; the card asks for that, and it needs the cel chunks, zlib and the blend of layers. (2) The fixture *made by Aseprite 1.3* is not in the repository: the pack's files are not ours to copy, and Aseprite is not on this machine. The reader was run over the pack's twelve files by hand (frame counts 1, 16 and 168, tags, a round trip through `write`), and the tests use files `write` makes, with unknown chunks put in by hand. (3) The pack's portal folder holds two exports under one stem (`PORTAL1` … `16` and `PORTAL 2` … `17`), so its numbers are not unique and it stays 31 pictures.

T16.21, fourth part — a bundle of packs, and sheets named by a number. **The bundle.** `pack_sniff.find_bundle` reads the names and sizes of a listing: ten or more folders with pictures, side by side at the top (inside the one folder an archive wraps everything in), no picture beside them. `PackSniff.bundle` lists them (name, path, pictures, bytes) and no file is opened: for a bundle the format question is left to the moment a folder is added. A folder's listing gives sizes through `os.scandir`, a 7z through `list()`. **One condition the card does not have:** at least half of the folders must be named as packs are (*pack, tileset, asset, kit, bundle, collection* in the name). Without it the ten part folders of a character pack (`body`, `hair`, `torso`, …) are a bundle of ten packs. *CuteSCKR*: 78 packs, 1,073 pictures, 690 MB, told in 0.02 s. The user's `side scrollers.zip` is another thing, a zip of eight zips, and is not handled: an archive inside an archive is not a folder. **Numbered sheets.** A picture whose name is only a number, at least 256 px on its short side, in a folder or a pack whose name says *tileset* or *tiles* (or of 768 x 768 whatever the folder says) is a tile sheet (`GenericGridAdapter._numbered_sheet`): 48 px for 768 x 768, the B to E sheet of RPG Maker MV, else the measured square grid, else the largest of 48, 32 and 16 that divides it. Its tags are its folders' words without those every pack of a bundle shares (`classifier.folder_tags`: *Medieval Fantasy Town Pixel Art Tileset Pack* gives fantasy, medieval, town); the pack's own name counts as the outermost folder, for a folder added on its own. Confidence 0.6, so *needs review*, and the *Why* line starts with *the file name is only a number: read by its folder's words*. The objects the picture analysis finds are kept when its grid is the one chosen. On the real town folder: 16 tile sheets of 48 px in one tile set, where there were eight tile sheets of three cell sizes and eight unknown sprites. **Seen and not solved:** *Monder Interiors* has `Tile-A2-11.png` and `Tile-A4-11.png` of 400 x 600 and `Tile-B-12.png` of 800 x 800, RPG Maker's names on sheets that are not RPG Maker's sizes (a 50 px tile, it seems); the card's point about RPG Maker names inside other packs has to deal with that.

T16.21, fifth part — what a sprite-cutting tool left behind. `adapters/cutter.py` (`CutterAdapter`, priority 45): a `manifest/sprites.json` or `sprites.csv` (or one anywhere under a folder that starts with `_organized_sprites`) is read, the JSON before the CSV; each entry's rectangle becomes an **object of its sheet**, with the category as its tag and `authoritative` set, so the rectangles the picture analysis guesses for that sheet give way. The sheet is found by the file name in `source_file` (the JSON has it; only the name is used, the path is of the artist's own disk and reaches no catalog), else by the number in `sheet_05`: the sheet called `5.png`, else the fifth. **The card says `sheet_01` is the pack's first sheet; in the real pack the sheets are 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8 and `sheet_05` is `5.png`**, so the number is tried as a name first. The cut copies and the tool's previews are *unused* with their reason (`unused_because:cut_copy`, `cut_preview`). A manifest that does not fit (a rectangle outside its sheet, a sheet that is not there, no entry, not JSON) is **ADAPT-007**, a note; the copies are then sprites like any picture and only the previews are unused. `ADAPT-007` has `{reason}`, which the card's wording did not need. On the real supermarket folder: 7 tile sheets of 48 px, **431 objects** from the manifest (the card's number), 445 copies and previews unused.

T16.21, sixth part — one art for several engines. `classifier.engine_plan`: a pack with two or more folders under `engines/` is read from one of them, Godot's when there is one, else RPG Maker MV's, else another RPG Maker's, else the first by name; `not_this_engine` gives the reason for every picture of another engine's folder (`unused_because:other_engine`), and the pack gets the warning `engines:<n>:<kept>` for its card. **How the real pack differs from the card's picture of it:** the engine folders of *Super Retro Ranch* are not copies of each other. `assets/` holds the art as single strips, and each engine folder holds that art put together for the engine (character sheets for RPG Maker, autotiles for Godot and Unity). So nothing is `duplicate_of` a file: there is no one file a sheet repeats, and the pictures of the other engines are *unused* with their reason and no `duplicate_of` tag. Godot's folder has every autotile twice, `2x2` (64 x 64) and `3x3` (192 x 64, the 47-tile sheet the terrain reader knows): with both there, `2x2` is unused (`other_layout`), and so are the raw 16 x 96 templates under `assets/autotiles` when Godot's folder has autotiles; the card would read those templates as 6-tile sheets, for which there is no reader, and with the 47-tile sheet there it is not needed. Everything unused this way is in the catalog and comes back with a role. **Not done:** the words on the pack card (*art for 5 engines: Godot's is used*; the warning is there for T16.16), and `_<N>split` / `_<N>dir`. On the real pack: 1,040 files, 40 left out of the inventory (21 of macOS, 19 links and *In development* files), 834 assets, 335 of them unused (288 of the four other engines, 24 of the 2x2 layout, 23 raw templates), `engines:5:Godot`.

T16.21, seventh part — copies and photographs. A `.jpg` or `.jpeg` has no transparency, so the generic reader never makes a tile sheet or a numbered sheet of one: a background from 256 px, a plain picture below. A file whose name says it is a copy (`classifier.copy_of`: a suffix that is not ASCII, as `_副本`, or *copy*, *(copy)*, *- Copy*) **and** whose original lies beside it is unused with the reason; without the original beside it, it is a picture like any other.

T16.21, eighth part — colour variants a pack ships as files, in the catalog. `classifier.colour_key` gives what a file is without its colour and the colour (a colour word or two joined, as a word of the file name or as a whole folder; numbers stay, so `cow_01` and `cow_02` are two animals; `Redwood` and `greenhouse` name no colour). `catalog.mark_colour_variants` groups the pictures of one pack, size and usage that share a key and have two colours or more: every one gets `colour:<name>`, and every one but the first `variant_of:<id of the first>`; the first is the file with no colour word, else the first colour by name. `catalog.colour_variants(catalog, id)` lists them and the *Why* line says *a colour variant of …*. **This is the catalog's half of the card's point, not the whole of it.** The card wants one card in the audit with a *Variants (8)* row and a choice on the Mapping page and in the dock; here every variant is still an asset and a card of its own, and nothing in the wizard reads the tags yet (T16.16 is the wizard's card). It does not go through the variant pipeline of T13.18 either: that one renders a recolour from a source, and these files exist. So the racing pack is 224 assets, not the 32 the card's *Done when* counts: 26 vehicles with 173 colour variants between them (groups of 4 to 7), and the ranch's cows are groups of 36.

T16.21, ninth part — RPG Maker's sheet names in a pack of another kind. `rpgmaker.marked_letter`: a name that puts the engine or the word *tile* beside the sheet letter (`prison_MV_A4.png`, `Tile-A2-11.png`, `Tile-B-12.png`) **and** a picture of that sheet's size (32 px or 48 px tiles) is an RPG Maker sheet wherever it lies, and one such file is enough for the adapter to run. A letter alone (`plan_B.png`) marks nothing outside `img/tilesets`, as before. The sheets of *Monder Interiors* have the names and not the sizes (400 x 600, 800 x 800, 815 x 819) and are left to the generic reader. The card's *a 768 x 768 sheet with a 48 px grid is offered as a B to E sheet when nothing else names its grid* is done for numbered sheets only (the fourth part).

**T16.21 against its *Done when*, on the user's packs (2026-10-10).** Not met as a whole; the card stays open.

| Pack | The card asks for | What the catalog has |
|------|-------------------|----------------------|
| *Wheels in Pixels* | 32 vehicles as 32 assets with their colour variants and 16 headings | 224 assets: 26 groups of colour variants (173 variants) in the tags, every variant still an asset; no headings (T16.02's rotation sheets); the vehicles are *decorations*. `Grid Slots.png` unused. |
| The side-scroller packs | each as ten tile sheets with the right tags | of the first pack's ten 256 x 256 sheets, two are tile sheets and eight are pictures: the names are read (`10. Doors, Windows & Architectural Pieces`), but a sheet of props with no regular grid is not made a tile sheet by its name. The archive is a zip of eight zips, which is not opened as eight packs. |
| *Dark Cursed Village* | the character as one sprite with named animations | one sprite with nine animations, `anim_1` to `anim_9`: the source's tags are all called *Tag*. Box, flame and post are one animated prop each; the portal is 31 pictures (two exports under one stem). |
| *Super Retro Ranch* | one copy of each picture, *needs credit* on its card | Godot's layout used, 335 pictures of the other engines and layouts unused with their reason; the licence line of the card was not checked. |
| *CuteSCKR* | a bundle of 78 packs | 78 packs, 1,073 pictures, from the listing. |
| A town, an interior and the supermarket of it | tile sheets with their folders' words; 431 cut objects | the town: 16 tile sheets of 48 px with their tags. The supermarket: 7 tile sheets and 431 objects. The interior (*Monder Interiors*): its four sheets carry RPG Maker's names on other sizes and are two odd tile sheets and two pictures. |

**Open in the card:** the three synthetic packs (`frames_export`, `multi_engine`, `bundle`) with their goldens, so what is tested here on files made in the tests is not yet in the corpus; typing a name for an animation in the audit; a `.aseprite` without an export rendered to a strip; `_<N>split` and `_<N>dir` used; the colour variants as one card in the wizard and through the variant pipeline; the pack card's words for engine layouts and for a bundle. **Not verified:** the private corpus (T12.26) is not on this machine, so *unchanged on all four measures* is not shown; the goldens of the synthetic packs in the repository did not move in any of the nine parts.

T16.21, tenth part — the three synthetic packs, and what making them found. `tools/make_synthetic_packs.py` has `frames_export` (a hero of 15 numbered frames with blank ones between three animations and a `.aseprite` whose tags name them; a slime exported the same way with no source; a flame of six frames whose source has no tag; three coins that are no frames; two background layers in a folder with a letter missing beside a one-frame source; a preview folder and a `test.png`), `multi_engine` (cow strips with sizes and frame counts in their names in three colour folders, crops, the raw autotile template, Godot's two autotile layouts, three RPG Maker folders, Unity's, an empty *In development* file, a link, a resource fork) and `bundle.zip` (twelve pack folders under one, sheets called `1.png` and `3.png`, one folder with five 768 px sheets, one with a cutter's manifest and copies). Each has its expected file and its golden, scores 100 % (`rpggen analyze --expected`), and generates as a top-down game and as a metroidvania with Godot (`test_all_synthetic_packs`, six runs). `tests/unit/test_real_pack_shapes.py` asserts on them what the card lists. The expected files of the three carry no `cell` for their tile sheets: that key is read by the grid detector's own test, and a numbered sheet gets its grid from its size and folder, not from the detector. The older packs and goldens are byte for byte what they were.

**Found on the way: the 47-tile sheet was not read.** The card says of the 192 x 64 sheets of *Wheels in Pixels* and of the ranch's Godot folder: *the 47-tile blob layout rpggen already reads*. It did not: of 19 such sheets of the racing pack 7 were tile sheets (6 of them with a 32 px tile) and 12 were pictures, and of the ranch's 20 none was a tile sheet. A sheet three times as wide as high whose folders or name say tile set, autotile or terrain is now a tile sheet of a quarter of its height (`GenericGridAdapter._autotile_sheet`; without such a word it is far more often a strip of three frames): all 19 and all 20 are 16 px tile sheets. **Still not read as a terrain:** `terrain.blob` knows one order of the 47 tiles, the masks in ascending order, and neither pack uses it (the ranch's are in the order of Godot's 3x3 template). So these sheets are tiles to paint with and not autotiles, and the ranch pack gets 25 warnings TILE-006 (*terrain layout not recognized*), which is what happened. Reading the usual 12 x 4 arrangement is work for the terrain reader, not for this card; it is the next thing the racing and the farm kind need from detection.

**T16.21 now:** every point of its spec has its core part and its tests, except these, which are the wizard's or another reader's: a name typed for an animation in the audit; a `.aseprite` without an export rendered to a strip (no pixel is decoded); `_<N>split` and `_<N>dir` used; the colour variants as one card and on the Mapping page; the pack card's words for a bundle and for engine layouts; a zip of zips. The *Done when* table above stands, with the racing pack's track sheets now tile sheets.

T16.21, eleventh part — a name for an animation, and nine-slice pieces. The audit's **Animations…** dialog (`AnimEditorDialog`, which was there; the earlier note that said there was none was wrong) has **Rename…**: the names the game plays are offered (eleven actions in four directions, those not taken), any other can be typed, a lone action word gets `_down`, and a name that is taken or is no name changes nothing. The animation keeps its place and its frames, and the override is the old name removed and the new one added (`anim_edit.renamed`, `clean_name`, `offered_names`). So the nine `anim_n` of the village pack's character can be named where they are played. A file named `…_<W>x<H>_9split` that is three cells by three is a UI piece with its border as `nine_slice` (the ranch pack's four themes). `_<N>dir` is still parsed and not used: no pack that was looked at has it. The French file has the three new texts, unfinished.

The 47-tile terrain layout that packs ship (2026-10-10, after T16.21's tenth part; no card of its own, the racing and the farm kind need it). **The order.** `terrain.blob` knew the 47 tiles in the ascending order of their masks, which is the order rpggen's own A2 composer writes and no pack that was looked at uses. Both real packs use **Godot's 3x3 minimal template**: 12 x 4 cells, the cell at column 10, row 1 empty. `blob.GODOT_3X3_MINIMAL` is the mask of each of its 48 cells. It was read off the template picture the ranch pack carries (the three by three fields of each cell), and checked: 47 different masks, every one normalized, exactly the set `blob_masks()` gives, and the racing pack's plain track sheet fits it at every one of its 280 seams. The table is a fact about a layout, not the pack's art. `recognize` tries both orders on a 12 x 4 sheet and takes the one whose seams fit better. **The test.** The seam test asked that tiles which belong side by side be nearly equal at the seam (a mean difference under 12 of 255) at 90 % of the seams. Drawn art has grain: with the right order the racing pack's grass sheets passed 30 % to 60 % of their seams, and only 2 of 19 sheets were accepted. What tells the right order is the **contrast**: seams that should fit differ much less than seams that should not. Measured on the 39 real sheets: 0.00 to 0.77 in the right order, 1.00 to 1.10 in the wrong one, and 0.96 to 1.10 for pictures that are no terrain (the two templates, the five fence sheets). A sheet is accepted when the old test holds **or** its contrast is at most `CONTRAST_LIMIT` (0.85), with the old conditions on filled and distinct tiles; the confidence of a sheet accepted by contrast is one minus it. **A plain ground.** A sheet whose tiles are all one picture (the ranch's `grass_01`, `snow_01`) is `flat`: no proposal and no TILE-006, where it used to be reported as a terrain that was skipped. **Result:** the racing pack 19 of 19 sheets with a proposal; the ranch 11 of 20 (the others: two plain grounds, five fences whose 22 tiles join at their sides only and are another kind of sheet, the two templates), with 13 TILE-006 where there were 25. A project generated from the racing pack's tile sheets with every proposal accepted has 19 terrain sets in its TileSet and validates (a run by hand, 44 s; no pack of ours has such a sheet with art, so the integration suite does not run this). **Seen on that run:** the whole racing pack does not generate. Its car sheets are read as characters and their scenes fail the validator (VAL-014); that is T16.02's rotation sheets, not the terrain. **Not done:** the fence sheets (a sides-only layout in the template's shape), animated autotiles (768 x 64: four sheets side by side), and Godot's 2x2 layout as a corner set (the ranch's 64 x 64 sheets are not in the order `corners16_4x4` assumes).

T16.02 done — Detection for the three kinds; what is the wizard's waits for T16.16. **Rotation sheets.** `analyzer.frames.heading_ring(mask)`: one row of 8, 12, 16, 24 or 32 equal cells (not square if the sheet says so) is a ring when every view, turned back by its angle, covers the first one well (mean 0.40, worst 0.30) **and** clearly better than left as it is (by 0.12). The thresholds are from the real pack: its cars measure 0.81 turned against 0.53 unturned, the narrowest bike 0.48 against 0.31; walk cycles, crops and effects measure higher unturned than turned, or under 0.2 both ways. A round thing is no ring (turned or not it looks the same). All 195 vehicle sheets of *Wheels in Pixels* are rings of 16; the analysis carries `heading_ring` (`ANALYZER_VERSION` 5), and the generic reader makes a sprite with `headings` and `heading_order` (`up_cw`, `up_ccw`, `right_cw`, `right_ccw`; the first view's long side says up or right, which cannot tell up from down) and no animation. **Deviations.** (1) There is no usage `rotation_sheet`: the card says *beside `sprite_sheet`*, and there is no such usage either; a usage is what the builder and every combo of the wizard switch on. The sheet is a `sprite` with the two facets, both new and optional in the schema. (2) Six of the real pack's cars come out `right_ccw` where the others are `up_ccw`: their first view is as wide as it is long. (3) For the older kinds a rotation sheet is scenery: whatever its folder says, it is not a PLAYER, an NPC, an ENEMY or an ITEM (`ROTATION_SHEET_ROLES`), because it has no walk and no attack, and the actor recipe failed on it (`no asset with role PLAYER`); its origin is the foot of its first cell, as for any single picture, or the validator warns for every car (VAL-014, 195 times on the real pack). The racing kind gives a car its role (T16.03) and turns it about its middle itself. **Tags.** `classifier.arcade_tags`: the card's words, the name's and its folder's, and for *car*, *bike* and *ship* also the folder above (`Cars/Classic_Bug/…`). Left out of the card's lists, because every other kind of pack has them as something else: *rock* (asteroid), *arm* (grapple), *flame* and *spread* (gun), *racer* stays. A file the side-view words already call an *ability* is not also a *powerup* (the platformer pack's orbs: its golden must not move), except in a pack that is a shooter or a racing pack, where it is a *powerup* and not an *ability*. `laser` and `missile` are PROJECTILE words now. **Pack view.** `classifier.arcade_view`: three names or more that say ship, bullet or asteroid make a shooter pack, three that say car, bike, road or tyre a racing pack, and a rotation sheet counts two for racing; the pack carries `view:shmup` or `view:racing` beside the `view:side` there was (a run and gun pack is a side-view pack; `catalog.pack_view` still answers side or top, for the wizard of the older kinds). *A vertical background taller than wide* is not used as a sign. **Packs.** `shooter`, `gunner` and `racer` in the synthetic tool, with expected files and goldens (23/23, 13/13, 12/12); the racer's cars are drawn as polygons turned by exact quarter turns and by rounded sines elsewhere, so that two machines draw the same pixels. No older golden moved. **Not done:** the grid editor showing the headings as a ring, the pack card's *Looks like a shooter pack* and the Archetype page's hint (T16.16); the corpus (T12.26) is not on this machine. **On the real pack:** the whole of *Wheels in Pixels* now generates a top-down project (it did not: its cars were actors without animations), with 19 terrain sets and no VAL-014.

A wish of the owner's, 2026-10-10, taken with T16.02: **LPC characters are to be recognized** (the Liberated Pixel Cup collection; the adapter knew only the one 832 x 1344 sheet and three of its actions). The next note has what was done.

LPC characters recognized (the owner's wish, 2026-10-10; no card). The adapter knew one sheet, 832 x 1344, and of it the walk, the slash and the hurt; a taller sheet got the warning *expanded rows ignored*. **Where the layout is from:** the Universal LPC Spritesheet Character Generator (github.com/LiberatedPixelCup; cloned sparse into the scratch folder with the owner's leave, 89,358 files in its tree, of which a body, one hair and one weapon were fetched; nothing of it is in the repository). Its `sources/state/constants.ts` gives the rows and frames of the 15 animations, which `lpc.ACTIONS` now is: spellcast, thrust, walk, slash, shoot (four rows each: up, left, down, right), hurt and climb (one row), then idle, jump, sit, emote, run, combat idle, backslash and halfslash, 54 rows in all. **The universal sheet.** Walk, attack and hurt as before, by the same names and cells; every other animation whose rows are on the sheet and have something drawn in them is added (`cast_*`, `thrust_*`, `shoot_*`, `climb_up`, `jump_*`, `sit_*`, `emote_*`, `run_*`, `combat_idle_*`, `backslash_*`, `halfslash_*`), and a sheet with idle rows stands in them instead of on the first cell of its walk. **The collection's files.** `lpc.action_of`: a PNG named by an animation, or in a folder named by one, whose size is exactly that animation's (so the 192 px frames of an oversize weapon sheet are not taken). The files of one thing are one sprite: its asset is the walk, every animation carries the `source` of its own file, and the other files are parts of it. **Layers.** The first folder that names a part decides (`lpc.part_of`): `body` is a character, tagged `lpc_body`; hair, head, torso, legs, weapon and the others are layers, tagged `lpc_layer` and `lpc_part:<part>`, with the role NONE and the reason *a layer of an LPC character … drawn over a body, which rpggen does not do yet*. **That is the limit of this:** a character of the collection is a body with its head, hair and clothes drawn over it, and putting layers together into one character is not done; a body alone has no head in the newer sets. The finished sheets people export from the generator are read whole. `pack_sniff` says `lpc` for three animation names or more in a listing. **What moved, on purpose:** the golden and the expected file of `lpc_like` (its sheet has cast, thrust and shoot drawn, which are animations now), and the old test's assertion of the warning that is gone. This goes against the ground rule that the older kinds' sprite frames do not move: an LPC project of 1.0 to 1.3 gets the added animations in its SpriteFrames on its next regeneration, with every animation it had unchanged. New: the pack `lpc_parts` (a body in eight animations, a hair, a shirt in two colours by folder, an oversize sheet, a sheet of 26 rows) with its golden, 14/14, generated with Godot as both older kinds; on the real sample the body's 15 files are one sprite of 55 animations and a project generates from it.

LPC layer composition (the owner's wish, 2026-10-10; no card), which takes back the limit of the last note. **The model** is the recolour variant's: a definition in the catalog (`characters`: `{id, name, body, layers}`, a new optional list with its schema) that is rendered on every analysis, so a new version of a pack is followed. `core/lpc_compose.py`: `render` lays the pictures of each animation over one another, from the back to the front, into one universal sheet, 832 px wide and as high as the body's last animation; `build_asset` makes the asset (`generated/lpc_characters/<id>.png`, `detected_by: lpc_compose`, `derived_from` its body, the body's role or NPC, the animations the LPC adapter reads from the finished sheet); `apply` is called by `catalog.assemble` after the variants, and by the audit's edits. A part's picture for an animation is its own file (the collection), or the rows of a universal sheet. **The order** is by part (`PART_ORDER`: shadow, the backs, body, feet, legs, dress, torso, arms, shoulders, neck, backpack, quiver, cape, head, ears, eyes, facial, beards, hair, hat, tools, weapon, shield), not the per-item z positions the generator's sheet definitions have; a layer in a `behind`, `background` or `universal_behind` folder goes under the body. On the real sample (a body, an afro, a mace with its *behind* layer) the mace is in the hand, the hair on the head, and the mace's back under the body in the row that faces away. **Which layer fits which body:** the build in a part's folders (`male`, `female`, `teen`, …); `adult` or none fits every body. **By itself:** `auto_definitions` makes a character for a body from every part that has exactly one layer that fits, when there is at least one; a part with two candidates is left out, and a pack with only a body gets nothing. That happens only while every definition of the catalog is an automatic one: once somebody makes, changes or removes a character, the packs add none behind their back, and a removed one is kept as `removed` so that it stays away. **The body** of a character is unused by itself (`unused_because:lpc_in_character`), and is what the classifier takes it for again when its last character goes. **The wizard:** Audit, right-click a body or a character → *Compose LPC character…* (`dialogs/character_dialog.py`: a name, a combo per part with the layers that fit, the character as it stands), and *Remove character*; both are one undo step (`edits.set_character`, `remove_character`, on copies of the assets, so that an undo step keeps the body it had). **ASSET-033** (a warning): a character whose body is gone has no asset and keeps its definition; one whose layer is gone is drawn without it. **Limits.** (1) A layer that has no picture for an animation is not drawn in it; the character has the animations of its body. (2) No colour of the generator's palettes is applied: a layer is used as its file is. (3) Oversize weapon frames (192 px) are not read, so such a weapon is not in the attack it has them for. (4) The dialog offers the layers of the body's own pack, as a list by path: with the whole collection that is thousands of entries and no search. (5) The z order is by part, so a few items the generator draws in another order (a hood under the hair) come out wrong. **Tests:** `test_lpc_compose.py` (the order of the parts at single pixels, the automatic rule, another build, a chosen character kept and rendered again, a removed one, a lost part), `test_character_dialog.py` (the dialog, the menu, undo, a second character, removal); the layers of the `lpc_parts` pack now cover only their part of the body, so that a second layer shows. A project generated from the real sample with the character as PLAYER has it as its player, with 55 animations in its SpriteFrames (a run by hand).

T16.03 (drawn stand-ins and new roles). **Roles:** `VEHICLE`, `EXPLOSION`, `POWERUP`, `HAZARD` in both schemas (and the registry's copy of the profile schema), `labels.ROLE_INFO`, the Mapping groups and the audit's role list; `PROJECTILE` was a role already. **Who gets them by itself:** a rotation sheet (T16.02's ring) with a car or bike tag is a `VEHICLE` in every pack; the other three come from the arcade tags only in a pack whose view is shmup or racing (`classifier.arcade_role`), so no golden of an older pack moved: only `racer` and `shooter` did, on purpose. **`arcade_kit.py`** beside `side_kit.py`: every picture of the card is drawn (bullet, laser, missile, six-frame explosion, capsule, ship, a car in 16 headings that `heading_ring` reads back, a nine-tile road sheet, hook, chain, five weapon icons), from the tile size alone, saved without metadata. `STAND_INS` says what a kind gets when the packs lack it: shmup a ship, bullet, explosion, capsule; run and gun a bullet, explosion, capsule, hook; racing a car and a road. A pack's own asset wins by role or by tag. The stand-ins are assets of the run-made pack `rpggen_arcade_kit` (`RUN_MADE_PACKS`), replaced on every run like the side kit's, so a repeat regeneration stays a repeat. The road is a tile sheet with `tile_overrides` (the barrier solid, `custom.road` naming each tile) and joins the tile sets. **Requirements:** per kind as the card has them; a required role the kit draws (shmup's `PLAYER`, racing's `VEHICLE` and `GROUND`) is an `ASSET-031` note and never an `ASSET-010` error; a run and gun still needs its player and ground from a pack. `ASSET-032` for a racing vehicle that is one picture. **The note is reported once,** by `requirements.check` (the run, the dry run and the wizard all have it); `arcade_kit.add` returns its own notes with the file, and the orchestrator does not add them a second time. The notes stay on later runs, as long as the packs lack the thing. **Deviations.** (1) `shmup`, `run_and_gun` and `racing` are not in the config schema's `archetype` enum yet, and `features.*_AVAILABLE` are off: nothing can build them before T16.04, T16.08 and T16.12, and a saved config naming one would be a project that cannot be generated. The requirement check, the kit and the sound slots take the kind from the config object, which is how the tests drive them. (2) The laser, missile, chain and weapon icons are drawn and tested but `add` puts none of them into a catalog: no kind asks for them yet (the shmup's weapons T16.05, the grapple T16.10, the HUD T16.15 will). (3) The tests of the requirement check per kind are in `test_arcade_kit.py`, not `test_requirements.py`, with the rest of the card's. (4) The synthetic arcade packs have no audio, so the slots' defaults are checked against `audio_basic` (its theme fills `music_stage` and `music_race`, its hit `sfx_bump`) and a small pack of named sounds made in the test. (5) A wizard page for the new rows is T16.16; `mapping.rows(kind)` has them. **Older tests changed:** `test_repeat_regeneration` lists the run-made packs and now names three. The full integration suite and the private corpus were not run again.

T16.01 (the arcade framework). **What it is.** `archetypes/arcade/` in the runtime, sixteen scripts as the card lists them, and `recipes/arcade/` in the builder. A stage is a map: `RpgArcadeStage` extends `RpgMapRoot`, so the router loads it, places the player on its spawn point and plays its music as for any map, and `RpgArcadeMode` (the combat mode, as `RpgMetroidvaniaMode` is one) binds the stage it hears of through `map_entered`. The mode owns the run: `RpgArcadeLives` and `RpgArcadeScore` are its children (no autoload), the stages are `RpgArcadeStageSequence` (read from the mode's `stages_path`, which the builder sets to `game/data/stages.json`: a runtime script names no path of the game, `test_runtime_scripts_do_not_depend_on_game_content_paths`), a cleared stage is tallied (`RpgArcadeStageClear.tally`: 100 a second under the par time, 1000 a life), then the next stage's scene is asked for; after the last one the high-score entry and the credits menu. **Stepping.** Every moving part has `advance(delta)` and a flag `auto_advance`; the stage calls its own from `_physics_process` (not `_process`: `RpgMapRoot.update_ambient` switches that off for a map without a day cycle), so a run is the same at any frame rate and a test steps it by hand in eighths of a second. **Waves.** `RpgArcadeWave.from_dict`, five formations with fixed places (`offsets`), four triggers; a spawner counts an enemy as gone when it leaves the tree and scores it only when it emitted `defeated`. The runtime reads the waves and adds none. **Scroller.** `hold(who)` / `release(who)` by instance id, so two holders (a blocking wave and a boss) do not undo one another. **Boss.** Phases are sorted by their `until` and the last one always lasts to zero; a warning (`telegraphed`) comes `telegraph_s` before every attack; it cannot be hurt in its intro or its defeat; the explosions of the defeat are at fixed offsets. **Continue.** Lives back to the start, the multiplier back to x1, the score kept (as the card says), and the extra lives the score has already paid for are not paid again; Quit enters the score in the table and goes to the title. **High scores** are in a new file `user://saves/meta.json` (`SaveManager.read_shared`, `shared_value`, `set_shared_value`), checksummed and written through a temporary file like a slot. That is how "a `meta` section shared by the three slots and the autosave" was read: a section inside each slot file could not be shared. `save_version` stays 1 and no slot knows of the file. **Saving.** `autosave_on_every_map` (the metroidvania's switch) saves when a stage is entered; the run is the entry `arcade` of the saved game's `flags` (stage, lives, score, weapon), so no key of the save format is new; the pause menu's Save is off with *Saved between stages* (`saves_between_stages`, read by `pause_menu.gd` as it reads `save_at_stations`). **Builder.** `arcade_build.gd` (paths, the combat mode's scene, a drawn rectangle for a thing without art), `stage_recipes.gd` (the scene of a stage: camera, scroller, `Entities`, the player's start; `game/data/stages.json`, written when it is missing or the caller says the plan changed) and `hud_recipe.gd` (`hud_arcade.tscn`, which the boot recipe now looks for). The stage helpers are functions the kinds call with their plan, not recipes in `RECIPE_ORDER`: no kind is registered in `build.gd` yet. **Python.** `staging.SHARED_FOLDERS`: `arcade` is installed for `shmup`, `run_and_gun` and `racing` only; the list of files an older kind gets is what it is without the folder (a test compares the lists). `config.default_features(archetype)`: an arcade kind starts with `dialogue` and `navigation` off. **Deviations.** (1) *Done when* asks for a test stage played from the title to the credits. No arcade kind can be generated before T16.04, so that was not done in a generated project; what stands in for it is the GDScript test that takes a run of two stages built by `stage_recipes.build_stage`, with drawn stand-ins as enemies, from the first stage through the tally to the high-score entry. Nothing was played by hand or seen on a screen: the HUD and the three screens are checked for their texts and nodes only. (2) The card's file is `core/save_system.gd`; the runtime's is `core/save_manager.gd`. (3) Of the options the card wants off by default only `dialogue` and `navigation` are options of their own; inventory, equipment, shops, quests, the party, interiors, the second map, the minimap and lighting have no switch in the config and come with the demo content, which each kind's recipes decide (T16.04, T16.08, T16.12). (4) The parallax layers following the scroller is the kinds' work: the scroller moves the camera, and `Parallax2D` follows a camera by itself. (5) The runtime's `VERSION` and `CHANGELOG.md` were not touched (they stand at 1.2.0); the entry for the framework is due with the release cards. (6) The translations recipe gives the three kinds the framework's strings; a kind's own strings need a second table there. **Shared files changed** (so every regenerated project gets them): `save_manager.gd` (the shared section), `pause_menu.gd` (one more branch), `boot_recipe.gd` (one more HUD name), `translations_recipe.gd` (three entries). None changes what an older kind builds. **Tests:** `test_arcade_runtime.gd` (16), `test_staging_regen.py` (2 more). The whole GDScript suite and the Python unit and GUI suites were run; of the integration suite, the samples of M6, M7 and M14 and `test_regeneration.py` were run (44 tests).

T16.04 (shoot 'em up: ship, bullets, scrolling). **The kind can be generated**: `shmup` is in the config's `archetype` enum and the CLI's choices; the wizard does not offer it (`features.SHMUP_AVAILABLE` is still off, its gate is T16.20). **Orientation** is `starter_map.orientation` (`vertical` by default, `horizontal`), where the metroidvania keeps the options of its world; the card's `shmup.orientation` would have been a new section of the config for one key. `RpgShmupMath` has what follows from it. It is a script of its own because the mode names the ship and the ship needed the same functions: two scripts that name each other were reported as still in use when a validation run quit (the metroidvania's notes have the same finding). **The ship** is moved by wants, like the platform actor; its place is kept relative to the middle of the screen (the stage's scroller), so it rides with the scrolling and the clamp to the play area is one line. Vertical play area: at most four wide for three high, in the middle. It leans with `bank_<side>`, else `walk_<side>`, else `idle_<side>` of its sheet. A hit destroys it, the mode takes a life and brings it back (`respawn`), blinking and safe for three seconds, with its bombs refilled and its weapon at level 1. **Bullets are not nodes.** `RpgShmupBulletPool` keeps 400 + 1,000 bullets in packed arrays, moves them, draws them in one `_draw` and finds hits by distance: a player's bullet against the group `shmup_targets`, an enemy's against one point of the ship. That is what "pre-made" became; 1,400 `Area2D`s would cost more than the game. A full pool fires nothing. **Weapons:** `shots(kind, level)`: straight 1/2/3 side by side, spread 3/5/7 over 12/22/32 degrees, a laser 1/2/3 wide that goes through what it hits, homing 1/2/3. **Patterns** (`aimed`, `ring`, `fan`, `spiral`) return velocities; `fire` puts them into the pool. **Recipes:** `shmup/ship_recipe` (the ship and the explosion, under `generated/scenes/shmup/`, not `actors/`: validation's origin check is for characters that stand on the ground), `shmup/mode_recipe`, `shmup/stage_recipe` (stage 1: the arcade stage, a background, the pool, the ship; `game/data/stages.json`), and `arcade/hud_recipe` with the kind's HUD script. `actor_recipes`, the interiors and both maps are skipped; `data_recipe` seeds no village and starts a new game in `stage_01.tscn`; the translations recipe adds the kind's strings beside the framework's. **Background:** every `BACKGROUND` picture is a `Parallax2D` that loops along the way of the game, the farthest slowest; without one, two layers of drawn stars (`RpgShmupStarfield`, from the seed) over a dark backdrop. The card says `ParallaxBackground`; `Parallax2D` replaced that node. **A ship that is no ship:** a picture tagged `ship` is turned to the right for a horizontal game; the hero of an RPG pack is left upright. **Python:** `default_input("shmup")` (`shoot` Z / J / pad A, `bomb` X / K / pad B, `focus` Shift / left trigger), `starter_map_defaults`, the options summary and the dry run say "1 stage, scrolling up" (the dry run crashed on the missing map size before), `smoke_steps_shmup.gd` (six steps: title and new game, the stage scrolls, the ship flies and stops at the edge, shoots, bombs, is destroyed and returns). **A bug of T16.01 found here:** `RpgArcadeMode` made its `Lives` and `Score` in `_init`, so the scene the builder saved held them and every instance made them twice; validation reported two resources still in use. They are made in `_ready` now (`_ensure_parts`), and a test says a mode that is only made has no children. **Seen on a screen:** both generated projects (the `shooter` pack scrolling up, the top-down sample scrolling right) were run with a window and `--write-movie` while the smoke steps flew the ship, and frames were looked at: HUD rows, the ship, its bullets ahead of it, the looping background and the stars are there. **Deviations and what is open.** (1) Auto-fire is a property of the ship's input (`auto_fire`, on); there is no switch in the settings menu, which the card asks for. (2) The stage has no enemies and no waves until T16.05: it ends where its scrolling ends, so in a generated game nothing can hit the ship yet; hits are tested with bullets a test or a smoke step fires. (3) `sfx_shoot` is not played (the weapon emits `fired`; who plays it is T16.07's sound pass); the bomb and the explosion play their slots. (4) The picture of a horizontal ship is rotated, not redrawn, and a side-view ship that already looks right would be turned wrongly: no pack of that shape was at hand. (5) A new CI job `shmup` was added to the archetype matrix and left out of the main integration run, as the two other kinds are; it has not run on CI. **Tests:** `test_shmup.gd` (15), `test_m16_shmup.py` (5: both packs, both orientations, the second run a repeat), `test_shmup_config.py` (4).

After T16.04, on this machine: the repository moved to a drive that does not record file ownership, so plain `git` refuses it and `test_repository_urls_are_consistent` (which runs `git ls-files`) fails here; it is the machine's, like the 7z and Zstandard tests. Run for the card: the whole GDScript suite (628 passed), the Python unit and GUI suites (only the machine's failures), and of the integration suite `test_m16_shmup.py`, the M6 sample and the M14 sample (25 passed). The action RPG's sample, the full integration suite and the private corpus were not run.

T16.05 (shoot 'em up: waves, enemies, power-ups). **Enemies** are `RpgShmupEnemy` (`enemies/enemy_ship.gd`) and four behaviours over it. An enemy either flies with the screen (its place is kept relative to the middle of the screen, as the ship's is) or sits in the stage and scrolls past (`in_stage`: the turret and the rock). It is a target of the bullet pool by its group and radius, destroys the ship by touch, and is taken away without points when it has left the screen. `popcorn`: six paths (`straight`, `sine`, `swoop`, `sweep`, `dive`, `close`), each a function of the time since the formation appeared, so its members fly as one; one aimed shot a second in when the wave says `shoots`. `turret`: a fan of three over thirty degrees every two seconds while it is on the screen. `asteroid`: drifts, takes bullets (that is how it blocks them), and breaks once into two pieces of 0.6 its size that fly apart. `carrier`: comes a third of the way in, weaves there for eighteen seconds, releases an escort every two and a half seconds (four at most), then flies on; it always leaves a power-up. **Formations move as one** because the path is the formation's, not the enemy's: a `line` sweeps, a `v` dives, a `circle` closes (its members draw to 0.35 of their distance), unless the wave names a `path`. Formations are laid out for a game that scrolls up and turned a quarter for one that scrolls right (`spawned_in_wave`). **The bonus** (`bonus` of a wave, 500 here) is paid when every enemy of the wave was shot; one that flew away loses it. **Framework additions** (arcade): `RpgArcadeWave.data` and `bonus`, the hook `spawned_in_wave(wave, index)`, `RpgArcadeSpawner.cleared_whole`, `RpgArcadeStage.formation_bonus` and `adopt(enemy, at)` (what a carrier releases and the pieces of a rock are scored without a wave waiting for them), `RpgArcadeLives.gain()`. **Power-ups** (`powerup.gd`): `weapon_up` (to level 3; points after that), `weapon:<kind>`, `bomb`, `life`, `shield`, `points`; a capsule drifts toward the ship's edge of the screen and is taken within `pick_radius`. Nothing is random: a carrier drops, and the last enemy of every fifth formation of small enemies carries one. **The shield** takes one hit and leaves the ship safe for half a second. **Recipes:** `shmup/enemy_recipes` makes `enemies/popcorn_NN.tscn` (one per small picture, four at most), `turret`, `asteroid`, `carrier` and `powerup.tscn`. The `ENEMY` pictures are shared out as the card says: one named turret, cannon or tower is the turret; of the others the largest is the carrier when there are two sizes; a `HAZARD` or `ENEMY` named asteroid, rock or meteor is the rock. A carrier without a picture of its own is the first small enemy, twice as large. The `POWERUP` pictures go to the kinds in the order weapon, bomb, shield, life, points; a kind without one is tinted. **The first stage** has ten waves at fixed places along it (six formations of small enemies, two pairs of turrets, three rocks, a carrier); `stage_recipe` is at version 2. An existing project keeps its `game/data/stages.json` (it is the user's once written), so it gets the enemies' scenes and no waves until the file is removed. **Which way an enemy ship looks.** The first version turned an enemy ship half round, taking its picture to look up like the player's. A recording of the generated game showed the enemies flying backwards: the `shooter` pack, like the packs of the genre, draws an enemy as it comes at the player, looking down. An enemy ship is now left as it is for a game that scrolls up and turned a quarter for one that scrolls right; what is no ship (a slime) is never turned. **Smoke steps:** the steps about the ship alone hold the scroller so that no wave comes during them; two more steps release it, wait for the first wave, see it on the screen, shoot it whole (the kills and the bonus are in the score) and pick up a weapon capsule. A lambda that waited for a freed node logged *Lambda capture was freed*, which validation counts as an error: it waits by instance id now. **Seen on a screen:** both generated games were recorded with a window while a script flew the ship through the whole stage: formations, turrets, rocks, the carrier, explosions and the multiplier are there in both orientations. **Deviations and what is open.** (1) An enemy or a turret without a picture is a shape drawn by the recipe (a rectangle of one colour), not a stand-in of `arcade_kit` with an `ASSET-031` note: the kit has no enemy. (2) The waves are a fixed table, not a plan from the seed; the planner of stages is T16.06. (3) The turret's fan and the popcorn's shot are not tuned for difficulty, and nothing was played by hand: the recordings were flown by a script with the ship made safe. (4) A power-up's kind is shown by its picture or a tint only; there is no text or icon of the kind on it. (5) The shield is shown as a tint of the ship; the HUD has no row for it. (6) `sfx_shoot` is still not played. **Tests:** `test_shmup_enemies.gd` (13), `test_m16_shmup.py` (8, three of them new).

T16.06 (shoot 'em up: stages and bosses, the wave plan). **`core/wave_plan.py`**, pure Python: `plan(options, seed, facts)` makes the run. A stage has a wave every half screen (the first and the last screen are free: the last is the boss's), a carrier in its middle slot, turrets or rocks in every third slot, formations of small enemies in the others, and a boss. **Difficulty is one number per wave,** `threat = stage * 4 + round(fraction * 4)`, which never falls from wave to wave nor from stage to stage, and `hardness(threat, difficulty)` makes everything else of it: enemies in a formation (4 to 8), the chance that a formation fires, the speed of bullets (`fast`), the health of hulls (`tough`), the bullets of a turret's fan. The setting (easy, normal, hard) scales bullet speed, bullet count and a boss's health. That is how the test can say that difficulty rises monotonically: it is so by construction, and the random choices (which formation, turret or rock, where across the screen, which patterns a boss has) do not touch it. **Bosses:** health `40 * (1 + 0.6 * stage)`, two phases in the first stage, three in the second, four from the third, each a different pattern of `ring`, `fan`, `spiral`, `aimed`, `sweep`, with a count and a speed that grow. **The check** (`check`, VAL-029): the bullets that can be on the screen at once (per wave: bullets a second times the time a bullet takes to cross the screen; summed over the waves within one screen of each other; and the worst phase of the boss) stay under 300, and every wave comes at least half a screen before the stage ends. `plan` tries the next seed up to eight times when the check fails and writes a note; with the numbers above no plan of the options fails (forty seeds, three settings, eight long stages are in the test), so the re-roll is tested with a check that is made to fail. A stored plan that fails (somebody edited it) is reported as VAL-029, a warning, and used as it is. **Stored, never planned again:** `rpggen/world_plan.json` gets a section `stages` (schema `Stages`), `for_project` keeps it once the project has `game/data/stages.json`, and *Added stages* appends one stage per new name, harder than the last (twelve at most). **Options** live in `starter_map` like the orientation: `stages`, `length`, `lives`, `bombs`, `auto_fire`, `difficulty`, `added_stages`. A config that changes one writes the whole set (the rule of the starter map), which changed two expectations of T16.04's tests. `lives` goes to the mode (`RpgArcadeMode.start_lives`), `bombs` and `auto_fire` to the ship's scene. **The boss** (`shmup/boss.gd`, over `RpgArcadeBoss`): a target of the bullet pool, a touch that destroys the ship, volleys by pattern; beaten, it leaves the targets and clears the enemies' bullets. Its scene is the `BOSS` picture, else the largest `ENEMY` picture three times as large, else a drawn shape. **Recipes:** `stage_recipe` builds a scene per stage of the stored plan and fills in the scenes of the enemies (`look` picks one of the small-enemy scenes); without a stored plan it falls back to the one stage of T16.05. **An added stage and the user's list.** `game/data/stages.json` is the user's, and a file of the user's that was changed is kept as it is by a regeneration, so the builder cannot append to it: the first version did, and the test that edits the list before adding a stage lost the new stage. The builder now also writes the plan to `generated/data/stages.json` on every build, and the mode reads the user's list and takes from the generator's the stages the user's does not have (`RpgArcadeStageSequence.merged`, `planned_stages_path`). What the user's list says of a stage always wins. **Smoke:** two more steps. `boss_is_beaten` puts the scroller at the end of the first stage, waits for the boss to come in and fire, takes it through a change of phase and beats it (points, no bullet left), and sees the second stage begin; `run_reaches_the_credits` does the same for every other stage and checks the high-score table, the cleared run and the credits menu. The stages are skipped, not flown: the ship is made safe and what is on the screen is shot by the step. **Seen on a screen:** a recording of the three boss fights of the `shooter` pack (the pack's boss, its bar, rings and fans, the change from stage to stage). **Deviations and what is open.** (1) *Done when* says the run can be played from the title to the credits; it is a smoke step that does it, with the stages skipped. Nobody played it, so whether a person can beat it, and how it feels, is not known. (2) Scores are large: with the multiplier at x8 a boss pays 40,000 and more, and an extra life every 20,000 points filled the lives to nine by the second stage in the recording. The numbers want tuning by somebody who plays. (3) The card puts the options in a `shmup` section; they are in `starter_map`. The Options page for them is T16.16. (4) A boss has no name (the HUD says *Warning!*). (5) The enemies' bullets have no picture of their own in a pack without one and are drawn as small squares. (6) `world_plan.py` itself was not changed: the section is written by `wave_plan.world` and only its schema is new. **Tests:** `test_wave_plan.py` (7), `test_shmup_enemies.gd` (16, three of them new), `test_m16_shmup.py` (9).

T16.07 (shoot 'em up: HUD, continue, high scores). **The HUD** (`RpgShmupHud`, `recipes/shmup/hud_recipe.gd`): the arcade HUD with the bombs, the weapon and its level (from T16.04) and now the high score (`Hi: N`, the best of the table, or the run's own score once it passes it). A game that scrolls up has `panels`: when the strips beside the play area are 48 px or wider, each gets a dark panel, the high score, the stage, the bombs and the weapon are on the left and the score, the multiplier and the lives on the right. At 640 x 360 the play area is 480 wide and a panel 80; a 4:3 screen has no strips and the HUD stays over the picture, as it does in a game that scrolls right. **The title screen** gets *High scores* above *Credits*. The title's scene is built by the shared UI recipe for every kind, so the entry is not in the scene: `title_screen.gd` asks the combat mode for entries of its own (`customize_title_screen`, the same kind of hook as the action RPG's `customize_inventory_menu`), and `RpgArcadeMode` adds the button, puts it into the focus chain and shows `RpgArcadeHighScoreTable` over the title; cancel or *OK* closes it and the focus returns to the button. An older kind's title is what it was. **Continue** was done with the framework (T16.01). **The shot's sound:** the ship plays `sfx_shoot` with every shot (it was open since T16.04). **The README:** `project_files/README.shmup.md` is section 22 of a shoot 'em up's README: the controls, what the game's rules are, where the stages, the scenes and the plan are, a wave explained key by key, the boss's entry, and how a stage is added; `staging.KIND_NOTES` says in the first paragraph that the sections about maps, doors, shops and quests are for the other kinds. A test compares the keys the README explains with the keys a wave of the generated game has. **Seen on a screen:** the title with its new entry and a stage with the panels, from a recording of the generated `shooter` game. **Deviations and what is open.** (1) *Done when* says the shoot 'em up is a whole game behind `SHMUP_AVAILABLE`: the flag stays off, the kind is generated from the command line or a config, and the wizard's card, its Options page and its sample are T16.16 and T16.17. (2) The README tells the user to add a stage through `starter_map.added_stages` of the config, since the Options page for it does not exist yet. (3) A boss still has no name, and the settings menu still has no auto-fire switch (it is an option of the config). (4) The panels are sized when the HUD is made, from the size of the screen at that moment: a window whose shape is changed afterwards keeps them. (5) The high score shown is the table's best when the stage began. **Tests:** `test_shmup.gd` (18, three of them new), `test_m16_shmup.py` (11, two of them new); the smoke run now also fails when the title has no *High scores* entry.

T16.08 (run and gun: the soldier on the platform actor). **The kind can be generated**: `run_and_gun` is in the config's `archetype` enum and the CLI's choices; the wizard does not offer it (`features.RUN_AND_GUN_AVAILABLE` is off). **Built on the metroidvania.** `RpgSoldier` extends `RpgPlatformActor` directly (not `RpgPlatformPlayer`, which brings a melee attack, health components and interaction), so a run and gun's project gets the metroidvania's runtime folder and recipes beside its own (`staging.BORROWED_FOLDERS`; not its smoke steps), its tile sets the side-view parts (`side_tileset.SIDE_VIEW_KINDS`), its packs the side-view kit and its project settings the one-way layer (`config.SIDE_VIEW_KINDS`). A hero without a jump animation is the same note as in a metroidvania (ASSET-030). **Aiming:** `RpgGunAim.direction` gives one of eight unit vectors from what is held and the facing; straight down only in the air; down on the ground with nothing held sideways is a crouch (lower and smaller to hit, fires forward, stands still); `aim_lock` holds the soldier in place so that the stick only aims. The sheet's `crouch`, `aim_up`, `aim_diagonal` and `shoot` animations are played when it has them (the animation driver's side-view lookup); a muzzle flash (a drawn rectangle, the child `Muzzle`) is at the muzzle and turned along the aim on every shot, so a sheet without them still shows where the shot goes. The action words of the classifier (T12.50) were not given `aim_up`, `aim_diagonal` or `crouch`: no pack at hand has such a sheet, and the animations are found by name when one does. **Lives, not health:** `max_health` 1 (the option `health`, 1 to 5); a death is an explosion and `destroyed`, the mode takes a life and calls `respawn`: dropped in at the top of the screen a quarter in from its middle, blinking and safe for two seconds. A fall below the screen and (with `left_edge_kills`) the left edge are deaths. **The camera.** The card names `SideCamera` in an auto-scroll with a leash. The stage of the arcade framework already has a camera that its scroller moves, and the spawners of the next card trigger on the scroller's progress, so the soldier leads the scroller instead: `RpgArcadeScroller.follow(wanted)` raises the progress to where the soldier is (kept at two fifths of the screen), never lowers it, stops at the length and waits while a blocking wave holds it. The camera does not move up or down: the first stage is flat. **Shared code moved.** The bullet pool and the explosion were the shoot 'em up's; a run and gun's project does not have that folder, so they are `arcade/bullet_pool.gd` (`RpgArcadeBulletPool`, group `arcade_targets`) and `arcade/explosion.gd` (`RpgArcadeExplosion`) now. Nothing was released with the old names. The pool gained what a side view needs: `wall_mask` (a bullet that flies into the ground is gone: a point query per bullet), `life_s` (the range of a gun), `hit_center()` of what it hits (a crouch), and `view_size` for a test whose window is not the stage's screen. The helpers that find the packs' pictures moved from `shmup_build.gd` to `arcade_build.gd` (the shmup's keep their names as wrappers). **The grenade** flies an arc under its own gravity and goes off when it lands, touches a target or its fuse runs out; the blast hurts every target within its radius (a target's own radius counts). It is a drawn square; three to start with, nine at most. **Recipes:** `run_and_gun/soldier_recipe` (soldier, grenade, explosion under `generated/scenes/run_and_gun/`), `mode_recipe`, `stage_recipe` (one flat stage six screens long: the parallax pictures through the metroidvania's helper, a layer `Ground` painted with the top and fill tiles the side kit finds, the pool with `wall_mask` 1, the soldier), and the arcade HUD recipe with the kind's script. **Options** in `starter_map`: `health`, `lives`, `left_edge_kills`. **Controls:** the metroidvania's, with `shoot` J / X / pad X, `grenade` K / C / pad Y, `aim_lock` Shift / left trigger; no dash, no attack. **Smoke:** nine steps: the title and a new game, running with the camera following, the camera not going back, a jump, a shot forward and one up, a crouch, a grenade, a death and the drop back in, and the end of the stage with the credits. **Seen on a screen:** a recording of the generated `platformer` game during the smoke run: the parallax, the floor, the soldier jumping, a shot upward, the grenade, the lives going from 3 to 2. **Deviations and what is open.** (1) `SideCamera` is not used (above). (2) The stage is flat and empty: platforms, pits, enemies, weapons and a boss are T16.09 and T16.10, so nothing in a generated game can hit the soldier yet. (3) The soldier's body does not shrink in a crouch, only what bullets hit. (4) There is no prone animation of its own: a crouched shot uses `crouch`. (5) The kind has no section of the generated README yet. (6) New CI job `run_and_gun` in the archetype matrix; it has not run on CI. (7) While writing the tests the GDScript runner hung on a test file whose preload was missing from the scratch copy: its known weakness (a script that does not parse stops it until the timeout), not a new one. **Tests:** `test_run_and_gun.gd` (9), `test_run_and_gun_config.py` (4), `test_m16_run_and_gun.py` (5: the scenes, the smoke run, a second run that is a repeat, the forgiving options).

T16.09 (run and gun: weapons, enemies, spawners). **Guns:** `RpgGun` now counts in tiles (`speed_tiles`, `range_tiles`, `tile`) and has a name; the four weapons are scripts under `weapons/` that set its numbers: machine gun (8 shots a second, 15 tiles a second), spread (five bullets over 40 degrees, 4 a second), laser (28 tiles a second, 3 damage, goes through), flame (20 a second, gone after 4 tiles, goes through). `RpgSoldier.equip(name)` replaces the child `Gun` by a node with the weapon's script; a death equips the machine gun again unless `keep_weapon` (an option). **The pickup** (`weapon_pickup.gd`) is its own carrier: it flies from the right across the screen, weaving, and is a target of the pool; shot, it falls, lies on the ground and is taken by a touch (`equip`); not shot, it flies off. Its picture is the one the packs name after the weapon (`weapon_pickup_laser`), else a tint. **Enemies** are `RpgGunEnemy` (a platform actor, so they stand on floors and fall) and five behaviours: `runner` charges and jumps when the soldier is within three tiles or a wall is in its way; `sniper` stands, flashes for 0.6 s and fires one aimed shot every 2.5 s; `turret` fires a fan of three every 2 s; `jumper` hops once a second and moves only in the air; `wall_cannon` is `fixed`, shut (nothing hurts it) and opens every 3 s for 1.2 s to fire once. A touch hurts the soldier; an enemy the camera has left behind is taken away without points. **Not the metroidvania's enemies.** The card says to reuse the patroller, the flyer and the shooter where they fit. They are hurt through hurtboxes and hitboxes on physics layers and shoot `RpgProjectile` nodes; a run and gun's bullets are the pool's, which hits by distance. Bridging the two would have been a second damage model in one game, so the five behaviours are written on the pool's contract (`take_hit`, `hit_radius`, `hit_center`) and the metroidvania's enemies are not used. **Spawners** are the framework's: the waves of the stage are placed in the stage (`anchor: stage`) and come when the camera has gone far enough; a wave may be `blocking`. **Two things the recording showed.** A trap of turrets was placed, like every wave, just beyond the right edge of the screen, and it stops the camera the moment it comes: it was never seen. A blocking wave now stands in the right third of the screen. And the soldier ran out of a screen the trap held: the right edge of the screen is a wall for the soldier now (the left one kills, or is a wall, as before). **The first stage** has nine waves at fixed places: three runners, a sniper, the spread on its carrier, two jumpers, the trap of two turrets, a wall cannon three tiles up, the laser, four runners, the flame. **Recipes:** `run_and_gun/enemy_recipes` (five enemy scenes and the pickup).
