A picture named turret, cannon or tower is the turret and the wall cannon; the other `ENEMY` pictures are the runner, the sniper and the jumper in turn; one without a picture is a drawn shape. **Two findings in older code.** (1) The `gunner` pack's hero is called a soldier, which the classifier takes for an NPC: the pack had no player and could not be a run and gun. In a side-view pack without a player the first soldier that can jump is the player now (`catalog._side_view_soldier`); the `gunner` golden moved on purpose, no other did. (2) `arcade_kit` took a pack to have bullets and power-ups when an asset was only tagged so (the gunner's weapon pickups are tagged `bullet` and `powerup`), drew none, and the game had squares for bullets while the report said the role was missing. A stand-in is skipped only for an asset that has the role now, and a role the kit draws is no longer also reported as missing (`ASSET-010`) beside its note. **Smoke:** three more steps (the first wave comes, moves toward the soldier and is shot; a weapon is picked up and fires five bullets; the trap holds the camera until it is destroyed) and the last one shoots what comes on the way to the end. The steps put the camera at a place through the scroller, since the soldier alone can no longer be put ahead of it. **Seen on a screen:** two recordings of the generated `gunner` game with a script running and firing: enemies, explosions, the carriers, the wall cannon, the score, the credits at the end. In the second the soldier is not drawn in some frames; that was the recording script, which reset the blink timer every frame. **Deviations and what is open.** (1) The metroidvania's enemies are not reused (above). (2) A sniper stands on the ground, not on a ledge, and the wall cannon hangs in the air three tiles up: a flat stage has neither ledge nor wall; the stage planner (T16.10) does. (3) The flame and the enemies' bullets have no picture of their own and are drawn as squares; the flame is short bullets, not a continuous jet. (4) A weapon does not show on the soldier, only in the HUD. (5) Nothing was played by hand; difficulty is untuned. **Tests:** `test_run_and_gun_enemies.gd` (9), `test_run_and_gun_config.py` (5, one new), `test_m16_run_and_gun.py` (7, two new, on the `gunner` pack).

T16.10 (run and gun: stages and bosses, the stage plan). **`core/stage_plan.py`**, pure Python. A stage is a row of chunks, each half a screen wide (20 tiles at 640 px and 16 px tiles) and the screen high, drawn in the room plan's alphabet: three flat chunks where the game begins (nothing comes there), then the features (`flat`, `gap`: a pit two or three tiles wide with no bottom; `step`: ground one or two tiles higher; `planks`: one-way planks two or three tiles up; `spikes`: one or two tiles of them; `wall`: a wall two tiles high), one of them a `trap` with a flat chunk before it, and two chunks of flat `arena` at the end, where the camera stops (`length` is the stage's width less a screen). No two features of a kind in a row. **Enemies per chunk** from a threat that never falls (`stage * 4 + round(fraction * 4)`, as in the shoot 'em up's plan): a wall gets a cannon on its face, a step or a plank a sniper once the threat allows one, the others runners (and jumpers later), two to five of them, standing on the ground of their chunk; every third chunk a weapon on its carrier; the trap is two turrets that come when their place is at seven tenths of the screen. A wave comes when its chunk is a quarter of a screen from the right edge. **Bosses:** `wall`, `walker` or `flyer`, turning from stage to stage from a random first one; health `30 * (1 + 0.6 * stage)`; two to four phases with attacks of the kind's own set. **The check** (`check`, VAL-029): `walk` goes over the solid ground of the stage column by column: a step higher than three tiles, a gap wider than four (three when the far side is higher), or ground that ends, is a problem. Three and four are `floor` of the room plan's `vertical_reach` and `horizontal_reach` with no abilities, the numbers the platform actor moves by; the planner itself stays a tile inside them (steps and planks of two, gaps of three), so no plan of the options fails (forty seeds, three settings, three lengths in the test) and the rejection is tested with stages made by hand. Planks do not count as ground in the walk: the soldier passes under them. Waves after the end and a boss with more than 150 bullets at once are problems too. **Stored like the shoot 'em up's:** the section `stages` of `rpggen/world_plan.json` with `kind: run_and_gun`; `for_project`, `extend` and `exists` follow `wave_plan`, and the orchestrator picks the planner by the kind. **Options:** `stages`, `length`, `lives`, `health`, `keep_weapon`, `left_edge_kills`, `difficulty`, `added_stages`, in `starter_map`. **Recipes:** `stage_recipe` builds a scene per stage of the stored plan with the four layers of a metroidvania's room and paints the plan's tiles with the rooms recipe's own `paint` (the side kit finds the top, fill, plank and spike tiles); the tiles stay out of `stages.json`. Without a stored plan it falls back to the flat stage of T16.09. `enemy_recipes` makes the three boss scenes from the `BOSS` picture (twice as large), else the largest `ENEMY` picture (two and a half times), else a drawn shape. **The boss** (`run_and_gun/boss.gd`, over `RpgArcadeBoss`): a target of the pool, attacks as volleys, a touch that hurts; the two that stand have their feet at their origin. **Spikes:** the soldier had no hazards (the metroidvania's are in its player, which the soldier does not extend); a tile with the `hazard` data now costs it a hit. **Smoke:** `stage_ends_with_the_credits` became `boss_is_beaten` (the camera is put at the arena, the boss comes in, fires, changes phase and is beaten; the second stage begins) and `run_reaches_the_credits` (every other stage and its boss, the high-score table, the credits). **Seen on a screen:** a recording of the generated `gunner` game: views along the first stage (a pit, steps, spikes, a sniper on a step, runners, a carrier) and the three boss fights (the wall at the right, the walker, the flyer in the air) with their health bars falling under the soldier's fire. **Deviations and what is open.** (1) The card has the room plan's chunk helpers reused. Its `_Room` draws a closed room with a ceiling and walls; a stage is open, so the chunks are drawn here, and what is shared is the alphabet, the painter (`RoomsRecipe.paint`) and the reach. (2) No ladders and no breakable blocks are planned: nothing in a stage needs to be climbed, and a soldier has nothing that breaks blocks. (3) The reachability is the walk along the ground; that a plank or a step can be reached is by construction (two or three tiles up), not checked. Enemies are not part of it. (4) *Done when* is again a smoke step with the stages skipped; nobody played a stage through, so whether its gaps and enemies are fair is unknown. (5) A wall is something to jump over with a cannon on its face, not the genre's wall that has to be destroyed. (6) Bosses have no name, and their pictures do not turn to face the soldier. **Tests:** `test_stage_plan.py` (5), `test_run_and_gun_enemies.gd` (12, three new), `test_m16_run_and_gun.py` (8).

T16.11 (run and gun: the grapple). **The arm** (`run_and_gun/grapple.gd`, `RpgGrapple`, the soldier's child `Grapple`): the hook is fired along the aim (up, the two upper diagonals, forward; an aim downward fires forward) at 45 tiles a second and flies 7 tiles; it is looked for in the tiles in steps of a quarter tile and holds on any tile with a solid or a one-way polygon. The soldier tells it what to do and it moves the body it is given (`move_and_collide`), so the script does not name the soldier's class. **A swing:** when the hook holds to the side, the rope is reeled in to 0.6 of the way to the hook (14 tiles a second); without that a soldier that stands on the ground would have a rope as long as the way down and would only be dragged along the floor. The soldier is then a pendulum on it: `RpgPlatformMath.swing_step` (half of the step's pull before the move and half after, as a jump is stepped; it keeps the swing's width over ten seconds in the test), started with what the body's speed gives along the swing, and pushed by half of the rope's pull when left or right is held. Something in the way stops the swing; a swing that is stopped for 0.3 s lets go by itself. **Letting go** (the button again, or jump): the soldier's velocity is the swing's, and it is kept until the soldier lands or meets a wall: the platform actor sets its horizontal speed from the input every step, so the soldier holds the actor's own wall-jump lock while it is carried, and marks the jump as already cut so that a rise is not halved. **A hoist:** a hook that holds within 0.2 rad of straight above. The soldier climbs at 9 tiles a second; when the two cells over the tile it hangs from are not solid it is put on the tile (a plank, the edge of a ledge), else it hangs under it until it lets go. **No jump:** `RpgSoldier.can_jump`; `press_jump` does nothing then, except that down with jump still drops through a plank (without it a soldier that was hoisted on to a plank could only walk off its end). **Options:** `grapple` and `jump` in `starter_map`; `jump: false` without `grapple: true` is refused (`CAT-002`, as every bad stage option). `stage_plan.PRESETS["bionic"]` is both. `default_input("run_and_gun")` has `grapple` (L, V, pad B) in every run and gun: the input map does not depend on the options. **The plan without jumping** (`_draw_for_the_arm`): the same kinds of chunks, drawn for the arm. A gap has a block of ground in the air over it, five rows above the floor and two tiles wider than the gap; spikes are sunk into the ground (a bed a tile deep) with the same block over them, because a swing passes 0.65 tiles over the floor and spikes that stand on it would be touched; a step (two or three tiles) and a wall (two) have two planks reaching out to the left at the height of their top, to be hoisted on to. A plan with jumping on is unchanged (the test compares it with and without `grapple`). **The check** (`check_grapple`, `VAL-030`, a warning): `climb` walks the ground; a step up needs a plank beside it at the height of its top within the hoist's reach; columns that cannot be stood on (a pit, spikes) need a `swing_point`: a tile whose underside the hook reaches when it is fired up and forward from ground left of them (the hook goes up as far as it goes forward, at most 7 tiles of rope, with a free way), and from which the swing comes to rest (rope 0.6, so 0.6 of the rise beyond the hook) over ground beyond them with the feet above it. That is the far end of the pendulum let go at rest; a player that lets go earlier flies further. `ground()` now takes the solid tiles that stand on the bottom of the stage, so a block in the air is not ground; for plans without one it gives what it gave. The planner's own stages all pass (forty seeds, three lengths in the test), so a re-roll is not seen; `VAL-030` is reported for a stored plan that was made for jumping when the option is turned off afterwards (the plan is kept, as always), and tested with stages made by hand. **The pendulum in both languages:** `stage_plan.swing_period` and `RpgPlatformMath.swing_period` are the same formula (2 pi sqrt(L/g) over the arithmetic-geometric mean of 1 and cos(amplitude / 2)); for 64 px, the actor's gravity and 45 degrees both tests have 1.947866 s, and the swing stepped at 30, 60 and 120 Hz is within 1 % of it (0.01 % at 60 Hz). **Recipes:** the soldier gets the arm when the option is on, with the picture of the packs' `ITEM` tagged `grapple` (or the kit's drawn hook) as its hook; `can_jump` from the option. **Smoke:** `soldier_jumps` shows, in a game without jumping, that the button does nothing; the new step `grapple_swings_the_soldier` (nothing to do in a game without the arm) finds the first block over a gap ahead, puts the soldier where the hook is fired from, fires it up and forward with the real input actions, waits for the far end of the swing, lets go and has the soldier land beyond the block alive; in a game with the arm and jumping (no blocks planned) it fires the hook up and sees it come back or hold. **Seen on a screen:** a recording of the generated `gunner` game as *Bionic*: the block over a pit, the rope of drawn links with the pack's hook at its end, the soldier in the air on both sides of the hook, and the plank before a step with the soldier on it after a hoist, then walking on to the step. **Deviations and what is open.** (1) The chain is drawn (dots); only the hook is a picture. The kit can draw a chain link (`arcade_kit.chain`) but does not write one as an asset. (2) `tagged_only` is a property of the runtime's node; there is no option of the generator for it and no tile of a pack has the `grapple` data, so nothing sets it. (3) The *Bionic* preset is a constant for the wizard of T16.16 to offer; the CLI has no flag for it. (4) *Done when* (played as Bionic from the start to the last boss): the smoke run of the Bionic `gunner` game passes every step, with one real swing over a pit; the ways to the bosses are skipped as before. Nobody played it. A hoist is tested in GDScript and was recorded, but is not a smoke step. (5) A hook fired forward at a wall drags the soldier along the floor to the wall and then lets go; the genre pulls the hero to the wall. (6) The check takes the soldier's hand to be 0.75 tiles over its feet (a body of a tile and a half, the most the recipe makes); a smaller soldier has a little more rope to spare, and the smoke step works the place out from the real body. (7) After a death the soldier drops in a quarter of a screen from the left as before, which can be over a pit or a bed of spikes; that is T16.08's and not looked at here. (8) Enemies are not part of the check, and a `runner` still jumps. **Tests:** `test_grapple.gd` (7), `test_stage_plan.py` (9, four new), `test_m16_run_and_gun.py` (10, two new: the Bionic game, and `VAL-030` for a plan made for jumping).

T16.12 (racing: the car and the camera). **The kind can be generated**: `racing` is in the config's `archetype` enum and the CLI's choices; the wizard does not offer it (`features.RACING_AVAILABLE` is off, its gate is T16.20). It is an arcade kind on the framework of T16.01: a track is an `RpgArcadeStage` that does not scroll (`axis: none`, `exit: custom`), `RpgRacingMode` is the arcade run with the player's car. **The arithmetic is written once** in `rpggen/core/car_math.py` and again in `racing/car_physics.gd` (`RpgCarPhysics`), function for function. `car_math.cases()` is a table of 347 inputs and results (every function, and a drive of 200 steps over road, grass and oil with a boost); `python -m rpggen.core.car_math` writes it to `godot_templates/tests/unit/car_math_cases.json`. `test_car_math.py` fails when the file is not what the module gives and when a constant of the GDScript file differs; `test_racing.gd` runs every case through `RpgCarPhysics` (to 0.0001, the drive to 0.001). **The model:** a heading and a speed along it. Top speed 12 tiles a second, 9 a second of acceleration, a brake of 22 that then reverses to 0.4 of the top speed, rolling out at 5. The wheel turns the car 3.4 rad a second at walking pace and 1.9 at the top speed, less below 0.8 tiles a second and not at all standing, the other way round in reverse. A surface is a grip and a part of the top speed: road 1 and 1, dirt 0.7 and 0.8, grass 0.45 and 0.55, oil 0.12 and 1; a car that is too fast for where it is slows at 14. Each step the velocity is split along the new heading: the part along it is the speed the pedals work on, the part sideways fades at 16 times the grip, and while it is over 0.18 of the top speed the car drifts. (20 was the first number: at the top speed a hard turn on the road then made too little sideways speed for a drift to begin, so it was lowered.) A bump gives back 0.3 of the speed into what was hit and keeps 0.75 of the speed along it. A boost is a second and a half at 1.5 times the top speed and three times the acceleration. `curve_speed(radius)` is for the planner of T16.13. **The car** (`RpgCar`) is told what to want, as the soldier and the ship are; `move_and_collide`, a bump, and the rest of the step along what was hit. It has three boosts to press (the card has the boost as a pickup or an arrow of the track; pickups are T16.14, so that the action does something now) and takes one from a `boost` cell without spending its own. **What it drives on:** the track's own node, `RpgTrackSurfaces`: rows of letters, one a cell (`r`, `g`, `d`, `o`, `b`), which the track recipe writes. The card has the surface come from the tile (its `surface` of the terrain facts, as footsteps use it). A pack's road sheet is one asset with one tag, whose tiles are asphalt and grass alike, and the tile set recipe does not write a tile's own `custom` data, so the tiles cannot say which cell is road; the plan of a track knows. A track without the node (one made by hand) is driven by what footsteps hear: the tile's `footstep`, else the surface of its asset, with the names a pack uses mapped to the four (`asphalt` and `stone` are road, `sand` and `gravel` dirt, `water` and `ice` oil). **The picture** (`RpgHeadingSprite`): a `Sprite2D` over the whole sheet with `hframes` and `vframes` from the frame size; `headings` from the catalog (T16.02). Sixteen headings are sixteen frames, the first pointing up and then clockwise; one picture is rotated (the `ASSET-032` case). **The camera** (`RpgRaceCamera`) is the stage's own `Camera` with another script, and the stage's scroller is told it has no camera, so nothing else moves it. It goes five times a second toward a point ahead of the car along its velocity (a quarter of the screen's height at the top speed) and toward a zoom of 0.85; `fixed` puts it over the middle of the track at a zoom that shows all of it. **Options:** `steering` (`relative`, `absolute`) and `camera` (`follow`, `fixed`) in `starter_map`. **Input:** `accelerate` (Up, W, pad A, the right trigger), `brake` (Down, S, pad B, the left trigger), `boost` (Shift, Space, pad X); `move_left` and `move_right` steer, and with absolute steering the four `move_` actions are the heading. **Recipes:** `racing/car_recipe` (the `VEHICLE` chosen on the Mapping page, else the first; a circle as its body), `racing/track_recipe` (one test track until T16.13: a field of 48 by 30 cells of drawn grass, a ring of road five cells wide, barriers at the four edges and a block in the middle, the car on the left straight heading up), `racing/mode_recipe`, and the arcade HUD with `RpgRacingHud` (the rows `speed` and `boosts`). The road is painted with the first tile of the `GROUND` sheet tagged `road`; when the packs have ground and none of it is a road, the road is drawn (a rectangle a run of cells), not painted with whatever ground there is. **Smoke** (`smoke_steps_racing.gd`, nine steps, all with the real input actions): the title and a new game, the pedal and the brake and reverse, the wheel (and the frame of the picture), the camera ahead of the car and zoomed out, a boost over the top speed, grass at its own top speed, a bump into the barrier at the edge, one lap by a simple driver (head for the next corner, brake before it; off the road for less than a tenth of its steps), and the track ended by hand through to the credits. **Seen on a screen:** a recording of the lap in the game generated from the `racer` pack: the car's picture points up, right, down and left as it goes round, the camera is ahead of it and zoomed out, the HUD shows the speed and the boosts. **Deviations and what is open.** (1) The surface is the track's, not the tile's (above). (2) The road of the test track is one tile repeated: the `racer` pack's first road tile is a straight with a centre line, so the whole ring is striped, also across its corners. Which tile goes where is T16.13 (`road`, `finish`, `tyre`). (3) Bumping into another car is the same code as bumping into a barrier, and is not tested: there is one car until T16.14. (4) No engine sound: `sfx_engine` is a loop whose pitch follows the speed, and nothing plays a loop yet; `sfx_bump` and `sfx_skid` are played. (5) The HUD still has the arcade rows *Lives* and *Stage*; the race HUD is T16.15. (6) A track never ends by itself: there are no laps until T16.14. The smoke step ends it. (7) `curve_speed` is not used by anything yet. (8) The numbers of the model are a first guess that drives round the test track; nobody has driven it by hand. (9) A new CI job `racing` was added to the archetype matrix and left out of the main integration run, as the other kinds are; it has not run on CI. **Tests:** `test_racing.gd` (10), `test_car_math.py` (7), `test_racing_config.py` (3), `test_m16_racing.py` (5: the `racer` pack; a pack without a car, with absolute steering and a fixed camera; the second run a repeat).

T16.13 (racing: the track plan and the track recipe). **`core/track_plan.py`**, pure Python. A track is a closed loop on a coarse grid (`track_size`: 4 by 3, 5 by 4 or 6 by 4 cells; the name `size` is the top-down starter map's width and height, and a first version that reused it replaced that key in the config schema, which two tests of the Options page caught), every cell a block of nine tiles with a road five wide through its middle and a verge of two at each side. **The loop** is not a random walk, which the card names: a walk has to be thrown away whenever it runs into itself. It begins as the ring round a rectangle (four cells wide at least) and is pushed out a piece at a time: two neighbouring cells of the loop are joined through the two free cells beside them instead of directly. A ring stays a ring under that, so it never crosses itself and nothing is thrown away; every push adds two cells and two corners, and two of those in a row are an S-bend. A track is pushed `1 + its number + the difficulty` times (fewer when the field is full), so later tracks are longer and have more corners. The field is then cut to the loop. **The start** is the first cell of the longest run of straight cells, two at least, so that what stands on the grid has room to get going; a loop without such a run is planned again with the next seed (the notes of the plan say so). The car stands a tile and a half into that cell, the line two tiles ahead of it. **On the road**, never in the first two cells: boost arrows (one, more on later tracks), patches of oil two by two tiles at one side of the road (there is a way past), stretches of dirt, each on a straight cell of its own. **Checkpoints** are the middle of every second cell, a whole cell wide, in the order of the loop; **waypoints** the middle of every cell, with the speed `car_math` allows: the top speed of the surface on a straight, `curve_speed(2.5)` (6.3 tiles a second) in a corner. **Barriers:** the four edges of the field and every cell the loop does not use, run by run; two places for scenery on each of those cells, a tyre at the outside of every corner, and places for pickups (two, more later) with a kind each. **The check** (`check`, VAL-029): the loop is closed and no cell is in it twice; from the start line every tile of road is reached (a flood over the road tiles), and the car, every checkpoint and every waypoint stand on it; the checkpoints are in the order of the loop; and where the road leaves a cell it is at least three tiles (two cars) wide across. The tests break a planned track in each of those ways. **A driver** in the test (and the same one in the smoke step) follows the waypoints on `car_math`: head for the next one, brake down to its speed in the distance the brake needs, no pedal while still turning at speed, and turn to the next waypoint four tiles before this one. With a turn two and a half tiles before it (the first try) the car was on the outer verge for up to a seventh of a lap; with four it stays on the road on every track of 12 seeds, three sizes and the hard setting, two laps each, through every checkpoint in its order. That is how *can be driven lap after lap* is tested for every track and not only the first. **Stored like the other arcade kinds:** the section `stages` of `rpggen/world_plan.json` with `kind: racing`, and the list the game reads is `game/data/stages.json`. The card has a section `tracks` and a file `game/data/tracks.json`; a track is a stage of the arcade framework (T16.01: the run, the save between stages, the stage-clear screen, the list a person owns once it exists), and a second list beside it would be the same data twice. `for_project`, `extend` and `exists` follow `stage_plan`; the orchestrator picks the planner by the kind. **Options:** `tracks`, `laps`, `track_size`, `difficulty`, `added_tracks`, beside `steering` and `camera`. **The recipe** (`racing/track_recipe`) builds a scene for every stored track; without a stored plan it still builds the test ring of T16.12. The road is painted with one tile of the pack's `GROUND` sheet tagged `road`: the plainest one, found by reading the pixels (grey, dark, of one colour), because a road sheet has tiles with centre lines, a start and a barrier, and one of those repeated over a road five tiles wide is stripes (T16.12 looked like that; a first version that read sixteen pixels a tile missed a line one pixel wide). A `GROUND` sheet tagged `finish` gives the tile of the start line; without one the line is drawn. The drawn road sheet of the kit is not used: its tiles are a road one tile wide with grass at its sides. When no ground of the packs is a road, the road is drawn, a rectangle a run of cells. Dirt, oil and boost arrows are drawn over the road (`Marks`). The edges of the field are red walls, the unused cells dark hedges with the `DECORATION` pictures of the packs on them, the tyres the `HAZARD` picture tagged `tyre` (or a drawn disc) with a round shape that stops a car. Pickups are markers with their kind. `stages.json` gets a track without what is painted. **The picture:** `map_preview.render_tracks(stages)` draws the tracks side by side from the plan (road, what lies on it, walls, hedges, checkpoints as dots, the car and the way it starts), `render_track` one of them, `track_rects` where each is; the orchestrator writes it as the picture of the Results page (`rpggen/results/starter_map.png`), as it does the world of a metroidvania. **Smoke:** the lap is two laps along the waypoints of the first track through every checkpoint; the grass and the barrier are found from what the track says of its cells (the verge above its topmost road, the wall above that), so the steps hold for any track; the last step goes through every track (each car stands on its road and gets going along it) to the credits. **Seen on a screen:** a recording of the generated `racer` game: the whole first track under a fixed camera (plain asphalt, the white line, a boost arrow, a patch of oil, the pack's cones at the corners, hedges, the red wall) and the car on its lap. **Deviations and what is open.** (1) Not a random walk (above). (2) `stages`, not `tracks` (above). (3) Corners are square with one tile cut off their outside; there are no curved tiles. A pack's curve tiles are not used: nothing says which tile of a sheet is which. (4) The `tyre` tag chooses a prop, not a tile; `road` and `finish` choose tiles. (5) Pickups are places in the plan and markers in the scene; what they are is T16.14. (6) Decoration is only on the unused cells; a track that fills its field has none. (7) The Options page of the wizard does not show the preview yet: the page is T16.16. The function it will call is there and tested. (8) The loop is always driven clockwise. (9) Two cells of the loop that lie side by side without being neighbours on it have only grass between their roads; a car can cross it, and the checkpoints are what makes that no shortcut. (10) Nobody has driven a track by hand. **Tests:** `test_track_plan.py` (9), `test_racing.gd` (12, two new), `test_racing_config.py` (3), `test_m16_racing.py` (7, two new: the stored plan and the picture; an added track).

Two things found while T16.13 was tested, and fixed with it. **A key twice in a schema.** JSON takes the last of two equal keys without a word; the second `size` of `starter_map` (above) is how that was learnt. `tests/unit/test_schema_keys.py` reads every file of `rpggen/schema/` and fails on a key that is twice in one object. **The page header kept its page wide.** `tests/gui/test_options_m11.py::test_the_page_uses_two_columns_only_when_wide` failed whenever its file ran by itself and passed in a whole run. Under the tests' offscreen platform the fallback font is wide, and the header of the Options page (its title, the preset bar and the help button on one line) then needed 1004 px, so the page could not be made 900 px wide and never left its two columns; in a whole run something an earlier test left behind made the header narrower. That is a trap of the real window too, with a wide font or a long translated title. `PageHeader` now puts the page's controls on a line of their own under the title when the three do not fit on one, and reports the width of the wider of those two lines as its minimum, so it never keeps its page from being narrowed (`stacked`; `test_the_header_gives_way_to_a_narrow_page`). The controls stay children of the header itself (their layout is what is moved): a first version put them in a widget of their own, and five tests that look for a page's controls in its header said so. **A card lost its height.** `test_the_welcome_cards_are_buttons_for_the_keyboard` failed when `test_m15_pages.py` ran before it and passed by itself: the three cards of the welcome view were 154 px high where 160 is their minimum. `setMinimumHeight(160)` never held: the stylesheet gives every `QPushButton` a `min-height`, and Qt sets that as the widget's minimum whenever it polishes it, so the card's own number was replaced by 30 and a page with little room squeezed the cards. `CardButton.set_floor_height` keeps the number and sets it again after every polish and change of style. **A cancel that was timed from the wrong moment.** `test_assemble_reports_images_and_a_cancel_stops_within_one_image` allowed two seconds from the call to the stop; on a busy Windows machine starting the worker processes alone takes longer. It now counts from the moment the cancel was first answered with yes, which is what the test is about, and still asks that the work stopped near the twentieth image.

T16.14 (racing: rivals, laps, checkpoints, pickups). **The race** (`racing/race.gd`, `RpgRace`, the child `Race` of a track's scene). `RpgRacingMode` sets it up when the track is entered, with the track's entry of the list of stages and every car of the scene, and begins it: the cars are put on the places they have in the scene, their engines are off, three seconds are counted, and they go. Per car it keeps the lap, the next checkpoint, where the car was a step ago and, once it has finished, its time. **A line** (`RpgRaceCheckpoint`: a checkpoint, or the start line) is a place, a way and a width; a step crosses it when it began before the line and ended on or past it, within the width, and was no longer than the width (a car that is put somewhere else did not drive over it). **A lap** counts at the start line and only when the next checkpoint is none: every one was passed, in its order, since the line was last crossed. Before its first crossing a car is on lap 0; the first crossing begins lap 1 and counts nothing. **Checkpoints moved** in the plan: they were the middle of every second cell, and a line across the middle of a corner cell is not crossed by a car that cuts the corner (a rival turns to the next waypoint four tiles before this one), so a rival drove lap after lap with one checkpoint never counted, and a player would have too. A checkpoint is now the middle of the edge where the road comes into its cell, which every car goes over. **The places:** a car that has finished is ahead of every car that has not, the earlier the further; the others by laps, then checkpoints, then how near the next line they are. **The wrong way:** the way of the track at a car is from the waypoint before the nearest one to the one after it; a player that moves faster than two tiles a second against it for a second is told, and is not told any more when it stops or turns. **The finish:** the race is over when the player has driven its laps. A rival that finishes before that drives on (a car that stops just past the line is in the way of everybody behind it); its time is kept. The mode gives the points of the place (3000, 1800, 1000, 500, 250, 100) and ends the track, and the arcade run goes to the next one. **Rivals** (`racing/rival.gd`, `RpgRival`) are what `RpgCarInput` is for the player: the child `Input` of a car, asked before every step. A rival is the driver of T16.13's test and smoke step, with a `skill`: it brakes down to the waypoint's speed times `0.7 + 0.3 * skill`, begins to brake `2 * (1 - skill)` tiles earlier, gives `0.85 + 0.15 * skill` of the pedal and none above `0.75 + 0.25 * skill` of the car's top speed, and aims up to `1.2 * (1 - skill)` tiles beside the middle of the road, the same way every lap. The car is the player's car with the player's physics; nothing else moves it. Skills: 0.9, 0.75, 0.6, 0.8, 0.5. **Stuck:** a rival that stands for a second and a half backs off for forty steps and then keeps a tile and a half to one side of the road for a while, the other side each time. The first version only backed off and drove into the same thing again: in the test a rival never got past the player's car, which stood on the grid. **The rubber band** (`rubber_band`, on): a rival more than two checkpoints ahead of the player gives up to a quarter less pedal. It never gives one more than its own. **The grid:** two cars side by side, row behind row, the first row just before the line; the rivals in order, the player last. **Pickups** (`racing/pickup.gd`) are what the plan put on the road; the race asks every step which car is on which. A boost is one more boost (a car that has nine leaves it lying), a missile or a bomb is the car's weapon (one at a time), a shield takes the next hit; they lie there again after six seconds. A part (`speed`, `tyres`, `acceleration`) is taken by the player only and once; the plan puts one on every track, a different one on each of three. **Weapons** (`racing/weapon.gd`): a missile flies the way the car pointed at 26 tiles a second for 1.6 s and makes the first other car within 0.7 tiles spin; a bomb lies a tile behind the car for twelve seconds, is armed after half a second, and goes off under the first car within 0.9 tiles (also the one that dropped it). A car that is hit spins for 1.2 s: it turns round, cannot steer or use the pedals, and loses speed. A rival uses a weapon fifty steps after it took it. **Parts** change the shared arithmetic: `car_math.step` and `RpgCarPhysics.step` take the car's own top speed, acceleration and grip factor (`part_factor`: 4 % of top speed, 8 % of acceleration and 8 % of grip a level, three levels), and the table of cases has a second drive with all three parts at their third level. The mode keeps the levels as text in the arcade run's `weapon` (`speed=1;tyres=0;acceleration=2`), which the run already saves and loads. **The car** stands while `engine_on` is off; it starts with one boost (three before: the rest lie on the track now). **Input:** `fire` (X, K, pad Y). **Options:** `rivals` (0 to 5) and `rubber_band`. **Recipes:** `racing/rival_recipes` makes `rival_N.tscn` and `pickup.tscn`; the track recipe puts the rivals and the player on the grid, the pickups at their places and the `Race` node into every track. A rival has the picture of another `VEHICLE` of the packs, then of an `ENEMY` sprite; when there are fewer of those than rivals, the rest have the player's picture with a tint. **HUD:** the rows `lap`, `place`, `weapon`, `shield` and `notice` (the countdown, *Go!*, *Wrong way!*, the place at the finish). **Tests without waiting:** the tests of the race step the cars and the race themselves, some thousand steps in a loop without a frame between them, so a rival's lap of each of the four planned tracks takes no time; the tracks are those of `track_plan.plan({}, 12345)`, written to `godot_templates/tests/unit/track_plan_cases.json` by `python -m rpggen.core.track_plan` and kept by a Python test. **Smoke:** the first track's race is seen counting down with every car on the road behind the line and the player last, and no car moving; after the start the rivals are parked and the car is tried alone as before; every kind of pickup of the track is driven over and a weapon fired with the `fire` action; then the race is run again with the rivals for one lap, the player's buttons held by the driver (which now backs off when it is stuck), to the finish: standings in the order of the places, every rival at least half way round, points for the place, and the next track begins. Every other track is seen counting down and starting. **Seen on a screen:** a recording of that race in the game made from the `racer` pack: four cars on the grid behind the line, the countdown in the HUD, the rivals' own pictures driving the track, pickups on the road. The player's driver came in fourth, 2.3 s behind the first. **Deviations and what is open.** (1) A rival without a picture of its own is the player's car with a tint (`modulate`), not a recoloured copy from the variant pipeline of T13.18. (2) The weapon has an action of its own (`fire`); the card's controls have one button, boost. (3) The standings are what `finished` carries and `standings()` returns; the screen that shows them is T16.15. A race that is lost goes on to the next track like one that is won: elimination is T16.15 too. (4) *Can be won and lost* is tested with cars a test moves (a rival that finishes first is first) and in the smoke race, where the place can be any; nobody has driven against the rivals by hand, and whether they are too fast or too slow is unknown. (5) Weapons pass through barriers. (6) A rival does not look ahead for other cars; it only gets round what it was stuck on. (7) Rivals do not take parts. (8) The pickup picture is the packs' first `POWERUP` for every kind, tinted by the kind; without one a drawn diamond (a square for a part). (9) `sfx_engine` is still not played.

T16.15 (racing: championship, results and the HUD). **The championship** (`racing/championship.gd`, `RpgChampionship`, plain data). `record(standings)` counts a race: 10, 6, 4, 3, 2, 1 points for the places and nothing behind the sixth; it returns the rows of the race (driver, place, time, points, the driver's total). `table()` is the drivers by their points; of two with the same points the one who was better in the last race is ahead, then the one who was seen first. The drivers are `player` and `rival_1`, `rival_2`, ..., by the number in the name of the rival's node, so a rival is the same driver on every track. These points are the championship's; the points of a place for the score of the arcade run (3000, 1800, ...) stay, and the score is still what the high-score table of the title screen keeps. **The mode** has one championship for the run. A race that ends is recorded before the track is ended; the screen after it is `RpgRaceResults` in the place of the arcade tally: the race (place, driver, time or a dash for a car that had not finished, the points), the parts won in it, and the table, the player's lines in another colour. After the last race its heading names the champion, and the high-score entry and the credits follow as in every arcade run. A track that is ended without its race (the smoke run's later tracks) still gets the arcade tally. **Saving between races:** the arcade mode got two hooks, `run_data()` and `restore_run_data(data)`, for what a kind keeps of a run beside the stage, the lives, the score and the weapon; the racing mode gives its table (`races`, `last`, `points`). It is saved when a race begins, so a loaded game drives that race with the table as it was before it. The parts are still the text in `weapon` (T16.14). A new run begins with an empty table. **Elimination** (`elimination`, off by default): the player who came in last twice is out. Last means the last of two cars or more; a car that races alone is first. The mode then shows the results with *Last twice: you are out*, puts the table back to what it was before that race, and goes the way of the arcade game over: the continue screen, where *Continue* drives the same race again (it then counts as if it had not been driven, and the first last place still stands) and *Quit* goes to the high-score entry and the title. The option is off by default because nobody has yet driven against the rivals by hand (T16.14): whether a new player comes in last twice in four races is not known, and a game that ends for that reason by default would be a bad first game. **The HUD** (`RpgRacingHud`; the card names the file `ui/race_hud.gd`, and the class has been `racing_hud.gd` since T16.12, beside `shmup_hud.gd` and `run_and_gun_hud.gd`): the *Lives* of the arcade HUD are hidden (a car has none), the rows are the stage, the lap, the place, the speed, the boosts, the weapon, the shield, the player's points once a race was driven, and *Last place: 1/2* in a game with elimination. The countdown, *Go!*, *Wrong way!* (in red) and the place at the finish are no longer a row in the corner but a sign of 32 px with an outline in the upper middle of the screen. **The map of the track** (`ui/race_minimap.gd`, `RpgRaceMinimap`) is what the card calls the minimap of T12.64 reused: it extends `RpgMinimap` (its size, its frame, the *Minimap* setting) and replaces what it shows. `RpgMinimap` shows a window of a map's picture round the player, from pictures the minimap recipe makes of `game/maps`; a race wants the whole track and every car, and a track has no such picture. So the road is drawn from the track's `RpgTrackSurfaces` (a pixel a cell, the start line white), fitted into the view with its shape kept, and every car that is on the track and shown is a dot, the player's larger and red. It is in the bottom-right corner, because the score is in the top-right one. `racing/hud_recipe` builds the HUD's scene (`track_map`), in the place of the shared arcade HUD recipe. **The README** of a racing game (`project_files/README.racing.md`, section 22, and a sentence in the first paragraph): the controls, the rules of a race, the points, elimination, the save, what the screen shows, where the files are, how to change a race (`title`, `laps`, `checkpoints`, `waypoints`, `start`), a rival's skill, and more tracks. **Smoke:** a new step after the race: the rows of the race as the mode had them (places in order, the points of each, the player's time), the table in the order of that race, the results screen built with them (a line a car in both halves, the player's line named *You* with a time, a heading, inside the screen), the table in the save of the run, and on the second track the HUD: no lives, lap, place and speed, the player's points, a sign that counts while the race counts down, and a map on the screen whose picture is as large as the track in cells, with a dot for every car and one of them the player's. **Seen on a screen:** a recording of the game made from the `racer` pack: the countdown as a large number over the grid, the map in the corner with the four cars as dots moving round the loop, and the results screen in the middle of the screen over a darkened game. **Two things found by looking, and fixed.** (1) *The results screen was at the top left with no shade.* The first recording showed it so, and the smoke step had said it did not fit the screen. A control that calls `set_anchors_preset(PRESET_FULL_RECT)` on itself in `_ready` is in the tree by then, and there the call keeps the control's rectangle: the anchors move and the offsets are changed to match, so a new control stays 0 by 0. The results screen had copied that line from the arcade tally, and the tally, the continue screen, the high-score table, the member picker and the equip picker all have it: every one of them was 0 by 0, its shade over nothing and its content hung from the top-left corner. All six now call `set_anchors_and_offsets_preset`. `test_a_screen_over_the_game_covers_the_whole_screen` puts each under a layer and compares its size with the viewport's. (2) *The minimap of T12.64 was off the screen.* `RpgMinimap` set the top-right preset and then `position = (-120, 8)` in `_ready`; a position is counted from the left edge of the screen, so the minimap of every game made since M12 was drawn 120 px left of the screen and nobody saw one. It now sets its offsets from the corner; `test_the_minimap_is_in_the_top_right_corner_of_the_screen`. The HUD boxes that use the same two lines are right, because they set them before the box is added to the tree, where the parent's rectangle counts as empty (`docs/godot_api_verified.md` has the three cases). Neither was caught earlier because no test looked at where a screen was, and the recordings of M12 to M16 did not happen to show these screens. **Deviations and what is open.** (1) `racing_hud.gd`, not `race_hud.gd` (above). (2) The minimap is a subclass that draws something else, not the same picture pipeline (above). (3) Elimination is off by default (above); with it on, *Continue* is always offered: there are no credits to spend. (4) The arcade tally's bonus (the time under the par time, 1000 a life for three lives a car never loses) is still added to the score of a racing game, and no screen shows it now that the results are in the tally's place. (5) A rival has no name: *Rival 1*, *Rival 2*. (6) The results close by themselves after ten seconds, the tally after four. (7) The title of a track without a name is *Stage 1*, the arcade word. (8) The minimap of the top-down kinds is now visible in games that are generated again; nobody has looked at it over each kind's HUD. In a metroidvania it was designed for that corner (T14.08). (9) The wizard still does not offer the kind: `RACING_AVAILABLE` is off until the gate (T16.20), and the Options page for it is T16.16. **Tests:** `test_championship.gd` (9: points and the table over four scripted races, through a save and back; elimination; the driver of a car; the lines of the results and the screen; the mode over four tracks with the table in the save; out, Continue and Quit; the map; the HUD; the screens' size), `test_minimap.gd` (one new), `test_race.gd` (the sign), `test_racing_config.py`, `test_track_plan.py`, `test_m16_racing.py` (9, one new: the README; the game without rivals is generated with elimination).

T16.16 done — Wizard: cards, roles, options and previews; the three kinds stay behind their flags until the gate, and the notes below say what differs from the card. **The cards.** The Archetype page has six cards in a scroll area: in a window 1000 px wide and more, two rows of three (`ArchetypeCard.set_shape("grid")`: the drawing between the text and the flow, 160 px high at most); below that, one column that scrolls, each card with its drawing (200 x 112) at the left (`"row"`). The width that counts is the window's, not the page's: beside the stepper the page of a 1000 px window is about 830 px. In the grid a card leaves its list of what is generated and its controls line out while two rows of whole cards would not fit (`FULL_CARD_HEIGHT`), which is what makes two rows fit at 720 px: at 1100 x 720 both rows are in view, at 1400 x 1000 the cards are whole. The card that is chosen is scrolled into view. `MockScreen` draws a ship over stars with a V of enemies, a soldier on a ledge that aims up at an enemy on a plank, and two cars on a road that bends (with a pack's art the ship and the soldier are its player and the enemies its enemy; the car is always drawn, a pack's vehicle is not in the cards' art). A kind whose flag is off has its card disabled with *Coming in a later version*; `kind_available` reads the five flags. **The hint.** `pack_kind_of(catalog)` reads `view:shmup` and `view:racing` (T16.02) and the page says *This pack looks like a shooter pack: a shoot 'em up fits it.* (or the racing one) when that card can be chosen; a side-view pack names both side-view kinds once the run and gun can be chosen, and the air warning (`ASSET-030`) is the run and gun's too. **Leaving the page** with another kind takes untouched features along as it took untouched bindings (an arcade kind starts without dialogue), and drops the `starter_map` options the new kind does not have when either kind is a planned one (rooms, stages, tracks): a metroidvania's `rooms` made a shoot 'em up's options invalid. **The Mapping page** needed no row of its own: `mapping.rows(kind)` and `requirements.REQUIRED_BY_ARCHETYPE` (T16.03) give the roles and their wording, the sound section the kind's slots; new is the heading count in the row of a rotation sheet (*player_car.png · racer · 16 headings*). **Options.** `ShmupBox`, `RunAndGunBox` and `RacingBox` are three subclasses of `ArcadeBox` in `options_sections.py`: the kind's options from a table of fields (spin boxes, combos, check boxes that wrap), a preview from the plan with a legend, the plan in words, and the table of added stages or tracks (a row that is built stays, as an added room does). In a run and gun *Jump* can be cleared only while *Grapple arm* is checked. The starter map box, the imported maps and the layer list are not shown for these kinds, and neither are the features `demo` and `navigation` (`options.ARCADE_HIDDEN`): the builders of the arcade kinds read neither, and `demo` needs `dialogue`, which is off for them, so the page would have switched `demo` off in every arcade config; their values stay as the config has them. **The previews** are pictures of `rpggen/core/map_preview.py` like the others: `render_waves` (a column a stage read from the bottom up, a mark a wave as wide as it has enemies and coloured by what comes, the boss a block at the top), `render_stage_strip` (every stage from the side: ground, planks, spikes, the lines between chunks, the arena shaded, dots for the start, the enemies, the weapons and the boss, and for a soldier that does not jump the tiles its arm can hold on to), and `render_tracks` of T16.13, which now draws the pickups too. The plan is made in the page's own thread on every change (it is cheap) with the screen and the tile size of the draft, so a change of the resolution redraws it; a project that has its stages shows the stored plan with what was added. **The summary** is *Shoot 'em up · vertical · 3 stages · 3 lives* (`options.arcade_numbers`, `Summary.numbers`) over *Stages: 3 stages, scrolling up*. **The estimate** learns per kind: `AppSettings.kind_timings` keeps the assets and the seconds of the last run of each kind, and the Options page uses the kind's own before the last run of any kind. **Presets.** `options.KIND_PRESETS`: *Arcade classic* (shoot 'em up and run and gun: 3 lives, hard), *Bionic* (run and gun: grapple, no jump) and *Sunday drive* (racing: absolute steering, 2 rivals, rubber-banding), listed after the three built-in ones for the kinds they fit; one sets its values among the kind's options and leaves the rest of the page as it is. **The command line** took the three keys already; a dry run now plans and says *3 stages, 30 waves, 3 bosses* or *4 tracks of 3 laps, 3 rivals* (`options.plan_text`) with the notes of the plan. **Deviations and what is open.** (1) *Arcade classic* has no *no continues*: nothing in the config says how many continues a run has (the continue screen of T16.01 offers Continue every time), so the preset is three lives and hard. (2) The presets are in `rpggen/core/options.py` with the other option presets, not in `profiles.py` (pack profiles). (3) A preset of a kind does not choose the kind: *Bionic* is offered on the Options page of a run and gun only. (4) The schema of the command line's documents did not allow what a dry run of an arcade kind wrote since T16.06 (`summary.map.stages`, and the three kinds in `summary.archetype`): both are added (`02_SCHEMAS.md`, `cli.schema.json`). (5) Still open from T16.02: the grid editor showing the headings as a ring, and the pack card's words. From T16.21: variants as one card. (6) The drawings of the three cards and the two new previews were looked at as pictures; nobody chose a kind in a window by hand. (7) `dev.LAYOUT_ARCHETYPES` is still the three older kinds (the layout matrix and the documentation's pictures); the arcade kinds have a layout test of their own with their flags on, at both window sizes in the dark theme. `layout_state` gives a run and gun the side-view sample; the three samples are T16.17. (8) The four goldens of the Archetype page were made again (six cards, three of them disabled); the others did not move. **Older tests changed:** `test_metroidvania_wizard` compared the list of cards with the three older kinds and now compares the first three. **Tests:** `test_archetype_page.py` (5 new: six cards and the flags; each kind chosen, with what follows it into the config; both shapes at three sizes; the drawings; the hints), `test_options_page.py` (7 new: the numbers and the plan in words, the estimate of a kind; the two pictures; each kind's box in place of the starter map; a shoot 'em up's options, an added stage, the reset, the resolution; the jump rule and the three presets; a game that has its stages), `test_layout_fit.py` (6 new: the three pages of each kind at both sizes), `test_cli_scripting.py` (4 new: a dry run of each kind, an unknown kind is exit 2). Only these files and the ones beside them were run (the GUI tests of the pages, the option model, the three kinds' config and plan tests); the full suite, the Godot tests and the integration tests were not.

T16.17 done — Three sample packs and *Try the sample*; the three entries are offered once their kinds are (the flags stay off until the gate), and the packs were not generated and played here (that is T16.18's smoke run). **The packs.** `tools/build_sample_pack.py --kind shmup | gun | race` writes `sample_pack_shmup/` (*Star lane*: a ship in three banking frames, four small ships, a turret and a carrier, a boss, a bullet, a laser and a missile, an explosion in six frames, three capsules, two rocks, two layers of stars, a panel; three loops and five sounds), `sample_pack_gun/` (*Jungle line*: a soldier in seven strips, a trooper, a leaper, a sniper, a turret and a wall cannon, a gate as the boss, a tile sheet with a rope bridge, stakes and a block with a ring, two layers, four weapon pickups, a hook, a bullet, an explosion, a mine, a fern, a panel; two loops and six sounds) and `sample_pack_race/` (*Dirt cup*: a car and three rivals in 16 headings, a road sheet with a start line, a cone, a tyre, oil, a boost arrow, three pickups, a tree, a grandstand, a panel; two loops and six sounds). Every pixel is integer drawing as in the two older packs; the cars are turned by `arcade_kit._turned`. Each has a `LICENSE.txt` (CC0), a `SOURCE.txt` and its profile. **Under 200 KB** (116, 100 and 94 KB with the profile): the sounds are mono, 8-bit, 11025 Hz (`sample_audio.to_wav_small`: every second sample of the 16-bit synthesis, its high byte) and the loops are one bar of 2.56 s; the two older packs are 1.9 MB each, nearly all of it sound. **Detection.** The file names are the ones T16.02 reads, so without a profile nothing stops a run: *Star lane* has no issue at all, *Jungle line* three notes (its explosion is an `EFFECT`, its weapon pickups and its mine are `ITEM`, outside a shooter pack), *Dirt cup* one (the rivals are vehicles, so no `ENEMY`). The profile of each pack, which *Try the sample* installs, sets those roles (and the grandstand's, and the bridge and the stakes of the tile sheet) and confirms the audit; with it `requirements.check` returns nothing for the pack's kind: no `ASSET-010`, no `ASSET-031`, no `ASSET-030`, music and sounds in the kind's slots. **Goldens:** `tests/golden/sample_pack_shmup|gun|race.catalog.json` (`dev.SAMPLE_GOLDENS`, written by `update-goldens`); no older golden moved. **The wizard.** `project_model.SAMPLES` is the list of five (key, label, tooltip, folder, profile, project name, kind) and `offered_samples()` the ones whose kind can be chosen. The welcome card's *Choose a sample* lists them (two today, five with the flags on) and each entry walks to the Generate page as its kind; the Project page's *Try with sample pack* opens the same list when there is more than one (a popup, not a modal menu) and an entry fills the page, sets the kind and goes to the Options. `paths.sample_pack_dir(folder)`; the packaging spec bundles the three; `dev.layout_state` lays every kind out with its own sample (`KIND_SAMPLES`). **Three things this card found and mended.** (1) The config's schema did not take `starter.vehicle`, `starter.projectile` and `starter.explosion`, the keys the Mapping page has written for those roles since T16.03: with a vehicle mapped the Options page refused the config (*Additional properties are not allowed ('vehicle' was unexpected)*), so no racing game could be generated from the wizard. The schema and `02_SCHEMAS.md` have them now. (2) A pack profile could not say `headings` and `heading_order` (the export wrote them, the schema refused them), so a profile of a pack with a rotation sheet could not be written; both are in `profile.schema.json` and in the registry's copy. (3) The sample walk sets the kind before the Project page is filled: the page's `changed` signal starts the walk before `sampleReady`. **Deviations and what is open.** (1) The soldier has idle, run, jump, fall, shoot, hurt and death, not *every animation of T16.08*: `crouch`, `aim_up` and `aim_diagonal` are not action words the strip adapter reads (T16.08 left that open), so strips of those names would not be animations; the game uses `shoot` and `idle` for them. (2) *A tile sheet with grapple ceilings*: the sheet has a block with a ring, drawn; nothing marks it for the arm, because no recipe reads a tile's `grapple` data (T16.11, deviation 2). (3) The spec's *three rivals, cones, tyres, oil, boost arrows, pickups* are all in *Dirt cup*, but the track recipe uses of them what it uses since T16.13 (one road tile, the tyre, the scenery; pickups and hazards are drawn marks). (4) The Project page's button no longer fills the top-down sample at one click when more than one sample is on offer: it opens the list, which is what the card asks. (5) In the grid of the Archetype page (T16.16) a card now grows with its row, and the drawing of a compact card may be as low as 48 px: with a hint line above the cards two rows did not fit at 720 px. The two goldens of the Archetype page at the default size were made again. (6) Nobody listened to the sounds or looked at the packs in a game; the pictures were looked at as contact sheets. (7) The perf rows and the integration jobs of the three samples are T16.18. **Older tests changed:** two tests of `test_sample_pack.py` clicked the Project page's sample button and now choose *Top-down village* from its list; `test_side_view_detection` names the two new goldens that are side-view or have scrolling layers among the ones it leaves out. **Tests:** `test_sample_pack.py` (12 new: each pack is CC0, documented, under 200 KB and byte for byte what the tool makes, twice; each is catalogued as its golden says and, with its profile, has nothing missing and nothing drawn; what each pack is made of; which samples are offered by flag; the welcome menu lists five and each lands on its kind at the Generate page; the Project page's list). Run: this file, the layout, welcome, project, archetype and wizard-flow tests, the visual goldens and the whole unit folder (1556 pass); not the other GUI tests, the Godot tests or the integration tests.

T16.21, twelfth part — what was left of the card, and the ring of T16.02. **A `.aseprite` with no export.** `aseprite_file.render` draws the frames: the layers, the cels (raw, zlib and linked) in RGBA, grayscale and indexed colour with the palette and the see-through index, layer and cel opacity, hidden layers and groups, reference layers, the z-index, put over each other with the normal blend in Aseprite's own whole-number arithmetic. Said in `Picture.inexact` and making the asset *needs review*: another blend mode is drawn as normal, a tile map layer is left out, a group's opacity is not applied. `SequencesAdapter._lone` writes one strip into the work folder (`frame_strips`, named after the source's bytes and `RENDER_VERSION`) and the catalog makes an asset whose picture is that strip (`generated/frame_strips/…`, as a terrain atlas is; the id is the source's), with the tags as animations, the file's own action word when it has no tag, `anim_n` and ADAPT-005 for tags without a name, and a still picture for one frame. A source with no cel is no asset; one that cannot be read is ADAPT-006. A source has an export when a picture of the pack has its name (or its name and a number), when a picture of its folder has all the words of its name, or when its folder is a frame export. **What real files taught** (37 sources of a character template beside their PNG strips, 22 of them pixel for pixel the same): artists draw an animation against a full opaque layer called *BG* or *gif bg* and switch it off to export, so in a file of several frames the lowest drawn layer with such a name that fills the sprite is left out (`Picture.backdrop`); Aseprite's own *Background* layer is kept. Of the 15 that differ, six were looked at: three have a backdrop with no name to tell it by, three were edited after the export; the other nine were not opened. **Deviations:** the asset's path is the strip, not the source, so a folder rule's glob does not reach it; ADAPT-006's words speak of pictures beside the file. Not run through a generation with Godot. **Colour variants as one card.** `catalog.colour_groups`, `colour_group` and `colour_name` read the tags; the audit grid folds the colours of a group into the card of its first picture (badge *⧉ 8*) while they have its status and its role, so what differs or needs a look has a card of its own, and a list of assets or a filter that leaves the card out shows every colour. Confirm, Exclude and a role, by key or in the panel, go to the card and the colours folded into it as one undo step; the panel has a *Variants (8)* row with *Show all* and *Select all*. The chips and the folder headers still count assets. The Mapping page says *4 colours* beside a choice and its list offers the other colours first; the gallery shows every colour with the mark. **Not done:** the editor dock (every colour is an asset there, ungrouped), and the variant pipeline of T13.18 is not used, since these files exist. **`_<N>dir`.** A name with `_4dir` or `_8directions` on a picture with that many rows is a directional sprite (0.85): the frame from the name's cell or frame count, else the measured one, else a square; four rows in the picture's order when it shows one, else RPG Maker's; eight rows go round from down by way of the left, which is an assumption, no pack that was seen has one. **A zip of zips.** `pack_sniff.find_archive_bundle`: two or more zips in one folder of a zip or a folder, no picture beside them, each with pictures, is a bundle whose packs are the inner archives (name, pictures and bytes from the inner listings, read in place; nothing is written; an archive the second does not reach is listed by its size). `open_inner_archive` copies one out and opens it as any archive, and `archive.open_pack` calls it for a member whose path is an archive, so the bundle chooser needs nothing new. Inner 7z files and a 7z around zips are not listed; a licence beside the inner zips does not go with a pack taken from one. The user's archive of eight side-scroller zips was not on the machine, so this was run on archives made in the tests only. **768 x 768.** A picture of that size with see-through pixels, no cell in its name and no grid that could be measured is offered as a 48 px tile sheet for review; a measured grid is not overruled, because such a sheet can be 24 tiles of 32 px. **T16.02's ring.** The grid editor shows a rotation sheet's views in a ring (`HeadingRing`), each where it points by `heading_order`, the first circled, following the grid being edited. No golden moved. **The card's *Done when* on the six real packs was not run again** after this part; the table above stands as it was measured on 2026-10-10, and what this part changes in it (the racing pack's variants as cards of their groups, the eight zips as a bundle) is not measured. **Tests:** `test_aseprite.py` (34), `test_naming.py` (80), `test_zip_of_zips.py` (9), `test_colour_variants.py` (7), `test_grid_editor.py` (11).

T16.22 (a bundle of packs: choosing which to add). A pack taken from a bundle is one PackRef with `source` the archive and `folders: [name]` (optional, additive; `config_version` stays 1). An entry that lists several folders is read as one entry per folder with ids from the folders' names and is written back that way. `archive.open_pack` takes `folder=`: only the entries under that folder are unpacked, named from the folder on, with the licence files of the bundle's top level beside them, into a cache folder of the pack's own (the archive's hash and the folder). The archive is hashed once per process for all its packs. The wizard names such a pack by a path that does not exist, `<archive>/<folder name>`; `pack_sniff.split_member` and `open_pack` know it, so `pack_sources`, the drafts and the analysis worker did not change shape. `pack_sniff.folder_in_bundle` finds the folder by name and raises `ARCH-001` for a source that is not a bundle and for a folder it does not have, naming the nearest; no new code was added. The Project page reads the listing when a pack is added (on the GUI thread, a second at most) and opens `BundleDialog` for a bundle: a row a folder with its pictures and its size, a search that keeps the ticks of the rows it hides, *Select all* and *Select none* (of the rows that are shown), the total beside *Add*; every row ticked up to twenty folders, none above. The ticked folders stand under a row for the archive, which has *Choose packs…*. Clearing a pack there asks first; the cross on one pack and the Remove button do not, as before. The licence is said once, as a chip on the archive's row. The pack card's second line says what the names (before the analysis) or the catalog's pack warnings (after it) say: *art for N engines: Godot's is used*, *Looks like a shooter pack*, *Looks like a racing pack* (the words T16.02 and T16.21 left open). The CLI has `--pack-folder` (repeatable); a bundle without it is a usage error (exit 2) that lists the folders and names the option. **Deviations and what is open.** (1) A bundle that is a folder on disk adds its ticked folders as plain folder packs, without a group and without the top-level licence. (2) A rar is never a bundle (it is not listed), and `folders` on one is refused. (3) Enter in the search field goes to the list instead of adding. (4) The Audit page's Packs tab still says *No licence file found.* for each pack of a bundle. (5) `cli.schema.json` did not change: it describes the documents the command line writes, and `folders` is the config's. (6) *Done when* was not run on the real *CuteSCKR* archive, only on the synthetic bundle of twelve. **Found:** `test_gate_m3.py::test_a_draft_resumes_after_closing_and_reopening` had failed since T16.17 (the Project page's sample button opens a list); it chooses the village from the list now. **Tests:** `test_bundle_dialog.py` (16), `test_cli.py` (5 new), the dialog in `test_design_system._dialogs`.

T16.18 (smoke sequences and integration tests). **The sequences** were there from the kinds' own cards and each lacked part of what the card lists. The shoot 'em up got the second wave (survived, not shot), the carrier (it holds, releases, takes more than one hit, leaves its capsule), a power-up that is the carrier's and is flown to with the move actions, the tally of the cleared stage (the mode's signal, the arithmetic, and the screen put up as the game shows it), and the saved run taken up again with Continue on the title screen: thirteen steps. The run and gun got `gap_is_crossed` (fifteen steps): a running jump over the first gap ahead, or, without a jump, the swing that the grapple step used to make only when a block happened to lie ahead; the camera does not go back, so a first stage with no gap left after its trap leaves the crossing to the first later stage that has one, and the log says which. The racing lap (eleven steps, as before) now counts every checkpoint where it lies and in its order, and the player, who starts last, passes a rival with the debug speed. **The codes** `SMOKE-010` to `SMOKE-012` were reserved and not defined; they have the placeholders of `SMOKE-001`, a step carries its code and the runner reports it (`step.get("code", "SMOKE-001")`), so the older kinds' failures are what they were. **Found.** The three samples generated and played at the first try and none needs a drawn stand-in. The steps were wrong in three ways: lives were compared for equality although the score gives a life at its thresholds (a swing in stage 2 read as a death, and the tally of a stage can give a life before its signal); a step that left for the title in the frame after a stage began ran before the save manager's own awaited frame, made the router busy and so kept the autosave from being written (the step waits for the slot's header to name the stage; a player cannot do this); and one and a half times the speed passes nobody on a road as wide as a car and a half, so the smoke's driver goes round a rival that is in its way. **Tests.** `test_m16_samples.py`: the samples as their kinds, with Jungle line a second time as Bionic, each with no note, the schemas of everything the run wrote and of `generate --json`, and a repeat run that moves nothing; determinism per kind; the perf rows (59, 41 and 52 s on the development machine, limit 120); the wizard walk of each kind from its sample to the Results page, marked for the kind's CI job. **Deviations.** The save and Continue step needs a second stage and passes with a note in a run of one; `test_regeneration.py` was not extended, since nothing moving and an added stage or track are held per kind in the kinds' own files. **Open.** Neither the shoot 'em up nor the run and gun keeps its weapon in the saved run (`RpgArcadeMode.weapon` is set by the racing mode only), so *Continue* begins a stage with the first weapon; Ubuntu was not run.

T16.19 (documentation, screenshots and changelogs). The user guide has *3b. The arcade kinds*: what the three share (the samples, the stand-ins, the roles, the stored plan, `stages.json`, the save) and a part a kind with its roles, its options, the picture of its plan and its controls. README, `00_START_HERE.md`, the architecture (a part *v1.4*, and its copy in `docs/spec/`), both changelogs. A run and gun had no section of the generated README (T16.08, deviation 5): `README.run_and_gun.md` and its sentence in the first paragraph (`staging.KIND_NOTES`). **Pictures:** `make_doc_screenshots.py` makes the Options box of each kind (`options_shmup.png`, `options_run_and_gun.png`, `options_racing.png`) and one picture of each kind's game (`game_*.png`): the sample is generated and its own smoke sequence plays it in a window, and `tools/doc_shots/game_shot.gd`, a steps script that hands on the kind's steps, saves the screen some frames into one of them. All pictures were made again, since the Archetype page has six cards and the sample list five entries. **Deviations.** The spec's *the six cards* is the picture of the Archetype page, which shows them; `docs/errors.md` is generated and got its three codes with T16.18. The application's and the runtime's version are still 1.2.0, as they were through M15: they are set when a release is tagged.

T16.20 (the gate of 1.4.0): the part a program can do. `SHMUP_AVAILABLE`, `RUN_AND_GUN_AVAILABLE` and `RACING_AVAILABLE` are on, so the wizard offers six kinds and five samples. Six tests that said what an arcade kind is like while it is off now switch the flags off themselves; three that asserted the flags were off assert that they are on. The French file has every new text of T16.16 to T16.22, unfinished. **Not done, and why the release is not tagged:** CI has not run on `m16` (Ubuntu, both PySide6 legs, the frozen build on three systems); the upgrade test over 1.0 to 1.3 projects with a 1.4 build; the corpus (not on this machine); and every manual row of the card (the three samples played with a gamepad, both orientations, Bionic, both steerings, a real pack of each kind, a 300-asset shooter pack through the audit). Nobody has played any of the three kinds by hand: what is known of them is what their smoke sequences and tests assert, and the difficulty of all three is untuned.

The weapon of an arcade run (2026-10-12, after T16.18, which found it). `RpgArcadeMode.weapon` was saved with the run and only the racing mode set it, so a shoot 'em up and a run and gun began every stage, and a saved game that was continued, with the first weapon. Each of the two modes now asks its ship or soldier for the weapon when the stage is cleared (its own handler of the stage's `cleared`, connected before the run's, while the ship is still there), keeps it in `weapon` (`spread:2` for a ship: the kind and the level; the name for a soldier), and gives it to the ship or soldier of the stage that is bound next: the following stage, or the stage of a loaded game. A death still costs the weapon within a stage, as before. *Continue* after the last life begins the stage again with the first weapon, as a death does, unless the soldier keeps its weapon (`keep_weapon`). A level outside the game's range is brought into it and a name that is no weapon is left out. The save is what it was: the key was there. **Tests:** one in `test_shmup.gd` and one in `test_run_and_gun.gd` (the next stage, a new game, the loaded game, Continue). Not looked at in a played game.

M17 begun on 2026-10-12, on the branch `m17`, before `v1.4.0` is tagged (the owner's word, as M16 was begun before 1.3). The farm's cards T17.01 to T17.11 were built on that one day, most of them by agents working side by side in one tree, a card or half a card each; what each reported is below, shortened. Cards that edited the same files were committed in the order they finished, so four commits say of themselves that they do not stand alone (`c67f778`, `e0bcbb8` and the first parts of T17.06 and T17.09): the tree is whole from `dae1486` on. **Nobody has seen the farm on a screen or played it**: everything below was checked headless, by unit tests and by the farm's smoke sequence.

T17.01 (detection for the farm). A strip of one row with no action and no direction is a **growth strip** (usage `growth_strip`, `stages: N`, no animations) by its name (a crop's name, `crop`, a growth word) or by its folder and shape (offered for review). The fifteen tags and `season:<name>` from the name and its nearest folder; `water` is a tool in a folder of tools only, `spring` and `fall` a season as a folder's word. Seasonal variants are grouped as colour variants are (`mark_season_variants`, `season_groups`). The pack view `farm`. `blob` reads a fence sheet (`sides16_godot_12x4`) and a drawn 2 x 2 corner sheet (`corners16_drawn_4x4`, art over nothing only). `ASSET-034` for a crop of one picture, in a farming pack only. The synthetic pack `ranch`. **A golden moved on purpose:** `multi_engine`, whose `corn_16x32_8frames` and `wheat_growth` were NPC sprites that needed review. **Not known:** which cells the real pack's fence sheets fill and the order of its 2 x 2 sheets; neither reader was run on *Super Retro Ranch*, which was not opened. Animated autotiles stay unread.

T17.02 (roles and drawn stand-ins). `CROP`, `ANIMAL`, `TOOL` in the schemas, the labels, the audit and the Mapping groups. A growth strip is a `CROP` in every pack; in a farming pack an animated sprite tagged `animal` is an `ANIMAL`, a still one tagged `tool` a `TOOL` (also from `WATER` and `WEAPON`), a crop of one picture a `CROP`, and, beyond the card, an animated `weather` overlay an `EFFECT`. `farm_kit.py`: three crops of five stages, soil and watered soil, four tools, a seed bag, a hen, an egg, a bin, a bed, rain, and `grown` (stages drawn up to a pack's single picture). A pack's own wins by its role, not by a tag. The farm's requirements (`PLAYER` and `GROUND`) and twelve sound slots. `ranch` and `multi_engine` moved again on purpose; no other golden did.

T17.03 (the calendar). `RpgCalendar` is a node a mode adds with `ensure()` and everything else finds with `current()` (null in a game without one), not an autoload. The minute is counted from the midnight before the morning (6:00 is 360, 2:00 of the night 1560). Time stands still when the tree is paused, in a dialogue, a cutscene and a battle, while the router is busy, and indoors with `stops_indoors`. A day ends in one order: the listeners of `on_night(priority, callable)` (crops 100, animals 200, shop 300, weather 400), `day_ended`, the new date at 6:00, `season_changed`, `day_began`. `calendar_math.py` and the script agree on 424 cases. The light of T12.62 takes its hour from the calendar when the game has one. Saved as the optional key `calendar` in `game_state.gd` (the card names `core/save_system.gd`, which does not exist). **Open:** with a calendar the light follows it even when the player's *Day and night* setting is off (`drives_light`); a farm morning at 6:00 is in dawn light, because the key table was left as it is.

T17.04 and T17.05 (the farmer, tools and energy; soil and crops), one commit, because the tools act on the field's cells. **The kind can be generated** (`farm` in the config and the CLI; `FARM_AVAILABLE` is off). `RpgFarmer` extends `RpgActor`, with the tile ahead and its frame; four tools as `RefCounted`s (`can_use`, `cost`, `apply`; a use that changes nothing costs nothing); a hotbar over the first ten stacks of the bag; energy 100, at zero half speed and no tool. `RpgField` keeps its cells in `flags.farm_field`, so a field whose map is not shown grows; what can be tilled is the tile's custom data; the night grows what was watered, dries the soil, withers what the next season does not have, grows some crops again, and turns bare soil to ground after three dry nights. `crop_plan.py`: every `CROP` strip a crop, days from its stages, two crops a season at least, stored as `farm` in `world_plan.json`; `crops.json` written once. A farm without areas has the plan file for its crops alone, and four places that took the file's existence for a world of areas now ask for areas. **Deviations and what is open:** the axe's item is `farm_axe` (`axe` is the starter weapon); one tile of a pack's soil terrain, so a field has no edges; `RECIPE_VERSION` of the shared recipes was not bumped, since their output for the six other kinds is what it was; a use animation of the pack plays only when the player's own sheet has it.
