T17.06 (the farm's plan and its map), in two parts. **The plan** (`farm_plan.py`): a grid of cells, whole numbers only, so an isometric view paints the same plan: the house with its room and bed, the plot (24, 96 or 160 cells), a pond or a well, the bin, a coop and a barn with yards, fences and a gate, paths, what is to be cleared, trees, the way out. Blocks are placed with a free cell round each, so the ground is one piece; the check (`VAL-031`) floods from the door all the same and takes branches and stumps for blocking. The option is `farm_size`, not `size`, which is the village's in the same section. The re-roll never fired in 6,480 plans and is tested by force. **The map** (`farm_map_recipe.gd`): `game/maps/farm.tscn`, built once, and a room a building by the recipe itself (the interior recipe cannot take a planned room). Tillable and water are the `Soil` layer's data; the can is filled at one `RpgWaterSource`, and the morning's refill is gone. A building is the pack's scene when its footprint and door are the plan's, else drawn; the planner sees only sheet objects, so the `ranch` gets a drawn house. A new game starts in the farmhouse, which took the farm out of the shared smoke sequence: its own file runs the shared steps in the village between its own. The Results picture is the plan beside the village. **Open:** `added_maps` are names only; a project generated before the second part keeps its old village and gets a farm with no door to it (`VAL-027`, `VAL-028`), reasoned from the code and not run; `farm_plan.exists` needs the map's file, so the map is planned again (the same) on every run until one succeeds.

T17.08 (the bin, the shop, the end of a day). The whole held stack goes into the bin and the last comes back out; it is sold in the night at `NIGHT_SHOP`, after the crops and the animals. The summary is shown only after a night that sold something. `RpgFarmShop`: the seeds of the season, open 9:00 to 17:00, closed on weekday 6, the day `people_plan.SHOP_CLOSED_DAY` gives the shopkeeper off (a test holds the two together). Sleeping ends the day once, fills the energy and saves into the autosave slot and the slot last used by hand; passing out is a day the clock ended: half the energy, a tenth of the money unless `pass_out_loss` is off, waking at the bed. **Decisions:** a new game has a hoe, a can, fifteen seeds and 100 money, and the sickle (50) and the axe (120) are bought; the counter pays what the bin pays; the bed asks nothing. *Working at zero ends the day* (T17.04) is not done.

T17.09 (people), in two parts. `RpgSchedule` is a child of an NPC, at work only with a calendar: the last entry whose hour has come among those of the day and the weather; the NPC is placed when its map is entered, walks on `NavigationServer2D.map_get_path` when the hour changes (no `NavigationAgent2D`) or fades where there is no path, and is away when its place is on another map. `RpgFriendship`: ten hearts of 250 points, a talk a day (20), two gifts a week by taste (80, 45, 20, -20), the optional key `friendship`, the atom `friendship:<id>>=N`. `people_plan.py`: every NPC a person, twelve at most unless the option says otherwise, three without a picture when the packs have none. **Wired in** by `people_recipes.gd`: all places are markers on the village, several spots a place so that people do not stand in each other (the plan gives one), a home spot never the cell beside a door; the demo's villager stays and the starter map's other NPCs are not placed in a farm's village; the People button is added whenever the pause menu opens; a gift is the held item through a defaulted `RpgInteraction.before`. **Open:** the village has no water, so nobody is an angler; a walking NPC does not avoid the player; fish, forage and animal products are in nobody's taste.

T17.11 (weather, the HUD, the seasons). The weather is not stored: `weather_of(seed, day index, days a season)` is an integer hash and only the seed is kept, so day N is the same before and after a save. Rain waters every field when the wet day begins. `enabled` is false until the mode's scene sets it and a smoke run is forced fair, because other cards' tests make modes by code and the smoke's earlier steps assert dry mornings: **natural weather is covered by unit tests only.** The overlay tiles one cell of the strip under a `CanvasLayer`, outdoors only; the duller light is a static tint of the outdoor ambient, white for every other kind. Swapping `TileSetAtlasSource.texture` at run time is sound in 4.7.2 (a probe); a tile set shared with an interior shows the season there too. The season's music through the farm's slots. The HUD's clock, date, weather and money. The farm's section of the generated README. **Open:** the season swap ran in a unit test only (the sample has no seasonal sheets); no sound for a harvest or the morning.

T17.10 (fishing and foraging). Forage places are chosen from the stored `farm.map` by `forage_plan.py` (six to ten free cells near the edge) and written as markers; what lies where on a morning is derived, and only what was picked today is a flag. The rod is a fifth tool, sold when the farm has a pond and `fishing` is on (not a row of `EXTRA_STOCK`, which is unconditional); a cast, a wait of one to four real seconds, a bite, a bar. Fish and forage are a table in data (`forage.json`, written once). The kit draws a rod, three fish and four things to find whenever the packs lack them, so a plain pack as a farm has 23 `ASSET-031` notes where it had 15. **With it, a fix:** in a farming pack a character strip named after another character strip of its folder and a tool word (`farmer_hoe_down`) is unused with `use_anim:<tool>` and `of:<id>`, not a person; the `ranch` as a farm then has no villager of its own. Putting those frames on the farmer is not done.

T17.07 (animals). The herd is rows in `flags.farm_animals`, so the night works on animals whose map is not shown and nothing walks between yard and stall: the animals of the shown map are made from the data when it is entered and when the hour (18:00) or the weather changes their place. One portion of hay feeds a house for the day; the pail was dropped; quality is an item (`egg_large` from five hearts), because a stack carries only its item and count; animals are walked through, so a hen cannot block the coop's door. An animal is sold as a key item that never reaches the bag (a stock row may name `deliver`). Cut grass is hay through `RpgField.cleared`. `animal_plan.py`: the kind from the words of the asset's id, a variant an animal and a colour with its walk and its sleep sheet together. A farm without animal houses gains only inert code. **Limit:** a coop on a pack that has only cows stays empty.

Seen while the farm's cards were tested, not confirmed: two smoke runs of a farm at the same time can fail each other's save steps, probably because every generated farm of the tests has one project name and so one `user://` folder. And two traps of the strict GDScript settings that three agents each met: a `Variant` cannot be cast with `as` (assign it to a typed variable), and nodes that may have been freed with their map must be kept untyped and asked `is_instance_valid` first.

The full run after the farm's cards (2026-10-12, Windows): unit and GUI with the slow tests 2595, GDScript 814, integration 205 passed and one failed, perf 9. The one: four runtime scripts of the farm had `res://game/data/...` as constants, which the runtime may not (`test_runtime_scripts_do_not_depend_on_game_content_paths`); the mode's recipe now gives the mode the folder (`data_dir`) and the mode tells the data classes before any reads, as the arcade mode is given its stages' path.

T17.12 (isometric packs: one picture a tile), written from the 23 Kenney packs in `D:\kenney assets\2D assets` (CC0; their pictures were opened to measure, none is in the repository). **The measurement the card asked for:** *Tile size* in a `Tile information.txt` is the diamond with the side of its block under it: *Medieval Town* says 180 x 115 and *Modular Roads* 180 x 125 for one diamond of 180 x 104, which is what *Minigolf*'s Tiled settings call the map tile size. Two whole tiles laid side by side show no open pixel from 104 up to 148, so laying cannot find the height: it is the slope of the flat tiles' upper edges times the width, and laying is the check. The footprint is Tiled's *Map tile size* (or an isometric Tiled map of the pack) when there is one, else the text's width with the height measured, else both measured; `TILE-010` for a pack that says isometric and has no flat tile. A tile is the new usage `iso_tile` with `projection`, `canvas`, `footprint`, `rise` and `origin` (the tile does not always rest on the bottom of its canvas: *Medieval Town* has 13 free rows under it, so the spec's sentence about the origin was wrong). An object keeps `sprite` with `stands_on`. Turns are assets with `turn`, grouped through `variant_of` (the audit says *Variants (4)*; the word *Turns* is the GUI's). A car's or a boat's turns are one strip in the work folder with `headings` 4 or 8. `joins` from capitals glued to a name several files share. `Angle` beside `Isometric` is unused; a `<TextureAtlas>` sheet beside its pictures is unused; a folder that is one archive beside previews is opened as it. `TILE-011` for isometric beside square ground. **Read wrongly or not at all:** the 480 civilian cars of *Tiles Vehicles* (numbered 000 to 015 in no order: four service cars are read); *Tiles Buildings* is measured at 99 x 50 from its small pieces and is probably 132 x 66; the characters of *Miniature Dungeon* and *Prototype* (which the spec said no pack had) stay single pictures; a few packs get a stray *looks like a racing pack* from the old word counts. The audit's diamond grid editor and the pack card's words are not done.

T17.13 (the isometric TileSet). `projection` in the config (`top_down`, left out of the JSON, or `isometric`), `CAT-002` for a kind without the view, for packs without isometric ground and for a generated project whose projection changed; `ISOMETRIC_AVAILABLE` is off, the CLI has `--projection`. `iso_atlas.py` packs the tiles of a pack into atlas pictures in the work folder; the cell of every tile is kept in `id_allocations.iso_atlases` and never changes (a new tile gets the next cell, one that left leaves its cell empty). **Godot, by a probe with a renderer:** the isometric shape with `TILE_LAYOUT_DIAMOND_DOWN`; a cell (x, y) has its middle at `((x - y + 1) * w/2, (x + y + 1) * h/2)`; `texture_origin` is the catalog's origin plus where the picture lies in its cell less half the cell, one rule for a flat tile, a block and a tall tile; an alternative tile has no region of its own, so a turn is a tile of its own (`tile_for`, `piece_for`). FULL collision is the diamond; a raised block with the role `GROUND` does not block (height is not modelled). Tiles on another diamond are left out (`TILE-012`). One rule for the two views (`catalog.fits_projection`, `CatalogReader.fits`): a project seen from above uses nothing isometric and an isometric one fills no role from a sheet of squares. **Found:** T17.12 had left the placeholders and the kits asking only whether any asset had the role, so a top-down game on an isometric pack got no placeholder ground and stopped with `ASSET-010`. **Open:** `piece_for`'s second rule takes a turn for a quarter turn clockwise, which was not checked on a real pack.

T17.14 (isometric maps and actors), in two parts. **The first:** no recipe turns a cell into a pixel by itself any more. `builder/lib/map_geo.gd` is the seam: what the recipes called for a project seen from above, `RpgIso` for an isometric one, with `iso_math.py` and 2,443 cases between the two. A tile, where a recipe needs one number, is one step from a diamond to the next (`RpgIso.unit`), not the diamond's width. An object on several cells is cut in two at its nearest corner and the halves sort apart. `iso_controls: screen | grid`; the *grid-bound movement of the turn-based kind* that the card names does not exist in the runtime (the player walks freely and steps are counted by distance). `iso_kit.py` draws a hero, a villager and an enemy. The smoke steps that pressed a key and read a pixel ask the map (`smoke_map.gd`). **The second, the farm:** the plans are the same grid; the `Soil` layer is diamonds drawn in GDScript; the tile ahead is `RpgIso.faced_cell`; stand-ins are boxes on their diamonds (`RpgFarmIso`); the kit's square soil, bin and bed are not drawn for this view, since the square soil made a 16 px tile set in an isometric project. **Seen on real packs:** *Medieval Town* with *Nature* passes its 35 steps, with thin dark lines along diamond edges at zoom 0.44, the first flat ground tile as its ground, no house (no building has the role) and no walls painted though the catalog has 60, causes not found; *Miniature Farm* passes the farm's whole sequence with its corn as crops, a drawn house and a well, an interaction prompt too small to read and a drawn hero too large on 256 px diamonds. **Open:** imported maps and the editor dock count squares; rooms are a floor with no walls; the farm's preview picture is a flat grid; `interact` reaches by the screen's direction while `use` acts on the framed cell.

T17.15 (isometric racing). A probe first: under a parent whose transform is the shear from squares to diamonds, drawing works and physics does not (`move_and_collide` takes its motion in global coordinates; the body stopped at (95.3, 101.4) where the plan has (95, 0)). So nothing of the flat world is transformed and `car_math`, `car_physics`, the race and the rivals are untouched: a lap on diamonds is the lap from above step for step and 0 px apart. `RpgRaceView` puts only the pictures on the diamonds (the car's `Visual`, pickups, weapons, the camera, the stick of absolute steering). East in the plan is `SE` of a car in eight headings. The road is laid from pieces that name their sides, one a cell of the loop (`MapGeo.paint_sides`), else drawn. **What the card did not foresee, the scale:** a road piece is a street a car of its pack half fills and the plan's road is three cars wide, so the scale is chosen for the car's picture. No isometric car and no people are drawn for a race: a pack without a car gets the kit's sheet from above, which looks flat. **Seen on *Vector Roads Base* with *Tiles Vehicles*:** it looks like an isometric racer, but the pack's corner is round and the plan's square, so a car is seen on the verge at the outside of a corner, and some sedans are catalogued as decoration and stand parked on the hedges. Nobody drove it by hand.

Seen four times now, by four agents, and never proved: two smoke runs at one time fail each other's save steps (`mutate_then_load`, `defeat_policy`), most likely through one `user://smoke_saves` for projects of one name. A run by itself has not shown it.

T17.16 (the wizard: the Farm card, the view, options and previews). Seven cards: rows of four from 1280 px on, rows of three below that with a third row that scrolls, one column in a narrow window; the Farm card with its drawing and its flow. A line under the cards for a farming pack and for an isometric one, with its diamond. A **View** choice (*From above*, *Isometric*) and, with the isometric view, a **Pad** choice, in the box of each kind that has the view (`ViewChoice`: the *Starter map*, the *Farm* box, the *Tracks*): chosen for the packs (`options.default_projection`: isometric when they have isometric ground and no square ground), refused with the run's own reason when they have no isometric ground, fixed once the project exists. A farm's options are a `FarmBox` above the village's (the starter map's box, renamed, without the two counts a farm does not read), with the planned farm as a picture on squares or on the project's diamonds, a legend and the plan in words; `days_per_season` and `day_length` are new options of the config, the schema and the mode; the presets *Cosy* and *Short year*; a farm's plan in a dry run. The audit says *Turns* and *Seasons* where it said *Variants*, the grid editor draws and edits the diamond of an isometric picture (`footprint` of a tile, `stands_on` of an object, one undo step with the grid), the pack card says a farming pack and isometric tiles, the Mapping page offers an isometric project only what fits its view and says when a car drawn from above will look flat. **Deviations.** The box has no combat option and no cave: nothing of a farm reads `combat`, and the feature is hidden for the kind (`options.FARM_HIDDEN`). The *loss for passing out* is a check box. *Added maps* are names, and the box says so. The picture of an isometric race is drawn from above. **Open:** the *Pad* choice is shown for an isometric race, which does not use it; the reason under a *View* that cannot be isometric is the command line's sentence; the View choice is shown, disabled, for every project of the four kinds, also one of square packs.

T17.17 (two sample packs). *Green acre* (`sample_pack_farm`, 174 KB) and *Stone isle* (`sample_pack_iso`, 160 KB), both drawn by `tools/build_sample_pack.py`, CC0, each with its profile and its golden; the welcome menu lists seven, and a sample carries the view it is tried in (`Sample.projection`, `project_model.try_as`). *Green acre*: a farmer with walk and tool strips, four crops as growth strips, soil and watered soil, grass in four season folders, a hen and a cow that walk and sleep, five tools, three villagers, rain, four loops and eight sounds; its house, coop and barn are objects of one sheet (`objects/farmyard.png`), because the farm's planner sees objects and not single pictures. *Stone isle*: one picture a tile on a diamond of 40 x 22 with its `Tile information.txt`, grounds, water, a path and walls in pieces that name their sides, a house on four diamonds, people seen from the front; it is tried as a turn-based game in the isometric view. Each generates as its kind and passes its whole sequence without a note. **With it, a fix:** the fishing step of the farm's smoke run pressed the button anew on every frame and could cast again as the fish was landed. **Open:** the farmer's tool strips are in the pack and are not played (T17.10); without its profile the slime of *Stone isle* is an NPC (a folder of characters), which only somebody who adds the pack by hand meets.

T17.18 (smoke sequences and integration tests). The farm's sequence ends with `save_and_load`: a real save, a night and changes to the field, the money, the bag, the energy, a friendship and the herd, a load, and the field compared cell by cell with the date and the rest. Isometric has no sequence of its own: the shared, the action RPG's and the racing steps run over *Stone isle* as they are (*Stone isle* as an action RPG, *Dirt cup* on *Stone isle*'s diamonds). Determinism, regeneration (a crop and an animal kind added to a pack are added to the plan, a stored farm is not planned again, the projection cannot be changed), the schemas, the wizard walks of both samples, perf rows, the markers `farm` and `isometric` with a CI job each. **Faults it found and mended.** *Two smoke runs at one time* (seen four times before, never proved): proved three times of three with two projects of one name; Godot keeps `user://` by the project's name, so the runs read and replaced each other's saves. A run is now named on the command line (`--rpg-smoke-run=<id>`) and saves into `user://smoke_saves_<id>`; `tests/integration/test_smoke_two_at_once.py` holds it. The dark kerb along isometric paths and the footing under far walls (a piece laid over the ground showed the earth under it: such pieces are laid in the place of the ground tile). A pack read through its profile lost its diamond. The farm's planner sees a pack's coop and barn whatever building the starter names. Farm rooms are boards and plaster (`room_recipe.gd`), not the grass and hedge of the maps outside. The cells an isometric building leaves of its planned footprint are blocked. Small diamonds are measured within what their staircase allows. **Left by the card:** a slime in a folder of characters is an NPC; the ranch with the wizard's starter failed `keys_and_switches` (mended below). The two CI jobs have not run.

After T17.18, three things before the gate (2026-10-13). **The ranch with the wizard's starter.** Reproduced: with `mapping.default_starter` the village has the pack's own house, and one of the planned people is at home at (200, 152), exactly three tiles above the middle of the map, which is where the shared step `keys_and_switches` has always put its locked gate; the interaction takes the nearest thing that answers `interact`, the two were equally near, and the farmer talked to her twice while the gate stayed shut. The product was not at fault; a generation failed all the same. The step now takes, for the gate and for the lever, the first of a few places near the usual one where no other node that answers `interact` is within a tile and a quarter (`_spot_to_reach`); the first is the usual place, so a map where nothing stands there is played as before. A/B on the top-down sample as a turn-based game, before and after: 530 files, the tile sets, maps, scenes, catalog and `build_state.json` the same (33 scenes differ in `unique_id`s only); what differs is the steps script itself, the results and reports, and `generated/i18n/strings_en.tres`, whose entries Godot writes in another order from run to run. `test_the_ranch_with_the_starter_the_wizard_chooses_passes_its_whole_sequence` generates it and holds that somebody still stands on the gate's place. **The same config showed a second thing:** the starter's ground was `ranch.autotiles.n01_soil_tilled`, the sheet the reader is surest of, so the village of a farming pack was laid with tilled soil (a unit test had noted it and worked round it). `mapping.best_for` ranks tilled and watered soil last for `GROUND`: they are the field's, and a map's ground only when the packs have no other. **The farm's sound slots were never played** (T17.02 defined seven, the Mapping page offers them, *Green acre* ships sounds for them; only `sfx_sell` was asked for, and `sfx_water` and `sfx_harvest` by the rod and the forage): `RpgFarmSounds` (`archetypes/farm/sounds.gd`) plays `sfx_hoe`, `sfx_water`, `sfx_plant`, `sfx_harvest`, `sfx_animal` and `sfx_rooster` where the thing happens; the axe has no slot. One GDScript test (`test_the_work_of_the_farm_asks_for_its_sound_slots`); not listened to.

T17.20 (the gate of 1.5.0): the part a program can do. `FARM_AVAILABLE` and `ISOMETRIC_AVAILABLE` are on, so the wizard offers seven kinds, seven samples and the View choice. Three tests that asserted the flags were off assert that they are on (`test_farm_config`, `test_iso_tileset`, `test_m17_farm`), one is the test that both are on since the gate (`test_archetype_page`), and two that describe the samples without the two of M17 switch the flags off themselves (`test_sample_pack`); the tests that turned the flags on still do. **Goldens:** six moved, and no more: the welcome view in both themes and both sizes (its three cards are 4 px taller with a menu of seven samples behind the button *Choose a sample*, and what is under them 4 px lower; why the menu's length reaches the button's height was not looked into) and the Options page at the default size in both themes (the scroll bar's handle is shorter: the page is longer by the View choice, which is below the fold). The Archetype page's four did not move: its seventh card is in the row below the picture. The French file has every text of M17, unfinished. **Not done, and why the release is not tagged:** CI has not run on `m17` at all (Ubuntu, both PySide6 legs, the frozen build on three systems, the two new jobs); the upgrade test over 1.0 to 1.4 projects; the corpus (not on this machine); every manual row of the card. Nobody has played the farm or an isometric game by hand. The versions are still 1.2.0, and no tag exists for any release.

T17.19 (documentation, screenshots and changelogs). The user guide has *3c. Farm* and *3d. The isometric view* (trying the sample, what rpggen looks for in a pack, the roles, the options with their picture, playing, what is the user's, the known limits, the command line), and the places that counted kinds and samples say seven. README, `00_START_HERE.md`, the architecture (a part *v1.5*, and its copy), the status (*What is left before 1.5.0*), both changelogs. The runtime's 1.5.0 entry, written by eleven agents a card at a time, was put in order (compatibility; the calendar and schedules; the farm by part; the isometric view; the smoke runner) and its contradictions removed: it said one new save key and has two (`calendar`, `friendship`), that the mode fills the energy and the can every morning (it stopped, twice over), and that the mode knows four tools (five). **The generated READMEs were read against the code** (a second reader checked every sentence of the farm's and the racing one's isometric part): the farm's said *nothing to fight unless you switch Combat on* (there is no such option), told the user to add tiles to `generated/tilesets/farm_soil.tres` (it is made again), called the bin, the bed and the trough ordinary nodes of the maps (two are generated scenes, the trough is made by the game), gave an animal's price as the ticket's value (it is `cost`) and `feed` as an item (it is a table), and did not say how a gift is given, that passing out saves, that these saves ignore the autosave setting, that the light follows the calendar, where a new game begins, or that the sign tells the weather; the racing one said eight pictures where four are read too. **Pictures:** `make_doc_screenshots.py` makes `options_farm.png` (the Farm box of *Green acre*), `options_isometric.png` (the Starter map box of *Stone isle* with its View and Pad and the map on diamonds), `game_farm.png` (the step `harvest`) and `game_isometric.png` (the step `npc_dialogue`); `game_shot.gd` takes the steps of a kind whose sequence is two scripts, and takes its picture the first time a step of the wanted name is played. All pictures were made again. **What the guide says that the spec did not:** no combat and no cave; the sickle, the axe and the rod are bought; the bed asks nothing; no pail; added maps are names; the tool strips are not played; an isometric race is previewed from above; the dock and imported maps count squares. `docs/errors.md` was generated again and did not change: the six codes of M17 were in it.

The full run after the gate's part (2026-10-13, Windows, the flags on): lint, format and types clean; unit and GUI with the slow tests 2850 passed and 3 skipped (16 minutes; `test_on_line_receives_lines_before_the_process_exits` passed in it, failed once in a run of four test files afterwards and passed three times alone: as flaky as it was, and not looked into); GDScript 866; integration 251 passed and 7 skipped (export templates, and the upgrade tests that need a `v1.1.0` tag or an `m13` branch this checkout does not have), in 1 h 54 min; perf 11. Nothing was run on Ubuntu.

The ten faults the documentation of M17 listed as wrong and not fixed (2026-10-13, after T17.20). **1, the dialogue's second choice was under the screen**: an old fault of every kind, not the isometric project's. `ui_recipes.gd` anchors the dialogue box's panel with `PRESET_BOTTOM_WIDE` and offsets for 84 px; `set_anchors_and_offsets_preset` sets no grow direction, so a panel whose minimum size is larger (a line with choices) took the room downward. Measured in a `SubViewport` of 640 x 360 with three choices: with 8 px text (a pack of 16 px tiles) the panel was 128 high from y 268, its second and third choice under the screen; with 16 px (*Stone isle*) 172 high, all three under it. Fixed in the runtime (`dialogue_box.gd` sets `grow_vertical = GROW_DIRECTION_BEGIN` on `%Panel` in `_ready`), not in the recipe, so `dialogue_box.tscn` is byte for byte what it was and no `RECIPE_VERSION` moved; a line without choices is where it was. `test_dialogue.gd::test_every_choice_of_a_line_is_on_the_screen` builds the recipe's scene and asserts the panel and three choices inside the screen at both sizes of text. (`root.free()` on the unsaved root right after `SceneUtil.save_scene` ended the headless run without a word; the test leaves the root alone, as `test_credits.gd` does.) A box with more choices than the screen is high is not scrolled: left. **2, the Pad for a race**: `ViewState.pad` (the model's: `config.ISO_CONTROLS_KINDS` are `turn_based`, `arpg`, `farm`) hides the Pad in the `RacingBox`; `config.iso_controls_for` makes `from_json` read the key as `screen` for a race and `to_json` not write it, so a race's config never carries it, whoever set it; the schema still accepts it. The dry run never printed it. **3, the View row**: `options.has_isometric_art` (a pack with `view:isometric` or a picture whose `projection` is isometric, whatever its role or status); without it `view_state()` is `ViewState(False)` and the row is not there, for a new project and for one that exists and is seen from above. With isometric pictures and no isometric ground the row is shown, disabled, with `VIEW_NO_GROUND_TEXT` (the wizard's sentence; `iso_atlas.NO_GROUND_REASON` stays the run's and the command line's). Of the six goldens that were made again at T17.20, two were the Options page's (default size, both themes): they are again byte for byte what they were before the row existed; the other four were the welcome view's (fault 9). **4, the README's opening**: `staging.opening(archetype)` fills `{opening}` of the template; `RPG_OPENING` is the old sentence, so a turn-based and an action README are byte for byte what they were (rendered before and after and compared); the metroidvania (*a small world of rooms seen from the side*, no longer *a small map*), the shoot 'em up, the run and gun, the race and the farm have their own in `KIND_OPENINGS`, three lines changed in each. `test_readme_openings.py`. **5, the slime**: `classifier.classify` gave NPC 1.0 (the folder word `characters` 0.5 + the walking sheet 0.5) against ENEMY 0.9 (`slime` in the file's name). The words that say only *somebody* (`character`, `characters`) no longer speak for a villager when the file's own name has a word of ENEMY or BOSS. The same rule for a name that says PLAYER was tried and left out: it changes no role (the hero wins by the table's order) and moved the hero's confidence from 0.4 to 0.5 in five more goldens (`audio_basic`, `basic16`, `platformer`, `zipped`, `sample_pack_side`). As it is, one golden moves, `sample_pack_iso` (the slime: ENEMY, 0.474); `_iso_overrides` no longer sets the role, and the pack and its profile, built again, are byte for byte what they were (the profile exports the effective role). **6**: the chooser's tooltips count neither kinds nor samples. **7, the axe**: no new slot. `tools/axe.gd` plays `sfx_hoe`; the sickle played `sfx_harvest` for whatever it cut and the rod `sfx_water` and `sfx_harvest` already; `sounds.gd` and 02_SCHEMAS have the rule, and *Green acre* did not change. **8**: `README.racing.md` says which nodes of a track may be missing (the recipes' `_keep` frees an empty layer). **9, the welcome cards**: no fault. With two, five and seven samples the cards and what is under them are in the same place; the cards have been 160 px high (`CARD_HEIGHT`) since `39d6c11` (M16: `CardButton.set_floor_height`; before it the stylesheet's `min-height` took the floor away and a card was as high as its content, 156 px). The goldens of T15.23 were not made again then because the difference stayed under the limits (16 % of the worst block against 20 %); T17.20 made every golden again and so wrote the four. `test_the_welcome_cards_are_as_high_with_seven_samples_as_with_five_or_two`. **10, removing a crop**: the rule stays (the user's file, then what only the planned file has; `stages.json` of the arcade kinds untouched). An entry `{"id": ..., "removed": true}` in the user's file keeps the id out in `RpgCropData`, `RpgAnimalData` (kinds, not houses) and `RpgForage` (things and fish); `RpgFriendship` reads `people.json` alone, so a deleted person was always gone, and it takes the mark as nobody. The four schemas have `Removed`; the farm's README and the guide say how. **Also:** `test_on_line_receives_lines_before_the_process_exits` asserted that a line printed to stderr reaches `on_line` and the log before a line printed to stdout right after it; the two pipes are drained by two threads and nothing orders them. It now asserts what `_run` does guarantee: every line arrives once, a stream keeps its order, and the line printed 1.5 s earlier is first. **Pictures:** `game_isometric.png` (three choices on the screen), `options_farm.png` and `options_racing.png` (70 px lower: no View row), `wizard_options.png` (the scroll handle), `wizard_results.png` (the id of its run, another every time). **A/B** of generated projects, the code of `03ba79d` against this, from the bare packs on the command line, compared with `unique_id=` and `uid://` taken out (Godot gives every node another number on every run): *the village* (`sample_pack`, turn-based, from above), 535 files, differs in `addons/rpg_runtime/ui/dialogue_box.gd` and the runtime's `CHANGELOG.md` and in nothing else but the records of the run (the reports, the ledger's hashes of those two files, the results with their times): every scene, the catalog, the config and the README are what they were. *Dirt cup* as a race, 548 files: the same two and `README.md` (its first paragraph). *Stone isle* as an isometric turn-based game without its profile: the slime is the pack's enemy now (`game/data/enemies/slime.tres`, `generated/scenes/enemies/slime.tscn`), where it was a third villager and an enemy was drawn (`generated/iso_kit/enemy.png` and its scenes are gone), so the catalog, the two maps, the encounter, the credits and the character index follow.

The full run after the ten faults (2026-10-13, Windows, each suite once, one after the other): lint, format and types clean; unit and GUI with the slow tests 2873 passed and 3 skipped (15 min 50 s; `test_template_only_has_known_placeholders` and `test_the_readme_lists_the_custom_slots` each failed one earlier run: the template's `{archetype}` became `{opening}`, and `archetype` stays a placeholder a template may use); GDScript 872 (866 and the six new ones); integration 251 passed and 7 skipped, as before, in 1 h 56 min; perf 11. Nothing was run on Ubuntu.

T17.27 (the editor dock on diamonds). The plugin asks the map through `RpgIso`, the runtime's arithmetic, as `world_repair.gd` did since T17.14: the builder's `MapGeo` and `IsoTileset` are not installed where the plugin could preload them (a project has them under `generated/builder/`, the template under `builder/`), and the plugin already names runtime classes (`RpgUtil`, `RpgTriggerArea`). `Placement.diamond_of(root)` is `RpgIso.footprint_of`: zero for a map of squares, and then every function does the line it did. **Placement.** On diamonds `plan` puts the instance on `RpgIso.cell_position(root, RpgIso.cell_at(root, mouse))`, the middle of the diamond under the mouse, which is `MapGeo.center` and so where the builder stands an actor, a marker and the origin of an object's picture; an object of several diamonds needs nothing more, because its scene's origin is the middle of its cell nearest the camera and it is already cut in two halves that sort (T17.14). The plan says `cell` and `cells` (`Placement.cells_under`, the loop of `IsoTileset.cells_under`, fed by the `stands_on` the object recipe writes on the scene; a test holds the two equal). The parent, the flip and the entity id are untouched code. **Snapping.** Every *Snap* choice but *Off* means the diamond: a grid of 8 or 32 px has no meaning there, and the dock's *Tile* was 16 in an isometric project anyway (its tile set is not called `overworld`). The picture follows the mouse freely **while** it is dragged, on diamonds as on squares: the dock never saw a drag, it mends what the editor dropped. **Drop-to-floor** has no meaning on diamonds and nothing is done: it is asked of a side-view room, and no room is isometric. **Occupied cells.** The dock on squares neither refuses nor warns, so nor does it on diamonds; the status line now says the diamond. **The world tools** take `RpgIso.unit` of the map being edited for a tile (they took the catalog's `overworld`, 16): a trigger area is its diamond, a gate gets a `Shape` child of its diamond as `WorldPlan.add_gate` gives the builder's (and `add_lock` now gives a child its owner), a switch keeps the square it makes, a zone with nothing selected is the outline of 6 x 6 diamonds. **The repair.** T17.14 had made it count cells; its door was still a square of one step. The actions of an isometric map now carry its diamond and `build` gives the door `RpgIso.cell_shape`, new in the runtime: `RpgIso.diamond` moved to the middle of its cell, no arithmetic of its own, held equal to `MapGeo.cell_shape` and `IsoTileset.diamond` by a test. It is not in the table shared with `iso_math.py`: Python never builds a shape. **Tests.** `test_placement_iso.gd` (nine, on maps built with the fixture's tile set: the four quadrants, the corners and the sides of a diamond, across a side, snapping, a layer and a parent that are moved, objects of 1 x 1 to 3 x 2 against `cells_under`, `building_position`, the footprint's outline and `sort_keys`, actors and markers with their ids, the tools, the repair's door against `add_exit_door`, the Fix undone, and a map of squares whose plan has the keys it had). `Placement.apply` takes the editor's undo manager, which no unit test has, so its undo runs in `test_m17_isometric.py`: a test-only plugin (`tests/integration/dock_probe/`) is put into a copy of *Stone isle*, the editor is started with `--headless --editor`, and in it the project's own dock places eight things (drops through `_fix_dropped`, double clicks through `place_new`), undoes and redoes each through the scene's history, saves, and adds a trigger area, a gate and a switch; then the game plays its 35 steps with them in it. **Looked at:** a picture of the running game with eleven things placed round the house of *Stone isle*: each stands where its diamond is by the arithmetic and beside what the builder placed, a fisher north-west of the old house has his legs behind its roof, a pine south of a placed house is drawn over it. The grass has no lines, so no diamond's edge is seen in the picture. **Nobody dropped anything with a mouse:** the 2D view's drag and drop was not run, only what the dock does after it. **Three old faults of the dock on any map,** found by the first run of it in an editor and left by this card, because it says a map of squares is placed in as it was: Ctrl+Z after a double click logged an engine error for each property set back (the node was out of the tree by then; the result was right); a double click was saved with `type=` and a copy of its scene root's properties (`place_new` instantiated without `GEN_EDIT_STATE_INSTANCE`), so a rebuilt scene did not reach them; a second drop of one asset was renamed `@StaticBody2D@…` by the reparent. **Mended since, on both views** (2026-10-14, `dock/placement.gd` only): (1) an undo runs its operations in the order they were added, not backwards (probed with a plain `UndoRedo` and in the editor's scene history: `do 1, do 2, undo 1, undo 2`), so `apply` adds the undo of the properties first and the one that takes a new node out of the map, or moves a dropped one back, last; (2) `place_new` instantiates through `Placement.instance_for_edit` (`GEN_EDIT_STATE_INSTANCE`), and a double click is saved as `instance=ExtResource(...)` with its position and entity id, as a drop is; (3) the plan has `name` and `old_name`: a node that moves to another parent, or a new one, gets `Placement.free_name`, the name the editor gives a second copy (`treeoak2`, `house3`, `tree_1` then `tree_2`: Node's serial names with no separator), set after the reparent and set back after the reparent of the undo. The probe now also says the order of an action's operations and the names; `tests/integration/dock_probe_run.py` holds the three on Stone isle (ten placements, two of them second copies) and on the top-down sample (`test_m8_sample.py::test_the_dock_places_on_the_squares_of_the_sample`, six placements): no `not inside tree` in the editor's log, every placed node saved as `instance=` with no `type=`, `script` or `metadata/`, readable distinct names kept through undo and redo. Unit tests: `free_name`, the plan's name on squares and on diamonds, and a double-clicked instance packed as an instance (a plain `instantiate()` packs as `StaticBody2D`). A map placed in before keeps its copies. A/B of the top-down sample before and after: only the dock's own scripts differ. The generated README says *snaps to the grid*, which holds on diamonds, and was not touched.

T17.23 (added maps are built). A row of *Added maps* is `{name, kind}`: a **forest** or a **beach**, the kind from the row, else from the words of the name, else a forest. `added_map_plan.py` plans each on a grid of cells like the farm's (a forest of 28 x 22 thick with trees, a clearing with a path to it; a beach of 30 x 20 with the sea three to five cells deep along one side and a place to cast from), from the farm's seed and the map's number, with a check of its own that `farm_plan.check` runs (the way in at the edge; nothing on something else; every forage place, the clearing and the place to fish from walked to from the way in), and the entries are stored as `farm.map.added`: a new row plans one more and the stored ones stay byte for byte, whatever the row, the seed or the packs say later. `recipes/farm/added_maps_recipe.gd` builds `game/maps/farm_map_<n>.tscn` once each through `MapGeo`, so on squares and on diamonds, with the `Soil` layer and a `Field` (the axe, the sickle and the rod work there), the farm's own things (`FarmMapRecipe.add_things`, now with the id of the map in what is remembered), `Forage/Spot<n>`, and the metadata `farm_kind`; a beach's sea carries the `water` data and its root says `farm_water: sea`. A beach has things and fish of its own (`maps: ["beach"]`, `water: ["sea"]`; the packs' by the tags `beach` and `sea`, else four drawn ones that the kit adds only for a farm with a beach), appended to a stored forage plan when the farm gets its first beach; `fishing` is true with a pond **or** a beach. **Three decisions.** (1) *Not areas of T13.20.* The card says the maps are joined through the areas; they are maps of the farm's own recipe instead, and what they take from the areas is the shape of a door pair (`MapGeo.add_exit_door`, a spawn `from_<x>`) and the repair's rule that a door added later stands on the floor beside the wall and not in it. A farm's plan file has no areas, and four places were taught that such a file is no world of areas (T17.05); as areas the added maps would have made a farm one, with the area recipe's biomes, enemies and chests, the second map's handling and the repair's bookkeeping of map hashes, and an area knows nothing of a field, of forage or of what a tool clears. (2) *How a farm that exists gains a door.* A map that exists is never written again, so the door cannot be a node of `farm.tscn`, and a gap cannot be opened in its edge. The farm's plan therefore sets aside **trails** when it is planned, whether or not it has added maps (`farm.map.trails`, eight at most: a cell beside the edge and the cell one further in, both walked to from the house, chosen after everything else with dice of their own so that the farm is cell for cell the farm it was, never a place where a thing to forage lies, the ones no tree stands before first), and the farm's map instances one generated scene, `generated/scenes/farm/trails.tscn`, which the recipe writes on every build: for every added map a door, a spawn and a patch of worn ground with an arrow. A row added later changes that scene and the plan's `added`, builds its map, and leaves every file under `game/maps` as it was; a test holds the bytes. The project tools' map graph walks the instantiated tree, so the doors are doors to the validator too. (3) *The cave* is a kind the table knows and does not build: a row of it is refused with a sentence (`an added map of the kind cave is not built yet`), the wizard does not offer it, and no name chooses it. **What a farm from before this card gets:** its plan has no trails and its map no `Trails` node, so its rows stay names as they were, the generation says so in a note, and nothing fails; there is no release with such a farm. **Not done.** The people's schedules name no place on an added map: a person is one NPC of the village's scene and is *away* when its place is on another map, so an angler on the beach would need an NPC of the same person on the beach, which is no small addition. The preview draws the added maps beside the farm and the legend still names only what the farm has. **A/B** (HEAD against HEAD with this card alone, *Green acre* without added maps and the top-down sample as a turn-based game): of the farm's 720 files 647 are the same and 54 scenes differ in `unique_id`s only; what differs is `farm.tscn` (the `Trails` instance and the numbers of its external resources), `world_plan.json` (`trails`, `added: []`), `build_state.json` (the hashes of the farm's maps and of the minimaps, whose files are the same), the scripts that changed, the config (the path of the pack) and the report, and three files are new (the recipe, its uid, the trails' scene); the turn-based game differs in the copy of `build.gd`. No golden moved. **Seen** (six pictures, in a window, during the smoke steps): the forest reads as one on both packs (trees close together, a path from the gap to a clearing with flowers, a mushroom lying); the beach reads as one (sand, a blue sea with a shore that wanders, shells, tufts, rocks); **wrong or poor:** the drawn sand is laid in rows of two colours and shows as bands, on diamonds plainly; the cell of the way in is the farm's ground, without the forest's shade or the beach's sand; in the first picture of the farm the mark of the trail lay hidden under the crown of a tree two cells south of it, which is why the trails nobody hides now come first (the first trail is still hidden in 4 of 900 plans, the fourth in 281); on diamonds the mark is plain to see, and what the kit draws to forage is as large as the farmer's head, as it was on the farm. **Open:** a gap in the farm's edge instead of a mark; the legend; the people; nobody walked there by hand.

T17.25 (festivals and birthdays). **The plan.** `event_plan.py` plans one festival a season and stores it as `farm.events` (the orchestrator plans it last, after the forage, since summer's festival depends on whether the farm can fish); the seed changes nothing of it. **The day** is a Friday (weekday 4, never the shop's Sunday): of the middle week in spring and summer, of the last in autumn and winter, so 12, 12, 26, 26 of 28 days, 5, 5, 12, 12 of 14 and 5 of every season of 7. The day is planned for the length the project has then; `days_per_season` is read on every generation, so the game folds a day a shorter season no longer has into it week by week (`RpgFarmEvents.day_in`), which keeps it a Friday, and the stored plan is never touched. **Birthdays** are `people_plan.with_birthdays`: whoever has none gets one (the season with the fewest so far, a day that is no festival's, not spring 1 and nobody else's), whoever has one keeps it, so the people a project stored before the card get theirs through the same function and nothing else of them changes (a unit test and the integration test hold this). Spring 1 is left out because the first morning of a game is where the smoke run gives its first gift, and a birthday there would make that gift another gift. **The option** `starter_map.festivals` (default true) governs both: off, no events and no birthdays are planned and the mode's `festivals` is not written. **The runtime.** `RpgFarmEvents` is a child `Events` of the mode like the weather; it is found through a meta of the game state (`rpg_farm_events`), not through the router, because the dialogue atoms live in `RpgCondition`, which is in every kind's project and may not name a farm's class, and because a unit test has no router. With `enabled` off it sets no meta and `current()` is null, which is how everything else knows that the farm has no festivals. **Schedules are overridden, not extended**: `RpgSchedule.override` is a static Callable that `entry_in_force` asks first; `pick` and `resolve` are what they were and their tests pass unchanged. The other way (a day-of-event condition in the entries) would have needed the entries of `game/data/people.json`, which is the user's and is never written again, so a farm from before the card would have had festivals without people. The shop is closed the same way (`RpgFarmShop.day_off`, a static Callable; `closed_all_day()` is new and the smoke step that waits for an open shop asks it instead of the weekday). **What there is to do** is kept small: eggs are found with `interact` and pay 20 each, the fifth pays 150; the third fish of the day pays 200; the table judges the dearest thing the bin sold this season against the middle price of autumn's crops and pays 300 (a farm that cannot fish has a second contest in summer, on ten things shipped, for 200); a food brought to the table gives everybody 60 points. The bin had no record of what it sold: `sell_all` keeps one now (`flags.farm_bin.shipped`, for the season the calendar shows, begun anew by the first sale of another). **A birthday gift** counts five times (`BIRTHDAY_FACTOR`) when it is loved, liked or taken politely and is none of the week's two; a disliked one is not multiplied (it costs its 20 points, and is the day's gift all the same); only the first gift of the day is a birthday gift, or a loved item given seven times would fill ten hearts in a day. **The things of a festival are made at run time** (`RpgFestivalThing`, as the things to forage are) at markers the village gets when it is built (`festival_table` below the square, `festival_egg_1` to `_5` on a ring round the middle of the map, never within two cells of a spot of the square: the first village had an egg behind the heads of the people). They are drawn shapes; no pack's picture is looked for. The table is an area, not a body: it can be walked through (a body put at run time on a cell an NPC may be walking over was not tried). **What exists is never built again**, so for a farm from before the card (or one that switches the option on): the calendar is the pause menu's page (added for every farm with festivals, as the People page is) and the wall has none; the eggs and the table are put on a ring of cells round the marker `square`, and one of them may lie where nobody can walk (the integration test plays the hunt on such a village of *Green acre* and finds all five; nothing checks that in general); the birthdays are read from `generated/data/people.json` for a person whose entry of the user's file has none; and the dialogues, written once, have no line for the day. **The wall calendar** hangs over the cell of the top row farthest from the bed; on squares it is drawn on the wall above that cell with its origin on the cell's upper edge (so whoever stands in the room is drawn over it), on diamonds it is a board on a post that stands on the cell, since nothing says where the wall of an isometric room is. **The calendar page closes when the keys are up**: Escape is both `cancel` and `menu`, and Space both `interact` and `confirm`, so a page that closed on the press would hand that press to the game. **`add_event`** is the hook for T17.26: an event of any kind on a date, kept in `flags.farm_events.added`, on the calendar, a day off for the shop, the people gathered at the village's square; `event:wedding` is true on it and `festival_began` is emitted in its morning. **The smoke sequence** has five steps before the last: the calendar is read (the real `interact` at the wall, then the pause menu's page), the next birthday is waited for by ending days and its gift given with the real `interact`, days are ended until tomorrow is a festival and the sign by the house is read, a year of days is run (every festival of `events.json` on its day and on no other, the shop closed, everybody sent to the square) which ends on the morning of the egg hunt, and the hunt is played in the village with the people standing on the square. A year is 112 to 223 calls of `end_day` and takes about five seconds; the five steps take about twelve of the fifty seconds the sequence of *Green acre* runs headless. `save_and_load` adds an event before the save, takes it away after it, and compares what was found, counted, added and shipped. **A/B** (the top-down sample as a farm, `HEAD` against this tree with `festivals` off, another agent's added maps in the same tree): the village, the farmhouse, the mode's scene, every file of `game/data` and the catalog are the same (scenes but for `unique_id`s); of mine there are the scripts (five new: `events.gd`, `festival_thing.gd`, `wall_calendar.gd`, `ui/calendar_page.gd`, `events_recipe.gd`), 27 new keys in `game/i18n/strings.tsv` and `generated/i18n/strings_en.tres` (the strings of a kind are added for every project of the kind), the README's section, and in `build_state.json` the hash of the strings and the hash of the starter map (it holds the config's `starter_map` section, which has the option in it; the map is the same file). With the option on a farm has besides: `events.json` twice, `birthday` in both `people.json`, three lines in each person's dialogue, six markers on the village, `WallCalendar` in the farmhouse, `festivals = true` in the mode's scene, `events` and the birthdays in the plan. No golden moved. **Seen** (in a window, during the smoke steps and a scratch step): the calendar page reads well at 640 x 360 (the weeks, today white, the birthday pink, the festival gold, the two lines under them, three buttons); under two lines the list leaves a gap of four empty lines above the buttons. The calendar on the wall is a small white sheet with a red head and three dark lines on the top wall, and the farmer who stands under it covers all of it but the red head. On the day of the egg hunt three people stand close together on the square and the eggs are coloured ovals two thirds of a tile high on the grass (the first ones were half a tile and easy to miss); nothing else says that the day is a festival (no stall, no banner, the demo's villager stands where he always stands). The table was a low white board with three coloured dots that the farmer who stood at it covered almost wholly; it is two tiles wide and higher now, and that one was not looked at again. In the smoke run's picture three toasts lie over the minimap, one of them the morning of a festival 85 days earlier: the run ends days faster than a toast fades. **Open:** nobody has played a festival by hand and the prizes are untuned; the calendar's board in an isometric room was not looked at; an egg of an old village can lie out of reach; the pictures of the eggs and the table are not a pack's; the people say one line each from a list of three and it does not name the festival; a festival is the same every year.

T17.24 (the cave: combat on a farm). **The choice the card left open: *Combat* on implies a cave, and a cave row requires *Combat*.** `added_map_plan.with_cave` adds `{"name": "Cave", "kind": "cave"}` after the rows when the feature is on and neither a row nor the stored plan has a cave, and refuses a cave row (by its kind or by its name: *cave*, *cavern*, *mine*, *grotto*, *tunnel*) with *switch on the feature Combat* when it is off. Why: the spec says *with it on, the farm has a cave* and *with it off there is no cave and nothing to fight*, so the feature is the one switch; a cave without *Combat* would be an empty hole, and *Combat* without a cave would sell a sword with nothing to swing it at. A row of the kind still names the cave (and places it in the order of the rows). Rows whose names the plan has are not read again (a forest called *Old mine* from before the card stays a forest), and a stored cave is never taken away: a farm whose plan has a cave **fights** whatever *Combat* says now (`added_map_plan.fights`, `FarmBuild.fights`), and the run says so in a note. **The plan.** `_cave` plans 26 x 20 cells: a door on an edge as the others, two or three chambers of 5-7 x 4-5 cells hollowed out of rock at least eight cells from the way in and a cell apart, tunnels two cells wide from the way in to the nearest and on from each to the next, up to three boulders where they touch nothing, up to three enemies in the chambers at least six cells from where the farmer arrives, and six places for the things of a cave; the check claims the rock and the enemies like everything else and wants every enemy reached and none at the way in (400 seeds plan at the first try). `farm_plan` itself is untouched: it plans whatever rows the orchestrator hands it (`orchestrator.farm_map_options`). **What a cave has** (`forage_plan.with_cave`): the packs' `ITEM`s tagged `forage` and `cave`, else the kit's drawn copper ore, amethyst and cave mushroom, `maps: ["cave"]`, every season, at 40, 60 and 90; only a thing whose name says food restores energy (the mushroom). The kit (`farm_kit.KIND_STAND_INS["cave"]`) also draws a **slime** (role `ENEMY`, a sheet laid out as the hen's, idle and walk four ways) for a farm whose packs have no enemy; a farm that does not fight gets none of it. **The enemies are the action RPG's.** `staging.OPTIONAL_FOLDERS` lets a farm borrow `archetypes/arpg/` and `recipes/arpg/` (not its smoke steps), and `orchestrator.phase_install` asks for them only for a farm that fights; `build.gd` has `OPTIONAL_RECIPES`, which puts `arpg/enemy_recipes` before `farm/added_maps_recipe` when `FarmBuild.fights` says so. The cave's recipe loads that recipe by path (never by a preload, so a farm without the folder compiles) and places `starter_scene_at(ctx, i)` at the planned cells, `entity_id` `<map>_enemy_<n>`. The farm's runtime names no class of the action RPG either: the smoke steps find an enemy by its id and its `Health`. **The farmer** fights with what `combat/realtime/` has: `RpgFarmFight` (the mode's child `Fight`, on with the mode's `fights`) gives the player an `RpgHealth`, an `RpgHurtbox` (layer 9) and an `RpgFarmBlade` (an `RpgHitbox` on layer 7 that adds the held weapon's `attack` modifiers) **at run time** when a map is entered, so that `player.tscn` of a farm that does not fight is the file it was. The action RPG's `RpgPlayerCombat` was not used: it reads the `attack` action, which a farm has not got (its `use` is the swing, through `RpgFarmer.use_held`), and its death calls the action RPG's defeat policy. The weapon `farm_sword` (`farmer_recipe.weapon`, written once; not `sword`, which is the starter gear a pack's picture may give every kind) is kept like a tool (category KEY: not sold back, offered once) and sold at 200 through a stock source of the shop. A swing costs no energy. On diamonds the blade goes to the cell the farmer faces (the frame on the ground), not along the name of its facing: in the first isometric picture the frame lay on one diamond and the swing went to the screen's right of it. **Defeat is passing out:** at 0 hit points the farmer lies down (`begin_down`, no input), and 1.2 s later `RpgDayEnd.pass_out()` ends the day at whatever hour; the night is `PASSED_OUT` and `knocked_out` changes the toast; the morning then is the 2:00 morning (the bed, half the energy, a tenth of the money with `pass_out_loss`). Every morning the hit points are full and the flags `defeated` of every `farm_map_*_enemy_*` are cleared, so the cave fills again; a defeated enemy leaves, beside its own loot (the potion of the demo, gold), one of the cave's things as an `RpgPickup`. Nothing new is saved. The HUD of a farm that fights has `%HealthBar` over the energy (`hud_recipe.build_hud(ctx, true)`; the scene of a farm that does not fight is the scene it was). The cave's sentences are `cave_strings.gd`, merged into `strings.tsv` only for a farm that fights. **The map.** The floor is the farm's ground under a gloom; the rock and the edge are drawn as flat dark shapes (`Rock`) and the rock that touches the floor blocks (`RockWall`, a cell shape each); the edge has the invisible border collision and no wall tile (the first pictures had the pack's hedge round the cave), and the tunnels are no roads (they were painted as the forest's path and read as sandy roads). The root is an interior (no rain, the dim ambient); `_added_go` of the smoke steps was told so. **The smoke sequence** has five steps after the beach and before the festivals: the sword is bought with the shop's menu in its hours and held; the farmer walks into the cave (the enemies where the plan says, the farmer armed, the health bar full); an enemy is defeated with the real `use` (2 swings on both packs; the farmer is made invulnerable meanwhile); what it left is walked over, a thing of the cave is picked up with `interact`, and both are carried to the farm; and at its last hit point beside an enemy the farmer is defeated by that enemy's own blow (a fallback strikes through the enemy's hitbox and says so; it was not needed on either pack), the day ends and it wakes at the bed, full, the enemy back. Each passes with an INFO line in a farm that does not fight. *Green acre* with *Combat* passes its whole sequence; the synthetic isometric pack passes the five steps on diamonds. **A/B** (`git archive HEAD` against the same tree with this card's changes alone, other agents' work left out; *Green acre* as a farm with *Combat* off and the top-down sample as a turn-based game and as an action RPG, no smoke run): the turn-based and the action projects differ in the copies of `build.gd` and `translations_recipe.gd` and what follows from them (their hashes in the ledger, the run id, the path of the pack), in 34 scenes by `unique_id`s only, and in `strings_en.tres` by the order of its messages only (the same lines); the farm has the three new scripts and their uids, the changed scripts and recipes, the README's new section, 56 scenes that differ by `unique_id`s only, the order of `strings_en.tres`, and the report's count of scripts; every map, every file of `game/data`, `strings.tsv`, `player.tscn` and `combat_mode.tscn` are the same. No golden moved. **Seen** (in a window, during the smoke steps, on both packs): the cave reads as one: a dark room of rock with brown chambers and tunnels, grey boulders, ore lying about, three green slimes; the farmer beside a slime with the sword's icon over its hands, the red health bar over the energy; *The Slime left Copper ore.* and *+3 gold* as toasts and the ore on the floor; at its last hit point the health bar is an empty dark strip; the next morning's toast *You were knocked out and woke up at home. 10 G is gone.* and the farmer by the bed with both bars full. On diamonds the same: the floor is the pack's grass in the gloom, the boulders are the pack's rock blocks, the amethyst and the mushrooms stand on their diamonds, and the frame of the faced cell lies on the slime that is hit. **Wrong or poor:** the rock and the edge are flat shapes, and on diamonds flat diamonds (no rock of a pack is looked for); the slime is a tile large: half the farmer's height on squares, as wide as the farmer on diamonds; the swing is the tool's icon over the hands (the sample's farmer has no attack strip); the cave's light is hardly dimmer than the farm's; the first pictures had the farm's hedge round the cave and sandy roads through it (mended). **Open:** nobody fought by hand, and the sword, the enemies and the prices are untuned; the enemies' demo variants (a charger, a shooter) are written to `game/data/enemies/` but the cave places the packs' enemies in turn; the people never go into the cave.

T17.28 (imported maps in the isometric view). **The reader.** `tiled.py` reads a map whose orientation is `isometric`; a `staggered` or `hexagonal` one is TILE-009 with its shape (*staggered maps are not imported*), anything else *only orthogonal and isometric maps are imported*. For an isometric map only, a tile set that is a collection of images is read (`_xml_collection`, `_json_collection`): a tile row is `[x, y, picture, 0, 0, transform]`, the raw map says `projection: isometric` and lists its `pictures`, and a tile layer keeps Tiled's `offset` (its groups' added). Objects keep Tiled's coordinates, in which both axes count the height of a diamond (the corner of an infinite map is taken off in those units). A map of squares goes through the same lines with the same numbers and gets no new key; the reader runs (0.6) for a pack whose only reason is an isometric map with a collection of its own. LDtk has no isometric maps: nothing changed in its reader. **The pictures.** Kenney's sample lies in `Samples/Tiled Sample.zip` (opened as `Samples/Tiled Sample_extracted/`), a folder of samples, whose pictures T17.12 leaves unused and the isometric reader did not measure. It now measures the pictures an isometric map of the pack paints wherever they lie (`tiled.map_pictures` of the results before it), with the diamond of the pack's own pictures when it has any; the catalog still marks them unused (*preview_folder*), so they fill no role and are no choice of the Mapping page. Making them pack art was tried in thought and left: the sample of *Miniature Farm* is drawn with another pack's pieces (a prototype's orange walls, stairs, a crate, a switch: none of them is in the farm's `Isometric/`), and they would have stood beside the farm's own ground and walls for every project of the pack. **The catalog** stores an isometric map by asset ids (`_iso_map`): layers of the new kind `iso_tiles` with `pictures` and runs `[count, index, transform]`, the map's `walls` (role WALL, or, for an unused picture, the role `classify` gives its name and shape: *wall_E*, *wallCurve_S*, *fence_W* of the sample, not the crate, the doorway or the window), `projection`. A tile is matched by the file of its picture when that was read as isometric, else by a picture of the same bytes that was (the first by id), which is how a sample that ships copies of the pack's own pictures would be read. **The tile set.** `iso_atlas.map_pictures` adds the pictures of the isometric maps chosen for import that are no tile of `iso_tiles` (an object of the pack, a picture of its samples) to the atlas, each with `maps_only`; `IsoTileset.pieces` and `plain_tiles` leave such a picture out, so nothing planned paints it, and `tile_for` finds it. A project that imports no isometric map has the tile set it had. **The builder.** `imported_maps_recipe.gd` builds an isometric map of an isometric project through `MapGeo` (`build_iso_map`, `iso_scene`): the first layer Tiled draws in its place is the `Ground` (the sample's first layer is *Second floor*, 78 px higher, so it is the second), every other one a `TileMapLayer` under `YSorted` before `Entities`, Y-sorted and colliding, moved by its offset in the project's px; each cell the tile of its picture **at that picture's turn** (`tile_for(id, 0)` would give the first turn of a tile turned four ways); the walls' cells an unseen `ImportedWalls` body of diamonds (`MapGeo.block_cells`, which `border_collision` now calls with its old name); spawns, doors and signs on the middle of their diamonds; without a spawn the free ground cell nearest the middle of the ground. A flipped tile is painted unflipped and said in one INFO line: an isometric tile has no flipped picture. **The other view.** `catalog.map_fits` (and `ImportedMaps.fits`): an isometric map only in an isometric project, a map of squares only in one seen from above. `requirements.check` reports a chosen map of the other view as TILE-013 (new: *The map … is drawn on diamonds and this project is seen from above: it is not imported.*); the recipe does not build it, does not take it for the starter map (the planner plans the map as without one, and so does the dry run) and leads no door to it. The recipe's hash has `iso_v` only in an isometric project. **Seen**: the Tiled sample of *Isometric Miniature Farm* (60 x 32 diamonds of 256 x 128, three layers, eleven pictures of 256 x 512, no objects) as the starter map of an isometric turn-based game of the pack: 35 smoke steps pass; in a picture taken in a window during `player_moves` the map is what a drawing of it made the way Tiled draws one shows, piece for piece (the wall with its doorway, its curve and its window, the stairs in an L, the switch, the crate, three fences), the drawn hero standing on a stair: the free ground nearest the middle is under the stairs, which are pictures without height. The pack's `Sample.png` is no picture of that map: it is a rendered farm (barn, corn, fences) that the Tiled sample does not hold, so the comparison the card asked for was made with the drawing instead. **A/B** (HEAD against HEAD with this card alone, from the command line's code path): the synthetic `tiled_map` with three maps imported, `ldtk_level` with its level as the starter map, and the sample pack as a turn-based game, all from above: the catalog, `build_state.json`, every map and scene (scenes but for `unique_id`s) are the same; what differs is the copies of the four builder scripts that changed, the config's path of the pack, the ledger's hashes of those, `strings_en.tres` (the same lines in another order, as before) and the records of the run. **Not done:** height (a stair is walked across; a second floor is only drawn higher, and nobody stands on it); Tiled's tile objects (a picture placed as an object) are not imported, as on squares; a door whose `target_map` differs from a map's name in case stays a marker, as on squares (the sample's maps are named `Sample`, the ids `sample`); the Options page lists the maps of the other view among the others and the run reports them; the audit shows a sample's pictures as unused although a chosen map paints them.

T17.26 (marriage). **Who.** `people_plan.with_courtable` gives every person `courtable` true or false while nobody has the key: `starter_map.courtable` people (0 to 6, default 2) chosen by the seed, the shopkeeper last; once given the key stays, a person added later is false and a changed count changes nobody, and 0 gives nobody the key, so a farm that switches marriage on later is chosen then (as T17.25 did for birthdays: a unit test and the integration test hold that nothing else of the people changes). The builder writes the key into both `people.json`; the game reads the user's, and the planned file's for an entry without the key. **The runtime** is `RpgMarriage` (`archetypes/farm/marriage.gd`), a child `Marriage` of the mode with `enabled` from the mode's new `courting`; it is found through a meta of the game state, and its state is `flags.farm_marriage`, so the save has no new key and the atoms `courting:<id>`, `engaged:<id>`, `spouse:<id>` of `RpgCondition` read the flags directly (no class name, no node). Somebody to court is held at eight hearts by `RpgFriendship.cap_of` (a new static Callable; points above a cap are never taken away); `RpgFriendship.gift` hands the bouquet and the pendant to `RpgMarriage.give`, so `interact` with either held works through the farmer's existing gift hook, and a refused one stays in the bag. The pendant adds the wedding with T17.25's `add_event` three days on, passing over a festival's day; **the wedding's night** (a night listener after the festival's) makes the spouse, whether the farmer went or not. A farm with marriage and without festivals turns the events on with `with_files` off (no festival, no birthday, only what is added). The bouquet (200) and the pendant (2000) are items `marriage_recipe.gd` seeds once, with drawn icons; the shop sells them through a stock source, so the shop's file, written once, is untouched. **The bed for two** is the plan's: `_interior` plans `bed {cell [1,1], size [2,2], sleepers 2}` and the start at `[3,2]` for every new farm whatever the option says, since a room is never built again; the recipe lays two `RpgBed`s side by side that wake the farmer on one cell. A farm planned before the card keeps its one bed (its plan has no `sleepers`, the hash and the room are what they were); its spouse sleeps there all the same (not seen at night) and stands beside the bed in the morning, which is what a spouse does on a new farm too: nobody lies in a bed, both stand on the cell east of where the farmer wakes (south or west when the room ends there). **The spouse** is in the village one NPC, sent away by an override, and in the farmhouse an NPC made at run time of the person's scene each time the room is entered, with a schedule and one line of its own about the morning; its day is code, not data (home from 6:00, its own square spot from 12:00 to 17:00 on Tuesday and Thursday when dry, home, asleep on no map from 22:00), and it wins over the person's entries through `RpgSchedule.override`, chained after the events' so that a festival wins over both. **The help** (`help_on`: the first morning after the wedding always waters, then a week of water, feed, table, water, quiet, table, feed turned by `String.hash()` of the spouse) runs on the morning before the day's end saves: watering marks at most eight, about half, of the dry cells of the farm's field in the flags (crops first) and reloads a field that is shown; feeding fills every empty trough; the table is `RpgSpouseTable`, a drawn small table with a produce of the season on it, taken with `interact`. A wet morning or a farm without animals is a quiet one. **Neglect:** every night everybody met and not talked to that day loses 2 points; it is part of marriage and only a farm with `courting` has it, so a farm with the option at 0 keeps T17.09's friendship exactly. **The smoke sequence** has five steps before the last: the bouquet and the pendant are bought with the shop's menu, the person is given seven hearts (the bouquet is refused) then eight (an INFO line says the hearts are given: a run that earned them would take a game's year), courted with the real `interact`, given ten, proposed to with `interact`, the days are ended to the wedding (the one to be married stands on its square spot, the shop is closed), four cells are planted dry and the wedding's night is ended, and the next morning part of the plot is wet and the spouse stands on its cell in the farmhouse. `save_and_load` compares the marriage's state. On *Green acre* the five steps take a few seconds. **A/B** (`git archive HEAD` against the working tree, which held the other agents' work of T17.24 and T17.28 too; the top-down sample as a turn-based game): every scene and map is the same but for `unique_id`s; what differs is `dialogue/condition.gd` and the runtime's changelog (mine: the new atoms, in every project like T17.25's), copies of the builder's scripts, the order of `strings_en.tres` (the same lines; another agent's change to the translations recipe), the report, the config's pack path and the ledger's hashes of the changed files. No golden moved. **Seen** (in a window, during the smoke steps of *Green acre*, `multi_shot.gd` in a scratch folder): the proposal is a toast over the village ("Willa says yes! The wedding is on spring 16, on the village square.") with the farmer below Willa; on the wedding's day three people and the farmer stand close together on the square and a toast names the day, but nothing on the square says it is a wedding (no arch, no flowers, the demo's villager stands where he always stands) and the farmer stands among them; the next morning the farmhouse has its two beds side by side under the calendar and Willa stands to the right of them, toasts "You and Willa are married." and "Willa watered 3 cells of the plot."; the watered cells are hard to tell from dry ones in the dim light of 6:00. A bed of the sample is drawn among the decorations of the village's top edge (not new). **Open:** nobody has courted by hand and the numbers are untuned; the spouse never walks onto the farm and is not seen at night; the dialogue of a person from before the card has no courting lines; the isometric farm plays the steps in the tests, its pictures were not looked at; a married farmer is never asked about it on the People page.

T17.29 (real farming packs), written from Kenney's *Tiny Farm*, EmanuelleDev's *Farm RPG* and LimeZu's *Modern Farm* where they lie on the owner's drives (none of their files is in the repository; the two licences that ask it allow reading them here). **Two places.** A new reader, `adapters/farm.py` (`farm_pack`, priority 52, after the readers of whole formats and before frames and strips), reads only a farming pack (`classifier.farm_view`, or the pack's own name says *farm*, *harvest*, *ranch*: *Tiny Farm*'s files are numbered) and only what no reader before it claimed: crops, animals, characters drawn in pieces or a file an action, and food icons. A pass after the readers, `pack_forms.py`, runs on every pack: previews, forms, sizes; it only sets aside (the role `NONE`, `unused_because:<why>`, and `same_as:<path>`) and never adds. **Forms by content.** Every still picture is cut in squares of every cell the pack has eight single files of, and of 16; a cell is the SHA-1 of its pixels with the transparent ones zeroed. Two sheets of one painted set (90 % both ways, eight cells at least) are one set: the one read as tiles is used, else the surer, else the first by name; two pictures of one size are the duplicate of T13.04 and stay information only (the `bundle` golden had two). A sheet whose painted cells are 90 % covered by two smaller pictures or more is `compiled_sheet` (*Farm RPG*'s season sheets, *Modern Farm*'s complete tile set); single files of a folder that are 90 % cells of a used sheet are `same_tiles` (*Tiny Farm*'s 132, *Modern Farm*'s single-file folders). A cell held by more than 64 pictures (plain grass) is not counted. A set-aside sheet joins no tile set (`catalog._another_form`). **Sizes.** Paths that say one `NxN` and, without it, the same path at another size (`Animals_32x32/Cows_32x32/Cow_32x32.png` is `Animals/Cows/Cow`), as much larger as the sizes say, three names at a size or more: the pack is drawn in those sizes (`size_form:<n>`). `choose_sizes`, after all packs: the user's size (`Catalog.pack_sizes`, kept from the previous catalog), else the cell most of the other packs' tile sheets have, else the smallest; the others `other_size`. Then, in such a pack and in every farming pack, a tile sheet of another cell is cut again at the pack's size when it divides, set aside when its cell is another of the pack's sizes (the RPG Maker MV copy at 48), else made a picture: no tile set in a size the pack does not draw.
