The Audit page's *Packs* tab has a *Tile size* combo (`edits.set_pack_size`: `pack_forms.use_size` in place, the restored pictures classified again, the tile sets made again; one undo step); the Project page has no such choice (the sizes are only known after the analysis). **Crops.** A picture wider than high in a folder of crops, plants, growth, seeds or farming (a season's folder counts only inside one: *Farm RPG*'s `Objects/Props/Fall` has stones), or named after a crop, not a tree, not in a folder of icons or food: the cell width is the name's `NxN` or the one of 16, 32, 8 whose borders the drawings cross least; the row of cells starts at the first band of paint and is as high as the smallest multiple of the width that no stalk crosses (a cell painted all over, LimeZu's label under the stages, counts as empty). The stages are the painted cells from the first, over one empty cell when two painted ones follow (*Farm RPG*'s pumpkin), stopped by the empty cell before the produce; they grow when each covers about as much as the one before or more (one dip, two in a folder of crops), the last at least 1.2 times the least (1.4 times the first outside a folder of crops) and no shorter, and the ripe one about the largest (a withered last stage is no stage). The stages are drawn into `generated/farm_crops/<pack>__<file>.png` (the reader's `rendered`), the asset a `growth_strip` with `produce:<name>` (the file's name without *growth*, *stages* and the size), and `crop_plan.facts_of` names the crop by it. The smallest pictures are read first and a picture whose cells are all among crops read before repeats them (a season's sheet) and is left to the forms. A picture of the crops' folder that does not grow is the note ASSET-035. In a pack where nothing else is a crop, the largest sheet (Kenney's spaced sheet: cell 16, a gap of 1 found by clear columns) is read for runs of three cells or more that grow by 1.8, begin small and are green at every stage (the withered plant, the seed bag and the crate after them are not); each is a variant `#crop_<n>` of the sheet with its own picture (`rendered["<path>#<variant>"]`, which `assemble` now looks up per variant). The growth strips the strips and grid readers find by a name are checked against their pictures in a farming pack unless the name gives a frame count (`corn_16x32_8frames` in the `multi_engine` corpus pack is drawn the same eight times and stays a crop): a butterfly called *Cabbage White*, a horse eating a carrot and *Farm RPG*'s 2-frame food icons are no crops any more. **Animals.** A picture whose folder or name says chicken, hen, rooster, cow, sheep, goat, pig, duck, horse, donkey, rabbit, ostrich: the cell width as for crops (16 to 64, a cow is 32), every row an animation of its painted cells from the first (cut where a cell is far larger than the others: *Modern Farm*'s *ROW:4 COL:24* written on the sheet), the first three `idle_down`, `walk_down`, `sleep_down`; the role `ANIMAL`. Dogs are not among them. **Layered characters.** A folder whose sub-folders are actions (`1. Idle`, `2. Walk`; of two folders for one action the one with fewer words, `24. Swim - Idle` is not the idle) holding folders of parts (skins/body, eyes, hair, clothes/*clothers*/outfits, acc/accessories/hats) is a set: every `<part>/<rest>` is one asset (`lpc_body` or `lpc_layer` with `lpc_part:<body|eyes|hair|torso|hat>`, `layered_piece`, `layered_set:<folder>`), its files of the other actions its animations' `source`s (four directions a row: down, up, left, right), the files of the actions that make no animation (fishing, riding, a bear) consumed. *Modern Farm*'s generator is a folder of parts under a folder that says *pieces* or *generator*, a sheet a piece in frames of a tile by two, rows idle and walk after the row of four standing pictures, directions right, up, left, down as LimeZu orders them. `lpc_compose` puts them together: `fits` asks one set and frames as large (and one picture size for sheets), `auto_definitions` makes one character a set (its first body and the first eyes, clothes and hair; accessories are choices), named by the nearest folder that says more than *character* and *png* (*Chef*, *Farmer*), `render_layered` lays every picture of the body with the same picture of each layer over it in `PART_ORDER` (body, clothes, eyes, hair, hat) and stacks the pictures, and the frames are cut to the columns the standing and walking frames paint (*Farm RPG* draws a character 14 px wide in frames of 32, and the builder makes the feet 0.6 of the frame: 19 px of feet walked through no door of a 16 px farm). The character is the player when nothing else of the pack is and its folder does not say NPC. The audit's dialog says *Compose character*, offers the set's bodies as a choice too and shows a frame of the character (`lpc_compose.preview`). A folder of files named by nothing but an action (`Pre-made/Alex/Walk.png`, three rows down, up, side) is one character with its sources, the rest of its folder consumed. **Previews.** A GIF whose name without its size is the name of a PNG of the pack. **Two faults met on the way, mended:** the Credits screen formatted `tr("UI_CREDITS_BY")` where no translation had the key (the validator), a runtime error once a pack named its author; the farm's `season_turns` smoke step failed for a pack of two crops, which grow all the year by the plan (it passes: nothing can wither). **The real packs** (analysis, before -> after): see the report of the card; generated as farms in the scratch folder, each passed the farm's smoke sequence and was looked at in pictures. *Tiny Farm*'s farmer and hen are numbered tiles that nothing names: they were chosen as a user would, by overrides on the single files (role and one-frame animations), and the ground is the sheet's first tile.

T17.29, what the three farms asked once generated (each a fault seen in a picture or the smoke sequence, mended where it lay). **Roles of a farming pack** (`catalog._farm_roles`, only for pictures the farm reader did not read): a folder or name of portraits or faces is `PORTRAIT`, of an animal word `ANIMAL`, of critters, pets or animals without one a `DECORATION`; a picture of a folder of Aseprite sources, of a folder of a layered set's parts or named a bare body is set aside (`aseprite_copy`, `layered_piece`); a pack whose crops were read from their pictures keeps its single pictures of a crop (a basket of cabbages) out of the crops. LimeZu's sheets that name the rows of a character in pictures (`*_GUIDE_*`) are `guide_picture`. **Copies and sheets set aside** (`pack_forms`): a folder of RPG Maker's layout in a pack that has other tiles is `engine_copy`; a tile sheet in a folder of characters, animals or NPCs is `character_sheet`; a sheet read as more than twelve buildings is `many_buildings` (*Modern Farm*'s barn sheet) and a building sheet whose name says no house (a bridge, a fence, railings, corners, stairs) is decoration; the objects cut from a sheet set aside are set aside with it (the *Modern Farm* build timed out on 20 000 objects of its complete sheet). The crop strips the reader draws carry `source_file:<path>`, so a pack's 32 and 48 px crops are its other sizes as its files are (*Modern Farm* grew every crop three times, *carrot*, *carrot_2*, *carrot_3*). **Ground and buildings of a farming pack** (`mapping.best_for`): the ground is the open ground of a tile sheet before any single picture (`open_ground_rank`), and the farm's buildings take the largest frame first (the scratch generations took the wizard's starter, `mapping.default_starter`). **Actors.** The facet `feet` (a sheet's character narrower than its frame, capped at 0.6 of it) is what `ActorBuild.set_feet` makes the feet; the farmhouse takes a bed or a bin a frame of at most three tiles by four (*Farm RPG*'s `Beds.png` holds thirty). **Choices made as a user would** to see the farms: *Farm RPG* and *Modern Farm* keep four villagers (the audit sets the others to none: a village of 40 people of the pack filled every path), *Modern Farm* takes placeholders for the UI, walls and portraits and no decorations on the starter map (its keys step found the way blocked).

CI on 2026-10-14, after T17.29: three failures that were not the code. Godot died while importing on the Ubuntu runners (`exit -6`, an abort) in four jobs of three runs, among them `main`'s own run of a commit that had passed two hours before on the same runner image; the import is now run once more when Godot is killed rather than exiting (`godot.crashed`: a negative code but -1, or an NTSTATUS code on Windows), with the note BUILD-006, and a second crash fails as before (`tests/unit/test_import_retry.py`). The perf test of parallel analysis read 1.8x and 1.9x on 4 cpus where T12.17 asks 2x, on code whose speed-up measured the same as the last green commit's on this machine (3.0x to 3.4x on 20 cpus, both); it then took the best of two serial and three parallel timings and still read 1.6x and 1.9x on the next run, so the limit is now 2x from 8 cpus and 1.5x from 4 (the runners' 4 cpus are two cores with two threads each), and T12.17's text says so. The cancel test raced the analysis cache (the second run found every image cached by the first and finished instead of failing) and now gives each run a cache of its own.

T17.30 (packs of other shapes), written from Screaming Brain's 13 isometric packs (CC0, unpacked from their `.rar`s into the scratch folder), *Lords Of Pain* (read in its zip), SakPix's 31 stage packs and its characters, and CuteSCKR's 80 tile sets, where they lie (none of their files is in the repository). **Isometric sheets by their names** (`adapters/iso_sheets.py`, priority 8, before the Tiled reader, which skips the tile sets of the sheets it cut): a picture whose name (or its folder's, or the Tiled tile set beside it, `tilewidth`/`tileheight`/`trans`) states a cell it holds whole is cut cell by cell, the background colour (the tile set's `trans`, else the corner's when 5 % of the sheet has it) made see-through, each cell a variant `#c<col>_<row>` with its picture in `generated/iso_cells/`. A cell twice as wide as high is the pack's diamond (`isometric:<w>x<h>:named`); a pack of wall faces that names none (the *Wall Pack*) takes the diamond from its faces' slant (a face `cw` wide whose foot falls `cw/2` stands along a side of a diamond `2cw` x `cw`); a square cell is a cube on the half-high diamond, a cell a little higher than its diamond a block. **The SE and SW of Screaming Brain's names are the light, not the side** (both files hold both faces; measured: the SE file's `/` faces are the bright ones), so the side comes from the picture: two neighbouring faces slanting `/` and `\` are put together into four pieces on the canvas of a diamond (`w` and `e` from `/`, `n` and `s` from `\`), checked by `measure.edge_side`, turns 0 to 3 grouped by the tag `turn_group:` (which `catalog.mark_turns` and `turn_sets` now read beside the names), tagged `door`/`window` by the name. An opaque picture is a texture: only a diamond's own picture that tiles on its diamond (`diamond_of`, its seams within 3 times its neighbouring pixels) is ground. A *Small* and a *Large* copy are tagged `size_form:<diamond width>` and `pack_forms.choose_sizes` keeps one (the smallest by default). Thick walls, corners and the paths' and roads' overlays (painted inside a diamond without filling it: needs review) are not read further. `TILE-014` when the flat tiles measure another diamond than the names. **A large ground picture** (`iso_sheets.ground_pictures`, from the isometric reader where it said TILE-010): an opaque picture named ground or floor of an isometric pack that tiles is cut on a 2 : 1 diamond as wide as it, halved while the half is still as wide as the middle character frame the earlier readers made (*Lords Of Pain*: 256 -> 128 x 64); a picture of one colour (`ground_darken`) is none; the pack's other pictures are then read as things standing on that diamond on their own feet, except those of folders of menus and effects (a full-screen vignette stood on 30 diamonds and walled an enemy in). **Characters in a folder of facings** (`adapters/facings.py`, priority 46): an action folder is a folder whose pictures or sub-folders are named by 4, 8 or 16 directions (`naming.direction_angle`: `north-east`, `NEE` 22.5, `S` 270), each with as many frames of one size; its parent is the character when the folder is named by an action, `rotations`, or after the parent (`fighter_special_select`); otherwise it is a thing of its own (*Lords Of Pain*'s props, a decoration, on one diamond in an isometric pack). Of the variants of an action the one of the variant that attacks is taken (`armed_walk` over `default_walk`); of 16 facings the 8 (`facings:16`, ADAPT-008); a character without a walk walks in its stills (`walk_from_still`); every frame is cut to the union of what the frames paint (alpha 64 and up: the soft shadow of a rendered figure goes), the feet at the frame's bottom, the facet `feet` the width of its standing picture's lowest quarter; the role from the folders (`playable` PLAYER, `none-playable` NPC, `enemy`, `boss`); a GIF named `<character>_showcase` beside it is a preview. **Object sheets and mixed tile sheets** (`adapters/sheet_parts.py`, priority 53, after the farm reader): a still PNG of 512 px each way or more with a fifth see-through. A pack that calls itself a tile set (its name or its one wrapping folder) has its sheets sorted on the cell of 16 to 64 that divides the sheet into 12 or more each way and whose borders the art crosses least: cells whose art goes on across a border (a quarter of it) are one thing; whole cells of a block of 2 x 2 that tiles with itself, in a thing that is 80 % whole cells, are ground (`#g<x>_<y>`, a tile sheet of them, `surface:grass` when green); the rest of a thing is one object (`#o<x>_<y>`, DECORATION, needs review). Any other such sheet is cut into islands (blocks of 6 px that touch), the banner across its top and specks left out, at least 4 islands (2 when the name says what they are); each an asset `#o<x>_<y>` with the role of the sheet's name (ROLE_WORDS: an animal is a sprite with one-frame `idle_down`/`walk_down`; a building under 96 px is a decoration) or none (needs review). A sheet named *tiles* is loose squares: each cut to the square three in four of them hold and quartered (never scaled) while larger than 64 (SakPix's 192 -> 48); a square of another size than the pack's is set aside by `pack_forms._one_tile_size`. Parts of sheets are no duplicates of their sheet (`similarity.CUT_PART_DIRS`) and `pack_forms._sheets_of_buildings` leaves their roles alone. **Cancel and parallel**: `AdapterContext.is_cancelled` (set by `catalog.assemble`) and `check_cancel()`; `iso_sheets.in_parallel` cuts sheets (and draws characters) in a pool of threads as `analyzer.prewarm` does, the results in order, the cancel polled before every item and after every result. **A licence stated by hand**: the Audit page's *Packs* card of a pack with no licence file has a *Credit line* (`license_overrides.credit`, a new key of `PackLicense` that `to_json` leaves out, so no detected licence changes); `CatalogPack.licence_stated()`; the run says ASSET-036 instead of ASSET-021 when the previous catalog or the profile states one; the profile exports `pack_license` and the profile adapter hands it to the new pack's overrides; `staging.credits_note` lists a stated pack with its credit line, *the licence its user stated*, and a sentence saying so (the README of a project without one is what it was). **AI-generated art, marked by hand** (added to the card by the owner): the same card has *AI-generated art* (`license_overrides.ai_generated`, kept in the profile's `pack_license`); `staging.ai_note` gives the README of a project whose catalog uses an asset of such a pack (one not `UNUSED` or `MISSING`) a section *AI-generated content* naming those packs, saying a store page (Steam's AI disclosure, for one) must declare them, suggesting the art be cleaned up in Aseprite by hand or with an AI assistant driving it, and saying that edited art still came from a generator; no section otherwise. CuteSCKR's *Farm Tileset* marked so gives its farm the section (its smoke run passes), and the same farm unmarked has none; a unit test and the integration test hold both on `mixed_sheet`. **The builder** (found in the *Lords Of Pain* game): `MapGeo.tile_unit` measured an isometric project by a square tile set of its packs (16 px of effects) and the action RPG's enemies and followers were scaled to 16 px on diamonds of 128: it now takes the isometric tile set first; an enemy now gets the `feet` facet as the player does (`enemy_recipes` `RECIPE_VERSION` 3). **The real packs** (share of pictures read with confidence: an asset of the picture CONFIRMED or AUTO_DETECTED, a frame consumed into such an asset, or set aside with a reason; measured by `scratchpad/t30/batch.py` on `31f1362` and on this card): Screaming Brain 1,853 pictures, 2 % sure (4 % with the set-aside) -> 15 % sure, 60 % with the *Large* copies and the overlays set aside (*Floor* 28 sure before were square tiles of its Tiled sets); *Lords Of Pain* 5,302, 0 % -> 99 % (its frames are 32 characters and props); SakPix stage 636 sheets, 0 % -> 50 %; SakPix characters 2,277, 0 % -> 33 % (40 % with previews; the characters whose folders say no role need review); CuteSCKR 570 sheets, 0 % -> 34 % (a sheet counts when one of its parts is sure: its ground; its objects all need review). Analysis time on a warm image cache: Screaming Brain 48 s -> 55 s, *Lords Of Pain* 65 -> 69 s, SakPix stage 139 -> 193 s, SakPix characters 71 -> 23 s, CuteSCKR 166 -> 246 s (78 packs). **The four games** (generated in the scratch folder, each through its whole smoke sequence, each looked at in a window with `tools/doc_shots/game_shot.gd`): Screaming Brain's *Floor*, *Wall* and *Doorways* packs as an isometric turn-based game (diamonds of 128 x 64): the map's edge is the brick wall and the village's house is put together from the wood walls with windows and a doorway, no roof (the packs have none), the drawn hero; *Lords Of Pain* as an isometric action RPG on its stone ground (128 x 64), its fighter the player, its skeleton the enemy, the boss and the props its own, everything dark and small on the screen; SakPix's *Cozy Farming Village* with the *Cozy Village NPC Collection* as a farm in 48 px (grass with flowers and the fertile soil of the field in the pictures; the buildings cut from the sheets of barns and windmills, the animals from *13. Farm animals*; the village's people the collection's); CuteSCKR's *Farm Tileset* with the same collection as a farm (its grass, its house, the kit's soil). **Overrides made as a user would**: the farms' player is SakPix's *Village Farmer* (role PLAYER: the collection says NPC), CuteSCKR's grey house and red barn are BUILDINGs (its sheets name nothing), the stage pack's licence was stated by hand (`commercial`, with a credit line: bought, and SakPix's itch.io terms let buyers use it freely), CuteSCKR's tile set was marked as AI-generated art and has no licence recorded, and the collection's licence was taken as CC0 (its `license.txt` lies in the folder above the one analysed), and *Lords Of Pain*'s slime is set aside: **an arrow flies at the middle of its shooter's body** and passes over an enemy smaller than half the player (`player_bow_shoots` failed with it), a fault of the runtime left as it is. **Not done**: Screaming Brain's thick walls, corners, roofs of the *Town Pack*, paths and roads as overlays; animals cut from a sheet have no species (they all live in the barn); CuteSCKR's crops in rows (sheet 1) are decorations; edges and corners of a terrain on a mixed sheet are not told from the rest; the HUD of a farm in 48 px tiles is large. No golden and no catalog of a synthetic or sample pack moved (A/B in the report of the card).

The arrow over the slime (a fault T17.30 found and left). **The cause:** `RpgPlayerCombat.fire_arrow` and `RpgEnemyShoot.fire` started a shot at the middle of the shooter's body (its hurtbox) and the shot hurt a hurtbox it overlapped; a hurtbox is 60 % of its picture's height around the picture's middle (`arpg_build.body_size`), so seen from above or on diamonds, where the screen's y is depth and height at once, an arrow loosed at the middle of a 64-px hero flew 32 px above the ground and passed over every enemy shorter than about 0.6 of the hero standing on the hero's line (*Lords Of Pain*'s slime: `player_bow_shoots` failed), and an enemy's shot passed over a player more than twice its height the same way. **The fix:** a shot of the action RPG flies on the ground (`RpgProjectile.set_on_ground(lift)`): it starts at the shooter's feet and hurts the first body of the other team whose footprint its way crosses, `RpgHurtbox.ground_rect()`: a square around the feet of the hurtbox's holder as wide as its shape (a body as deep as it is wide), whatever its height; the way is walked in steps of the shot's radius (an arrow of 14 tiles a second on diamonds of 128 makes 30 px a frame, more than a slime is wide); the masks 9 and 10 are off, so physics does not also hit the body's rectangle (a tall body standing a little nearer the camera, whose picture covers the line but whose feet do not, is passed behind); walls still stop it, at the feet. Its picture is drawn at the hand when loosed and comes down evenly to the ground at the end of its range (`drawn_lift`), with a small shadow on the ground: at a target 4 tiles away of the bow's 12 it is drawn a third of the way down. The targets come from the group `rpg_hurtboxes` every hurtbox joins. The metroidvania's shots (its shooter aims at the player's middle, its guardian drops a rock) are seen from the side, where height is height: nobody puts them on the ground and they fly and hit as before; the run and gun has shots of its own. The farm's blade (T17.24) is a melee area, not a shot: a tile high from the farmer's middle, it reaches the ground of a farmer up to about two tiles tall, so it was left. **The smoke step had a fault of its own:** `player_bow_shoots` read the player's last shot once the target's HP had moved, when an arrow that hit had been freed at the end of its frame, and said *the attack with a bow fired no arrow* (what the slime's game said before and after the fix); it now looks at the arrow every frame while it waits, and then shoots a second enemy of enemy 2's scene made 2/5 of the player's height (its picture and hurtbox scaled). **Seen on *Lords Of Pain*** (generated with its slime as an enemy, no override, `F:\rpggen_scratch\fix_arrows`): with the runtime of `dee548e` and the new step, *the arrow did not hurt the enemy 4 tiles away*; with the fix the whole smoke sequence passes, and in a window the arrow leaves the fighter's hand, comes down along the line and reaches the slime, where it stops. A/B on the sample packs (turn-based and action RPG from above and on diamonds, the farm, the side-view sample as a metroidvania, the run and gun, `basic16`): nothing differs but the changed runtime scripts, their uids and the ext_resource lines naming them, the node `unique_id`s, the generation reports and the `map_hash` of the plan (both new on every save, base against base) and the order of a translation's lines (the same lines); the action RPG's smoke steps file.

After T17.30, the farm's known shortfalls mended (the faults the T17.24, T17.29 and T17.30 paragraphs list; the owner's review moved painting with terrains, a ground that varies and edges of soil, water, paths and rock, to a card of its own, T17.31, so none of that is here). **The ground** (`core/farm_ground.py`, `builder/lib/plain_ground.gd`): every tile of the ground sheet the config chooses is measured (opaque all over; tiles with itself: its left and right columns and its top and bottom rows differ no more than 3 times its neighbouring pixels; 85 % of its pixels one surface, grass by hue 60-175 or earth by hue 10-60; the sum of its channels' standard deviations at most 0.35), the most even of the sheet's surface (`surface:grass` first) is the ground, the first of the sheet when several are as even, and copies count once; it is stored as `farm.ground` of the plan file (schema `FarmGround`, measured on every run, absent for an isometric farm) and the farm, the village of a farm (which no longer gets patches of the sheet's next tiles: on real sheets they are its edges and corners) and the added maps lay it everywhere in place of the sheet's first tile. A sheet with no such tile gets one drawn in its most frequent green (`rpggen_farm_kit.ground`, ASSET-031). *Tiny Farm*: no tile qualifies (its grass is only the green round its beds), a plain green is drawn; *Farm RPG*: `Tileset Grass Caves` (9, 2) instead of the corner of a blob; *Modern Farm*: `1_Terrains` (3, 2), plain grass; *Green acre*: (2, 1), since its first tile (0, 0) is a gradient that does not tile (ratio 4.8). A variant scatter and patches of other shades were written and taken out again at the owner's word: one tile until terrains are painted. **Water** (`catalog._farm_water`, in `_farm_roles`): in a farming pack a picture in a folder of icons, items or food is never `WATER` nor `GROUND` (it becomes `ITEM`: *Farm RPG*'s `Icons/Fish/Sea/Dolphin.png`, a 64 x 64 sheet of fish read as water tiles, was the pond's tile), and a single square picture of 8 to 64 px of the role `GROUND` or `WATER` whose name says water or whose surface is water, not in a folder of tools and not tagged `tool` (the `ranch` golden's watering can), is a tile of water (*Farm RPG*'s `Water tile.png`). **The cave** (`recipes/farm/cave_rock.gd`, `added_maps_recipe` `RECIPE_VERSION` 2): over the drawn rock the packs' own is laid where they have it. From above: of the pictures whose own name says rock or boulder (rank 0), cave, mine or cliff (1), stone (2), then the tile sheets of the role CLIFF or WALL that are no fence (3), with a role of ground, wall, cliff, decoration or road (no item, character, crop), the surest rank that has one gives a cell of the tile size that is opaque, tiles with itself, is less than 30 % grass and 30 % water and at most 0.35 uneven, a textured one (0.05 and more) of a value of 0.3 and more first, then a light one, then any, laid as a `Sprite2D` on every cell of rock and of the edge under the node `Rock`; *Farm RPG*'s `Rock Caves.png` (a 576 x 720 picture read as one sprite) gives a cell of its textured rock face. Else the packs' isometric rock tile on diamonds (by the same names), else their boulders (`farm_map_recipe.thing_entries(.., "rock")`) one on every cell of rock (*Green acre*'s `props/rock.png`), else the drawn shapes alone (*Tiny Farm*, whose rocks nothing names). The cave's root sets `ambient_override` (0.5, 0.48, 0.6), darker and bluer than an interior's (0.78, 0.74, 0.68). An enemy whose first picture is painted less than 0.7 of the farmer's painted height (`AtlasTexture.get_image().get_used_rect()` of `idle_down`) is scaled to that, in quarters, never smaller and at most 3 times (`scale_to_farmer`): *Green acre*'s kit slime 1.25; *Farm RPG*'s goblins and *Tiny Farm*'s slime beside a one-tile farmer are left as they are. **Crops of a mixed sheet** (`sheet_parts.crop_rows`, only in a pack whose name or wrapping folder says farm, or that the farm reader takes for one): rows are read from the bottom up; a cell is a stage when it paints 2 % to 97 % of itself, stands within an eighth of its cell of the bottom, is at least 10 % green and its lowest four rows of paint are earth (red over green) within 0.2 of the first stage's; a run of such cells is a crop when T17.29's `growth_run` (ratio 1.4) holds and the first covers at most 0.6 of a cell; a run's frames reach into the row above while something crosses the border there and the cell above is no plant in soil of its own (two rows at most). Each crop is a `growth_strip` variant `#crop<x>_<y>` named `crop_<n>` in the pack's order; its cells are left out of the sheet's things. CuteSCKR's sheet 1 gives ten crops (corn, carrot, cabbage, cotton, pepper, tomato, strawberry, watermelon, potato, garlic: 3 to 5 stages, most two cells high); a garlic's first sprout and the produce after a row (a cob, a carrot) stay things; sheets 2 to 7 give none. **Synthetic packs and tests**: `farm_cave` (new: a ground sheet beginning with a striped edge, a cave sheet of rock, a picture of water, a sheet of fish icons; golden and expected file), `mixed_sheet` (a row of four stages, the last reaching into the row above, and its produce; its golden gains the crop and the produce's object), `tests/unit/test_farm_ground.py`, three tests in `test_farm_cave.gd`, `tests/integration/test_farm_cave_ground.py` and one test in `test_t17_30_pack_shapes.py`. **Seen** (windowed pictures during the smoke runs, `tools/doc_shots/game_shot.gd`): *Farm RPG* with *Combat*: before, the farm a field of teal blobs and the pond of fish icons, the cave flat dark rock; after, plain teal grass, a blue pond of its water tile, the cave walled with its own reddish rock texture in a dark light. *Tiny Farm* with *Combat*: before, the farm laid with orange beds on green; after, plain green, and its cave still drawn (it has no rock that names itself). *Modern Farm*: plain grass round the field and the pond. CuteSCKR's *Farm Tileset* (with SakPix's villagers, at 48 px, the overrides of T17.30): ten crops of its own planned, its corn ripe in the field. *Green acre* with *Combat*: the cave's rock is rows of its rock picture, the slimes larger. All passed their smoke runs. **A/B** (`git archive dee548e` against the worktree, no smoke run, the same pack files): the top-down sample as a turn-based game and an action RPG, the isometric sample as a turn-based game and as a farm: every map and scene the same but for `unique_id`s; what differs is the new `plain_ground.gd` (and `cave_rock.gd` in a farm), the copies of the changed recipes, the runtime's changelog, `strings_en.tres`'s order, the reports, the ledger's hashes (and the isometric farm's `build_state.json`). *Green acre* (Combat off and on): `farm.tscn` and `starter_map.tscn` (the ground tile, the village without patches), the cave `farm_map_1.tscn` (boulders, the light, the slimes' scale), their minimaps and `world_plan.json` (`farm.ground`). `farm_crops` with `farm_layered`: its sheet of spring grass has no plain tile, so a plain ground is drawn (the kit's `ground.png` joins the catalog and the tile set). Goldens moved: `mixed_sheet` (the crop and the produce) and the new `farm_cave`; `ranch` stayed (its watering can is no water). **Not done**: the ground is one tile with hard edges to soil, water and paths, and the cave's rock one cell with a hard edge to the floor (T17.31); a crop of a mixed sheet has no name of its produce; the cave's floor is the farm's grass in the gloom.

The isometric view after T17.30: thick walls, corners and roofs (the owner's review of 2026-10-14; Screaming Brain's paths and roads were taken out of it on the way: they are an autotiling problem, card T17.31). **Screaming Brain** (`adapters/iso_sheets.py`): a sheet whose corner is painted (the *Town Pack*'s walls start at its top left pixel) takes its background from the commonest colour of its border (`_border_key`: a quarter of the border, `KEY_SHARE` of the sheet, an opaque sheet only); a sheet up to `SHEET_SLACK` (4) px wider or higher than its whole cells is read (*Town Roofing*, 432 wide in cells of 143). A wall face whose foot runs back the other way at one end is a **thick wall** (`_straight_ends`): it no longer gives a diamond of its own (the *Wall Pack*'s thick faces of 72 made a diamond of 144 x 72 and were read on it, unusable beside the 128 x 64 of the rest) and is read by cutting the end off each face (the left 8 of a `/` face, the right 8 of a `\` one: `_thick_faces`), then as a flat face, tagged `thick`; its top keeps the wall's thickness. A cell named *Corner* (or pillar, post, pole) between half a diamond and a diamond wide is a **post** (`_posts`): the upright block is cut out and put on the four corners of a diamond's canvas, four turns of a set with the facet `post` (`nw`, `ne`, `es`, `sw`), `DECORATION` tagged `post`. A cell named *roof* up to 1.3 diamonds wide is a **roof over one diamond** (`_roofs`), its origin the middle of the diamond its eaves cover; Screaming Brain's are whole low hipped and flat roofs of one cell, so they have no shape. **Roof pieces and posts of single pictures** (`adapters/isometric.py::_roofs_and_posts`): a picture with *roof* in its name (not after *wall*: `woodWallRoofLeft` is a gable wall) gets `roof`, its shape and turn, by drawing every shape (`analyzer/isometric.py::roof_shape`: a slope high along a side, the quarter of a hipped roof high at a corner, a gable along a line between two sides, a pyramid, an inner corner) as a surface over the diamond raised by its height and taking the one whose upper outline is the picture's, the shapes a name allows (`corner` a quarter, `inner corner` a valley, `slant` a slope, `straight` or `single` a gable, `point` a pyramid, no word a slope, a gable or a pyramid); the outline alone confuses a quarter high at the far corner with one twice as high at the near one, so a pack's pieces are read twice, the second time at its slopes' rise (`roof_rise`). Measured: *Medieval Town* `roof_slant` turns 0 to 3 slope n, e, s, w, `roof_corner` quarters high at nw, ne, es, sw, `roof_straight` gables ns and ew, `roof_point` pyramids, `roof_inner_corner` valleys; *Miniature Farm* `roof_N` to `_W` slopes w, n, e, s, `roofCorner` quarters sw, nw, ne, es, `roofSingle` and `roofSingleWall` gables. Laid by the shapes, *Miniature Farm*'s four quarters on two cells by two make one pyramid two cells wide; *Medieval Town*'s make one a cell wide in the middle and its slopes a gable one cell deep: they cover the half of their cell next to their high side and carry walls of their own under their eaves (tried by laying them at half cells and lifted rings: the hidden walls and ridge caps show). `measure.roof_covers_cells` lays the four quarters (else two slopes) and measures the span; where it is short the pack's slopes and quarters get no `roof` (they are read as before) and its gables and pyramids do. A picture with *pole*, *post*, *pillar* or *corner* in its name (not *slanted*, *broken*) narrow and upright whose foot is near a corner (`post_corner`) is a post, when every turn of its set is on a corner of its own (*Medieval Town*'s `grey_pole`, `wood_pole`; *Miniature Farm*'s `woodWallCorner`; *Medieval Town*'s `grey_corner` is a wall seen from its end in two turns and is not). A set of turns is a roof's piece only when every turn has one shape, each a turn of its own (a tree among the turns of a `roof_corner` is none). Both are tiles (`iso_tile`), `DECORATION` (`catalog._iso_roles`), and no tile a map is filled with (`plain_tiles`). **The builder**: `IsoTileset.post_sets`, `post_piece`, `roof_sets`, `members_by`, `nearest_in_colour` (the mean colour of the pictures, read once a build) and `post_set_for` (of the posts no higher than the wall, 1.5 times its rise and 4 px: a storey's pole at the corners of *Medieval Town*'s low edge wall stood out like a mast; the one of the wall's colour of Screaming Brain's 72 sets of posts in 12 colours); `MapGeo.add_edge_posts` puts a post at each corner of the map where two sides of an edge of segments meet, a sprite under `YSorted/EdgePosts` sorted a pixel after the walls of its cell, the nearest one at `z_index` 11 over the near wall on `Overhead` (painting them into cells outside the map would have changed the used rectangles the camera, the minimap and the repair read); `IsoHouse.kit` adds `post` and `roofs` (one family: the first set with a slope, and of each other shape the set whose name begins and ends most like it, `roof_corner_green_01` with `roof_slant_green_01`) and is worked out once a catalog (every room's recipe asked for it again: with the colours read for each, a Screaming Brain village's build passed 600 s in the interior recipe); `IsoHouse.parts` adds the four posts (the far one after the far walls, the others after the near ones) and `roof_plan`: a cell's ring is how far in it lies from the eaves, the sides nearest them say its piece (one side: the slope rising away from it; two neighbouring sides: the quarter high at the corner across; two opposite or three: a gable along the ridge; four: a pyramid), each ring a slope's rise higher; when a cell has no piece, a gable along every row of the longer way (*Medieval Town*); else a roof tile a cell as before. A door of another pack than the walls' is chosen by the names without the packs' and folders' parts (`_name_of`: Screaming Brain's wood walls got a brick doorway). `MapGeo.EDGE_VERSION` 2. **Synthetic packs**: `iso_kit` gains `roof_slant_01`, `roof_corner_01`, `stone_post_01` and `stone_short_post_01` in four turns (its house: four quarters, four posts; its edge of short walls: the short posts); `iso_named` a thick wall with the wall's end beside each face, a sheet of posts, a town's walls on teal whose corner is painted and roofs in a sheet two pixels wider than its cells. **Goldens**: `iso_kit` (the new pictures, `roof`/`roof_rise` on `roof_point_01`) and `iso_named` (the new sheets; `Thick_Stone_01` is read) move, no other; the `iso_kit` fixture of the GDScript tests is made again. **A/B** against `git archive dee548e`, from the bare packs without a smoke run, `unique_id=` and `uid://` taken out: the village (`sample_pack`), *Green acre* (farm), *Dirt cup* (race), *Stone isle* as an isometric turn-based game and as an isometric farm, and `iso_tiles` as an isometric turn-based game differ in the three builder libraries copied into the project, the runtime's `CHANGELOG.md`, the run's records and `generated/i18n/strings_en.tres` (the same lines in another order; no string changed); `iso_kit` as an isometric turn-based game differs in its catalog, tile set, atlas, maps and house, as meant. **The three games** (each generated in the scratch folder on F:, through its smoke run, looked at in a window): Screaming Brain's *Floor*, *Wall*, *Doorways* and *Town* packs: the edge is the *Town Pack*'s facade with a stone post at each corner, the house thick wood walls with windows, wood posts at its four corners and a *Town* roof on each cell (before: no house, the starter building was a whole sheet of the *Town Pack*); *Medieval Town* with *Nature*: wood posts at the house's corners and two gables along it, the low edge wall without posts (before: four green pyramids); *Miniature Farm*: one hipped roof over the farmhouse and posts at its corners (before: a gable a cell side by side).

T17.31 (autotiling: terrains read from real sheets, maps painted with them). **The probe first** (`tests/probes/probe_terrain_paint.gd`, rows in `docs/godot_api_verified.md`): `set_cells_terrain_connect` with peering bits of -1 where a terrain ends paints the edges right over plain tiles without a terrain and over empty cells alike, with `ignore_empty_terrains` false or true, and changes no cell round the area; painting the terrain -1 empties a cell and repaints its neighbours; `set_cells_terrain_path` joins a cell to the cells before and after it only (a loop closes with its first cell again at the end); on `TILE_LAYOUT_DIAMOND_DOWN` the side bits are `TOP_RIGHT_SIDE` n, `BOTTOM_RIGHT_SIDE` e, `BOTTOM_LEFT_SIDE` s, `TOP_LEFT_SIDE` w and the corners `TOP_CORNER` nw, `RIGHT_CORNER` ne, `BOTTOM_CORNER` es, `LEFT_CORNER` sw. **Godot's choice among tiles of one terrain and peering is not stable** (two paintings in one run differ; it draws from the global generator), so `RpgTerrain.settle` (new, `world/terrain.gd`, the runtime's, used by the builder and the field) chooses again among the tiles of the same signature by the cell and a seed, weighted by `TileData.probability`. **Blocks inside sheets** (`rpggen/core/terrain/blocks.py`; `terrain.generate` on every sheet of a role of ground, water, road, wall, cliff, autotile or interior, or tagged soil or watered, cut by its grid with its margin and separation, a picture rpggen cut taken from its generated file; in a pool of threads, the cancel polled after every sheet): the cells' edges are read once (`_Sheet`), a seam fits within 10 or twice the neighbouring pixels' difference; in order Godot's 47-tile template and the blob in the masks' order as windows of 12 x 4 anywhere (judged as `blob._judge`, the seams that should not fit at least 1/0.85 worse; a window that is a ring round a hole is taken and gives nothing), 16-tile corner sets in their bits' order and, over nothing, in any order (read off the corners' pixels) and edge sets over nothing (off the sides' middles) as windows of 4 x 4, RPG Maker's 2 x 3 (a patch of 2 x 2 painted all over in its middle, its edge on the two outer sides of every cell, the inner corners' cell of the middle's colour; composed into 47 tiles as an A2 sheet's are, `generated.<sheet>_a2_<x>_<y>`), blocks of 3 x 3 (a middle that tiles with itself and is of one colour to its edge, the eight round it fitting it, the edges repeating along their sides, the terrain ending round it) with their four inner corners, each the cell within 4 whose four seams fit (`rpg13_3x3`, else `minimal9_3x3`), and beds one cell wide (a column and a row of an end, a middle and an end with a border on both long sides, of one colour, and the single bed); then plain variants of a middle (tiling with it every way, of its colour, laid at probability 0.35) and groups of plain tiles of one tone (0.035) that tile with each other (`plain`, the variants of a ground; a flat colour has none). Each block is a terrain set of one terrain, -1 outside, with `surface` (by the middle's colour and the sheet's words: grass, dirt, sand, water, stone, snow, path, road, soil, soil_wet, rock, dark), `over`, `shape` (`any`, `area`, `path`, `plain`), `layout` and `sheet`; an RPG Maker A2 block gets the same keys. Whole sheets of a known layout keep their proposals (T8.06c). **On diamonds** (`terrain/iso.py`, after the A2s): flat pieces of a role of road, water or ground are read from their pictures (the sides where the middle's colour goes on, sampled 0.4 of the way out, the corners 0.34; the face's middle taken a few px lower where the face lies lower, Kenney's water), grouped by sheet (Screaming Brain's cells) or by pack, whether any corner is open and the middle's colour, then by name and what lies in their closed corners (a bridge is no road, a shore of sand none of grass); names that give joins (T17.12) are read the way their pictures say (`roadES` the sides the road goes on, `waterE` and `lotE` the land's side, `Corner` the land in a corner); a group of pieces without names must pass edge matching along the sides of the diamonds (the seams that should meet at most 0.8 of the others). A group of three pieces or more is a `sides` terrain when its corners are closed (a piece that fills a corner, a lot or a plaza, left out) else `corners_and_sides`; a piece whose role is not the group's (Kenney's kerbed `street` turns among the `road` pieces) is left out, or a painted road lays it in its turns; the facet `iso_terrain` carries it, `iso_atlas.add` gathers the sets (the tile set's own first), `tileset_recipe.apply_iso_terrain` maps the grid's keys to the diamond's neighbours. **Painting** (`builder/lib/terrain_paint.gd`, `MapGeo`): roads take a terrain of path or road that joins at its sides (else every shape; earth, sand or stone of every shape last), water one of water, patches one of another surface than the ground's, a field's plot dirt, sand or soil of areas first (beds one cell wide in a pack with nothing else, each column a bed), tilled and watered soil `soil` and `soil_wet`; a sheet of a season's copy (`variant_of:`) or one that says dungeon, cave, house and the like comes last. The ground is the middle of the terrain its first tile is a piece of (an A2 sheet's first composed terrain) with its variants; the starter map, the second map and the areas, the interiors, the farm, its added maps (the sea with its shore, the land the pack's sand) and the tracks paint so; on diamonds a terrain over nothing goes on `GroundDetails`. The farm's soil tile set has the terrains of the pack's soil and a sixth tile that marks ground to till and shows nothing; `RpgField` paints a tilled cell with the terrain (`soil_terrain`, `watered_terrain`) and takes it out again at night; the smoke steps ask `shows_soil`. **Maps that exist** are never repainted (all are seeded once under `game/maps/`): each map recipe adds `terrain_v` to its hash only when a tile set has terrain sets, and says TILE-015 for a map that exists and was made from other inputs. **The farm's ground** (`farm_ground.choose`) prefers, of a surface, the tiles that are the middle of a terrain with variants. **Synthetic packs and tests**: `terrain_blocks` (a block of every layout in one mixed sheet of 32 x 24 cells) and `iso_terrain` (a pond named by its land, a sheet of paths without names), `tests/unit/test_terrain_blocks.py`, `test_terrain_paint.gd`. **Goldens moved**: `rpgmaker_like` (the A2 sets gain `surface`, `over`, `shape`, `layout`, `sheet`), `farm_forms`, `farm_sizes`, `sample_pack_farm`, `sample_pack_race`, `gunner`, `platformer`, `sample_pack_gun`, `sample_pack_side` (terrain sets and `tile_overrides` of the blocks and plain variants their ground sheets have), `iso_kit`, `sample_pack_iso` (`iso_terrain` of their road, path and water pieces), and the new `terrain_blocks`, `iso_terrain`; no other. The samples' profiles, the iso fixtures of the GDScript tests, the registry's copy of the profile schema and six pictures of the wizard (the archetype and mapping pages show the samples' previews on the middle of their terrains) were made again. **Overrides as earlier cards recorded them**: *Tiny Farm*'s farmer and hen, *Farm RPG*'s and *Modern Farm*'s four villagers, *Modern Farm*'s placeholders and no decorations, CuteSCKR at 48 px with SakPix's villager as player and its two buildings, the SakPix stage pack's licence stated, the villagers' CC0; the Screaming Brain village is its *Floor*, *Wall*, *Doorways* and *Town* packs with *Pathways* (small, *Dry*) and *Roads* (small, *Realistic*) as packs of their own (their ids say *isometric*: the folders alone do not, and the whole packs made the build pass 600 s before this card); the race is *Vector Roads Base* with *Tiles Vehicles* under their folder names. **Share of the real sheets whose terrains are read** (sheets of tiles in use; before -> after): *Tiny Farm* 0/1 -> 1/1 (its beds), *Farm RPG* 1/35 -> 25/35, *Modern Farm* 0/11 -> 4/11, CuteSCKR 0/2 -> 2/2 and SakPix 0/2 -> 2/2 (plain variants of their grounds only), the village sample 0/5 -> 3/5, *Green acre* 0/8 -> 5/8; of the isometric pieces of roads, paths and water: *Vector Roads* 0/36 -> 32/36, Screaming Brain 0/627 -> 550/627, *Stone isle* 0/25 -> 22/25, *Medieval Town* 0/8 -> 0/8 (plates of road with no joins). **Analysis time** on a warm cache, before -> after: *Farm RPG* 12 -> 14 s, Screaming Brain (six packs) 12 -> 18 s, CuteSCKR 1 -> 1 s, SakPix 2 -> 3 s, *Modern Farm* 178 -> 182 s. **Seen** (whole maps in a window after the smoke steps, and `tools/doc_shots/game_shot.gd`): *Tiny Farm*: the field is rows of its own beds with their round ends (before: one tile of soil); *Farm RPG*: a brick path with its kerbs to the house, the pond with a shore of sand, the plot orange earth edged with grass, a tilled cell a round bed of soil (before: a square pond of one tile, a flat field, no path); *Modern Farm*: the plot with rounded edges, the pond with a shore, thin dirt paths; the village sample: plain grass from its A2 block, sand patches and a sand path (before: the block's corner tile everywhere, a grid of brown lines); *Green acre* and CuteSCKR: grounds that vary a little; SakPix: its flowered grass, varied; the Screaming Brain village: one joined dirt path with its corners (before: the cross piece on every cell); the race on *Vector Roads* and *Stone isle*: the same pieces as before, now laid by the terrain; *Medieval Town*: as before. All passed their smoke runs. **A/B** (`git archive c8fb70e`, bare packs, no smoke run, `unique_id` and `uid://` taken out): `basic16` (turn-based), `iso_tiles` (isometric turn-based and farm) and `ranch` (farm), whose catalogs have no terrain: the same maps, tile sets and scenes; what differs is the builder's copies, the two new scripts, the runtime's changelog, the report, the ledger and `strings_en.tres`'s order; `mixed` as an action RPG fails alike in both (it has no player). **Not done**: cliffs, walls and a cave's rock are not painted with terrains (no real pack has them as a sheet of tiles: *Farm RPG*'s rock is one picture); a road one cell wide is not painted with a terrain that has only a 3 x 3 block; on diamonds the ground, its patches and sand are as before; a plain variant of a ground with a seam of its own (CuteSCKR's) shows the seam; *Modern Farm* analysed with the memory of this machine nearly full gave ADAPT-001 in `sheet_parts` in both runs.

T17.32 (real side-view packs), written from the 63 side-view packs of the card where they lie (Kenney's 29, ansimuz's four, pozak's 20 folders, ten of the *Platformer & Beat 'Em Up Pack*; none of their files is in the repository). **Characters as frames in folders** (`adapters/action_frames.py`, priority 54, before the numbered frames): a folder of two action folders or more (a folder whose pictures are the numbered frames of one stem and one size; `Sprites`, `PNG`, `frames` are looked through: *Hell-Beast*'s `Burn/Sprites`) at least one of them named by an action word in its name or its frames' stem is one character; so are folders of frames of one size side by side in a folder of sprites (*Dancing Girl*'s dances), a generic folder alone under the character (`flying-eye-demon/Sprites`), and a folder whose files are the character's name, an action and a frame's number (`alienBeige_walk1`, `_jump`, `_stand`, `_hit`; strips, wider than twice their height, are the strips reader's). Animations are named by the folders' words without the character's name and the words every action has (`player` of `player-idle`): `attack`, `attack_crouch`, `walk_unarmed`, `run` beside `walk`, with `_down` as the other readers write them; a folder of frames much smaller than the character's (a fireball) is none of its animations; frames of several sizes are laid on the largest, bottom and middle where they were. What the numbered-frame reader already reads as one character (`bat/attack/0001.png`) is left to it (the `sequences` golden is unchanged). The character's strips (as wide as its frames side by side, or named by an action), its GIFs and its Aseprite files are set aside (`same_frames`, `preview_gif`), and in the catalog's side-view pass anything else of a character's role in its folder (an atlas cut in parts, a sheet of all frames) too. The role: *player*, *players*, *hero*, *heroine* (also in the names of files beside the folders: *Warped*'s `space-marine-lite/player.ase`) the player, *boss* the guardian, *npc* a villager, any other an enemy (0.8). **Sheets of frames named by their size** (`side_packs.frame_sheets`): pozak's `Character 160x160.png` is one character, each row of painted frames an animation `anim_<row>` (needs review: no name says which, and its `.ase` has no tags), the islands the object reader cut of it and the pack's numbered pictures of that size (its frames exported one by one) set aside (`frames_of_sheet`); the card's 200 NPC objects of *Dark Castle Interior* were these frames, not its props. **Tile sheets read from their pictures** (`core/side_tiles.py`, `side_packs.read_tiles`, after every pack, the sheets in a pool of threads with the cancel polled as the terrain reader's): a fill is an opaque cell that tiles with itself; every cell painted 85 % and more, 65 % in the fill's colours, is compared with the fills along its sides (a seam within 0.07 and 1.5 times the grain next to the edge, and no outline: an edge that differs from the line through the middle of its cell is a border, so two outlined blocks make no seam), then a surface piece with the pieces of its surface beside it; the sides that join are its peering bits in a terrain set of the mode `sides` and the new shape `side`. Planks (painted across in the top part, empty below, tiling sideways), ladders (two rails, rungs, tiling upwards), spikes (teeth on a base, grey), water (blue, tiling) and coins (a small round gold shape in the middle) get their collision and custom data; every other painted cell stays unread and the sheet's note TILE-016 (new) counts them. *Pixel Platformer*: its sand-topped earth (the 3 x 3 body with its faces and bottom, the one-high ledges, a column's top), its spikes, water and a coin; its grass tops have an outline under them as well as over them and join no fill; its ladders have a dark backdrop and are not read. **Pieces named by their place** (`side_packs._piece`, `_layout`): pictures named by a surface's words and place words (`left`, `mid`, `right`, `center`, `cliff`, `hill`, `half`, `corner`, `ledge`, `alt`, `round`; a number glued to a word dropped) of a folder that has a `center` and a `mid` are laid into `generated/side_terrain/<hash>.png` (pack `generated`): row 0 the terrain (center, mid, left, right, a single block, the cliff ends at probability 0), row 1 the `half` planks (`ONE_WAY`), row 2 slopes and inner corners (read, not painted: rooms have no slopes); the pieces are set aside (`terrain_piece`) as pictures. A ledge, a sign, a door, a lever among the ground's pieces is a decoration. **A sheet of characters** (`side_packs.character_sheets`): a sheet whose own name says characters, in a cell of its own (else the cell of 16 to 64 whose borders no figure crosses), is cut into runs of alike frames (four at most), each a variant `#ch<x>_<y>` with `idle_down` and `walk_down`, the first the player; the sheet is set aside and joins no tile set (before, *Pixel Platformer*'s rooms were floored with its 24-px aliens: the character sheet was the `overworld` tile set). **Parallax sets** (`side_packs.parallax_sets`): the pictures of a folder that says background or layers (or whose names all do), half as wide as high at least, of the most common height (two at least), not all opaque (a folder of whole pictures is backgrounds to choose from: *Remastered*'s eight), ordered by depth words, else by number (turned round when the last is the opaque one: pozak's `1 (5)`), else opaque first; a second whole picture is left out; tags `parallax:<n>` and `parallax_set:<folder>`; `Parallax.layers` uses the set nearest the ground's sheet. **One tile size** (`side_packs._one_size`): the cell of most of a pack's ground sheets (its named pieces' sheets count three); a sheet of another cell is cut again when it divides, else read as a picture; previews and set-aside sheets join no tile set. **Licence above**: `licenses.licence_above` for a folder pack without a licence file, the pack warning `licence_above:<file>` (the card's *New Platformer Pack* "without a licence" was the measuring script reading the `generated` pack: its `License.txt` was found before and is now). **View**: the pack's folder name counts twice, and *beat 'em up* is seen from the side. **The builder**: `SideKit.side_terrain`, and `RoomsRecipe.paint` and `StageRecipe.paint_floor` paint the ground's `#` cells with it (`RECIPE_VERSION` 2 and 4); the probe is the GDScript test `test_side_terrain.gd` (Godot fills every cell of an area in *match sides* with the tiles a pack has, the nearest where none fits). **The 63 packs** (`scratchpad/t32/batch.py`: a picture is read with confidence when an asset of it is CONFIRMED or AUTO_DETECTED, or set aside with a reason): before (`1951bec`) 46 of 14,892 pictures sure (0.3 %), 21.0 % with the set-aside; after 1,250 (8.4 %), 41.4 %. *Gothicvania* 0.1 % -> 58 % sure (86 % with the set-aside), *Warped* 0 -> 34 % (64 %), *Misc* 0 -> 39 %, *Platformer Characters 1* 0 -> 67 %, *Platformer Pack Remastered* 1 -> 14 % (41 %), *Platformer Assets Base* 0.3 -> 11 % (48 %), pozak's beat-'em-up packs 5 % -> 96-100 % with the set-aside (their sheets need review); 208 s for the 63 on a warm cache. Still low: pozak's *Dark Castle Interior* and *Burning Village* props (`.aseprite` sources drawn as strips), Kenney's *Platformer Pack Industrial*, *Medieval*, *Nautical* and *Tile Extensions* (no `center` beside a `mid`), *Explosions and Magic* (effects need review), and the *Platformer & Beat 'Em Up Pack*'s character folders (sheets without sizes in their names). **The four games** (generated in the scratch folder on F:, each through its smoke sequence, looked at in windowed pictures with `tools/doc_shots/game_shot.gd`): *Gothicvania* as a metroidvania: before, the heroine on flat cave rock before a lone tree layer, the kit's spikes; after, the caverns tile set painted as its terrain (its edges), the caverns' own back walls and background as the parallax set, the sheet's bone spikes, the heroine with her named run, jump and attack, its creatures enemies, the dragon the guardian. *Warped* as a run and gun: before, the space marine on a flat dark floor before a grey void; after, the same marine (read from his action folders, the player by `player.ase`) on *another-world*'s tiles before its layers. *Pixel Platformer*: before, no player (a user's override was needed: a numbered alien with a one-frame idle) and every room floored with rows of alien heads (the character sheet was the tile set); after, sand-topped earth with outlined faces, the pack's spikes, the green alien the player and the others enemies, nothing overridden. *Platformer Pack Remastered*: before, no room could be built even with a player and a ground set by hand (the rooms' tile set was the 32-px one, BUILD-002); after, rooms of its dirt pieces with their surface and ends, its lava as the hazard, the beige alien with his walk, jump and climb; the one override is the user's resolution 2560 x 1440 (at 128 px a 640 x 360 view shows five tiles). **A/B** (`git archive 1951bec`, bare packs, no smoke run): the village (turn-based), *Green acre* (farm) and `basic16` differ only in the builder's copies, the runtime's changelog, the error codes and the run's records; the side sample, the gun sample and `platformer` differ in their rooms or stages, tile set, minimaps and catalog (their ground sheets are side terrains now, the gun sample's spikes and the side sample's planks their own); `sequences` as an action RPG fails alike in both (no player). Goldens moved: `platformer`, `gunner`, `sample_pack_side`, `sample_pack_gun` (side terrains of their ground sheets) and the new `side_numbered_platformer`, `side_named_platformer`, `side_action_frames`, `side_layered_platformer`; the side and gun samples' profiles were made again. **Not done**: pozak's beat-'em-up and stage characters have no animation names; Kenney's grass caps with an outline under them, ladders on a backdrop, doors, keys and flags stay unread; slopes are read, not built; a 128-px pack needs a larger resolution by hand; nobody played by hand.

Decisions of 2026-10-15 (the owner's review of the pictures). **Aim:** the owner builds levels by hand from imported assets, which must carry their logic; generated maps are examples, so cards are judged by painting with Godot's Terrain tool and placing from the dock in the editor, beside the pack's own sample picture (`docs/status.md`, *The aim*). The after-pictures of T17.31 and T17.32 were not good enough and had been reported from the agents' words: a visible 48 px grid and a quilt of variants (CuteSCKR), flat grass and a wrong ring round the plot (*Farm RPG*), rock as an outline and a smeared background (*Gothicvania*), box houses, placeholder trees in a ring, props sprinkled, small NPCs; T17.33 now carries these. Pictures are looked at by the session itself before they are reported. **Process:** full suites and CI once per batch of cards; nothing on C:, all scratch under `F:\rpggen_scratch\`. **Local vision (T17.34):** the owner's ComfyUI (`F:\stuff from desktop\ComfyUI`, port 8188) has no captioning node; its llama.cpp build with Qwen3.5 9B and the projector `mmproj-F16.gguf` (`studio\llm\`) answers questions about a picture in about 0.3 s on port 8390 with thinking off and the sprite enlarged with hard edges (a probe named Kenney's alien rightly and *Tiny Farm*'s hen as a cat). Windows had reserved ports 8069 to 8368 (WinNAT); the owner restarted WinNAT and reserved 8188 to 8207 for Image Studio, so rpggen's vision port is a setting, by default outside that range.

T17.33 (everything still unread, judged on levels built by hand), parts 1 to 4 written from the real packs of T17.29 to T17.32 (their commit messages say what each read), parts 5 to 7 after the owner dropped the generated games the same day. **The assets-only project.** `archetype: assets` (`config.ASSETS_KIND`, `view` `top`/`side` or "" until `orchestrator.decide_view` sets it from what most packs are drawn as; isometric packs give `projection: isometric`). The orchestrator plans no world, draws nothing but an isometric pack's atlases and a farming pack's plain ground (`farm_ground`), requires no role, and skips the smoke run and the export check with `VAL-032`; staging lends it the action RPG's and the metroidvania's folders (`BORROWED_FOLDERS`), because its view is known only once the packs are read. `build.gd` runs `ASSETS_RECIPES` and the enemies of its view; `project_settings_recipe` writes no main scene (and takes its own boot scene out of a project that had one); `actor_recipes` builds people and animals without needing a player, with the side-view body when seen from the side; `SideTileset.side_view(config)` is the one test of a side view for the tile set, the objects and the physics layer names; the enemy recipes make no stand-in enemy or guardian. Its README is `README.assets.template.md`. The wizard has its card (first of eight) and pages (mapping without roles, options with the view). **The level probe** (`tests/integration/level_probe/plugin.gd`, run by `level_probe_run.make_level`) makes a new scene in a headless editor (`Ground`, `GroundDetails`, `YSorted/Entities`) with the tile set of most terrains, lays a grass first (else the terrain of most tiles; a terrain of strips alone is painted as straight paths over the plain ground), paints up to four more terrains in blobs with inner corners and a two-cell path in Connect mode, puts a side view's one-way, ladder, hazard and slope tiles, places from the dock with its own `Placement.place_new`, saves, and reports per terrain `wrong` (a piece whose bits do not fit the cells around, judged by Godot's own `get_neighbor_cell` on squares and diamonds), `holes`, `stand_ins` and the main tile's share of the middle, and per placed thing its scales, filters, shapes and frames; `picture_level` pictures the level in a window (`level_shot.gd` run as a scene, so the autoloads are there). The editor's `Parameter "t" is null` on saving is Godot making a thumbnail with no renderer, not ours. **What the probe found and what was done.** (1) Godot's Terrain tool leaves a cell empty when no tile of the terrain fits: a plain ground (one tile and its variants) lost the border of every patch, a side-view ground the ends of a one-tile ledge, *Farm RPG*'s sand painted nothing. `tileset_recipe.complete_terrains` gives every set of one terrain an alternative tile of the nearest drawn piece for each of its 16 (sides) or 47 (corners and sides) ways of meeting others that no tile fits; nearest is a weighted count of differing bits (seen from the side the top 4, the sides 2, the bottom 1; from above 2 each; a corner 1), the main tile before a variant. A plain set matches by its sides. Made once (a source whose terrain tiles have alternatives is left alone), on squares and diamonds. (2) A side ground's fills were all laid as often (the side sample's earth with its dark cave wall: a patchwork): `side_tiles._middles` makes the plainest the middle, fills within 12 % of its brightness variants sharing 15 % of the ground, others laid only where nothing else fits. (3) The side sample's ladder (its rung across the middle row was taken for an outline: `_rails_meet`) and its plank's ends (posts under them: `_plank_ends`). (4) The things of a tile sheet were nowhere in the dock (`sheet_things`): cells no terrain or tile of a kind takes, painted 8 to 90 % on see-through pixels, at most one border painted along half of it, joined to a neighbour where at least a quarter of the pixels both borders paint are of much the same colour and no outline (`_continues`: two things side by side meet with their outlines), groups of at most 2 by 2 cells; on a spaced sheet a joined thing is drawn into a picture of its own so no line of space runs through it; a row of six or more glyphs (little painted, at most four colours in all, a run going on over a row's end) is a font; objects of a background picture or on cells of a kind are dropped (`drop_misread`). In an assets-only project every thing cut from a sheet is in the dock with a collision at its foot (`object_entries._foot`). (5) Reading: an icon read as an item stays one; a person three times as large as another of its folder that only stands is its portrait; Kenney's `Variable sizes` copies are `trimmed_copy`; a fence of a props folder stays a prop (`classifier.UNNAMING_ROLES`); the new strip reader of part 1 leaves strips that name their frames, and folders the strips reader reads by name, to that reader (it had merged the ranch's farmer and the gunner's soldier into wrong characters); a farmer's tool strip is an animation beside the container-folder weights of part 4; a plain ground's middle is its plainest seamless tile (part 3 had taken the sample pack's tufted grass); the terrain names say surface and sheet (`terrain_label`, `sheet_name`, `set_words`). **Measured** (each project built from its packs where they lie with no audit override, a level made by the probe; pictures and reports in `F:\rpggen_scratch\t33\review\<pack>\`): seen from above, *Tiny Farm*, *Farm RPG*, *Modern Farm*, CuteSCKR, SakPix's *Cozy Farming Village*, *Green Acre* and the village sample paint with 0 holes and 0 wrong pieces, the middle the main tile 75 to 100 % of the time; seen from the side, *Gothicvania*, *Pixel Platformer*, *Platformer Pack Remastered* and *Warped* the same (74 to 100 %), their one-way, ladder and hazard tiles found as tile data; everything placed in all 17 is at a whole-number scale with nearest filtering and in the y-sorted holder. **What is left** (for the owner, and for T17.34 where it is reading): the isometric packs are weakest: *Medieval Town* and *Lords Of Pain* give no terrain (Kenney's rendered plates, `plate_road`, `plate_curve`, `plate_corner` in four turns, do not say which sides join; reading their joins from their pictures on diamonds is a job of its own), Screaming Brain's set paints without holes but its middle is a patchwork (15 % the main tile: its isometric variants have no probability yet) and its dock is empty, Kenney's racing packs show 2 wrong pieces; *Warped*'s backdrop layers and *Gothicvania*'s hills and waterfall are placed from the dock as decorations; the things of a sheet are named by colour and shape (`orange_round_15`) until T17.34 names them; *Modern Farm*'s dock holds 3128 things; the wizard keeps the turn-based card selected at first. Runs: ruff, ruff format, mypy; godot-test 960 passed; the unit suite; the integration test `test_t17_33_assets_only` (a top-down, a side-view and a sheet-of-things project, each with a level).

After T17.33, the isometric packs (the owner's word of 2026-10-15: Medieval Town and Lords Of Pain terrains, Screaming Brain's patchwork middle and empty dock), **paused at the owner's word, then finished on 2026-10-15**. **A ground's middle and its variants** (`terrain.iso._middle_chances`): a terrain's whole pieces were laid as often as each other (Screaming Brain's grassy floor: nine of them, 15 % the main tile); the plainest is the middle, those within 0.06 of its colour variants sharing 15 % of the ground, the others laid only where nothing else fits; the builder sets the probability of an isometric terrain tile from its facet (`apply_iso_terrain`). **Plain isometric grounds** (`plain_sets`): whole diamonds of the role `GROUND` that no terrain takes, of one pack, footprint and colour, are a terrain of their own matched by its sides (`shape: plain`), so the Terrain tool paints them and `complete_terrains` gives them their edges (Kenney's *Nature* grass, Screaming Brain's floors, Lords Of Pain's stone). **Lords Of Pain's patches** (`iso_sheets.ground_pictures`, `patch_on`): a see-through ground picture (`ground_variation1`, `2`) is laid on the ground's diamond fading out toward its edges (`PATCH_FADE`), a variant tagged `variant_of` the ground's diamond; the one-colour `ground_darken` is set aside (`ground_overlay`). **Sets of two terrains joined at the corners** (`corner_sets`, the terrain shape `corners`, mode `corners`, two `terrains`, a piece's `terrain` in its facet): pieces of one family (pack, footprint, first word of the name) whose four face corners (`FACE_REACH`) are two flat tones (`_two_tones`: the colour most corners are near, then the one most are near of those `TONE_GAP` away in pieces that also have the first, so a plate of wood drawn as often and flatter than the stone is no tone; then 2-means within half the gap), crisp (each corner within a third of the gap), with both whole pieces and at least six of the ways between them making 30 % of the pieces; run before the side reader for pieces without join names and not cut from a sheet. Kenney's *Medieval Town* plates become one set of 20 (road, pavement, sidewalk, corner, curve; its wood plates a plain set of their own); Screaming Brain's textured floors, whose corners are noise round two means, no longer do. `complete_corner_sets` gives such a set its missing ways (the plates draw 14 of 16). **The dock in an assets-only project** takes every isometric piece of a role it places (a crate, a statue, a house), placed on its diamond. **Probe:** a set matched by corners is judged as Godot paints it (a painted cell is whole). **Finished** (2026-10-15): the synthetic `iso_corners` read as one corner set of 14, its wood plate a plain set and its plainest grass the middle; Medieval Town's plates stay one set of 20 (the darkest-seeded k-means had taken the wood for a tone once the outlier limit was raised). **A variant whose picture is not in its plain set** is a ground of its own there (`plain_sets`): Kenney's *Nature* turns (`_1` to `_3`) of grasses whose first turn is an edge of another terrain made a set of 29 with no main tile (6 %); the variants and the main one's alike middles now share one `MIDDLE_VARIANTS`. **A turn is no variant laid on a ground** (`_laid_on`): Miniature Farm's `planksCornerLeft_E` (tagged `turn:` and `variant_of` its `_N`) is another shape, not a plain ground. **Slopes** (`SLOPE_WORDS`: hill, slope, ramp in the name's stem) are no flat terrain: the racing packs' `hill*` were read as a path of two shades (its 2 wrong). **Corner sets in the builder**: `complete_corner_sets` also makes the ways with a corner on an empty cell (the edge of a painted area, 81 ways but nothing at all), the piece whole to that edge preferred, so Medieval Town's plaza paints without holes; a corner set's two terrains keep their own names (`terrain_label`: "pavement · plate corner 01 0", not both "road"); the reader names the light tone `pavement` when the names say pavement or sidewalk. **Stand-ins** (`complete_terrains`) keep the probability of the piece they copy and take the most often laid of the nearest: Screaming Brain's base set lies in two sheets (flat and thick grounds), and the thick sheet's stand-ins, made of a piece laid only where nothing else fits, were laid round the border and the water as often as the main tile's. **Probe:** a corner set is an area, not strips; a stand-in of a corner set is judged by the piece it shows. Tests: `tests/unit/test_iso_corners.py` (corner set, flat outlier tone, plain middle, Lords-style patch variant, orphan variant). **Measured** (probe, every set rebuilt): Screaming Brain 0 holes, 0 wrong, main 84 %, dock 539; Medieval Town 0 holes, 0 wrong (grass, plaza of road and pavement, wood), main 78 % (was 6 %), dock 739; Lords Of Pain 0/0, 86 %; racing 0/0 (was 2 wrong), 81 %; isle 0/0, 100 %; the 12 top-down and side-view sets unchanged at 0 holes, 0 wrong. **Left:** Miniature Farm's plank plates read their boards' gaps as edges (a path of planks, 2 wrong; its dirt and farmland are plain sets with their main tile): a textured face needs a reader of its own (T17.34's vision step); Lords Of Pain's `filter_vignette` and `highlight_*` rings are placed as decorations (an effect and selection marks); Medieval Town's road and pavement are 5 % apart, so the plaza reads almost one colour.

Gridwright's isometric kits and stacking (2026-10-26, branch `gridwright`; the owner: "stop skipping things. like the buildings with stacking. obviously, ill need stacking"). **The reader** (`adapters/gridwright.py`, begun in `b6b2cdd`) reads all thirteen kits by their JSON and sets nothing aside but copies (2x/3x, `Sheets/`, a character pack's `PNG/` frames, previews, the later frames of an animation, shadows, which are drawn by what they belong to). A tile kit's pieces that were set aside are now tiles with the facet `stack` (`block`, `ramp` with `up:<side>`, `storey` with `facade:`/`level:`/`storey:`, `roof` with `joins`, `on_roof`, `on_storey` with `face:<side>`, `on_wall` with `wall:<side>`, `overlay`; a mine's rails are a ROAD with `rail`) and `lift` (its `height`, the top of a ramp's). A door is a WALL piece on its edge tagged `door`, a post and the corner of two back walls `post: nw` (the schema's corner; `post: True` failed it), a fence or wall that joins nothing a plain WALL (its empty `joins` failed the schema). Frames (`_fN`): a tile gets `tile_frames`/`tile_fps`, a thing a strip put together in the work folder (`compose`, `generated/frame_strips/`) played as `idle_down`; frames that are poses (`open_degrees`, `raised_degrees`, `portcullis_lift`, `arm_degrees`) are a tile a pose (`pose:<n>`) or a thing that plays them once (`open_down`, `use_down`). `shadow` (a project path) for a thing from its manifest's `shadow`, for a character from its JSON or `Effects/1x/shadow.png` / `Effects/shadow_1x.png`. **Characters**: `role_of` (side; an animal's `group`: Farm ANIMAL, Pets DECORATION `pet` as the farm reader's pets, Wild DECORATION `wild` but bear, wolf and boar ENEMY; an army's `unit` ENEMY when it has `attack`, else NPC (bearer, priest, worker); a creator's ready-made one PLAYER of a hero creator, else NPC). **Effects**: a sheet of 32 px cells, 8 rows a direction (`fly_<dir>`, PROJECTILE when its name says arrow, bolt or fireball) or one row (`idle_down`, EFFECT, once); the hero creator's picture-a-direction-a-frame effects are put together into such a sheet; `shadow_bolt` is no shadow (`_SHADOW_FILE`). **Creators**: `layers.json` gives every layer as a piece of a layered character (`lpc_compose` tags, set `layers.json`'s folder + build, parts by `GROUP_PARTS`), so each build is one composed character with its choices; `lpc_compose` now gives the composed character its body's origin when the frames are one size (Gridwright's feet at 32, 46; LimeZu's and the farm golden's unchanged) and its `directions`, `projection`, `shadow`. **Pack names**: `archive.pack_name` names a zip whose stem an id cuts (`ids.PACK_ID_LENGTH`, 40) and that holds one folder by that folder (`gridwright_isometric_tiles`, not thirteen `isometric_map_builder_pixel_art_godot_ti`, `_2`, ...); used by `open_pack`, `default_config_for_pack` and the wizard; a saved config keeps its ids. **The builder**: `iso_atlas` gives a tile that moves a run of cells on one row (`spans`; a tile that had one cell keeps it), pastes its frames, and writes `frames`/`fps` on its tile; `IsoTileset.animate` sets Godot's tile animation; the custom data `lift` and `stack`; `ActorBuild.add_shadow` (a Sprite2D `Shadow` at 35 %, before the picture: a thing's lies at the thing's offset, drawn with its anchor whatever its size (an altar's shadow is 48 x 48 under a 32 x 48 altar; centred, it lay 24 px low and the altar seemed to float), a character's oval is centred on the feet); `ObjectEntries` in an assets-only project makes a thing that moves an object with an AnimatedSprite2D, PROJECTILE and EFFECT objects too, and leaves out the pieces only painted (`on_storey`, `on_wall`, `overlay`); the validator's VAL-014 skips a `Shadow`, lets an EFFECT or PROJECTILE stand on its middle, and in an isometric project lets a figure's drawn feet lie up to half a diamond below its origin (Gridwright's feet point is the ground under the middle; the soles are drawn 1 to 3 px lower). `farm_dress.animal_houses` takes no shadow and no picture a manifest named for a farmhouse (`cottage_left_shadow#house` was a black building). **The dock**: `tools/height_layers.gd` (a layer `height` px higher, `y_sort_origin = height`, meta `rpg_height`, under YSorted; `top_at`, `suggested` from the highest lift of the layers at the top) and *World tools* → *Height layer at* / **Suggest** / **Add height layer**; `placement.gd` stands what is placed on a cell with a stack in a `Raised` Node2D on the cell's middle, the instance `raise` px up in it (it sorts as the cell does). **Tests**: `tests/unit/test_gridwright.py` (stacks, frames, poses, shadows, effects, layers, roles, names), `test_iso_tileset.py` (runs of cells), GDScript `test_height_layers.gd` and two tests in `test_iso_tileset.gd`; the level probe stacks a hill of blocks with a low and a high ramp and a house of three storeys on blocks under a roof whose pieces join (by `roof_NN`), an awning on its front and a thing on its roof, each layer where the dock suggests, and reports `stack`, `raised` of what is placed. **Measured** on Tiles + City + Characters + Hero Creator (`F:\rpggen_scratch\gwp`): generated in 12 min (catalog 160 s, import 217 s), regenerated in 14 min; every picture of the 13 kits is read or set aside as a copy (no picture unread); probe: 0 holes, 0 wrong on its three terrains; layers at 0, 16, 36, 56, 76, 77 (City's README: storey k at 16 + 20 k, the roof on the last); an anvil dropped on the hill stands in `Raised` 16 px up; the ambulances' lights play; shadows under the cottages.
