On the other nine kits (Interiors, Dungeon & Caves, Graveyard & Crypt, Castle & Siege, Wasteland, Monsters, Animals, Army, Character Creator; `config2.json`), after the four fixes below: generated in 21 min (catalog 404 s, import 114 s, build 76 s, validate 30 s, promote 617 s for 100,000 pack files; regenerated: promote 335 s), 21 folders of copies left out of the import; the dock holds 168 buildings, 647 decorations, 44 enemies, 32 NPCs, 11 animals, 3 projectiles, 2 effects, 2 players; probe 0 holes, 0 wrong; the same stack with Wasteland's storeys on its old pavement under its rusty roof; an archery target dropped on the hill stands on it; the altars and the campfires play. **Copies out of the import** (`staging.ignore_copies`, after the regeneration's prune): the nine other kits hold about 90,000 copies (2x/3x, `PNG/` frames) and Godot's import of them timed out at 600 s (BUILD-003); a folder all of whose files are assets set aside as `other_size`, `sheet_frame` or `atlas_copy` that nothing references (an animation's source, `tile_frames`, `shadow`, `heading_files`, an object's sheet) gets an empty `.gdignore` (the outermost such folder, never a pack's root); the markers are added to the run's pack files, so a regeneration keeps them. **Previews are no tile sets** (`catalog.assign_tilesets`): the kits' 1920 x 1080 `Preview/` pictures, set aside, were sources of square tile sets in cells of 8 and 12 px (about 30,000 tiles each) and the tile set recipe ran past 600 s (BUILD-003); a sheet set aside is a source only when the previous catalog had it as one (a painted map keeps its tiles), so no golden and no older project changes. **The builder's catalog reader indexes its assets** (`CatalogReader.asset`): it scanned the list for every id, and `MapGeo.camera_zoom`, asked once an actor, makes an `IsoTileset` that asks for every tile: with 935 tiles and 100,000 assets an actor scene took 40 s and the build ran past 600 s at the actor recipe. The index is made again when the list is another or grew, and a miss scans as before (an entry whose id changed in place). **The things of a preview are none** (`catalog.set_aside_preview_objects`): the previews, read as tile sheets, were cut into 19,600 objects (an object scene each, and a validation past 300 s); an object of a sheet set aside as `preview_folder` is set aside unless confirmed or in use in the previous catalog. **Left:** a stack is drawn, not walked (no height in the game's movement); the vehicles face the diagonals and a race turns by up, right, down and left, so they are placed, not driven.

## M18 (release 1.5.0, finishing)

T18.01 done — French out: `rpggen/gui/i18n/`, `translations.py`, `tools/update_translations.py` and its tests, the `.qm` step of `tools/package.py`, the `I18N` data of `rpggen.spec`, the call in `app.py` and the GUI tests' `install("en")` are gone; `AppSettings.language` is no longer a field (an older file's key is ignored without a warning, and the next save drops it; `test_app_settings`). Deviation: the *Language* choice was in the **View** menu (`window_menus.py`), not in a settings dialog, so it went from there. The runtime's test locale is `eo` with made-up texts; the README and the comment of `RpgSettings.language` name `de`. `tools/package.py's` temporary prefix is `rpggen-frozen-` so the card's `git grep` finds nothing; the CHANGELOG of 1.0 and the older specs and notes keep their mentions as history. `run_tests.gd` takes `RPGGEN_TEST_ONLY` (file-name words, comma separated) to run part of the GDScript suite.

T18.02 done — the dock's Shops tab (`dock/shop_tab.gd`, on `data_tab.gd`): id, title, the stock as rows (an item picked from game/data/items, price, quantity; -1 is the item's value and no limit, and a row without a limit has no `qty` key), sell ratio, the categories bought, restock (minutes, each morning), the shop's gold, the rolled wares (none, the files of `game/data/loot_tables/`, the loot of each enemy, copied into the shop when picked, or the shop's own), buyback and repairs; one undo step a change, JSON export and import (`RpgShopDefinition.to_dict`, `apply_dict`, `from_dict`; `RpgFarmShop` adds its hours, and an import over a farm's file stays a farm's shop). A row whose item has no file is red with the reason, which VAL-034 now gives too (`row_problem`, `stock_problems`, `_check_game_data`). Deviation: the loot tables of a project are embedded in its enemies, so the picker offers those (copied) beside files of a new folder, `game/data/loot_tables/`, which nothing seeds. Tests: `test_shop_tab.gd`.
T18.03 done — the dock's Items tab (`dock/item_tab.gd`): every field of `RpgItemDefinition`, the modifiers as rows, the battle group shown only where `archetypes/turn_based` is installed, *Wear and sets* with a set picker and *Show in Item sets*; the English of the two keys beside them, read from `game/i18n/strings.tsv` by `tools/strings_tsv.gd`, which also appends the rows of a new or imported item's keys. `RpgItemDefinition.to_dict` / `from_dict` (an icon cut from a sheet as `{atlas, region}`). Tests: `test_item_tab.gd` (the sword's durability and the traveler's set from the tab, then a game's bag wearing both: the set's bonus and the slot's `{"wear": n}`).
T18.04 done — with `features.progression` and a village (`data_recipe._starter_shop`), the starter shop has three potions that restock (each morning of a farm, every 10 minutes of play elsewhere: a game without a calendar has no day), 300 gold, buyback and repairs, and the starter weapon a durability of 40; `starter_shop: 2` joins the hash only then. **Repair**: `RpgShopDefinition.repairs`, `repair_rows` (worn gear, then the bag), `repair_cost` (half the value by the share worn, at least 1), `repair`; `RpgGameState.mend`, `RpgInventory.mend_at`. Deviation: the shop menu's **Repair** tab is made by the script beside the others (a duplicate of the Sell tab), not by the UI recipe, so an older game's scene has it too and no scene changes; and "the starter smith" is the village shop, the one shop a new project has. Smoke step `shop_stock_and_repair` (after `shop_buy_sell`): the last potions bought say *Sold out*, a day later (the calendar's next open day on a farm, else the restock minutes of play) they are back, the starter weapon worn by 3 is mended at the Repair tab; run on the turn-based and action-RPG samples and the farm's whole integration suite. A 1.4 project's shop file is never rebuilt (`SeedUtil.missing`) and a config without `progression` builds the 1.4 shop.
T18.05 done — `starter_map.animals` takes `auto` (the houses whose animals the packs have: `animal_plan.pack_houses`, the farm kit's drawn hen not counting, the coop first; `farm_plan.facts_of(animal_houses=...)`, `houses_for`) and `barn`. The Options page lists the five choices and, under one whose house no animal of the packs lives in, says so with a button *Use the packs' animals* (`options.plan_farm` gives `empty_houses`; a stored farm is not asked). Deviation: `auto` is not the default of `starter_map_defaults` but one of **`config.NEW_FARM_OPTIONS`**, the choices a new farm writes into its config (`default_for`, the kind page switching to a farm, Reset on the Options page): the defaults fill missing keys, so a default of `auto` would have changed every farm config of before, which lacks the key (written only when not default). The same holds `exhaustion` (T18.11). Tests: the four choices on hens-only, cows-only, mixed and animal-less packs; the farm sample without its hens (and its profile) plans a barn and no coop by default.
T18.06 done — `RpgSettingsStore.day_night` is a choice once set (`day_night_chosen`); `day_night_on()` is the choice, else the game's default (on with a calendar that drives the light, off elsewhere). `settings.cfg` stores `day_night` (with `day_night_chosen`) only once it was chosen; a file of before, which always had `day_night`, counts only an *on* as a choice. `RpgMapRoot.update_ambient` asks `day_night_on()`, and listens to `RpgSettings.changed`, so the menu's toggle shows at once; off in a farm, the light stays full day (weather tints still apply) and the calendar goes on. The settings menu's toggle is pressed by `day_night_on()`. Tests: `test_day_night.gd`; `test_calendar.gd`'s light test starts from no choice.
T18.07 done — a walking villager looks `LOOK_AHEAD_PX` ahead with the shape of its own body (`RpgSchedule.blocker`, layers of the player and of people; a person's own mask is the world only, so this is what keeps it off a body), slows, steps to the free side (`free_side`: no other body, no wall), keeps to it a moment (`KEEP_SIDE_S`) and passes by a point of its way that the other stands on; with no room it waits facing them and after `WAIT_S` asks for a new way when they move; its walk time counts only while it was free. Deviation: no `NavigationObstacle2D` on the player and no avoidance agents: Godot's avoidance changes velocities a frame late and never the path, and a villager without navigation does not walk at all (it fades), so one steering serves both; the player's scene does not change. Tests: a wide field (it goes round), a walled corridor (it waits, never overlaps, and arrives once the player steps away), another villager in the way.

Test: 20 choices on 640 x 360, the down key reaches the 20th and it is in view.

T18.16 done — the farm option *Cooking* (`starter_map.cooking`, NEW_FARM_OPTIONS): a farmhouse that is being built gets a `Kitchen` (the pack's stove/oven, else drawn, with an RpgStation `kitchen`) against the top wall; one built before has none and the shop sells a stove (`stove_for_sale`, which the mode recipe knows from the room's file), placed with `use` on a map of the farm, which is a kitchen. Dishes (`crafting_recipe.DISHES`): those whose parts the plan has (its crops, its first fish and thing to forage, egg and milk of its animals), each restoring 1.5 times its parts plus 10 and worth 1.5 times them (`use_effect` heal, which a farm's eating reads); the first is known, the others are taught one at a time by a person whose hearts reach a level they never taught at, and on a festival morning. Deviation: the kitchen does not need a room planned for it (it stands against the top wall). Smoke step `cook_a_dish`. Tests: `test_cooking.gd`.

T18.19 done — a pack without a car of eight headings or more on an isometric view gets `arcade_kit.iso_car`: sixteen headings in the order RpgHeadingSprite reads (`up_cw`), each the screen's direction taken back to the ground, a box with a cabin and four wheels seen at the view's angle (the faces towards the viewer, the sides darker), a diamond and a half wide in the project's diamond (`iso_atlas.project_footprint`, passed by the orchestrator); the rivals are its tints as before. The Options page's picture of an isometric race is `map_preview.render_track_iso` (each tile a diamond, a barrier or wall raised half a diamond with darker sides; `RacingBox.set_footprint`). The racing option *Racers and spectators* (`starter_map.racers`, in `config.NEW_KIND_OPTIONS`, which now holds NEW_FARM_OPTIONS beside the race's choices: on for a new race, off for a config of before; the archetype page and Reset apply it): the mode recipe writes `generated/data/drivers.json` (in its outputs and hash only then) — the player's driver is the packs' first PLAYER, every rival's one of their other people (PLAYER and NPC sprites with frames) shuffled by the seed, a name from `DRIVER_NAMES` shuffled by the seed, the face a PORTRAIT tagged `portrait_of:<person>`, the colour of its car — and the mode's export `racers`. `archetypes/racing/race_people.gd` (RpgRacePeople, the track's child `People`, made in `bind_stage` after the race begins): the drivers stand beside their cars at the first count of the lights and get in at the next (every countdown); the marshal stands past the end of the start line and waves at the start and the player's last lap; spectators stand on the cells of the long thin barriers (the walls of the straights), four cells apart, at most twelve; everyone is a bodiless AnimatedSprite2D (or a drawn figure in the car's colour), placed through RpgRaceView on diamonds; spectators and the podium bob two steps. `RpgChampionship.driver_name` gives the driver's name (the player stays *You*), the results put the face beside the name (`grid_texts` reads through it), a rival's skill moves up to `SKILL_SPREAD` by its driver's name, and the last race of a cup puts its first three on the podium. The racing smoke checks the drivers at the grid and in the cars, the names in the results, and ends the last track's race as finished to check the podium. Tests: `test_race_people.gd`, the iso car and picture tests in `test_arcade_kit.py`/`test_track_plan.py`.
T18.18 done — while a library item is dragged, the dock redraws the 2D view each frame (`_process`, the plugin's `set_force_draw_over_forwarding_enabled` and `_forward_canvas_force_draw_over_viewport` → `draw_drag_over`): on an isometric map the diamond under the mouse, every cell of a larger footprint (`stands_on`, read once per scene), and on a raised cell the lifted outline with "+N px" (`drag_shown` keeps what was drawn, for the probe). The drop is one undo step: the scene's history version is noted when the drag starts; when the editor's own drop is the one action after it, `_fix_dropped` (now deferred, after the editor's commit) undoes it and places the scene anew where it was dropped with `Placement.place_new`, and selects it; a drop the history does not know (the headless probe's) is planned and applied as before. `godot.run_godot(headless=False)` opens a window; the dock probe has a `mouse` place (a real drag with `Input.parse_input_event`, each motion with its `relative` (a drag starts once those add up past the threshold) and the real cursor warped along (the editor finds the drop target under it), after switching to the 2D screen, from the dock's item to a cell `offset` from the view's middle, `raise` adds a raised cell first) and stops at once on a script error of its own. Tests (`windowed` marker; skipped on Linux without a display, and in CI unless `RPGGEN_WINDOWED` is set: on GitHub's Windows runner no drag starts from the probe's events, so they run on a person's desktop): `test_the_mouse_drops_onto_the_squares_of_the_sample`, `test_the_mouse_drops_onto_the_diamonds_of_stone_isle`. Also fixed on the way: `RpgShopDefinition`, `RpgFarmShop` and `RpgItemDefinition` are `@tool` (the dock's Shops and Items tabs called methods on placeholders: a script error in the editor).
T18.20 done — `world/levels.gd` (RpgLevels, the map's child `Levels`, made by RpgMapRoot when the map is on diamonds and has height layers): a cell is as high as the highest layer with a tile there plus its `lift` (anything but what hangs on a wall or a storey, stands on a roof or lies over a tile); a ramp is the tile data `stack` "ramp:<low>:<high>:<side>" (iso_tileset `stack_data` from the reader's new facet `ramp`; a plain "ramp" rises by its lift towards n), as high as its ends and between them along its side. Each level has a StaticBody2D on physics layer 17 + its index (eight at most): diamonds on the cells beside its walkable cells that are not walkable at it, and thin segments along a ramp's two sides (a ramp is entered at its ends only). `RpgActor._keep_level` (each physics step, the levels found once) calls `follow`: the actor's meta `rpg_level` (smoothly up a ramp), its Visual drawn that many px up, its mask the wall of its level. `path` is a breadth-first way over cells and levels (up and down ramps); RpgSchedule uses it when the navigation's way leaves the NPC's level, RpgChase when the player is on another level (no way: it gives up). Shots take their shooter's level (`RpgProjectile.level`, the ground level's wall in a shot from the ground) and skip targets above it; a swing (`RpgHitbox.try_hit`) reaches its level and below; an overworld enemy starts a battle only with a body on its level. Terrains of sides: the Gridwright reader gives a block its `side_terrain` (the surface its name says, `surface_word`); the dock's *Give raised ground its sides* (`HeightLayers.plan_sides`/`face_sides`, one undo step) stacks blocks of the surface under every cell of a raised layer whose camera-facing sides (e, s) stand over lower ground, on height layers it makes as needed; the level probe paints a plateau of floor 32 px up and gives it sides. Tests: `test_levels.gd` (the walls, a ramp, the way, an RpgActor walked with the physics up the ramp and against the hill's side and edge, a shot from below), `test_height_layers.gd` (sides), the reader's `ramp` and `side_terrain` in `test_gridwright.py`.
T18.21 done — the user guide, the status, the changelog of the generator and of the runtime, the runtime README and the screenshots (`tools/make_doc_screenshots.py` on Windows' own Qt platform: the offscreen one has no fonts there). Found by CI (`test_determinism_of_a_farm` on Windows, once in two runs): a Translation keeps its messages in a hash table and saves them in that table's order, which is not the same in every run, so the same strings could make a different `generated/i18n/strings_<locale>.tres`. `translations_recipe._sort_messages` writes them by key after saving (a text of several lines stays with its entry); test in `test_languages.gd`. A project of before gets the file once more in that order when it is regenerated; what it says does not change.

Real-pack coverage, 2026-10-28 — at the owner's request, read `G:\All 2D Assets Stay Here` in place and sampled 30 inputs (28 top-down plus two isometric extensions). All 30 analyses completed without errors (one TILE-009 warning); 443 source image files became 13,645 active catalog image entries, of which 9,671 need review. Visually inspected 109 entries: 55 acceptable on checked fields, 27 corrections required, 27 unresolved; 11 correction cases were confident. Three assets-only projects generated and validated; the editor probe found 128 terrain mismatches in LPC Floors, and the isometric floor render showed magenta mask cells despite zero reported topology errors. The report tracks eight findings, including false buildings/interiors, labels and promotional material imported as art, character strips cut as static objects, and battlers cut into fragments. No audit edits were applied and no profile was promoted from unverified answers. Full audit counts, owner approval, first-paint timing, genuinely held-out validation and T17.34 remain open. A short-ID pilot was discarded: pack IDs carry classification clues, so the published baseline uses normal descriptive IDs. Timings are observed with mixed/warm caches, not cold benchmarks. The two Screaming Brain wrapper folders held only RARs; the actual payload archives were used. All seven source archives and 292 accepted folder files were rehashed afterward and unchanged. Catalogs, art, projects, six review sheets, three editor renders, logs and study scripts stay under `F:\rpggen_scratch\real_pack_coverage_20261028\`; only metadata and the report enter the repository.
